Whether this came from utter ignorance, or from familiarity with the human appearance of beings who never hurt them, I could not tell. As I stood once, looking up to the splendid flower of a parasite, which hung from the branch of a tree over my head, a large white rabbit cantered slowly up, put one of its little feet on one of mine, and looked up at me with its red eyes, just as I had been looking up at the flower above me. I stooped and stroked it; but when I attempted to lift it, it banged the ground with its hind feet, and scampered off at a great rate, turning, however, to look at me several times before I lost sight of it. Now and then, too, a dim human figure would appear and disappear, at some distance, amongst the trees, moving like a sleepwalker. But no one ever came near me.

===Chinese===
我走在清新的晨气里，恍如新生。唯一败坏我兴致的事，是一团介于哀愁与欢喜之间的云影，随着昨夜那位女主人的念头频频回返，不断掠过我的心头。“可是话说回来，”我心想，“倘若她伤心，我也无可奈何；况且她依然享有她素来的一切欢愉。这样的日子，对她必定也是喜悦，至少不亚于我。她的一生或许还将因此更丰盈，因为从今往后，其中便存着一段记忆——记着那来过、却不能停留的东西。倘若有一天她真成了女子，谁知我们会不会在什么地方重逢呢？宇宙茫茫，尽有相逢的余地。”我如此宽慰着自己，却仍隐隐怀着愧疚，仿佛我本不该离开她；我继续前行。今天的树林与我故土的树林几乎没有分别；只是所有的野生之物——兔子、鸟儿、松鼠、老鼠，以及数不清的其他居民——都十分驯顺；也就是说，它们并不逃开我，而是在我经过时凝望着我，还常常凑得更近些，仿佛要把我看得更仔细。这究竟出于全然的蒙昧，还是因为它们熟稔了那些从不伤害它们的生灵的人形，我说不上来。有一回，我站着仰望头顶树枝上垂挂着的一株寄生藤的绚烂花朵，一只大白兔缓缓小跑而来，把它的一只小脚搭在我的一只脚上，抬起红眼睛望着我，恰如我仰望头顶那朵花。我俯身抚摸它；可当我想把它抱起来时，它便用后脚把地面蹬得砰砰作响，飞快逃走了，不过在消失于我的视线之前，还几次回头望我。不时有模糊的人影在远处的树木间忽隐忽现，行走如梦游者。但从来没有人走近过我。

===Original===
This day I found plenty of food in the forest—strange nuts and fruits I had never seen before. I hesitated to eat them; but argued that, if I could live on the air of Fairy Land, I could live on its food also. I found my reasoning correct, and the result was better than I had hoped; for it not only satisfied my hunger, but operated in such a way upon my senses that I was brought into far more complete relationship with the things around me. The human forms appeared much more dense and defined; more tangibly visible, if I may say so. I seemed to know better which direction to choose when any doubt arose. I began to feel in some degree what the birds meant in their songs, though I could not express it in words, any more than you can some landscapes. At times, to my surprise, I found myself listening attentively, and as if it were no unusual thing with me, to a conversation between two squirrels or monkeys. The subjects were not very interesting, except as associated with the individual life and necessities of the little creatures: where the best nuts were to be found in the neighborhood, and who could crack them best, or who had most laid up for the winter, and suchlike; only they never said where the store was. There was no great difference in kind between their talk and our ordinary human conversation. Some of the creatures I never heard speak at all, and believe they never do so, except under the impulse of some great excitement. The mice talked; but the hedgehogs seemed very phlegmatic; and though I met a couple of moles above ground several times, they never said a word to each other in my hearing. There were no wild beasts in the forest; at least, I did not see one larger than a wild cat. There were plenty of snakes, however, and I do not think they were all harmless; but none ever bit me.

===Chinese===
这一天，我在林中寻得充足的食物——一些我从未见过的奇异坚果与果实。我迟疑不敢就吃；但转念一想：我既能以仙境的空气为生，自然也能以仙境的食物为生。事实证明我的推理不错，结果还超出了期望；因为这些食物不仅解了我的饥饿，还以一种奇妙的方式作用于我的感官，使我与周围的事物进入了远为完密的关联。那些人形显得凝实得多、轮廓分明得多；不妨说，可见得更为可触了。每逢踌躇，我似乎也更能知道该选哪一条路。我开始多少听得懂鸟儿歌声里的意思，虽然无法用言语表达，正如你无法用言语表达某些景致。有时，我惊讶地发现自己正凝神细听两只松鼠或猴子的谈话，仿佛这于我本是常事。谈的题目并不怎么有趣，无非系于这些小生灵各自的生活与生计：左近哪里能找到最好的坚果，谁磕坚果磕得最利索，谁为过冬储得最多，诸如此类；只是它们从不肯说储粮在哪里。它们的言谈与我们寻常人的交谈，在性质上并无大异。有些生灵我从未听它们说过话，我相信它们从不开口，除非被某种强烈的激动所驱使。老鼠会说话；刺猬却显得十分迟钝木讷；我虽几次在地上遇见一对鼹鼠，却从未听见它们彼此交谈过一句。林中没有猛兽；至少，我未曾见过比野猫更大的。不过蛇倒是有许多，我想它们未必全都无害；却没有一条咬过我。

===Original===
Soon after midday I arrived at a bare rocky hill, of no great size, but very steep; and, having no trees—scarcely even a bush—upon it, entirely exposed to the heat of the sun. Over this my way seemed to lie, and I immediately began the ascent. On reaching the top, hot and weary, I looked around me, and saw that the forest still stretched as far as the sight could reach on every side of me. I observed that the trees, in the direction which I was about to descend, did not come so near the foot of the hill as on the other side, and was especially regretting the unexpected postponement of shelter, because this side of the hill seemed more difficult to descend than the other had been to climb, when my eye caught the appearance of a natural path, winding down through broken rocks and along the course of a tiny stream, which I hoped would lead me more easily to the foot. I tried it, and found the descent not at all laborious; nevertheless, when I reached the bottom, I was very tired, and exhausted with the heat. But just where the path seemed to end, rose a great rock, quite overgrown with shrubs and creeping plants, some of them in full and splendid blossom: these almost concealed an opening in the rock, into which the path appeared to lead. I entered, thirsting for the shade which it promised. What was my delight to find a rocky cell, all the angles rounded away with rich moss, and every ledge and projection crowded with lovely ferns, the variety of whose forms, and groupings, and shades wrought in me like a poem; for such a harmony could not exist, except they all consented to some one end! A little well of the clearest water filled a mossy hollow in one corner. I drank, and felt as if I knew what the elixir of life must be; then threw myself on a mossy mound that lay like a couch along the inner end. Here I lay in a delicious reverie for some time; during which all lovely forms, and colors, and sounds seemed to use my brain as a common hall, where they could come and go, unbidden and unexcused. I had never imagined that such capacity for simple happiness lay in me, as was now awakened by this assembly of forms and spiritual sensations, which yet were far too vague to admit of being translated into any shape common to my own and another mind. I had lain for an hour, I should suppose, though it may have been far longer, when, the harmonious tumult in my mind having somewhat relaxed, I became aware that my eyes were fixed on a strange, timeworn bas-relief on the rock opposite to me. This, after some pondering, I concluded to represent Pygmalion, as he awaited the quickening of his statue. The sculptor sat more rigid than the figure to which his eyes were turned. That seemed about to step from its pedestal and embrace the man, who waited rather than expected.

===Chinese===
午后不久，我来到一座光秃秃的石山前；山不甚大，却极陡峭；山上一无树木——几乎连一丛灌木也没有——全然曝露在烈日的炎热之下。我的路似乎正要翻越此山，我便立即开始攀登。到了山顶，又热又乏，我环顾四周，只见森林仍在四面八方绵延，一望无际。我注意到，在我即将下去的那一侧，树木不像另一面那样贴近山脚；我正懊恼这遮荫之地的意外延宕——因为这一面下山，似乎比那面上山更难——忽然瞥见一条天然小径的影子，蜿蜒穿过嶙峋乱石，沿着一道细流的河道而下，我希望它引我更省力地到达山脚。我试着一走，发觉下山毫不费力；然而到达坡底时，我已十分疲惫，被暑热耗尽了气力。就在小径仿佛到了尽头之处，矗立着一块巨岩，满被灌木与蔓生植物，有些正开着繁盛绚烂的花：它们几乎遮住了岩上一处开口，小径似乎便通向那里。我走了进去，渴慕着它所许诺的荫凉。发觉洞中竟是一间岩穴石室时，我何等欢喜：所有棱角都覆着丰厚的苔藓，浑然圆润，每一条石缘、每一处凸起都挤满了可爱的蕨类，那形体的繁多样式、聚散与浓淡，在我心中激荡如一首诗；因为若非万物同趋于一个目的，这样的和谐便不可能存在！一角石洼生满青苔，一泓至清的泉水盈盈其间。我饮了，便觉得自己仿佛知道了长生不老药必是何等滋味；随后我便在一座苔藓小丘上躺下——它沿着洞穴深处横陈，宛如一张卧榻。我在甘美的遐想中躺了许久；其间一切可爱的形、色、声，似乎都把我的头脑当作一座公共的厅堂，可以不召自来、不告罪而去。我从不曾想到，我身上竟蕴藏着这样单纯幸福的能力，如今被这形与灵感的聚会唤醒了；只是这一切太过缥缈，无法译成任何我与另一颗心灵共通的形体。我大约躺了一个钟头，也许远不止此；待我心中和谐的喧腾稍稍松弛下来，才发觉自己的目光正凝在对面岩壁上一帧奇异而饱经岁月的浅浮雕上。思索一番之后，我断定它表现的是皮格马利翁（Pygmalion），正当他等候他的雕像获得生命之时。那位雕刻家坐得比他所凝望的石像还要僵直。那石像却仿佛正要从座上走下来，拥抱那个等待着、而非指望着的男子。

===Original===
“A lovely story,” I said to myself. “This cave, now, with the bushes cut away from the entrance to let the light in, might be such a place as he would choose, withdrawn from the notice of men, to set up his block of marble, and mould into a visible body the thought already clothed with form in the unseen hall of the sculptor’s brain. And, indeed, if I mistake not,” I said, starting up, as a sudden ray of light arrived at that moment through a crevice in the roof, and lighted up a small portion of the rock, bare of vegetation, “this very rock is marble, white enough and delicate enough for any statue, even if destined to become an ideal woman in the arms of the sculptor.”

===Chinese===
“一个可爱的故事，”我自言自语。“瞧这洞穴，倘若把入口处的灌木斩除，放进光来，倒真像他会选中的地方：避开世人的耳目，在此安放他的一方大理石，把那已在雕刻家无形的殿堂中披上形体的思想，塑成可见的肉身。而且，倘若我没有弄错，”我说着便一跃而起——恰在此时，一缕天光透过洞顶的裂隙射入，照亮了一小片寸草不生的岩石——“这岩石正是大理石，其洁白与细腻，足配任何雕像，纵然它注定要在雕刻家的怀抱中化作一个理想的女子。”

===Original===
I took my knife and removed the moss from a part of the block on which I had been lying; when, to my surprise, I found it more like alabaster than ordinary marble, and soft to the edge of the knife. In fact, it was alabaster. By an inexplicable, though by no means unusual kind of impulse, I went on removing the moss from the surface of the stone; and soon saw that it was polished, or at least smooth, throughout. I continued my labor; and after clearing a space of about a couple of square feet, I observed what caused me to prosecute the work with more interest and care than before. For the ray of sunlight had now reached the spot I had cleared, and under its luster the alabaster revealed its usual slight transparency when polished, except where my knife had scratched the surface; and I observed that the transparency seemed to have a definite limit, and to end upon an opaque body like the more solid, white marble. I was careful to scratch no more. And first, a vague anticipation gave way to a startling sense of possibility; then, as I proceeded, one revelation after another produced the entrancing conviction, that under the crust of alabaster lay a dimly visible form in marble, but whether of man or woman I could not yet tell. I worked on as rapidly as the necessary care would permit; and when I had uncovered the whole mass, and, rising from my knees, had retreated a little way, so that the effect of the whole might fall on me, I saw before me with sufficient plainness—though at the same time with considerable indistinctness, arising from the limited amount of light the place admitted, as well as from the nature of the object itself—a block of pure alabaster enclosing the form, apparently in marble, of a reposing woman. She lay on one side, with her hand under her cheek, and her face towards me; but her hair had fallen partly over her face, so that I could not see the expression of the whole. What I did see appeared to me perfectly lovely; more near the face that had been born with me in my soul, than anything I had seen before in nature or art. The actual outlines of the rest of the form were so indistinct, that the more than semi-opacity of the alabaster seemed insufficient to account for the fact; and I conjectured that a light robe added its obscurity. Numberless histories passed through my mind of change of substance from enchantment and other causes, and of imprisonments such as this before me. I thought of the Prince of the Enchanted City, half marble and half a living man; of Ariel; of Niobe; of the Sleeping Beauty in the Wood; of the bleeding trees; and many other histories. Even my adventure of the preceding evening with the lady of the beech-tree contributed to arouse the wild hope, that by some means life might be given to this form also, and that, breaking from her alabaster tomb, she might glorify my eyes with her presence. “For,” I argued, “who can tell but this cave may be the home of Marble, and this, essential Marble—that spirit of marble which, present throughout, makes it capable of being moulded into any form? Then if she should awake! But how to awake her? A kiss awoke the Sleeping Beauty: a kiss cannot reach her through the incrusting alabaster.” I kneeled, however, and kissed the pale coffin; but she slept on. I bethought me of Orpheus, and the following stones; that trees should follow his music seemed nothing surprising now. Might not a song awake this form, that the glory of motion might for a time displace the loveliness of rest? Sweet sounds can go where kisses may not enter. I sat and thought.

===Chinese===
我取出小刀，从方才躺卧的那块石体上铲去一片苔藓；不料我发现它不像寻常的大理石，倒更像雪花石膏，刀刃触之竟觉绵软。事实上，它正是雪花石膏。出于一种虽不寻常、却也屡见不鲜的冲动，我继续铲去石面的苔藓；不久便看出整块石头通体磨光，至少是光滑的。我继续劳作；清出约莫两平方英尺的一片之后，我发现了令我以更大的兴致与细心继续下去的东西。因为那缕阳光此刻已照到我清出的地方，在它的光泽之下，雪花石膏显出磨光后惯有的微微透明，唯独被我的刀尖划伤之处除外；我又看出，那透明仿佛有一个确切的界限，止于一个不透明的物体之上，如同更坚实洁白的大理石。我小心不再下刀划刻。起初，一种隐约的预感让位于一种令人心惊的可能之感；继而，随着我步步推进，一个启示接着一个启示，产出了令人心醉的确信：雪花石膏的壳层之下，卧着一个隐约可见的大理石形体，只是属男属女，尚难断言。我尽必要的谨慎所容许的速度工作着；待我剥露了整个形体，从膝上起身，退开几步，好让整体的效果落于我眼中时，我看见面前——虽然由于此地所得的光线有限，加之对象本身的性质，同时仍有相当的模糊——一整块纯净的雪花石膏，其中包着一个显然是大理石的、卧憩的女子形体。她侧身而卧，一手垫在颊下，脸朝着我；但她的头发有一部分垂覆在脸上，以致我看不全她整体的神情。我所见到的部分，在我看来可爱已极；比我此前在自然或艺术中所见的任何事物，都更接近那个与我一同降生在我灵魂里的面容。形体其余部分的实在轮廓那样模糊，雪花石膏那超出半透明的浑浊似乎已不足以解释；我猜想是一袭轻软的长袍又添了一层晦暗。无数的故事涌过我的心头——魔法或其他缘由造成的质体变换，以及如我眼前这样的禁锢。我想起那着魔之城的王子，半身是大理石，半身是活人；想起爱丽儿（Ariel）；想起倪俄柏（Niobe）；想起林中睡美人；想起流血的树木；还有许多别的故事。甚至前夜与山毛榉女神的那段奇遇，也助长了我狂野的希望：或许有什么法子，能把生命也赋予这个形体，让她挣脱雪花石膏的坟墓，以她的光临荣耀我的双眼。“因为，”我推想道，“谁能说这洞穴就不是大理石的家园，而这就不是本元的大理石——那遍贯于大理石之中、使它得以被塑成任何形体的精气？那么，倘若她醒来！可怎么唤醒她呢？一个吻唤醒了睡美人；一个吻却隔着包壳的雪花石膏，到达不了她。”然而我还是跪下去，吻了吻这苍白的棺椁；她依然沉睡。我想起俄耳甫斯（Orpheus），想起追随他的群石；此刻连树木追随他的乐声，也不足为奇了。难道一首歌不能唤醒这个形体，让运动的荣光暂时替换静憩的可爱？甜美的声音，能到吻所不能入的地方。我坐下沉思。

===Original===
Now, although always delighting in music, I had never been gifted with the power of song, until I entered the fairy forest. I had a voice, and I had a true sense of sound; but when I tried to sing, the one would not content the other, and so I remained silent. This morning, however, I had found myself, ere I was aware, rejoicing in a song; but whether it was before or after I had eaten of the fruits of the forest, I could not satisfy myself. I concluded it was after, however; and that the increased impulse to sing I now felt, was in part owing to having drunk of the little well, which shone like a brilliant eye in a corner of the cave. I sat down on the ground by the “antenatal tomb,” leaned upon it with my face towards the head of the figure within, and sang—the words and tones coming together, and inseparably connected, as if word and tone formed one thing; or, as if each word could be uttered only in that tone, and was incapable of distinction from it, except in idea, by an acute analysis. I sang something like this: but the words are only a dull representation of a state whose very elevation precluded the possibility of remembrance; and in which I presume the words really employed were as far above these, as that state transcended this wherein I recall it:

> Marble woman, vainly sleeping
 

 In the very death of dreams!
 

 Wilt thou—slumber from thee sweeping,
 

 All but what with vision teems—
 

 Hear my voice come through the golden
 

 Mist of memory and hope;
 

 And with shadowy smile embolden
 

 Me with primal Death to cope?
> Thee the sculptors all pursuing,
 

 Have embodied but their own;
 

 Round their visions, form induing,
 

 Marble vestments thou hast thrown;
 

 But thyself, in silence winding,
 

 Thou hast kept eternally;
 

 Thee they found not, many finding—
 

 I have found thee: wake for me.

===Chinese===
我素来喜爱音乐，却从未蒙受歌唱的禀赋，直到我踏进这仙境的森林。我有嗓音，也有纯正的音感；可当我试图歌唱，这一个总不能满足那一个，于是我只好沉默。然而今天早晨，我不知不觉发现自己正沉醉于一首歌中；只是那是在吃了林中果实之前还是之后，我却无法断定。不过我认定是在那之后；而此刻这愈加强烈的歌唱冲动，一部分也该归因于喝了那口小井的水——它在洞穴一隅像一只明亮的眼睛般闪耀。我在那「未生之墓」旁的地上坐下，倚着它，脸朝着里面那个形体的头部的方向，唱了起来——字与调一同涌出，不可分离地联结着，仿佛字与调本是一物；又仿佛每个字只能在那音调里吐出，除了凭精微的分析在观念上把二者分开，便再无从将字与调分别开来。我唱的大致如下：但这些字句只是那一状态的黯淡摹本——那状态的高华本身便排除了记忆的可能；我料想，我在其中真正吐出的言辞，远在这些之上，正如那状态超胜于我如今追忆它的境地：

> 大理石的女子啊，枉自沉睡
 

 在梦的至死之中！
 

 你可愿——将睡梦从身上挥散，
 

 只留那满盈幻象的一切——
 

 听我的声音穿过金色的
 

 记忆与希望之雾而来；
 

 并以朦胧的微笑壮我的胆，
 

 使我敢与原初的死相周旋？
> 雕刻家们人人将你追寻，
 

 铸入形体的却只是他们自己；
 

 在他们的幻象四周，你赋予形体，
 

 为之披上大理石的衣袍；
 

 而你自身，在静默中缠裹，
 

 你永远深藏不失；
 

 寻者众多，却无人寻见你——
 

 我寻见了你：为我醒来吧。

===Original===
As I sang, I looked earnestly at the face so vaguely revealed before me. I fancied, yet believed it to be but fancy, that through the dim veil of the alabaster, I saw a motion of the head as if caused by a sinking sigh. I gazed more earnestly, and concluded that it was but fancy. Neverthless I could not help singing again—

> Rest is now filled full of beauty,
 

 And can give thee up, I ween;
 

 Come thou forth, for other duty
 

 Motion pineth for her queen.
> Or, if needing years to wake thee
 

 From thy slumbrous solitudes,
 

 Come, sleepwalking, and betake thee
 

 To the friendly, sleeping woods.
> Sweeter dreams are in the forest,
 

 Round thee storms would never rave;
 

 And when need of rest is sorest,
 

 Glide thou then into thy cave.
> Or, if still thou choosest rather
 

 Marble, be its spell on me;
 

 Let thy slumber round me gather,
 

 Let another dream with thee!

===Chinese===
唱着唱着，我热切地凝望那张隐约显露在我面前的脸。我仿佛透过雪花石膏昏昧的帷幕，看见那头部微微一动，仿佛被一声下沉的叹息牵动；我疑心是幻象，却又宁愿相信它只是幻象。我愈加热切地凝视，断定那确实只是幻象。然而我情不自禁，又唱了起来——

> 静息如今满盛着美，
 

 我料想，它已舍得将你放弃；
 

 出来吧，因为运动正渴念着女王，
 

 要她来司掌另一种职分。
> 或者，若须漫长岁月才能将你
 

 从昏睡的孤寂中唤醒，
 

 就梦游着来吧，前往
 

 那些友善而沉睡的树林。
> 森林里有更甜的梦，
 

 你四周永无风暴咆哮；
 

 而当最需要歇息之时，
 

 你再悄然滑入你的洞穴。
> 或者，倘若你仍宁愿
 

 是大理石，就让它的魔咒加于我；
 

 让你的沉睡将我环绕，
 

 让另一个梦与你同在！

===Original===
Again I paused, and gazed through the stony shroud, as if, by very force of penetrative sight, I would clear every lineament of the lovely face. And now I thought the hand that had lain under the cheek, had slipped a little downward. But then I could not be sure that I had at first observed its position accurately. So I sang again; for the longing had grown into a passionate need of seeing her alive—

> Or art thou Death, O woman? for since I
 

 Have set me singing by thy side,
 

 Life hath forsook the upper sky,
 

 And all the outer world hath died.
> Yea, I am dead; for thou hast drawn
 

 My life all downward unto thee.
 

 Dead moon of love! let twilight dawn;
 

 Awake! and let the darkness flee.
> Cold lady of the lovely stone!
 

 Awake! or I shall perish here;
 

 And thou be never more alone,
 

 My form and I for ages near.
> But words are vain; reject them all—
 

 They utter but a feeble part:
 

 Hear thou the depths from which they call,
 

 The voiceless longing of my heart.

===Chinese===
我再度停下，透过那石制的殓衣凝望，仿佛要凭洞察的目光之力，把那张可爱面容的每一处轮廓都看得清清楚楚。此刻我觉得，那只原本垫在颊下的手，已稍稍向下滑动了。可我又不敢断定，自己起初是否把它的位置看得分明。于是我又唱起来；因为那渴望已长成一种灼热的需求——我要看见她活转过来——

> 抑或你就是死，女子啊？因为自从我
 

 在你身旁坐下歌唱，
 

 生命便离弃了头顶的天空，
 

 而外面的整个世界都已死去。
> 是啊，我已死去；因为你已把
 

 我的生命尽数向下摄到你处。
 

 死了的爱的月亮！让曙色黎明；
 

 醒来！让黑暗奔逃。
> 可爱之石中的冰冷淑女！
 

 醒来吧！不然我将灭于此地；
 

 而你也将永不再孤单，
 

 我的形体与我，将亘古依傍你身旁。
> 然而言语无用；把它们全都抛却——
 

 它们吐出的只是一点微弱的余音：
 

 请听它们自怎样的深处呼唤，
 

 听我这颗心无声的渴望。

===Original===
There arose a slightly crashing sound. Like a sudden apparition that comes and is gone, a white form, veiled in a light robe of whiteness, burst upwards from the stone, stood, glided forth, and gleamed away towards the woods. For I followed to the mouth of the cave, as soon as the amazement and concentration of delight permitted the nerves of motion again to act; and saw the white form amidst the trees, as it crossed a little glade on the edge of the forest where the sunlight fell full, seeming to gather with intenser radiance on the one object that floated rather than flitted through its lake of beams. I gazed after her in a kind of despair; found, freed, lost! It seemed useless to follow, yet follow I must. I marked the direction she took; and without once looking round to the forsaken cave, I hastened towards the forest.

===Chinese===
一声轻微的碎裂声骤然而起。像一个倏来倏去的显圣，一个白色的形影，罩着一袭轻软的白袍，从石中迸涌而起，立定，飘然而出，闪逝着朝树林去了。因为待那惊愕与凝定的狂喜容许行动的神经重新运转，我便追到洞口；望见那白色的形影正在树间，横过森林边缘一方小小的林间空地，阳光正满泻其上，仿佛把更炽烈的光华聚拢在那唯一的形体上——它是在那片光的光湖里漂浮，而非掠影而过。我怀着近乎绝望的心情凝望她远去；找到了，解救了，又失去了！追赶似乎无用，却不能不追。我记下她所去的方向；对那被舍弃的洞穴头也不回，便向森林疾奔而去。

## VI / 第六章

===Original===
> Ach, hüte sich doch ein Mensch, wenn seine erfüllten Wünsche auf ihn herad regnen, und er so über alle Maasse frölich ist!

> Ah, let a man beware, when his wishes, fulfilled, rain down upon him, and his happiness is unbounded.

> Thy red lips, like worms,
 

 Travel over my cheek.

===Chinese===
> Ach, hüte sich doch ein Mensch, wenn seine erfüllten Wünsche auf ihn herad regnen, und er so über alle Maasse frölich ist!

> 唉，当实现了的心愿如雨点般倾落在一个人身上，他的幸福漫无边际之时，这个人可要当心啊！

> 你那红唇，像蠕虫，
 

 爬过我的脸颊。

===Original===
But as I crossed the space between the foot of the hill and the forest, a vision of another kind delayed my steps. Through an opening to the westward flowed, like a stream, the rays of the setting sun, and overflowed with a ruddy splendor the open space where I was. And riding as it were down this stream towards me, came a horseman in what appeared red armor. From frontlet to tail, the horse likewise shone red in the sunset. I felt as if I must have seen the knight before; but as he drew near, I could recall no feature of his countenance. Ere he came up to me, however, I remembered the legend of Sir Percival in the rusty armor, which I had left unfinished in the old book in the cottage: it was of Sir Percival that he reminded me. And no wonder; for when he came close up to me, I saw that, from crest to heel, the whole surface of his armor was covered with a light rust. The golden spurs shone, but the iron greaves glowed in the sunlight. The “morning star,” which hung from his wrist, glittered and glowed with its silver and bronze. His whole appearance was terrible; but his face did not answer to this appearance. It was sad, even to gloominess; and something of shame seemed to cover it. Yet it was noble and high, though thus beclouded; and the form looked lofty, although the head drooped, and the whole frame was bowed as with an inward grief. The horse seemed to share in his master’s dejection, and walked spiritless and slow. I noticed, too, that the white plume on his helmet was discolored and drooping. “He has fallen in a joust with spears,” I said to myself; “yet it becomes not a noble knight to be conquered in spirit because his body hath fallen.” He appeared not to observe me, for he was riding past without looking up, and started into a warlike attitude the moment the first sound of my voice reached him. Then a flush, as of shame, covered all of his face that the lifted beaver disclosed. He returned my greeting with distant courtesy, and passed on. But suddenly, he reined up, sat a moment still, and then turning his horse, rode back to where I stood looking after him.

===Chinese===
可当我穿过山脚与森林之间的那片空地时，另一类的异象又延阻了我的脚步。落日的光芒从西面一处豁口涌来，宛如一道溪流，以红艳的辉光漫溢着我所在的空地。一名骑士仿佛正顺着这道光流朝我驰来，他身上的甲胄看上去是红色的。那马从头额到尾梢，也在落日里通体闪着红光。我觉得自己仿佛从前必定见过这位骑士；可当他驰近，我却记不起他容貌的任何特征。不过，在他来到近前之前，我已想起小屋里那本旧书中、我未曾读完的那则披锈甲的珀西瓦尔爵士（Sir Percival）的传奇：他使我想起的正是珀西瓦尔爵士。这也无怪；因为当他紧挨着我时，我看见，自盔顶至脚跟，他甲胄的整个表面都覆着一层薄锈。金马刺灿灿生光，铁胫甲却在日光中泛出暗红。悬在他腕上的那颗「晨星」锤，银与铜的质地上闪烁辉耀。他的全副气象令人生畏；他的面容却与这气象殊不相称。那面容是悲哀的，甚而近于阴沉；其上仿佛还蒙着一层羞惭。虽然如此晦暗，它却依然高贵而崇隆；那身形也显得轩昂，虽然头颅低垂，整个躯干都伛偻着，像负着一种内心的悲恸。那马似乎也分担着主人的沮丧，走得无精打采、缓慢迟滞。我还注意到，他盔上的白翎已经褪色、低垂。「他是在长枪比武中跌落了，」我自忖；「然而身体既已跌倒，精神便也随之被征服——这于高贵骑士殊不相宜。」他似乎没有察觉我，因为他正头也不抬地骑了过去；可我的话音甫一入耳，他便蓦然摆出临战的姿态。随即，一阵仿佛羞惭的红晕，涌上他掀起的护面所露出的整张脸。他以疏隔的礼数还了我问候，便继续前行。但忽然，他勒缰立马，静坐了片刻，随即拨转马头，驰回我伫立目送他的地方。

===Original===
“I am ashamed,” he said, “to appear a knight, and in such a guise; but it behooves me to tell you to take warning from me, lest the same evil, in his kind, overtake the singer that has befallen the knight. Hast thou ever read the story of Sir Percival and the”—(here he shuddered, that his armor rang)—“Maiden of the Alder-tree?”

“In part, I have,” said I; “for yesterday, at the entrance of this forest, I found in a cottage the volume wherein it is recorded.”

“Then take heed,” he rejoined; “for, see my armor;—I put it off; and as it befell to him, so has it befallen to me. I that was proud am humble now. Yet is she terribly beautiful—beware. Never,” he added, raising his head, “shall this armor be furbished, but by the blows of knightly encounter, until the last speck has disappeared from every spot where the battle-axe and sword of evildoers, or noble foes, might fall; when I shall again lift my head, and say to my squire, ‘Do thy duty once more, and make this armor shine.’ ”

===Chinese===
「我很羞惭，」他说，「身为骑士，却以这副模样示人；但我不能不奉告你，请以我为鉴戒，免得那降临在骑士身上的邪恶，改换其类，也降临到歌者身上。你可曾读过珀西瓦尔爵士与——」（说到此处他一颤栗，甲叶铮然作响）「——赤杨树少女（Maiden of the Alder-tree）的故事？」

「读过一部分，」我说；「因为昨天，在这座森林的入口处，我在一间小屋里找到了记载这故事的那本书。」

「那么千万留心，」他答道；「瞧我这副甲胄；——是我将它卸下的；他如何遭际，我也如何遭际。昔日倨傲的我，如今谦卑了。然而她美得骇人——务必提防。除非凭着骑士交锋的击打，」他抬起头来，又道，「这副甲胄再无磨亮之日；要等到恶徒或高贵敌手的战斧与长剑所能砍落的每一处地方，最后一点锈斑都已消尽；到那时，我才重新昂起头来，对我的侍从说：『再尽一次你的本分，让这副甲胄焕发光彩。』」

===Original===
Before I could inquire further, he had struck spurs into his horse and galloped away, shrouded from my voice in the noise of his armor. For I called after him, anxious to know more about this fearful enchantress; but in vain—he heard me not. “Yet,” I said to myself, “I have now been often warned; surely I shall be well on my guard; and I am fully resolved I shall not be ensnared by any beauty, however beautiful. Doubtless, some one man may escape, and I shall be he.” So I went on into the wood, still hoping to find, in some one of its mysterious recesses, my lost lady of the marble. The sunny afternoon died into the loveliest twilight. Great bats began to flit about with their own noiseless flight, seemingly purposeless, because its objects are unseen. The monotonous music of the owl issued from all unexpected quarters in the half-darkness around me. The glowworm was alight here and there, burning out into the great universe. The nighthawk heightened all the harmony and stillness with his oft-recurring, discordant jar. Numberless unknown sounds came out of the unknown dusk; but all were of twilight-kind, oppressing the heart as with a condensed atmosphere of dreamy undefined love and longing. The odors of night arose, and bathed me in that luxurious mournfulness peculiar to them, as if the plants whence they floated had been watered with bygone tears. Earth drew me towards her bosom; I felt as if I could fall down and kiss her. I forgot I was in Fairy Land, and seemed to be walking in a perfect night of our own old nursing earth. Great stems rose about me, uplifting a thick multitudinous roof above me of branches, and twigs, and leaves—the bird and insect world uplifted over mine, with its own landscapes, its own thickets, and paths, and glades, and dwellings; its own bird-ways and insect-delights. Great boughs crossed my path; great roots based the tree-columns, and mightily clasped the earth, strong to lift and strong to uphold. It seemed an old, old forest, perfect in forest ways and pleasures. And when, in the midst of this ecstasy, I remembered that under some close canopy of leaves, by some giant stem, or in some mossy cave, or beside some leafy well, sat the lady of the marble, whom my songs had called forth into the outer world, waiting (might it not be?) to meet and thank her deliverer in a twilight which would veil her confusion, the whole night became one dream-realm of joy, the central form of which was everywhere present, although unbeheld. Then, remembering how my songs seemed to have called her from the marble, piercing through the pearly shroud of alabaster—“Why,” thought I, “should not my voice reach her now, through the ebon night that inwraps her.” My voice burst into song so spontaneously that it seemed involuntarily.

===Chinese===
不等我再问下去，他已把马刺刺进马腹，疾驰而去，铿锵的甲胄之声把我呼唤的声音隔绝在外。因为我曾在他身后呼唤，急欲多知道一些这位可怖妖妇的事；却是徒然——他没有听见。「然而，」我自忖，「我如今已屡受警诫；我必然会严加提防；我已拿定主意，决不被任何美貌诱陷，无论她多么美。无疑，总会有一个人得以逃脱，而我就会是那一个人。」于是我继续向林中走去，仍盼着能在这神秘林莽的某处幽僻角落，寻回我那失落的大理石少女。晴朗的午后渐渐死去，化作最可爱的薄暮。巨大的蝙蝠开始翩然飞掠，以它们那种无声的飞翔，看似全无目的，因为其目标非肉眼所能见。猫头鹰单调的音乐，从我四周半明半暗处一切意想不到的角落里传出。萤火虫这里那里地亮起来，向着浩大的宇宙燃烁着自己的光。夜鹰那反复响起的、不谐和的鸣噪，反而使这一切和谐与静谧愈加深邃。无数未知的声音从未知的昏暝中传出；但全都带着薄暮的气质，仿佛以一层凝缩的大气覆压着心，那大气满是如梦的、无从名状的爱与渴慕。夜的芬芳升起来，以它们特有的那种丰饶的哀感沐浴着我，仿佛那些芬芳所自浮出的草木，都曾被逝去的泪水浇灌过。大地牵引我趋向她的怀抱；我觉得自己简直可以扑倒在地，亲吻她。我忘了自己身在仙境，仿佛正走在我们自己那古老的、哺育过我们的尘世的一个十足的夜里。粗大的树干在我四周耸起，在我头顶擎起一座由枝、桠、叶构成的厚重繁复的屋顶——那是一个鸟与虫的世界，被高举在我的世界之上，自有它的山水景致，自有它的丛莽、小径、林间空地与居所；自有它的禽鸟之途与虫豸之乐。巨大的树枝横过我的去路；巨大的根株稳立着那一根根树木的廊柱，雄劲地攀抱大地，既能奋力擎举，又能坚牢撑持。这仿佛是一片古老而又古老的森林，于森林的种种法度与欢趣无不圆熟具足。而当我正在这心醉神迷之中，想起在某处密叶织成的华盖之下、某株巨干之旁，或在某个生苔的洞窟里，或在某眼绿叶掩映的泉井畔，正坐着那位大理石少女——我的歌声曾把她召到外面的世界，此刻她正等待着（岂不正是？）在一层足以掩住她羞赧的薄暮里，与她的搭救者相会并向他致谢——整个黑夜便化作一个欢乐的梦境之国，那梦境的中心形象虽不可见，却无处不在。继而，想起我的歌声似乎曾穿透雪花石膏那珠贝色的殓衣，把她从大理石中唤了出来——「那么如今，」我想，「我的声音为何不能穿透这裹着她的乌黑夜幕，抵达她那里呢。」我的声音便这样自发地迸发为歌，竟似出于无意。

===Original===
> Not a sound
 

 But, echoing in me,
 

 Vibrates all around
 

 With a blind delight,
 

 Till it breaks on thee,
 

 Queen of Night!
> Every tree,
 

 O’ershadowing with gloom,
 

 Seems to cover thee
 

 Secret, dark, love-still’d,
 

 In a holy room
 

 Silence-filled.
> Let no moon
 

 Creep up the heaven tonight;
 

 I in darksome noon
 

 Walking hopefully,
 

 Seek my shrouded light—
 

 Grope for thee!
> Darker grow
 

 The borders of the dark!
 

 Through the branches glow!
 

 From the roof above,
 

 Star and diamond-spark,
 

 Light for love.

===Chinese===
> 没有一点声响
 

 只在我心中回荡，
 

 在四下里震颤
 

 带着盲目的欢喜，
 

 直到它碎落在你身上，
 

 夜的女王！
> 每一棵树，
 

 以幽暗垂荫，
 

 似乎都要把你掩住
 

 隐秘、幽暗、为爱所静，
 

 在一间圣洁的屋里
 

 充满静默。
> 莫让月亮
 

 今夜爬上天穹；
 

 我在昏黑的正午里
 

 满怀希望地行走，
 

 寻找我被裹藏的光——
 

 摸索着你！
> 愈发昏暗了，
 

 黑暗的边界！
 

 透过枝叶闪耀吧！
 

 从头顶的穹顶上，
 

 星与金刚石的火花，
 

 为爱而发光。

===Original===
Scarcely had the last sounds floated away from the hearing of my own ears, when I heard instead a low delicious laugh near me. It was not the laugh of one who would not be heard, but the laugh of one who has just received something long and patiently desired—a laugh that ends in a low musical moan. I started, and, turning sideways, saw a dim white figure seated beside an intertwining thicket of smaller trees and underwood.

===Chinese===
末一个音几乎还未从我自己的耳畔飘散尽，我便听见近旁响起了一声低低的、甘美的轻笑。那不是一个不愿被人听见者的笑，而是一个刚刚得到自己久已耐心渴慕之物的笑——一声终于低抑成如乐的呻吟的笑。我悚然一惊，侧身望去，只见一个朦胧的白影，坐在一丛较小树木与矮林交缠纠结的灌木旁。

===Original===
“It is my white lady!” I said, and flung myself on the ground beside her; striving, through the gathering darkness, to get a glimpse of the form which had broken its marble prison at my call.

“It is your white lady!” said the sweetest voice, in reply, sending a thrill of speechless delight through a heart which all the love charms of the preceding day and evening had been tempering for this culminating hour. Yet, if I would have confessed it, there was something either in the sound of the voice, although it seemed sweetness itself, or else in this yielding which awaited no gradation of gentle approaches, that did not vibrate harmoniously with the beat of my inward music. And likewise, when, taking her hand in mine, I drew closer to her, looking for the beauty of her face, which, indeed, I found too plenteously, a cold shiver ran through me; but “it is the marble,” I said to myself, and heeded it not.

===Chinese===
「我的白衣女郎！」我说着，便扑身倒在她身旁的地上；竭力想透过愈聚愈浓的夜色，看一眼那个应我呼唤而挣破大理石囚牢的形体。

「是你的白衣女郎！」那最最甜美的声音答道；一阵无可言喻的欢喜随即直贯心底——这颗心，已被前一日与前一夜的种种爱的魅术淬炼停当，只待这一圆满时刻的到来。然而，倘若我肯承认的话，无论那嗓音的音色——尽管它甜得无以复加——还是这全然不等温柔阶梯逐级递进的委身，其中总有某种东西，与我内心乐曲的节拍不能谐和地共振。同样，当我握起她的手放在我的手中，向她凑近，瞻望她面容的美——那美我也确实寻见得丰盈有余——一阵冷颤却贯遍我全身；但「这只是大理石罢了」，我对自己说，便没有在意。

===Original===
She withdrew her hand from mine, and after that would scarce allow me to touch her. It seemed strange, after the fullness of her first greeting, that she could not trust me to come close to her. Though her words were those of a lover, she kept herself withdrawn as if a mile of space interposed between us.

“Why did you run away from me when you woke in the cave?” I said.

“Did I?” she returned. “That was very unkind of me; but I did not know better.”

===Chinese===
她把手从我手中抽了回去，此后便几乎不容我再碰触她。在她最初问候的那一番丰盈之后，她竟不能放心让我挨近她，这显得很奇怪。她的话语虽出自一个情人之口，她自己却始终退守着，仿佛有一英里之遥的空间横隔在我们之间。

「你在洞窟里醒来的时候，为什么从我身边跑开了？」我说。

「我跑开过吗？」她答道。「那是我太无情了；只是我那时还不懂得别样做法。」

===Original===
“I wish I could see you. The night is very dark.”

“So it is. Come to my grotto. There is light there.”

“Have you another cave, then?”

“Come and see.”

===Chinese===
「但愿我能看见你。夜色真黑。」

「是黑。到我岩窟里来吧。那里有光。」

「这么说，你还有另一处洞穴？」

「来瞧瞧吧。」

===Original===
But she did not move until I rose first, and then she was on her feet before I could offer my hand to help her. She came close to my side, and conducted me through the wood. But once or twice, when, involuntarily almost, I was about to put my arm around her as we walked on through the warm gloom, she sprang away several paces, always keeping her face full towards me, and then stood looking at me, slightly stooping, in the attitude of one who fears some half-seen enemy. It was too dark to discern the expression of her face. Then she would return and walk close beside me again, as if nothing had happened. I thought this strange; but, besides that I had almost, as I said before, given up the attempt to account for appearances in Fairy Land, I judged that it would be very unfair to expect from one who had slept so long and had been so suddenly awakened, a behavior correspondent to what I might unreflectingly look for. I knew not what she might have been dreaming about. Besides, it was possible that, while her words were free, her sense of touch might be exquisitely delicate.

===Chinese===
可她并不移动，直到我先站起身来；随后，还没等我伸手相扶，她已立在足尖之上了。她紧偎我的身侧，领着我穿行林中。但有一两次，当我们行在温软的幽暗里，我几乎出于不由自主，正要把手臂环上她的身，她便倏然跳开几步，脸却始终完完全全地朝着我，随后微微伛着身立定，摆出一个惧怕什么半隐半现的仇敌的姿势，定定望着我。夜色太暗，辨不出她脸上的神情。之后她又走回来，重新紧偎着我走，仿佛什么都不曾发生。我觉得这事蹊跷；但一来，我几乎已经——如前文所说——放弃了为仙境中的种种情状寻索因由的尝试；二来我判定，对一个沉睡如此之久、又被如此猝然唤醒的人，期望她的举止一一合乎我未经思量便会怀有的期待，是很不公平的。我不知道她一直梦见的会是什么。再者，也有可能：她言语虽然自如，触觉却纤敏异常。

===Original===
At length, after walking a long way in the woods, we arrived at another thicket, through the intertexture of which was glimmering a pale rosy light.

“Push aside the branches,” she said, “and make room for us to enter.”

I did as she told me.

“Go in,” she said; “I will follow you.”

===Chinese===
终于，在林中走了很远的一段路之后，我们来到又一丛灌木跟前，交织的枝叶之间，隐约闪烁着一抹淡淡的玫瑰色的光。

「把枝条拨开，」她说，「腾出个空儿让我们进去。」

我照她说的做了。

「进去，」她说；「我跟着你。」

===Original===
I did as she desired, and found myself in a little cave, not very unlike the marble cave. It was festooned and draperied with all kinds of green that cling to shady rocks. In the furthest corner, half-hidden in leaves, through which it glowed, mingling lovely shadows between them, burned a bright rosy flame on a little earthen lamp. The lady glided round by the wall from behind me, still keeping her face towards me, and seated herself in the furthest corner, with her back to the lamp, which she hid completely from my view. I then saw indeed a form of perfect loveliness before me. Almost it seemed as if the light of the rose-lamp shone through her (for it could not be reflected from her); such a delicate shade of pink seemed to shadow what in itself must be a marbly whiteness of hue. I discovered afterwards, however, that there was one thing in it I did not like; which was, that the white part of the eye was tinged with the same slight roseate hue as the rest of the form. It is strange that I cannot recall her features; but they, as well as her somewhat girlish figure, left on me simply and only the impression of intense loveliness. I lay down at her feet, and gazed up into her face as I lay.

===Chinese===
我依她所愿走了进去，发觉自己置身于一个小小的岩窟，与那大理石的岩穴颇为相像。窟中如结彩张帷，缀悬着各种各样攀附幽岩的绿植。在最深处的那个角落，半隐在枝叶之间，一盏小小的陶灯上燃着一簇明亮的玫瑰色火焰，光透过叶隙，在叶与叶之间织入可爱的影子。女郎贴着墙，从我身后悄然滑行过去，脸始终朝着我，在极深处的角落坐了下来，背对着那盏灯，把灯光完全遮住，不让我望见。于是我真的看见一个全然可爱的形体在我面前。那玫瑰灯的光几乎像是透过了她的身体（因为它不可能从她身上反射出来）；一抹那样娇嫩的粉色阴影，覆在本必然是大理石般白皙的色泽之上。然而我后来才发现，其中有一点是我不喜欢的：那便是她眼白的部分，也染着与形体其余部分相同的那层淡淡的玫瑰色。奇怪的是，她的容貌我竟无从追忆；它们，连同她那略显少女气的身姿，留给我的印象，单纯而唯一，只是极度可爱的印象。我在她脚边躺下来，就那样躺着，仰望她的脸。

===Original===
She began, and told me a strange tale, which, likewise, I cannot recollect; but which, at every turn and every pause, somehow or other fixed my eyes and thoughts upon her extreme beauty; seeming always to culminate in something that had a relation, revealed or hidden, but always operative, with her own loveliness. I lay entranced. It was a tale which brings back a feeling as of snows and tempests; torrents and water-sprites; lovers parted for long, and meeting at last; with a gorgeous summer night to close up the whole. I listened till she and I were blended with the tale; till she and I were the whole history. And we had met at last in this same cave of greenery, while the summer night hung round us heavy with love, and the odors that crept through the silence from the sleeping woods were the only signs of an outer world that invaded our solitude. What followed I cannot clearly remember. The succeeding horror almost obliterated it. I woke as a gray dawn stole into the cave. The damsel had disappeared; but in the shrubbery at the mouth of the cave, stood a strange horrible object. It looked like an open coffin set up on one end; only that the part for the head and neck was defined from the shoulder-part. In fact it was a rough representation of the human frame, only hollow, as if made of decaying bark torn from a tree. It had arms, which were only slightly seamed, down from the shoulder-blade by the elbow, as if the bark had healed again from the cut of a knife. But the arms moved, and the hand and the fingers were tearing asunder a long silky tress of hair. The thing turned round—it had for a face and front those of my enchantress, but now of a pale greenish hue in the light of the morning, and with dead lusterless eyes. In the horror of the moment, another fear invaded me. I put my hand to my waist, and found indeed that my girdle of beech-leaves was gone. Hair again in her hands, she was tearing it fiercely. Once more, as she turned, she laughed a low laugh, but now full of scorn and derision; and then she said, as if to a companion with whom she had been talking while I slept, “There he is; you can take him now.” I lay still, petrified with dismay and fear; for I now saw another figure beside her, which, although vague and indistinct, I yet recognised but too well. It was the Ash-tree. My beauty was the Maid of the Alder! and she was giving me, spoiled of my only availing defence, into the hands of my awful foe. The Ash bent his Gorgon-head, and entered the cave. I could not stir. He drew near me. His ghoul-eyes and his ghastly face fascinated me. He came stooping, with the hideous hand outstretched, like a beast of prey. I had given myself up to a death of unfathomable horror, when, suddenly, and just as he was on the point of seizing me, the dull, heavy blow of an axe echoed through the wood, followed by others in quick repetition. The Ash shuddered and groaned, withdrew the outstretched hand, retreated backwards to the mouth of the cave, then turned and disappeared amongst the trees. The other walking Death looked at me once, with a careless dislike on her beautifully moulded features; then, heedless any more to conceal her hollow deformity, turned her frightful back and likewise vanished amid the green obscurity without. I lay and wept. The Maid of the Alder-tree had befooled me—nearly slain me—in spite of all the warnings I had received from those who knew my danger.

===Chinese===
她开了口，给我讲了一个奇异的故事；那故事我也同样无从追忆了；但它每一次转折、每一度停顿，总会以这样那样的方式，把我的目光与思虑牢牢系在她的极致之美上；它仿佛总在某种事物上达于顶点，而那事物与她自身的可爱之间总有一种关系——或显或隐，却始终在起作用。我卧在那里，心醉神迷。那是一个如今追忆起来，便唤回一种仿佛冰雪与暴风雨之感的故事；其中有湍流，有水精；有久别的恋人终于重聚；末了，以一个华美的夏夜收束全篇。我听着听着，直到她与我一同融进了故事；直到她与我就成了整部历史。而我们终于就在这同一座绿荫的洞窟里相会了，夏夜沉沉地悬在我们四周，满载着爱；从沉睡的林木间潜越过寂静袭来的芬芳，便是侵入我们孤独的唯一的外部世界的征象。此后发生的事我不能清楚记得了。接踵而至的恐怖几乎把它涂抹净尽。当一道灰白的晨光潜入洞窟时，我醒了。那女郎已经不见；但在洞口的灌木丛中，立着一个古怪而可怖的东西。它看上去像一具竖立起来的敞开的棺材；只是头颈的部分与肩部有所分界。实际上，那是一个人类躯壳的粗劣摹形，只是中间镂空，仿佛用从树上撕下的腐朽树皮做成。它有双臂，臂上只依稀有些缝合的细痕，自肩胛延到肘部，仿佛树皮在刀割之后又自行愈合了。但那手臂在动，那只手与那些手指，正在撕扯一根长长的、丝绸一般的发生。那东西转过身来——它拿来做脸孔与前脸的，正是我那妖妇的脸孔，只是此刻在晨光里泛着一层灰绿之色，一双眼睛死灭而无光泽。在这惊怖的一刻，另一种恐惧又侵袭了我。我把手伸向腰间，发觉我的山毛榉叶腰带确实不在了。头发又一次到了她手里，她正凶狠地撕扯着。当她再一次转过身来时，又低低地笑了一声，但此刻那笑里满是轻蔑与讥嘲；然后她说，仿佛在对一个同伴说话——在我睡着的时候，她想必一直同那同伴交谈着——「他在那儿了；你现在可以把他带走。」我一动不动地躺着，惊骇与恐惧把我石化了；因为此刻我在她身旁看见了另一个身影，那身影虽然模糊难辨，我却认得太清楚了。那是那株梣树。我的美人正是赤杨树少女！而她正把我——在夺去我唯一有效的防御之后——交到我那可怖仇敌的手中。梣树垂下他那戈耳工式的头颅，走进洞来。我动弹不得。他向我逼近。他那食尸鬼的眼睛和他那死白的脸，把我摄住了。他躬着身走近，伸出那只可憎的手，像一头食肉的野兽。我已自认必死于这深不可测的恐怖，忽然——恰在他将要抓住我的那一刻——一声沉钝而沉重的斧击之声在林中回荡起来，紧接着又是一声声急促的重复。梣树战栗、呻吟，缩回伸出的手，倒退着退到洞口，随即转过身，没入树木之间。那个行走的死神看了我一眼，她那雕琢极美的面容上带着一种漫不经心的厌恶；随后，她不再顾忌掩藏自己那中空的畸形，转过她那可怖的背影，同样消失在外面幽绿的暗昧之中。我躺着哭泣。赤杨树少女愚弄了我——几乎杀死了我——尽管深知我处境危险的人们，曾给过我种种警诫。

## VII / 第七章

===Original===
> Fight on, my men, Sir Andrew sayes,
 

 A little Ime hurt, but yett not slaine;
 

 Ile but lye downe and bleede awhile,
 

 And then Ile rise and fight againe.

===Chinese===
> 打下去，我的弟兄们，安德鲁爵士说道，
 

 我受了一点伤，却还不曾丧命；
 

 我只消躺下流血片刻，
 

 便再起身，重新战斗。

===Original===
But I could not remain where I was any longer, though the daylight was hateful to me, and the thought of the great, innocent, bold sunrise unendurable. Here there was no well to cool my face, smarting with the bitterness of my own tears. Nor would I have washed in the well of that grotto, had it flowed clear as the rivers of Paradise. I rose, and feebly left the sepulchral cave. I took my way I knew not whither, but still towards the sunrise. The birds were singing; but not for me. All the creatures spoke a language of their own, with which I had nothing to do, and to which I cared not to find the key any more. I walked listlessly along. What distressed me most—more even than my own folly—was the perplexing question—How can beauty and ugliness dwell so near? Even with her altered complexion and her face of dislike; disenchanted of the belief that clung around her; known for a living, walking sepulchre, faithless, deluding, traitorous; I felt, notwithstanding all this, that she was beautiful. Upon this I pondered with undiminished perplexity, though not without some gain. Then I began to make surmises as to the mode of my deliverance; and concluded that some hero, wandering in search of adventure, had heard how the forest was infested; and, knowing it was useless to attack the evil thing in person, had assailed with his battle-axe the body in which he dwelt, and on which he was dependent for his power of mischief in the wood. “Very likely,” I thought, “the repentant-knight, who warned me of the evil which has befallen me, was busy retrieving his lost honor, while I was sinking into the same sorrow with himself; and, hearing of the dangerous and mysterious being, arrived at his tree in time to save me from being dragged to its roots, and buried like carrion, to nourish him for yet deeper insatiableness.” I found afterwards that my conjecture was correct. I wondered how he had fared when his blows recalled the Ash himself, and that too I learned afterwards.

===Chinese===
可我再不能待在原地了，虽然白昼令我憎恶，想到那伟大、无辜而无所忌惮的日出，更叫我无法忍受。这里没有一眼泉水，能凉一凉我被自己苦泪蜇得刺痛的脸。即便那洞窟里的泉水流得如乐园诸河一般清澈，我也决不肯在其中洗濯。我站起身，虚弱地离开了那墓穴般的洞窟。我踏上行程，不知去向何方，却始终朝着日出的方向。鸟儿在歌唱；却不是为我。一切生灵都说着它们自己的语言，与我毫不相干，我也不愿再去寻索那种语言的钥匙了。我无精打采地走着。最令我苦恼的——甚至胜过我自身的愚行——是那个缠磨人的问题：美与丑怎能住得这样近？纵然她肤色已变，面容可憎；纵然缠附在她身上的那层妄信已被祛除；纵然她已被认作一座活的、行走的坟墓，背信、欺瞒、叛卖；尽管如此，我仍觉得她是美的。我怀着不减的困惑对这一点冥思苦索，却也并非全无所得。继而，我开始揣测自己获救的方式；并断定：某位漫游觅奇的英雄，听闻这片森林闹着妖祟；又深知亲自去攻那邪恶之物毫无用处，便挥动战斧，猛劈那恶物栖居其内的躯壳——它正是倚仗这躯壳，才能在林中作祟行害。「这很可能，」我想，「那位警诫过我这场灾祸的悔悟骑士，正当我也坠入与他同样的悲苦之际，正忙着挽回他失去的荣誉；他听闻了那个危险而神秘的存在，及时赶到了它的树前，救下我，使我不致被拖到树根旁，像腐尸一样埋掉，去滋养它那愈来愈深的贪欲。」后来我发觉，我的猜想是正确的。当他的斧击把梣树本尊召出来时，他自己遭遇如何，我深为纳闷；而这，也是我后来才得知的。

===Original===
I walked on the whole day, with intervals of rest, but without food; for I could not have eaten, had any been offered me; till, in the afternoon, I seemed to approach the outskirts of the forest, and at length arrived at a farmhouse. An unspeakable joy arose in my heart at beholding an abode of human beings once more, and I hastened up to the door, and knocked. A kind-looking, matronly woman, still handsome, made her appearance; who, as soon as she saw me, said kindly, “Ah, my poor boy, you have come from the wood! Were you in it last night?”

I should have ill endured, the day before, to be called “boy”; but now the motherly kindness of the word went to my heart; and, like a boy indeed, I burst into tears. She soothed me right gently; and, leading me into a room, made me lie down on a settle, while she went to find me some refreshment. She soon returned with food, but I could not eat. She almost compelled me to swallow some wine, when I revived sufficiently to be able to answer some of her questions. I told her the whole story.

“It is just as I feared,” she said; “but you are now for the night beyond the reach of any of these dreadful creatures. It is no wonder they could delude a child like you. But I must beg you, when my husband comes in, not to say a word about these things; for he thinks me even half crazy for believing anything of the sort. But I must believe my senses, as he cannot believe beyond his, which give him no intimations of this kind. I think he could spend the whole of Midsummer-eve in the wood and come back with the report that he saw nothing worse than himself. Indeed, good man, he would hardly find anything better than himself, if he had seven more senses given him.”

===Chinese===
我走了整整一天，中间歇了几回，却没有吃东西；因为即便有人递给我，我也咽不下去；直到午后，我似乎临近了森林的边缘，终于来到一座农舍前。重新望见一处人烟，我心中涌起难以言喻的欢喜，急忙奔到门前，敲起门来。应门的是一位面容和善、一派主妇模样的妇人，风韵犹存；她一看见我，便和蔼地说：「啊，我可怜的孩子，你是从林子里来的吧！你昨儿夜里在林子里吗？」

若是昨天，被人唤作「孩子」，我准会老大不愿；可此刻这话里慈母般的温情直落进我心里；我像个真正的孩子似的，眼泪扑簌簌涌了出来。她极其温柔地抚慰我；领我进了一个房间，让我在高背长椅上躺下，自己去找些吃食。她不久便拿着食物回来了，可我吃不下。她几乎硬灌我咽下一点葡萄酒，我这才缓过些精神，能够回答她的几个问题了。我把整个经过都讲给了她。

「正如我所担心的，」她说；「不过今夜你已在这一切可怕生灵的魔掌所及之外了。像你这样的孩子会被它们迷惑，本不足为怪。但我得求你，等我丈夫回来时，对这些事一个字也不要提；因为就连相信一点这类事，他都当我是半疯。可我不能不相信自己的感官，正如他不能相信自己感官之外的东西，而他的感官从不给他这一类的预示。我想，就算他在林子里待上一整个仲夏前夜，回来也会报告说，他没看见什么比自己更糟的东西。说实在的，这位老实人，就算再添七种感官给他，他也几乎找不出比自己更好的东西来。」

===Original===
“But tell me how it is that she could be so beautiful without any heart at all—without any place even for a heart to live in.”

“I cannot quite tell,” she said; “but I am sure she would not look so beautiful if she did not take means to make herself look more beautiful than she is. And then, you know, you began by being in love with her before you saw her beauty, mistaking her for the lady of the marble—another kind altogether, I should think. But the chief thing that makes her beautiful is this: that, although she loves no man, she loves the love of any man; and when she finds one in her power, her desire to bewitch him and gain his love (not for the sake of his love either, but that she may be conscious anew of her own beauty, through the admiration he manifests), makes her very lovely—with a self-destructive beauty, though; for it is that which is constantly wearing her away within, till, at last, the decay will reach her face, and her whole front, when all the lovely mask of nothing will fall to pieces, and she be vanished forever. So a wise man, whom she met in the wood some years ago, and who, I think, for all his wisdom, fared no better than you, told me, when, like you, he spent the next night here, and recounted to me his adventures.”

I thanked her very warmly for her solution, though it was but partial; wondering much that in her, as in woman I met on my first entering the forest, there should be such superiority to her apparent condition.

===Chinese===
「可请告诉我，她怎么会全然没有心，却还能那样美——甚至没有一处可供心居住的地方。」

「这个我也说不十分清楚，」她说；「但我确信，倘若她不是有法子把自己弄得比本来更美，她是不会显得那样美的。况且你要知道，你在看见她的美之前，就已经先爱上了她，把她错当成了那位大理石少女——我以为那完全是另一类存在。而使她显得美的最主要的原因是这个：她虽然谁也不爱，却爱着任何人的爱；一旦发现有人落入她的掌控，她那种想要蛊惑他、赢得他的爱的渴望（却也并非为了他的爱本身，而是要借他所表露的爱慕，对自己的美重新有所自觉），便使她变得非常可爱——尽管那是一种自我毁灭的美；因为正是这种渴望，在不断从内里消损她，直到最后，衰朽终于侵及她的脸、她的整个表面，那时，这『虚无』的可爱假面将整个碎裂，她也将永远消散。几年前，她在林中遇见过一位智者；我想，此人智慧虽高，遭际却并不比你强；他像你一样，也在此地宿了翌夜，向我讲述他的历险；这番道理便是那时他告诉我的。」

我非常热忱地谢过她的这一番解释，虽然它也只是部分的；心中深自诧异：她同我初入森林时遇见的那位妇人一样，竟能如此超拔于她表面上的身份境况。

===Original===
In half an hour, I heard a heavy step approach and enter the house. A jolly voice, whose slight huskiness appeared to proceed from overmuch laughter, called out “Betsy, the pigs’ trough is quite empty, and that is a pity. Let them swill, lass! They’re of no use but to get fat. Ha! ha! ha! Gluttony is not forbidden in their commandments. Ha! ha! ha!” The very voice, kind and jovial, seemed to disrobe the room of the strange look which all new places wear—to disenchant it out of the realm of the ideal into that of the actual. It began to look as if I had known every corner of it for twenty years; and when, soon after, the dame came and fetched me to partake of their early supper, the grasp of his great hand, and the harvest-moon of his benevolent face, which was needed to light up the rotundity of the globe beneath it, produced such a reaction in me, that, for a moment, I could hardly believe that there was a Fairy Land; and that all I had passed through since I left home, had not been the wandering dream of a diseased imagination, operating on a too mobile frame, not merely causing me indeed to travel, but peopling for me with vague phantoms the regions through which my actual steps had led me. But the next moment, my eye fell upon a little girl who was sitting in the chimney-corner, with a little book open on her knee, from which she had apparently just looked up to fix great inquiring eyes upon me. I believed in Fairy Land again. She went on with her reading, as soon as she saw that I observed her looking at me. I went near, and peeping over her shoulder, saw that she was reading *The History of Graciosa and Percinet*.

===Chinese===
半个钟点之后，我听见一个沉重的脚步走近，进了屋子。一个快活的声音喊了起来，那微微的沙哑，看来是笑得太多所致：「贝茜，猪食槽全空了，这可是桩憾事。给它们倒食吧，丫头！它们除了长膘别无用处。哈！哈！哈！暴饮暴食在它们的诫命里可不禁。哈！哈！哈！」这嗓音本身，亲切而欢畅，仿佛霎时剥去了这房间在一切陌生之地都带着的那种异样神情——把它从理想之国祛魅而出，送回现实之境。它看上去竟像是我已熟识了二十年的一个角落；不久，女主人来领我去同吃他们开得颇早的晚饭，他那大手的一握，还有他慈和脸面上那轮收获月——要照亮脸面底下那个圆球的浑圆，正少不了它——在我身上激起这样一股反作用，以致有那么一刻，我几乎不能相信世上有仙境；不能相信我离家以来所经历的一切，并非一种病态的想象在过于易感的躯体上所做的游荡的梦——那想象非但实实在在地引我远行，还为我双足实际踏过的地域布满了虚幻的幽灵。但下一刻，我的目光落在一个小女孩身上，她正坐在烟囱角落里，膝上摊开一本小书；她显然刚从书上抬起头来，把一双大大的、好奇的眼睛定在我身上。我又相信仙境了。她一见我察觉她在望我，便又低头读她的书。我走近些，从她肩头望过去，见她读的是《格蕾西奥莎与珀西内的故事》（*The History of Graciosa and Percinet*）。

===Original===
“Very improving book, sir,” remarked the old farmer, with a good-humored laugh. “We are in the very hottest corner of Fairy Land here. Ha! ha! Stormy night, last night, sir.”

“Was it, indeed?” I rejoined. “It was not so with me. A lovelier night I never saw.”

“Indeed! Where were you last night?”

“I spent it in the forest. I had lost my way.”

===Chinese===
「很有教益的书，先生，」老农夫带着一副好性儿的笑，评论道。「咱们这儿正是仙境里最热的一角。哈！哈！昨儿夜里风雨大作呢，先生。」

「是吗，当真？」我答道。「我那里可不是这样。那么可爱的夜，我还从未见过。」

「哦！昨儿夜里您在哪儿？」

「我在林子里过的夜。我迷了路。」

===Original===
“Ah! then, perhaps, you will be able to convince my good woman, that there is nothing very remarkable about the forest; for, to tell the truth, it bears but a bad name in these parts. I dare say you saw nothing worse than yourself there?”

“I hope I did,” was my inward reply; but, for an audible one, I contented myself with saying, “Why, I certainly did see some appearances I could hardly account for; but that is nothing to be wondered at in an unknown wild forest, and with the uncertain light of the moon alone to go by.”

“Very true! you speak like a sensible man, sir. We have but few sensible folks round about us. Now, you would hardly credit it, but my wife believes every fairytale that ever was written. I cannot account for it. She is a most sensible woman in everything else.”

“But should not that make you treat her belief with something of respect, though you cannot share in it yourself?”

“Yes, that is all very well in theory; but when you come to live every day in the midst of absurdity, it is far less easy to behave respectfully to it. Why, my wife actually believes the story of the ‘White Cat.’ You know it, I dare say.”

===Chinese===
「啊！那么，或许您能让我家这位好太太相信，这林子并没有什么了不起；因为说句实话，它在这一带名声可不大好。我敢说，您在那儿没看见什么比自己更糟的东西吧？」

「但愿如此，」这是我心里的回答；但作为出声的回答，我只满足于这样说：「唔，我的确见到一些自己几乎无法解释的情形；可在一片陌生的莽林里，只凭着月亮那点不定的光赶路，这也算不得什么可怪之事。」

「一点不错！先生，您说话像个明白人。我们左近明白人可不多。喏，您简直不会相信：凡是写下来的神仙故事，我妻子没有不信的。我实在无法理解。别的方面她可是个顶明白的女人。」

「可即便您自己不能分享这种信仰，难道不该因此对她的信仰抱几分敬意吗？」

「是啊，这在理论上自然很动听；可当您天天生活在荒唐事当中，要对它恭恭敬敬，就难得多了。您瞧，我妻子真的相信《白猫》的故事。那故事您总知道吧。」

===Original===
“I read all these tales when a child, and know that one especially well.”

“But, father,” interposed the little girl in the chimney-corner, “you know quite well that mother is descended from that very princess who was changed by the wicked fairy into a white cat. Mother has told me so a many times, and you ought to believe everything she says.”

“I can easily believe that,” rejoined the farmer, with another fit of laughter; “for, the other night, a mouse came gnawing and scratching beneath the floor, and would not let us go to sleep. Your mother sprang out of bed, and going as near it as she could, mewed so infernally like a great cat, that the noise ceased instantly. I believe the poor mouse died of the fright, for we have never heard it again. Ha! ha! ha!”

The son, an ill-looking youth, who had entered during the conversation, joined in his father’s laugh; but his laugh was very different from the old man’s: it was polluted with a sneer. I watched him, and saw that, as soon as it was over, he looked scared, as if he dreaded some evil consequences to follow his presumption. The woman stood near, waiting till we should seat ourselves at the table, and listening to it all with an amused air, which had something in it of the look with which one listens to the sententious remarks of a pompous child. We sat down to supper, and I ate heartily. My bygone distresses began already to look far off.

===Chinese===
「这些故事我小时候全都读过，那一篇尤其熟。」

「可是，爸爸，」坐在烟囱角落里的小姑娘插嘴道，「您明明知道，妈妈正是那位被恶毒仙女变成白猫的公主的后代呀。妈妈跟我说过好多好多回，她说的每一句话您都该相信才对。」

「这个我倒容易相信，」农夫答道，又大笑了一阵；「因为前几天夜里，有只老鼠在地板底下又啃又抓，闹得我们睡不着。你妈妈一骨碌跳下床，凑到离它最近的地方，喵喵地叫，活像一只大猫，叫得那么出神入化，那声响立刻便停了。我猜那可怜的老鼠是吓死了，因为我们再没听见过它。哈！哈！哈！」

儿子是个面相不善的年轻人，在谈话间已经进了屋，也跟着父亲大笑；但他的笑与老人的笑大不一样：里面掺着讥诮，是被玷污了的笑。我留神看他，见笑声一停，他神色便慌起来，仿佛害怕自己的放肆会招来某种恶果。女主人站在近旁，等我们入席，带着一副觉得有趣的神情听着这一场谈话，那神情里，有点像人们听一个自命不凡的孩子煞有介事地发议论时的模样。我们坐下来吃晚饭，我吃得津津有味。昔日种种苦痛，看上去已经开始变得遥远了。

===Original===
“In what direction are you going?” asked the old man.

“Eastward,” I replied; nor could I have given a more definite answer. “Does the forest extend much further in that direction?”

“Oh! for miles and miles; I do not know how far. For although I have lived on the borders of it all my life, I have been too busy to make journeys of discovery into it. Nor do I see what I could discover. It is only trees and trees, till one is sick of them. By the way, if you follow the eastward track from here, you will pass close to what the children say is the very house of the ogre that Hop-o’-my-Thumb visited, and ate his little daughters with the crowns of gold.”

“Oh, father! ate his little daughters! No; he only changed their gold crowns for nightcaps; and the great long-toothed ogre killed them in mistake; but I do not think even he ate them, for you know they were his own little ogresses.”

“Well, well, child; you know all about it a great deal better than I do. However, the house has, of course, in such a foolish neighborhood as this, a bad enough name; and I must confess there is a woman living in it, with teeth long enough and white enough too for the lineal descendant of the greatest ogre that ever was made. I think you had better not go near her.”

===Chinese===
「您打算往哪个方向去？」老人问。

「往东，」我答道；我也给不出更确切的回答了。「这林子朝那个方向还延伸得很远吗？」

「噢！一英里又一英里地延伸呢，我不知道有多远。因为我虽说在林边住了一辈子，却总忙得没工夫进去探幽寻胜。再说我也看不出我能发现什么。除了树还是树，直到叫人腻味为止。对了，您要是从这儿顺着往东的小路走，会紧挨着经过一处地方，孩子们说那就是食人魔的家——就是『小拇指』（Hop-o’-my-Thumb）造访过的那个食人魔，他吃掉了自己那些戴金冠的小女儿。」

「啊，爸爸！吃了他自己的小女儿！不是的；他只是把她们的金冠换成了睡帽；是那个长着长牙的大食人魔误杀了她们；可我想，就连他也没吃她们，因为您知道，她们本是他自家的小食人魔女儿呀。」

「好了，好了，孩子；这一切你知道得比我多得多。不过，在这样一个蠢地方，那房子的名声自然够坏的；我也不得不承认，那里面住着一个女人，那口牙齿又长又白，真配得上做古往今来最了不起的食人魔的嫡传后代。我看您顶好别走近她。」

===Original===
In such talk as this the night wore on. When supper was finished, which lasted some time, my hostess conducted me to my chamber.

“If you had not had enough of it already,” she said, “I would have put you in another room, which looks towards the forest; and where you would most likely have seen something more of its inhabitants. For they frequently pass the window, and even enter the room sometimes. Strange creatures spend whole nights in it, at certain seasons of the year. I am used to it, and do not mind it. No more does my little girl, who sleeps in it always. But this room looks southward towards the open country, and they never show themselves here; at least I never saw any.”

I was somewhat sorry not to gather any experience that I might have, of the inhabitants of Fairy Land; but the effect of the farmer’s company, and of my own later adventures, was such, that I chose rather an undisturbed night in my more human quarters; which, with their clean white curtains and white linen, were very inviting to my weariness.

===Chinese===
我们就在这样的闲谈里消磨着夜晚。晚饭吃了好一阵才罢，女主人便引我去我的卧房。

「倘若你还没有受够它们的话，」她说，「我本会把你安置在另一个房间里，那间朝向森林；在那里，你多半还能多见识一些林中居民。因为它们时常打窗前经过，有时甚至走进屋里来。一年里某些时节，奇怪的生灵会在那屋里待上一整夜。我习惯了，并不在意。我的小姑娘也一样，她总睡在那间屋里。可这间房朝南，面向旷野，它们从不在这儿露形；至少我从未见过。」

仙境居民的经历，凡可能有的，我本来都想亲身领受；如今一样也捞不着，我不免有些怅然；但农夫作伴的影响，连同我自己近来的种种历险，在我身上起了这样的作用，使我宁可在我这更具人间气的居处里，讨一个不受搅扰的夜；那屋里洁净的白帷与白床单，对我这疲惫之身，实在诱人。

===Original===
In the morning I awoke refreshed, after a profound and dreamless sleep. The sun was high, when I looked out of the window, shining over a wide, undulating, cultivated country. Various garden-vegetables were growing beneath my window. Everything was radiant with clear sunlight. The dewdrops were sparkling their busiest; the cows in a nearby field were eating as if they had not been at it all day yesterday; the maids were singing at their work as they passed to and fro between the outhouses: I did not believe in Fairy Land. I went down, and found the family already at breakfast. But before I entered the room where they sat, the little girl came to me, and looked up in my face, as though she wanted to say something to me. I stooped towards her; she put her arms round my neck, and her mouth to my ear, and whispered:—

“A white lady has been flitting about the house all night.”

“No whispering behind doors!” cried the farmer; and we entered together. “Well, how have you slept? No bogies, eh?”

“Not one, thank you; I slept uncommonly well.”

“I am glad to hear it. Come and breakfast.”

===Chinese===
早晨，经过一场深沉而无梦的酣睡，我醒来时神清气爽。我望向窗外时，太阳已经升得老高，照着一片广阔起伏、耕种有年的乡野。我窗下生长着各色园蔬。一切都在明澈的日光里焕发光彩。露珠儿在尽情闪烁；近旁田里的牛群嚼着草，仿佛昨天一整天不曾吃过似的；女仆们往来于屋舍之间，一边干活一边唱歌：我不相信仙境了。我下楼去，见一家人已在用早饭。但在我走进他们坐着的房间之前，小姑娘迎着我来了，仰起脸望着我的脸，像有话要对我说。我向她俯下身去；她伸出两臂搂住我的脖子，把嘴凑到我的耳边，悄声说：——

「一个白衣女郎整夜绕着这房子飘飞呢。」

「不许躲在门后嘀嘀咕咕！」农夫喊道；我们便一起进了屋。「嗐，睡得怎么样？没闹鬼吧，啊？」

「一个也没有，多谢；我睡得好极了。」

「听你这么说我很高兴。来吃早饭吧。」

===Original===
After breakfast, the farmer and his son went out; and I was left alone with the mother and daughter.

“When I looked out of the window this morning,” I said, “I felt almost certain that Fairy Land was all a delusion of my brain; but whenever I come near you or your little daughter, I feel differently. Yet I could persuade myself, after my last adventures, to go back, and have nothing more to do with such strange beings.”

“How will you go back?” said the woman.

“Nay, that I do not know.”

===Chinese===
早饭后，农夫和他儿子出门去了；屋里只剩下母女两个同我在一起。

「今天早晨我望着窗外的时候，」我说，「我几乎断定仙境全是我头脑的一场幻妄；可每当我一走近您，或您的小女儿，我就又觉得不是这样了。不过，经过了上一次的历险，我本可以说服自己掉头回去，再不同这类奇怪的生灵发生瓜葛。」

「您怎么回去呢？」那妇人说。

「唉，这我可不知道。」

===Original===
“Because I have heard, that, for those who enter Fairy Land, there is no way of going back. They must go on, and go through it. How, I do not in the least know.”

“That is quite the impression on my own mind. Something compels me to go on, as if my only path was onward, but I feel less inclined this morning to continue my adventures.”

“Will you come and see my little child’s room? She sleeps in the one I told you of, looking towards the forest.”

“Willingly,” I said.

===Chinese===
「因为我曾听人说过，进了仙境的人，是没有回头路可走的。他们只能前行，把仙境走穿。至于怎么走，我可一点也不知情。」

「我自己心里也正是这个印象。有什么在逼我前行，仿佛我唯一的路就是向前；可今天早晨，我却不大想再继续我的历险了。」

「您愿意来看看我家小孩子的房间吗？她睡的就是我同您说起的那间，朝着森林的那间。」

「愿意的，」我说。

===Original===
So we went together, the little girl running before to open the door for us. It was a large room, full of old-fashioned furniture, that seemed to have once belonged to some great house. The window was built with a low arch, and filled with lozenge-shaped panes. The wall was very thick, and built of solid stone. I could see that part of the house had been erected against the remains of some old castle or abbey, or other great building; the fallen stones of which had probably served to complete it. But as soon as I looked out of the window, a gush of wonderment and longing flowed over my soul like the tide of a great sea. Fairy Land lay before me, and drew me towards it with an irresistible attraction. The trees bathed their great heads in the waves of the morning, while their roots were planted deep in gloom; save where on the borders the sunshine broke against their stems, or swept in long streams through their avenues, washing with brighter hue all the leaves over which it flowed; revealing the rich brown of the decayed leaves and fallen pine-cones, and the delicate greens of the long grasses and tiny forests of moss that covered the channel over which it passed in motionless rivers of light. I turned hurriedly to bid my hostess farewell without further delay. She smiled at my haste, but with an anxious look.

===Chinese===
我们便一同前去，小姑娘跑在前头替我们开门。那是一间大屋子，满是老式家具，仿佛曾属于某座大宅。窗户筑成一道低低的拱券，嵌满菱形的玻璃。墙极厚，用整块而坚实的石料砌成。我看得出来，屋子的一部分是倚着某座古城堡、古修道院或别的大建筑的残址盖起来的；那些倾颓的石块，多半便被取来补筑了这一部分。可我一朝窗外望去，一股惊异与渴慕之情，便如大海的潮水般漫过我的灵魂。仙境横陈在我面前，以一种无可抗拒的引力牵引着我。群树把他们巨大的头颅浸在清晨的波涛里，根却深深扎在幽暗之中；唯有在林缘处，阳光撞上树干碎开，或是一道道长流扫过林间夹道，把它流过的每一片叶子都洗上更亮的颜色；映出朽叶与落地松球的浓褐，映出长草与细小苔藓林的娇嫩青绿——它们覆满那道光流的河床，光便在其间化作凝然不动的光之河流过。我急忙转过身，要事不宜迟，立刻向女主人辞别。她笑我这般急切，眉目间却带着忧色。

===Original===
“You had better not go near the house of the ogre, I think. My son will show you into another path, which will join the first beyond it.”

Not wishing to be headstrong or too confident any more, I agreed; and having taken leave of my kind entertainers, went into the wood, accompanied by the youth. He scarcely spoke as we went along; but he led me through the trees till we struck upon a path. He told me to follow it, and, with a muttered “good morning,” left me.

===Chinese===
「依我看，您顶好别走近食人魔的那座房子。我儿子会指您走另一条小路，那条路在房子过去的地方与头一条路汇合。」

我再不愿刚愎自用、过分自信了，便依从了；与我那好客的一家人道过别之后，我走进树林，由那年轻人相送。一路上他几乎不开口；但他领着我穿行树木之间，直到撞见一条小路。他嘱咐我循着这条路走，含混地道了声「早安」，便离我而去。

## VIII / 第八章

===Original===
> Ich bin ein Theil des Theils, der anfangs alles war.

> I am a part of the part, which at first was the whole.

===Chinese===
> Ich bin ein Theil des Theils, der anfangs alles war.

> 我是太初之全的那一部分的一部分。

===Original===
My spirits rose as I went deeper into the forest; but I could not regain my former elasticity of mind. I found cheerfulness to be like life itself—not to be created by any argument. Afterwards I learned, that the best way to manage some kinds of painful thoughts, is to dare them to do their worst; to let them lie and gnaw at your heart till they are tired; and you find you still have a residue of life they cannot kill. So, better and worse, I went on, till I came to a little clearing in the forest. In the middle of this clearing stood a long, low hut, built with one end against a single tall cypress, which rose like a spire to the building. A vague misgiving crossed my mind when I saw it; but I must needs go closer, and look through a little half-open door, near the opposite end from the cypress. Window I saw none. On peeping in, and looking towards the further end, I saw a lamp burning, with a dim, reddish flame, and the head of a woman, bent downwards, as if reading by its light. I could see nothing more for a few moments. At length, as my eyes got used to the dimness of the place, I saw that the part of the rude building near me was used for household purposes; for several rough utensils lay here and there, and a bed stood in the corner. An irresistible attraction caused me to enter. The woman never raised her face, the upper part of which alone I could see distinctly; but, as soon as I stepped within the threshold, she began to read aloud, in a low and not altogether unpleasing voice, from an ancient little volume which she held open with one hand on the table upon which stood the lamp. What she read was something like this:

===Chinese===
愈往森林深处走，我的精神愈是振作；却再不能恢复先前那种心气的弹性。我发现愉快之心犹如生命本身——绝非任何论证所能造出。后来我才知道，对付某些种类的痛苦念头，最好的办法是向它们挑衅，任它们作恶到极处；任它们伏在你心头啃啮，直到疲乏；而你会发觉，自己仍有一点它们杀不死的生活的余烬。就这样，时好时坏，我继续前行，直到来到林中一小块空地。空地中央立着一座又长又矮的小屋，一端倚着一株孤高的柏树而建，那柏树如尖塔般高耸，直对屋顶。我一看见它，心头便掠过一阵隐约的不安；可我偏偏非要走近些不可，从离柏树较远的另一端附近一扇半开的小门向里张望。窗是一扇也没有的。我一探头，朝屋子更深处望去，只见一盏灯燃着，火焰幽暗而微红，一个女人的头低垂着，仿佛正就着灯光读书。片刻之间，我什么也看不真切。待到眼睛渐渐习惯了屋中的昏暗，我才看出，靠近我的这部分粗陋建筑是供居家使用的；因为几件粗糙的器皿散置各处，角落里还立着一张床。一种不可抗拒的引力引我走了进去。那女人始终没有抬起脸来，我所能看清的只有她脸的上半部；但我一跨进门槛，她便开始朗声诵读起来，声音低沉，却也并非不悦耳，读的是一册古旧的小书，她一手把它摊开按在桌上，桌上便立着那盏灯。她读的内容大致如下：

===Original===
> “So, then, as darkness had no beginning, neither will it ever have an end. So, then, is it eternal. The negation of aught else, is its affirmation. Where the light cannot come, there abideth the darkness. The light doth but hollow a mine out of the infinite extension of the darkness. And ever upon the steps of the light treadeth the darkness; yea, springeth in fountains and wells amidst it, from the secret channels of its mighty sea. Truly, man is but a passing flame, moving unquietly amid the surrounding rest of night; without which he yet could not be, and whereof he is in part compounded.”

===Chinese===
> “这样看来，黑暗既无开端，也永无终结。这样看来，黑暗便是永恒。对其余一切的否定，便是它的肯定。光所不能到之处，黑暗便居于彼处。光不过是从黑暗的无垠延展中凿出一座矿穴。而黑暗永远踏着光的足迹；诚然，它从自己浩海的秘密水道里，在光的中间涌作泉与井。诚然，人不过是一星过眼的火焰，在四围长夜的静息中不安地跃动；然而没有这静息他便无从存在，他本身也有一部分是由它复合而成的。”

===Original===
As I drew nearer, and she read on, she moved a little to turn a leaf of the dark old volume, and I saw that her face was sallow and slightly forbidding. Her forehead was high, and her black eyes repressedly quiet. But she took no notice of me. This end of the cottage, if cottage it could be called, was destitute of furniture, except the table with the lamp, and the chair on which the woman sat. In one corner was a door, apparently of a cupboard in the wall, but which might lead to a room beyond. Still the irresistible desire which had made me enter the building urged me: I must open that door, and see what was beyond it. I approached, and laid my hand on the rude latch. Then the woman spoke, but without lifting her head or looking at me: “You had better not open that door.” This was uttered quite quietly; and she went on with her reading, partly in silence, partly aloud; but both modes seemed equally intended for herself alone. The prohibition, however, only increased my desire to see; and as she took no further notice, I gently opened the door to its full width, and looked in. At first, I saw nothing worthy of attention. It seemed a common closet, with shelves on each hand, on which stood various little necessaries for the humble uses of a cottage. In one corner stood one or two brooms, in another a hatchet and other common tools; showing that it was in use every hour of the day for household purposes. But, as I looked, I saw that there were no shelves at the back, and that an empty space went in further; its termination appearing to be a faintly glimmering wall or curtain, somewhat less, however, than the width and height of the doorway where I stood. But, as I continued looking, for a few seconds, towards this faintly luminous limit, my eyes came into true relation with their object. All at once, with such a shiver as when one is suddenly conscious of the presence of another in a room where he has, for hours, considered himself alone, I saw that the seemingly luminous extremity was a sky, as of night, beheld through the long perspective of a narrow, dark passage, through what, or built of what, I could not tell. As I gazed, I clearly discerned two or three stars glimmering faintly in the distant blue. But, suddenly, and as if it had been running fast from a far distance for this very point, and had turned the corner without abating its swiftness, a dark figure sped into and along the passage from the blue opening at the remote end. I started back and shuddered, but kept looking, for I could not help it. On and on it came, with a speedy approach but delayed arrival; till, at last, through the many gradations of approach, it seemed to come within the sphere of myself, rushed up to me, and passed me into the cottage. All I could tell of its appearance was, that it seemed to be a dark human figure. Its motion was entirely noiseless, and might be called a gliding, were it not that it appeared that of a runner, but with ghostly feet. I had moved back yet a little to let him pass me, and looked round after him instantly. I could not see him.

===Chinese===
我走得愈近，她读得愈久；她微微一动，去翻那深色旧书的一页，我这才看清她的脸面黄肌瘦，略带拒人之色。她额头很高，一双黑眼睛压抑着，十分安静。但她毫不理会我。小屋的这一端——倘若它还称得上小屋的话——除了一张摆灯的桌子和女人坐的椅子，再无家具。一角有一扇门，看似墙内壁橱的门，却或许通向更里面的一个房间。那股引我走进这建筑来的不可抗拒的欲望仍在催迫我：我必须打开那扇门，看看门后是什么。我走近去，把手搭在那粗糙的门闩上。这时女人开了口，却不抬头，也不看我：“你最好不要开那扇门。”她说得十分平静，随后继续读她的书，时而默然，时而出声；但这两种方式似乎都同样只为她自己。然而这道禁令只使我愈想看个究竟；见她再不理会，我便轻轻把门开到尽处，向里望去。起初，我看不到什么值得留意的东西。那似乎是一个寻常的壁橱，两侧各有搁板，上面立着小屋卑微用度所需的各种零星物件。一角立着一把两把扫帚，另一角放着一把短柄斧和其他常用工具；可见它一天到晚都为家务所用。然而望着望着，我发现橱的后壁没有搁板，还有一处空隙伸向更深处；它的尽头仿佛是一道微光闪烁的墙或帘幕，只是比我站立的门洞略窄略矮。但当我继续朝那微光的界限望了几秒钟，我的眼睛与那对象进入了真实的关系。刹那间，我打了一个寒噤——正如一个人独居一室数小时、自以为只有自己，却猛然觉察室内另有他人时那样——我看清了：那仿佛发光的尽头，原是一片夜的天空，是透过一条又窄又暗的甬道的深远透视望见的；那甬道穿行于何物之中，或由何物筑成，我说不上来。我凝望之际，清清楚楚辨出两三颗星在遥远的蓝色里微微闪烁。可是突然之间，仿佛有什么东西从极远处一路疾奔，专为此地而来，转弯时也不曾稍减它的迅疾，一个黑色的形体从甬道尽头的蓝色开口飞掠而入，沿着甬道奔来。我吓得倒退，浑身战栗，却不能不看着，因为我无法自制。它来而又来，趋近得极快，抵达却迟迟；终于，经过一层又一层的趋近，它仿佛进入了我自身所及之域，直冲到我面前，又从我身旁掠过，进了小屋。关于它的形状，我所能说出的只是：那似乎是一个黑色的人形。它的行动全然无声，或许可以称之为滑行——若不是它分明显出一个奔跑者的姿态，只是长着幽灵的脚。我已稍稍退后，让它经过，随即回头望它。我看不到它了。

===Original===
“Where is he?” I said, in some alarm, to the woman, who still sat reading.
“There, on the floor, behind you,” she said, pointing with her arm half-outstretched, but not lifting her eyes. I turned and looked, but saw nothing. Then with a feeling that there was yet something behind me, I looked round over my shoulder; and there, on the ground, lay a black shadow, the size of a man. It was so dark, that I could see it in the dim light of the lamp, which shone full upon it, apparently without thinning at all the intensity of its hue.
“I told you,” said the woman, “you had better not look into that closet.”
“What is it?” I said, with a growing sense of horror.
“It is only your shadow that has found you,” she replied. “Everybody’s shadow is ranging up and down looking for him. I believe you call it by a different name in your world: yours has found you, as every person’s is almost certain to do who looks into that closet, especially after meeting one in the forest, whom I dare say you have met.”
Here, for the first time, she lifted her head, and looked full at me: her mouth was full of long, white, shining teeth; and I knew that I was in the house of the ogre. I could not speak, but turned and left the house, with the shadow at my heels. “A nice sort of valet to have,” I said to myself bitterly, as I stepped into the sunshine, and, looking over my shoulder, saw that it lay yet blacker in the full blaze of the sunlight. Indeed, only when I stood between it and the sun, was the blackness at all diminished. I was so bewildered—stunned—both by the event itself and its suddenness, that I could not at all realize to myself what it would be to have such a constant and strange attendance; but with a dim conviction that my present dislike would soon grow to loathing, I took my dreary way through the wood.

===Chinese===
“他在哪里？”我有些惊惶地问那女人；她仍坐在那里读书。
“就在那里，地板上，你身后，”她说着，手臂半伸地指着，却不抬眼。我转身去看，什么也没有。随后，怀着身后另有一物的感觉，我回头越过肩头望去；就在地上，躺着一团黑色的影子，人一般大小。它黑得那样浓，在灯的幽光里我也能看见它；灯光满满地照在它上面，却显然丝毫没有把那颜色的浓黑冲淡。
“我跟你说过，”女人说，“你最好不要朝那壁橱里看。”
“那是什么？”我说，恐怖之感愈涌愈浓。
“那不过是你的影子找到你了，”她回答。“每个人的影子都在上上下下地游走，寻找他的主人。我相信在你们的世界里，你们用另一个名字称呼它：你的影子已经找到了你；凡朝那壁橱里望过一眼的人，几乎个个都难逃这一遭，尤其是在林中遇见过一位之后——我敢说，你已经遇见过了。”
说到这里，她才头一遭抬起头来，直直地望着我：她满口长牙，又长又白，闪闪发光；我于是明白，自己是到了食人妖的家里。我说不出话，转身出了屋子，那影子紧跟在我的脚跟。“好一个体面的贴身跟班，”我跨进阳光里时辛酸地自语，回头一望，只见它在烈日的炽辉里躺得愈发黑了。的确，唯有当我站在它与太阳之间时，那黑色才稍稍减淡。我心乱如麻——呆若木鸡——这桩事本身与它的猝然都令我震骇，我竟全然无法向自己想象，身边带着这样一个形影不离的古怪随从，将是何等光景；然而我却隐隐确信，此刻的厌恶不久便将滋长为憎恨；我便带着一路凄惶，穿林而去。

## IX / 第九章

===Original===
> O Lady! we receive but what we give,
 
 And in our life alone does nature live:
 
 Ours is her wedding garments, ours her shroud!
 
 ⋮
 
 Ah! from the soul itself must issue forth
 
 A light, a glory, a fair luminous cloud,
 
 Enveloping the Earth—
 
 And from the soul itself must there be sent
 
 A sweet and potent voice, of its own birth,
 
 Of all sweet sounds the life and element!

===Chinese===
> 啊，夫人！我们领受的，不过是我们所给予的，
 
 唯有在我们的生命里，自然才活着：
 
 她的嫁衣是我们的，她的殓衣也是我们的！
 
 ⋮
 
 啊！从灵魂本身必得涌出
 
 一道光，一晕辉，一朵明媚的祥云，
 
 笼罩着大地——
 
 也必从灵魂本身送出
 
 一个甜美而有力的声音，出于它自身的孕生，
 
 是一切甜美声音的生命与本原！

===Original===
From this time, until I arrived at the palace of Fairy Land, I can attempt no consecutive account of my wanderings and adventures. Everything, henceforward, existed for me in its relation to my attendant. What influence he exercised upon everything into contact with which I was brought, may be understood from a few detached instances. To begin with this very day on which he first joined me: after I had walked heartlessly along for two or three hours, I was very weary, and lay down to rest in a most delightful part of the forest, carpeted with wildflowers. I lay for half an hour in a dull repose, and then got up to pursue my way. The flowers on the spot where I had lain were crushed to the earth: but I saw that they would soon lift their heads and rejoice again in the sun and air. Not so those on which my shadow had lain. The very outline of it could be traced in the withered lifeless grass, and the scorched and shriveled flowers which stood there, dead, and hopeless of any resurrection. I shuddered, and hastened away with sad forebodings.

===Chinese===
从这时起，直到我抵达仙境的仙宫，我已无法再把我的漫游与历险作连贯的记述了。从此以后，一切事物之于我，都只在与我那位随从的关系中存在。他对我所接触到的一切施加着怎样的影响，从几件孤立的实例中便可以明白了。就从他初来附随的这一天说起：我意兴索然地走了两三个钟头之后，已疲乏之极，便在林中最令人愉悦的一段躺下歇息，那里满地铺着野花。我木然地静卧了半个钟头，随后起身赶路。我躺过之处，花儿都被压伏在地；但我看得出，它们很快便会重新抬头，再度在阳光与空气里欢欣。我的影子躺过之处，却全然不是这样。影子的轮廓竟在枯死无生的草叶间历历可辨，那里立着的花都焦黑皱缩——死了，对任何复活都不再抱希望。我打了个寒噤，怀着悲哀的预感匆匆离去。

===Original===
In a few days, I had reason to dread an extension of its baleful influences from the fact, that it was no longer confined to one position in regard to myself. Hitherto, when seized with an irresistible desire to look on my evil demon (which longing would unaccountably seize me at any moment, returning at longer or shorter intervals, sometimes every minute), I had to turn my head backwards, and look over my shoulder; in which position, as long as I could retain it, I was fascinated. But one day, having come out on a clear grassy hill, which commanded a glorious prospect, though of what I cannot now tell, my shadow moved round, and came in front of me. And, presently, a new manifestation increased my distress. For it began to coruscate, and shoot out on all sides a radiation of dim shadow. These rays of gloom issued from the central shadow as from a black sun, lengthening and shortening with continual change. But wherever a ray struck, that part of earth, or sea, or sky, became void, and desert, and sad to my heart. On this, the first development of its new power, one ray shot out beyond the rest, seeming to lengthen infinitely, until it smote the great sun on the face, which withered and darkened beneath the blow. I turned away and went on. The shadow retreated to its former position; and when I looked again, it had drawn in all its spears of darkness, and followed like a dog at my heels.

===Chinese===
不出几日，我便有理由惧怕它那祸殃的影响扩张开来了，因为事实是：相对于我，它已不再固守一个位置。在此之前，每当我被一种不可抗拒的欲望攫住，要看一看我那邪恶的魔怪（这渴望会在任何时刻莫名其妙地攫住我，间隔或长或短地反复来袭，有时一分钟一次），我只得把头扭向后方，越过肩头去看；只要还能保持这姿势，我便被摄住，目不能移。可是有一天，我走上一座明净的青草山冈，冈上视野壮丽——虽则壮丽在何处，如今已说不上来——我的影子却转动过来，到了我的面前。不多时，一种新的显形又加深了我的痛苦。因为它开始迸闪，向四面八方射出幽影的辐射。这些幽暗的光线从中央那影子发出，宛如出自一轮黑太阳，忽长忽短，变化不歇。但光线击中何处，何处的那一片地、那一片海、那一片天，便化作虚空，化作荒漠，令我心中悲凉。在这新威力的首次施展之中，有一缕光线射得比其余都远，仿佛无限伸长，直到击中大太阳的面门；太阳在这击打之下枯萎、晦暗了。我转身走开，继续赶路。影子退回原位；我再回望时，它已把所有黑暗的长矛尽数收回，像狗一样跟在我的脚跟。

===Original===
Once, as I passed by a cottage, there came out a lovely fairy child, with two wondrous toys, one in each hand. The one was the tube through which the fairy-gifted poet looks when he beholds the same thing everywhere; the other that through which he looks when he combines into new forms of loveliness those images of beauty which his own choice has gathered from all regions wherein he has traveled. Round the child’s head was an aureole of emanating rays. As I looked at him in wonder and delight, round crept from behind me the something dark, and the child stood in my shadow. Straightway he was a commonplace boy, with a rough broad-brimmed straw hat, through which brim the sun shone from behind. The toys he carried were a multiplying-glass and a kaleidoscope. I sighed and departed.

One evening, as a great silent flood of western gold flowed through an avenue in the woods, down the stream, just as when I saw him first, came the sad knight, riding on his chestnut steed. But his armor did not shine half so red as when I saw him first. Many a blow of mighty sword and axe, turned aside by the strength of his mail, and glancing adown the surface, had swept from its path the fretted rust, and the glorious steel had answered the kindly blow with the thanks of returning light. These streaks and spots made his armor look like the floor of a forest in the sunlight. His forehead was higher than before, for the contracting wrinkles were nearly gone; and the sadness that remained on his face was the sadness of a dewy summer twilight, not that of a frosty autumn morn. He, too, had met the Alder-maiden as I, but he had plunged into the torrent of mighty deeds, and the stain was nearly washed away. No shadow followed him. He had not entered the dark house; he had not had time to open the closet door. “Will he ever look in?” I said to myself. “*Must* his shadow find him some day?” But I could not answer my own questions.

===Chinese===
有一次，我经过一座小屋，一个可爱的仙童走了出来，两手各执一件奇妙的玩具。一件是那种镜筒，受仙赐的诗人透过它观看时，处处看见的都是同一事物；另一件则是他透过它观看时，能把自己亲手拣选、采自行历诸方的种种美的意象，组合成新的可爱的形相。孩子的头上环绕着一圈放射的光轮。我正又惊又喜地看他，那黑黢黢的一团从我背后悄悄绕了过来，孩子便立在了我的影子里。转眼之间，他成了一个寻常的男孩，戴着一顶粗糙的宽檐草帽，阳光从背后透过帽檐照在他身上。他手中的玩具，成了一具多重镜和一只万花筒。我叹息一声，走开了。

一天傍晚，正当一大股金色的洪流自西方静静涌过林间一条夹道，顺着那流光，一如我初见他时，悲哀的骑士骑着他的栗色骏马来了。但他甲胄的红亮，已不及我初见他时的一半。巨剑与巨斧的多少次斫击，都被他锁子甲的坚固所偏转，滑掠过甲面，沿途扫去了蚀蚀的锈迹；那光华的精钢便以重返的光辉，答谢这番善意的击打。这些条纹与光斑，使他的甲胄看上去宛如阳光筛落的林间地面。他的额头比先前开阔了，因为聚拢的皱纹几乎消失；他脸上残存的悲哀，是凝露的夏日暮色的悲哀，而非降霜的秋日清晨的悲哀。他也同我一样遇见过赤杨树少女，但他已纵身投入壮举的激流，那污痕几乎洗净了。没有影子跟随他。他不曾走进那黑暗的小屋；他也不曾来得及去开那壁橱的门。“他日后会不会朝里张望呢？”我自忖道。“他的影子就*必定*会在某一天找到他吗？”可我无法回答自己的问题。

===Original===
We traveled together for two days, and I began to love him. It was plain that he suspected my story in some degree; and I saw him once or twice looking curiously and anxiously at my attendant gloom, which all this time had remained very obsequiously behind me; but I offered no explanation, and he asked none. Shame at my neglect of his warning, and a horror which shrunk from even alluding to its cause, kept me silent; till, on the evening of the second day, some noble words from my companion roused all my heart; and I was at the point of falling on his neck, and telling him the whole story; seeking, if not for helpful advice, for of that I was hopeless, yet for the comfort of sympathy—when round slid the shadow and inwrapt my friend; and I could not trust him. The glory of his brow vanished; the light of his eye grew cold; and I held my peace. The next morning we parted.

But the most dreadful thing of all was, that I now began to feel something like satisfaction in the presence of the shadow. I began to be rather vain of my attendant, saying to myself, “In a land like this, with so many illusions everywhere, I need his aid to disenchant the things around me. He does away with all appearances, and shows me things in their true color and form. And I am not one to be fooled with the vanities of the common crowd. I will not see beauty where there is none. I will dare to behold things as they are. And if I live in a waste instead of a paradise, I will live knowing where I live.” But of this a certain exercise of his power which soon followed quite cured me, turning my feelings towards him once more into loathing and distrust. It was thus:—

===Chinese===
我们同行了两天，我开始爱他。显然，他多少已猜到我的遭遇；我有一两次看见他好奇而不安地望着缀在我身后的那团幽暗——它这一路始终极其恭顺地随在我背后；但我不作解释，他也未问。我因辜负了他的警告而羞惭，又怀着一种连起因都不愿触及的恐怖，因此缄默不言；直到第二天傍晚，同伴的几句高贵言辞唤醒了我整个的心；我几乎要扑上去搂住他的脖颈，把整段故事向他倾吐；所求的纵然不是有益的忠告——那我是绝望的——至少也是同情的慰藉——就在此刻，影子滑转过来，把我的朋友裹了进去；我再也信不过他了。他额上的光辉隐没了；他眼中的光芒冷了下来；我便沉默不语。次日清晨，我们分手了。

而最可怕的事情是：如今我开始在这影子的随伴里感到一种近似满足的东西。我对这位随从竟渐渐颇为自负，自言自语道：“在这样的地方，四周幻象重重，我需要他的襄助，来解除周围事物的魔咒。他把一切表象尽数除去，把事物的真色真相显给我看。而我绝非那种肯被俗众的浮华愚弄的人。无美之处，我不肯看见美。我敢于直视事物的本相。倘若我住在一片荒漠而非乐园里，我也要明明白白地知道自己住在何处。”但这念头，很快便被他威力的某一次施展治得烟消云散，使我对他的情感重新化作憎恶与猜疑。事情是这样：—

===Original===
