One bright noon, a little maiden joined me, coming through the wood in a direction at right angles to my path. She came along singing and dancing, happy as a child, though she seemed almost a woman. In her hands—now in one, now in another—she carried a small globe, bright and clear as the purest crystal. This seemed at once her plaything and her greatest treasure. At one moment, you would have thought her utterly careless of it, and at another, overwhelmed with anxiety for its safety. But I believe she was taking care of it all the time, perhaps not least when least occupied about it. She stopped by me with a smile, and bade me good day with the sweetest voice. I felt a wonderful liking to the child—for she produced on me more the impression of a child, though my understanding told me differently. We talked a little, and then walked on together in the direction I had been pursuing. I asked her about the globe she carried, but getting no definite answer, I held out my hand to take it. She drew back, and said, but smiling almost invitingly the while, “You must not touch it;”—then, after a moment’s pause—“Or if you do, it must be very gently.” I touched it with a finger. A slight vibratory motion arose in it, accompanied, or perhaps manifested, by a faint sweet sound. I touched it again, and the sound increased. I touched it the third time: a tiny torrent of harmony rolled out of the little globe. She would not let me touch it any more.

===Chinese===
一个晴朗的正午，一个小姑娘来与我同行，她穿过树林而来，来的方向与我的路径恰成直角。她一路又唱又跳，快乐得像个小孩子，虽然她看上去几乎已是个成年女子。她手里——忽而这只手，忽而那只手——拿着一颗小小的球，明净澄澈，宛如最纯的水晶。这球仿佛既是她的玩物，又是她最珍视的宝物。有一刻你会以为她对它全然漫不经心，另一刻又见她为它的安危忧心忡忡。但我相信她无时无刻不在照管着它，也许在她最不着意的时候，照管得最紧。她含笑在我身旁停下，用最甜美的嗓音向我道了声日安。我对这孩子生出一种奇妙的喜爱——因为她给我的印象更像孩子，尽管我的理知告诉我并非如此。我们谈了片刻，随后便一道朝我原本行进的方向走去。我问她所拿的这球的来历，却得不到确定的回答，我便伸出手去接它。她缩身退让，同时脸上却带着近乎引人相邀的笑意，说道：“你不可以碰它；”——顿了一顿——“不过你若要碰，也得轻轻地。”我用一根手指碰了碰它。球内泛起一丝微颤的动，伴着——或者不如说是显现为——一缕清甜的声音。我再一碰，声音便增强了。第三次碰它时，一股细小的和声之流从那小小的球里滚滚涌出。她再也不许我碰它了。

===Original===
We traveled on together all that day. She left me when twilight came on; but next day, at noon, she met me as before, and again we traveled till evening. The third day she came once more at noon, and we walked on together. Now, though we had talked about a great many things connected with Fairy Land, and the life she had led hitherto, I had never been able to learn anything about the globe. This day, however, as we went on, the shadow glided round and inwrapt the maiden. It could not change her. But my desire to know about the globe, which in his gloom began to waver as with an inward light, and to shoot out flashes of many-colored flame, grew irresistible. I put out both my hands and laid hold of it. It began to sound as before. The sound rapidly increased, till it grew a low tempest of harmony, and the globe trembled, and quivered, and throbbed between my hands. I had not the heart to pull it away from the maiden, though I held it in spite of her attempts to take it from me; yes, I shame to say, in spite of her prayers, and, at last, her tears. The music went on growing in intensity and complication of tones, and the globe vibrated and heaved; till at last it burst in our hands, and a black vapor broke upwards from out of it; then turned, as if blown sideways, and enveloped the maiden, hiding even the shadow in its blackness. She held fast the fragments, which I abandoned, and fled from me into the forest in the direction whence she had come, wailing like a child, and crying, “You have broken my globe; my globe is broken—my globe is broken!” I followed her, in the hope of comforting her; but had not pursued her far, before a sudden cold gust of wind bowed the treetops above us, and swept through their stems around us; a great cloud overspread the day, and a fierce tempest came on, in which I lost sight of her. It lies heavy on my heart to this hour. At night, ere I fall asleep, often, whatever I may be thinking about, I suddenly hear her voice, crying out, “You have broken my globe; my globe is broken; ah, my globe!”

===Chinese===
那一整天我们继续同行。暮色四合时她离开了我；但第二天正午，她又像先前那样来会我，我们又同行到傍晚。第三天她又在正午来了，我们继续同行。话说回来，虽然我们谈过许多与仙境相关的事，谈过她迄今所过的生活，关于那颗球，我却始终未能探得一点消息。然而这一天，走着走着，影子滑转过来，把少女裹住了。它却改变不了她。但我想要探知这球底细的欲望，变得不可抗拒——那球在他的幽暗里开始微微明灭，仿佛内蕴光华，迸射出五彩火焰的闪光。我伸出双手，把它抓住。它像先前一样鸣响起来。声音迅速增强，直到汇成一场低回的和声风暴；那球在我两手之间颤抖、悸动、搏跳。我不忍把它从少女身边夺走，然而我仍握着它，不顾她要把它夺回去的挣扎；是啊，说来可耻，不顾她的恳求，最后，不顾她的眼泪。乐声不断在强度与音调的繁复上增长，那球震颤着、起伏着；终于它在我们手中迸裂，一股黑色的雾气从中向上腾起；随即像被侧风吹送似的横转过来，把少女裹住，连那影子也掩没在它的黑暗里。她紧攥着那些碎片——我早已弃之不顾——朝她来的方向逃进森林，像孩子一般哀哭，喊着：“你把我的球打破了；我的球破了——我的球破了！”我跟在她后面，指望安慰她；但没追出多远，一阵骤起的冷风蓦地把我们头顶的树梢吹得俯首，又从我们四周的树干间横扫而过；一朵大云遮暗了白昼，一场猛烈的暴风雨袭来，我便望不见她了。时至今日，这事仍沉沉压在我心头。夜里，在我入睡之前，不论我正想着什么，我常常会突然听见她的声音，喊着：“你把我的球打破了；我的球破了；啊，我的球！”

===Original===
Here I will mention one more strange thing; but whether this peculiarity was owing to my shadow at all, I am not able to assure myself. I came to a village, the inhabitants of which could not at first sight be distinguished from the dwellers in our land. They rather avoided than sought my company, though they were very pleasant when I addressed them. But at last I observed, that whenever I came within a certain distance of any one of them, which distance, however, varied with different individuals, the whole appearance of the person began to change; and this change increased in degree as I approached. When I receded to the former distance, the former appearance was restored. The nature of the change was grotesque, following no fixed rule. The nearest resemblance to it that I know, is the distortion produced in your countenance when you look at it as reflected in a concave or convex surface—say, either side of a bright spoon. Of this phenomenon I first became aware in rather a ludicrous way. My host’s daughter was a very pleasant pretty girl, who made herself more agreeable to me than most of those about me. For some days my companion-shadow had been less obtrusive than usual; and such was the reaction of spirits occasioned by the simple mitigation of torment, that, although I had cause enough besides to be gloomy, I felt light and comparatively happy. My impression is, that she was quite aware of the law of appearances that existed between the people of the place and myself, and had resolved to amuse herself at my expense; for one evening, after some jesting and raillery, she, somehow or other, provoked me to attempt to kiss her. But she was well defended from any assault of the kind. Her countenance became, of a sudden, absurdly hideous; the pretty mouth was elongated and otherwise amplified sufficiently to have allowed of six simultaneous kisses. I started back in bewildered dismay; she burst into the merriest fit of laughter, and ran from the room. I soon found that the same undefinable law of change operated between me and all the other villagers; and that, to feel I was in pleasant company, it was absolutely necessary for me to discover and observe the right focal distance between myself and each one with whom I had to do. This done, all went pleasantly enough. Whether, when I happened to neglect this precaution, I presented to them an equally ridiculous appearance, I did not ascertain; but I presume that the alteration was common to the approximating parties. I was likewise unable to determine whether I was a necessary party to the production of this strange transformation, or whether it took place as well, under the given circumstances, between the inhabitants themselves.

===Chinese===
这里我还要提一件更奇的事；但这一特性究竟是否该归因于我的影子，我自己也无从断言。我来到一座村庄，村里的居民乍看之下，竟分辨不出与我们本土的居民有何不同。他们与其说是寻求与我作伴，不如说是避着我，不过我一开口攀谈，他们便都十分和悦。可我终于注意到，每当我与其中任何人接近到某段距离之内——这段距离却因人而异——那人的整个容貌便开始改变；我愈走近，这改变便愈甚。待我退回原先的距离，原先的容貌便告复原。这变化的性质是怪诞的，没有固定的规则。就我所知，与它最相近的，是你从凹面或凸面——譬如一把光亮汤匙的任一面——里照见自己面容时所生的那种变形。这现象我最初察觉的方式颇为滑稽。我房东的女儿是位极可爱、极标致的姑娘，她待我比周围大多数人都和悦亲热。那几天，我那随行的影子不像往常那样碍眼了；折磨稍有减轻，精神便起了这样的反拨，以致我虽然另有足够的理由阴郁，却觉得轻快，相对而言还算快乐。我的印象是，她十分清楚这地方的人与我之间存在的形貌法则，并打定主意要拿我来取乐；因为一天傍晚，一番玩笑戏谑之后，她不知怎地竟惹得我试图去吻她。但她对这一类的进袭，防卫得十分周全。她的面容霎时变得荒唐地狰狞；那张秀美的嘴拉长了，并以别的方式放大开来，足以容人同时吻上六下。我惊惶错乱地向后一缩；她却爆发出最欢畅的一阵大笑，跑出屋去了。我很快发现，同样那一条难以名状的变化法则，也在我与所有其他村民之间起作用；而若要觉得相与的人令人愉快，我绝对必须找出并遵守我与每个交往之人之间恰当的焦距。做到了这一点，一切都进行得相当愉快。至于我偶尔疏忽了这重防范时，在他们的眼中我是否也呈现出一副同样可笑的模样，我未曾查明；但我推想，这变化是为彼此趋近的双方所共有的。我同样无法断定，我对于这奇怪变形的产生是否为一方必要因素；抑或在既有的情形之下，这变形也会在村民们彼此之间发生。

## X / 第十章

===Original===
> From Eden’s bowers the full-fed rivers flow,
 

 To guide the outcasts to the land of woe:
 

 Our Earth one little toiling streamlet yields,
 

 To guide the wanderers to the happy fields.

===Chinese===
> 从伊甸的绿亭，满涨的江河滚滚流下，
 

 引领被逐之人去往苦难之邦：
 

 我们的大地却只献出一条小小的、劳作不息的溪流，
 

 引领漂泊者去往幸福的田野。

===Original===
After leaving this village, where I had rested for nearly a week, I traveled through a desert region of dry sand and glittering rocks, peopled principally by goblin-fairies. When I first entered their domains, and, indeed, whenever I fell in with another tribe of them, they began mocking me with offered handfuls of gold and jewels, making hideous grimaces at me, and performing the most antic homage, as if they thought I expected reverence, and meant to humor me like a maniac. But ever, as soon as one cast his eyes on the shadow behind me, he made a wry face, partly of pity, partly of contempt, and looked ashamed, as if he had been caught doing something inhuman; then, throwing down his handful of gold, and ceasing all his grimaces, he stood aside to let me pass in peace, and made signs to his companions to do the like. I had no inclination to observe them much, for the shadow was in my heart as well as at my heels. I walked listlessly and almost hopelessly along, till I arrived one day at a small spring; which, bursting cool from the heart of a sun-heated rock, flowed somewhat southwards from the direction I had been taking. I drank of this spring, and found myself wonderfully refreshed. A kind of love to the cheerful little stream arose in my heart. It was born in a desert; but it seemed to say to itself, “I will flow, and sing, and lave my banks, till I make my desert a paradise.” I thought I could not do better than follow it, and see what it made of it. So down with the stream I went, over rocky lands, burning with sunbeams. But the rivulet flowed not far, before a few blades of grass appeared on its banks, and then, here and there, a stunted bush. Sometimes it disappeared altogether underground; and after I had wandered some distance, as near as I could guess, in the direction it seemed to take, I would suddenly hear it again, singing, sometimes far away to my right or left, amongst new rocks, over which it made new cataracts of watery melodies. The verdure on its banks increased as it flowed; other streams joined it; and at last, after many days’ travel, I found myself, one gorgeous summer evening, resting by the side of a broad river, with a glorious horse-chestnut tree towering above me, and dropping its blossoms, milk-white and rosy-red all about me. As I sat, a gush of joy sprang forth in my heart, and over flowed at my eyes. Through my tears, the whole landscape glimmered in such bewildering loveliness, that I felt as if I were entering Fairy Land for the first time, and some loving hand were waiting to cool my head, and a loving word to warm my heart. Roses, wild roses, everywhere! So plentiful were they, they not only perfumed the air, they seemed to dye it a faint rose-hue. The color floated abroad with the scent, and clomb, and spread, until the whole west blushed and glowed with the gathered incense of roses. And my heart fainted with longing in my bosom. Could I but see the Spirit of the Earth, as I saw once the indwelling woman of the beech-tree, and my beauty of the pale marble, I should be content. Content!—Oh, how gladly would I die of the light of her eyes! Yea, I would cease to be, if that would bring me one word of love from the one mouth. The twilight sank around, and infolded me with sleep. I slept as I had not slept for months. I did not awake till late in the morning; when, refreshed in body and mind, I rose as from the death that wipes out the sadness of life, and then dies itself in the new morrow. Again I followed the stream; now climbing a steep rocky bank that hemmed it in; now wading through long grasses and wild flowers in its path; now through meadows; and anon through woods that crowded down to the very lip of the water.

===Chinese===
离开那个我休整了近一个星期的村庄之后，我穿行于一片干旱的荒漠地带，遍地是干沙与熠熠生辉的岩石，栖居其间的主要是妖怪仙灵（goblin-fairies）。初入它们领地时——其实，此后每逢我撞见它们的另一支部族亦然——它们便开始嘲弄我，捧上一把把金子与宝石，对我做着狰狞的鬼脸，行种种滑稽可笑的致敬之礼，仿佛它们以为我指望受人尊崇，便打算像哄一个疯子那样迁就我。但每当其中一个把目光落在我身后的影子上，他便会把脸一垮，一半出于怜悯，一半出于轻蔑，并现出羞惭之色，仿佛被人撞见做了什么不合人道的事；随后，他把手里的那把金子一丢，收起一切鬼脸，站到一旁让我安然走过，并打手势叫同伴们照办。我无心多去观察它们，因为影子既跟在我脚跟，也盘踞在我心头。我无精打采、几近绝望地走着，直到有一天来到一处小小的泉眼；泉水从一个被太阳晒热的岩石心里凉凉地迸涌而出，朝着我行进方向偏南流去。我喝了这泉水，觉得自己奇妙地恢复了精神。我心里对这条欢快的小溪生出了一种爱意。它生于荒漠；它却似乎自言自语：「我要流淌，要歌唱，要浸润我的两岸，直到我把我的荒漠变成乐园。」我想，最好是跟着它走，看看它究竟会把它造成什么模样。于是我顺着溪流往下走，走过被日光灼烧的岩石之地。溪流没流出多远，岸边便冒出几茎草叶，随后又东一丛西一丛地出现矮墩墩的灌木。有时它整个儿隐入地下；等我朝它似乎所取的方向胡乱走了一段路之后，又会蓦地听见它重新歌唱起来，有时在我右方或左方的远处，在新生的岩石之间，为那些岩石织出新的、以水造成的旋律的瀑布。两岸的青翠随它流淌而愈见繁茂；别的溪流纷纷汇入；终于，在多日的跋涉之后，一个绚丽的夏日傍晚，我发觉自己正歇息在一条宽阔的大河之畔，头顶高耸着一株壮丽的马栗树（horse-chestnut），纷纷扬扬地把乳白与玫瑰红的花朵落在我四周。我坐在那里，一股欢喜从心里迸涌而出，漫溢到眼中来。透过泪水，整片景色闪烁着令人目眩神迷的可爱，我觉得自己仿佛是初次踏进仙境，仿佛有一只慈爱的手正等着来凉一凉我的额头，有一句慈爱的话等着来暖一暖我的心。玫瑰，野玫瑰，到处都是！它们多得不仅香透了空气，还仿佛把空气也染成了一层淡淡的玫瑰色。那颜色随香气飘散开来，攀升，铺展，直到整个西天都因玫瑰汇聚的香火而绯红、而灼灼生光。我的心因渴慕在胸中几近昏厥。倘若我能看见大地之灵（Spirit of the Earth），如同我一度看见山毛榉中栖居的女子、看见我那苍白大理石的美人，我就满足了。满足了！——哦，若能死于她眼眸的光里，我多么甘愿！是的，倘若这能从那一张口中为我换来一句爱语，我甘愿就此不复存在。暮色四合，将我裹入睡乡。我睡了几个月来不曾有过的沉酣一觉。直到早晨很晚我才醒来；那时，身心俱已一新，我起身如从一场死亡中起身——那死亡抹去生命的哀愁，随后自身也消逝在新的明天里。我再次顺着溪流走；一时攀上夹束着它的陡峭石岸；一时蹚过它道上的长草与野花；一时穿过草甸；一时又穿过一直挤挤挨挨长到水边的树林。

===Original===
At length, in a nook of the river, gloomy with the weight of overhanging foliage, and still and deep as a soul in which the torrent eddies of pain have hollowed a great gulf, and then, subsiding in violence, have left it full of a motionless, fathomless sorrow—I saw a little boat lying. So still was the water here, that the boat needed no fastening. It lay as if someone had just stepped ashore, and would in a moment return. But as there were no signs of presence, and no track through the thick bushes; and, moreover, as I was in Fairy Land where one does very much as he pleases, I forced my way to the brink, stepped into the boat, pushed it, with the help of the tree-branches, out into the stream, lay down in the bottom, and let my boat and me float whither the stream would carry us. I seemed to lose myself in the great flow of sky above me, unbroken in its infinitude, except when, now and then, coming nearer the shore at a bend in the river, a tree would sweep its mighty head silently above mine, and glide away back into the past, never more to fling its shadow over me. I fell asleep in this cradle, in which mother Nature was rocking her weary child; and while I slept, the sun slept not, but went round his arched way. When I awoke, he slept in the waters, and I went on my silent path beneath a round silvery moon. And a pale moon looked up from the floor of the great blue cave that lay in the abysmal silence beneath.

===Chinese===
终于，在河流的一个幽僻角落——那里悬垂的枝叶沉沉压下，一派幽暗；水静而深，像一个灵魂，痛苦的激流漩涡在其中凿出一个巨大的深渊，随后激流收敛了暴烈，只留下一片凝止的、深不可测的悲哀——我望见一叶小船停泊着。这里的水那样平静，小船无须任何系缆。它泊在那里，仿佛有人刚刚上岸，片刻就要回来。但既然四下没有人在场的迹象，灌木丛中也无路径可寻；何况我身在仙境，这里的人行事大可随心所欲，我便拨开枝条挤到水边，跨进小船，借着树枝的帮助把它撑进水流，在船底躺下，任凭小船载着我随波漂去，漂向流水愿意带我们去的地方。我仿佛把自己迷失在头顶天空的浩大流淌里，那无穷无尽从无间断——只是偶尔，在河流转弯处驶近岸边时，会有树木把它硕大的头颅无声地掠过我的头顶，随即滑退回既往之中，从此再不会把它的影子投到我身上。我在这摇篮里睡着了，大自然母亲正摇着她倦怠的孩子；我睡时，太阳不曾睡去，而是沿他拱形的轨道走完全程。待我醒来，他已睡在水中，我便在一轮银亮的圆月之下，继续我静默的行程。而在下方那深处无底的寂静里，那个巨大的蓝色洞窟，也有一轮苍白的月亮自洞底仰望上来。

===Original===
Why are all reflections lovelier than what we call the reality?—not so grand or so strong, it may be, but always lovelier? Fair as is the gliding sloop on the shining sea, the wavering, trembling, unresting sail below is fairer still. Yea, the reflecting ocean itself, reflected in the mirror, has a wondrousness about its waters that somewhat vanishes when I turn towards itself. All mirrors are magic mirrors. The commonest room is a room in a poem when I turn to the glass. (And this reminds me, while I write, of a strange story which I read in the fairy palace, and of which I will try to make a feeble memorial in its place.) In whatever way it may be accounted for, of one thing we may be sure, that this feeling is no cheat; for there is no cheating in nature and the simple unsought feelings of the soul. There must be a truth involved in it, though we may but in part lay hold of the meaning. Even the memories of past pain are beautiful; and past delights, though beheld only through clefts in the gray clouds of sorrow, are lovely as Fairy Land. But how have I wandered into the deeper fairyland of the soul, while as yet I only float towards the fairy palace of Fairy Land! The moon, which is the lovelier memory or reflex of the down-gone sun, the joyous day seen in the faint mirror of the brooding night, had rapt me away.

===Chinese===
为什么一切倒影都比我们所谓的实体更可爱？——或许不如它壮丽，不如它强劲，却总是更可爱？正如滑过闪光海面的单桅帆船何其美丽，下方那摇曳、颤动、从不安歇的帆影却更美。是啊，就连那映照万物的海洋本身，映在镜中时，它的水也带着一种奇异的魅力，而当我转向海洋自身，那魅力便消减了几分。一切镜子都是魔镜。最平常的房间，当我转向镜中之时，便成了诗中的一间屋子。（写着写着，这又提醒我想起一个奇异的故事，那故事是我在仙宫里读到的，日后到了该说的地方，我将勉力为它留一份拙劣的记述。）无论这该如何解释，有一点我们可以确信：这种感受绝不是欺骗；因为在自然之中，在灵魂那些质朴的、不求自来的情感之中，没有欺骗。其中必然蕴含着某种真实，纵然我们只能部分地把握其意义。甚至对过往痛苦的回忆也是美的；而过往的欢愉，即便只能透过哀愁的灰色云层的裂隙窥见，也如仙境一般可爱。可我这是怎么搞的，明明还只是漂向仙境的仙宫，却已漫游进了灵魂更深处的仙境！那月亮——它是已经沉落的太阳那更可爱的记忆或回光，是欢乐的白昼在沉思的黑夜这面隐约的镜中的映像——早已把我的魂摄走了。

===Original===
I sat up in the boat. Gigantic forest trees were about me; through which, like a silver snake, twisted and twined the great river. The little waves, when I moved in the boat, heaved and fell with a plash as of molten silver, breaking the image of the moon into a thousand morsels, fusing again into one, as the ripples of laughter die into the still face of joy. The sleeping woods, in undefined massiveness; the water that flowed in its sleep; and, above all, the enchantress moon, which had cast them all, with her pale eye, into the charmed slumber, sank into my soul, and I felt as if I had died in a dream, and should never more awake.

===Chinese===
我在船中坐起身来。参天的森林巨树环立在我四周；那条大河如一条银蛇，在树间蜿蜒盘绕。当我在船中挪动，细小的波浪便起伏拍溅，声如熔化的白银，把月亮的影像碎成千片，又重新熔而为一，恰似欢笑的涟漪渐渐平息，敛入欢乐那静默的面容。沉睡的林木，浑然不辨轮廓的巍峨；睡梦中依然流淌的水；而尤其是那妖妇般的月亮，正是她用苍白的眼睛，把这一切都施了魔咒，催入蛊惑的沉眠——这一切沉入我的灵魂，我觉得自己仿佛已在梦中死去，从此再不会醒来。

===Original===
From this I was partly aroused by a glimmering of white, that, through the trees on the left, vaguely crossed my vision, as I gazed upwards. But the trees again hid the object; and at the moment, some strange melodious bird took up its song, and sang, not an ordinary birdsong, with constant repetitions of the same melody, but what sounded like a continuous strain, in which one thought was expressed, deepening in intensity as evolved in progress. It sounded like a welcome already overshadowed with the coming farewell. As in all sweetest music, a tinge of sadness was in every note. Nor do we know how much of the pleasures even of life we owe to the intermingled sorrows. Joy cannot unfold the deepest truths, although deepest truth must be deepest joy. Cometh white-robed Sorrow, stooping and wan, and flingeth wide the doors she may not enter. Almost we linger with Sorrow for very love.

===Chinese===
把我从这境地中部分唤醒的，是一点白色的微光；当我正仰望上方，它透过左边的树木，隐约掠过我的视野。但树木又把那东西遮住了；恰在此时，一只陌生而鸣声悦耳的鸟儿唱起了歌，它唱的不是寻常鸟鸣那种同一旋律的不断重复，而像一支连绵不断的曲调，其中表达着一个思想，随曲调的推进而愈见深沉强烈。它听来像一句欢迎，却已被将至的道别预先罩上了阴影。正如一切最甜美的音乐那样，每一个音符里都含着一丝哀愁。我们并不知道，就连生命中的欢愉，又有多少当归功于交织其间的悲哀。欢乐无法展开最深的真理，虽然最深的真理必然是最深的欢乐。身着白衣的悲哀俯身而来，脸色苍白，将她本不得进入的门扉豁然推开。我们几乎要因深爱而流连于悲哀。

===Original===
As the song concluded, the stream bore my little boat with a gentle sweep round a bend of the river; and lo! on a broad lawn, which rose from the water’s edge with a long green slope to a clear elevation from which the trees receded on all sides, stood a stately palace glimmering ghostly in the moonshine: it seemed to be built throughout of the whitest marble. There was no reflection of moonlight from windows—there seemed to be none; so there was no cold glitter; only, as I said, a ghostly shimmer. Numberless shadows tempered the shine, from column and balcony and tower. For everywhere galleries ran along the face of the buildings; wings were extended in many directions; and numberless openings, through which the moonbeams vanished into the interior, and which served both for doors and windows, had their separate balconies in front, communicating with a common gallery that rose on its own pillars. Of course, I did not discover all this from the river, and in the moonlight. But, though I was there for many days, I did not succeed in mastering the inner topography of the building, so extensive and complicated was it.

===Chinese===
歌声将尽时，水流带着我的小船，绕着河流的一道弯缓缓一荡；看哪！在一方宽阔的草坪上——它自水边以一道长长的绿色斜坡升起，达到一处清爽的高地，树木在四面纷纷退让——矗立着一座恢宏的宫殿，在月光里幽灵似的微微闪光：它似乎通体以最白的大理石建成。窗上没有月光的反射——似乎根本没有窗；因此没有冷的闪烁；只有，如我所说，一种幽灵似的微光。无数的阴影冲淡了那光辉，来自廊柱、阳台与塔楼。因为到处都有回廊沿建筑的立面伸展；翼楼朝许多方向铺开；无数洞口——月光经此没入建筑内部，它们兼作门与窗——前面各有各自的阳台，与一条凭自身廊柱升起的公共回廊相通。当然，这一切并不是我在河上、在月光下一眼看尽的。而且，尽管我在那里住了许多天，我仍未能摸清这座建筑内部的结构布局，它实在太恢宏、太繁复了。

===Original===
Here I wished to land, but the boat had no oars on board. However, I found that a plank, serving for a seat, was unfastened, and with that I brought the boat to the bank, and scrambled on shore. Deep soft turf sank beneath my feet, as I went up the ascent towards the palace. When I reached it, I saw that it stood on a great platform of marble, with an ascent, by broad stairs of the same, all round it. Arrived on the platform, I found there was an extensive outlook over the forest, which, however, was rather veiled than revealed by the moonlight. Entering by a wide gateway, but without gates, into an inner court, surrounded on all sides by great marble pillars supporting galleries above, I saw a large fountain of porphyry in the middle, throwing up a lofty column of water, which fell, with a noise as of the fusion of all sweet sounds, into a basin beneath; overflowing which, it ran in a single channel towards the interior of the building. Although the moon was by this time so low in the west, that not a ray of her light fell into the court, over the height of the surrounding buildings; yet was the court lighted by a second reflex from the sun of other lands. For the top of the column of water, just as it spread to fall, caught the moonbeams, and like a great pale lamp, hung high in the night air, threw a dim memory of light (as it were) over the court below. This court was paved in diamonds of white and red marble. According to my custom since I entered Fairy Land, of taking for a guide whatever I first found moving in any direction, I followed the stream from the basin of the fountain. It led me to a great open door, beneath the ascending steps of which it ran through a low arch, and disappeared. Entering here, I found myself in a great hall, surrounded with white pillars, and paved with black and white. This I could see by the moonlight, which, from the other side, streamed through open windows into the hall. Its height I could not distinctly see. As soon as I entered, I had the feeling so common to me in the woods, that there were others there besides myself, though I could see no one, and heard no sound to indicate a presence. Since my visit to the Church of Darkness, my power of seeing the fairies of the higher orders had gradually diminished, until it had almost ceased. But I could frequently believe in their presence while unable to see them. Still, although I had company, and doubtless of a safe kind, it seemed rather dreary to spend the night in an empty marble hall, however beautiful, especially as the moon was near the going down, and it would soon be dark. So I began at the place where I entered, and walked round the hall, looking for some door or passage that might lead me to a more hospitable chamber. As I walked, I was deliciously haunted with the feeling that behind some one of the seemingly innumerable pillars, one who loved me was waiting for me. Then I thought she was following me from pillar to pillar as I went along; but no arms came out of the faint moonlight, and no sigh assured me of her presence.

===Chinese===
我想在这里上岸，可船上没有桨。不过我发现充当座板的一块木板并没有钉死，便用那木板把船撑到岸边，攀爬着上了岸。我向宫殿的坡上走去时，脚下深软的草皮微微下陷。走到宫前，我看见它矗立在一座巨大的大理石平台上，平台四周绕以同一石材的宽阔阶梯，可拾级而上。登上平台，我发现此处视野开阔，可以望远及整片森林，尽管那森林在月光下毋宁说是被遮掩着，而非被显露着。从一个宽阔的门道——却没有门扇——走入一座内院，院子四围是擎举着上方回廊的巨大大理石廊柱；院子正中，我看见一座斑岩砌成的大喷泉，喷起一柱高高的水，那水带着仿佛一切甜美乐音熔于一体的声响，落进下面的水池里；池水漫出之后，沿一道单独的水渠流向建筑内部。此时月亮在西方已是这样低，她的一线光也越不过四周建筑的高度照进院子里；然而院子仍被照亮着，凭借来自另一个世界的太阳的二次反光。因为那水柱的顶端，恰在铺散开来下落之际，接住了月光，像一盏巨大的苍白灯盏，高悬在夜气里，朝下面的院子投来一种（仿佛是）光的朦胧记忆。这院子以白色与红色大理石的菱形石块铺地。按照我进入仙境以来养成的习惯——凡最初发现朝什么方向移动之物，便拿它作向导——我顺着喷泉池中流出的水走。水把我引到一扇敞开的巨门前，水流在门内上行的台阶下穿过一道低矮的拱洞，消失不见了。从这里走进去，我发觉自己置身于一座大厅，四围环以白色廊柱，地上以黑白两色石块铺砌。这一点我可以借着月光看清，那月光从另一侧经敞开的窗子涌进大厅。厅有多高，我却看不分明。刚一进去，我便有一种感觉——那是我在树林里所常有的一种：除了我，那里还有别人，虽然我谁也看不见，也听不见任何显示有在场者的声息。自从我造访黑暗教堂（the Church of Darkness）之后，我看见高阶仙灵的能力便逐渐减退，直至几乎丧失。但即使看不见他们，我仍能时常确信他们在场。虽然如此，尽管我有陪伴，而且无疑是无害的一类，在一座空空荡荡的大理石厅堂里过夜，仍显得相当凄清，无论它多么美，何况月亮已近沉落，天很快就要黑了。于是我从进来的地方起步，绕着大厅走，寻找可能通向某一更为殷勤好客的房间的门或通道。我走着走着，一种甜美的萦绕缠磨着我：仿佛在那些看似无穷无尽的廊柱中的某一根后面，有一个爱我的人正在等我。继而我又想，她正随着我的走动，从这根柱到那根柱地追随我；可是没有手臂从微茫的月光里伸出来，也没有一声叹息向我担保她在场。

===Original===
At length I came to an open corridor, into which I turned; notwithstanding that, in doing so, I left the light behind. Along this I walked with outstretched hands, groping my way; till, arriving at another corridor, which seemed to strike off at right angles to that in which I was, I saw at the end a faintly glimmering light, too pale even for moonshine, resembling rather a stray phosphorescence. However, where everything was white, a little light went a great way. So I walked on to the end, and a long corridor it was. When I came up to the light, I found that it proceeded from what looked like silver letters upon a door of ebony; and, to my surprise even in the home of wonder itself, the letters formed the words, **The Chamber of Sir Anodos**. Although I had as yet no right to the honors of a knight, I ventured to conclude that the chamber was indeed intended for me; and, opening the door without hesitation, I entered. Any doubt as to whether I was right in so doing, was soon dispelled. What to my dark eyes seemed a blaze of light, burst upon me. A fire of large pieces of some sweet-scented wood, supported by dogs of silver, was burning on the hearth, and a bright lamp stood on a table, in the midst of a plentiful meal, apparently awaiting my arrival. But what surprised me more than all, was, that the room was in every respect a copy of my own room, the room whence the little stream from my basin had led me into Fairy Land. There was the very carpet of grass and moss and daisies, which I had myself designed; the curtains of pale blue silk, that fell like a cataract over the windows; the old-fashioned bed, with the chintz furniture, on which I had slept from boyhood. “Now I shall sleep,” I said to myself. “My shadow dares not come here.”

===Chinese===
后来我来到一条敞开的廊道，便转了进去；尽管如此一转，我便把光留在了身后。我伸着两手，摸索着沿廊前行；走到另一条似乎与我所在这条成直角岔开的廊道时，我望见尽头有一点隐约闪烁的光，那光太淡了，连月光也称不上，倒像是四处游散的磷火。不过，在处处皆白的地方，一点点光便能派上大用场。于是我朝尽头走去——那条廊道真长。当我走近那光，发现它出自一扇乌木门上看来像是银质的字母；而且，纵然身在奇迹的本乡本土，我仍不免一惊：那些字母拼成的字样，正是**阿诺多斯爵士之室**。虽然我至今还没有享受骑士荣誉的资格，我却斗胆断定这房间确实是为我预备的；于是我毫不迟疑地开了门，走了进去。我这样做得对不对，任何疑虑都很快消散了。一片在我那黑暗中的双眼看来不啻光焰的东西，扑面而来。壁炉里燃着大块的芳香的木柴，架在银柴架上；桌上一盏明灯，四周摆着一席丰盛的饭食，显然正在等候我的到来。而最令我惊奇的，是这房间在每一方面都是我自己房间的复本——就是那个房间，盆中流出的那道细流正是从那里领我进入了仙境。那地毯就是我亲手设计的，草与苔与雏菊的地毯；那淡蓝的丝绸帘幔，如瀑布般垂覆在窗上；那张老式的床，带着印花棉布的铺陈，我从孩提起就一直睡在上面。「如今我可以睡觉了，」我对自己说。「我的影子不敢来这里。」

===Original===
I sat down to the table, and began to help myself to the good things before me with confidence. And now I found, as in many instances before, how true the fairy tales are; for I was waited on, all the time of my meal, by invisible hands. I had scarcely to do more than look towards anything I wanted, when it was brought me, just as if it had come to me of itself. My glass was kept filled with the wine I had chosen, until I looked towards another bottle or decanter; when a fresh glass was substituted, and the other wine supplied. When I had eaten and drank more heartily and joyfully than ever since I entered Fairy Land, the whole was removed by several attendants, of whom some were male and some female, as I thought I could distinguish from the way the dishes were lifted from the table, and the motion with which they were carried out of the room. As soon as they were all taken away, I heard a sound as of the shutting of a door, and knew that I was left alone. I sat long by the fire, meditating, and wondering how it would all end; and when at length, wearied with thinking, I betook myself to my own bed, it was half with a hope that, when I awoke in the morning, I should awake not only in my own room, but in my own castle also; and that I should walk, out upon my own native soil, and find that Fairy Land was, after all, only a vision of the night. The sound of the falling waters of the fountain floated me into oblivion.

===Chinese===
我在桌边坐下，放心大胆地享用起面前的美餐来。此刻我发现，一如先前的许多情形，童话故事是多么真实；因为整个用餐期间，始终有看不见的手在侍奉我。我几乎只需朝我想要的任何东西望上一眼，它便被送到我面前，仿佛它自己走了过来。我的酒杯一直被斟满我所选的酒，直到我朝另一只酒瓶或细颈壶望去；于是一只新杯换了上来，另一种酒也续上了。自从进入仙境以来，我从未吃得这样痛快、这样欢乐；当吃饱喝足之后，几名侍者把一切撤了下去——从我揣度盘子从桌上被端起的方式、以及它们被端出房间的动作来看，其中有的是男人，有的是女人。它们全被撤走之后，我听见一响仿佛关门的声音，便知道只剩我一人了。我在炉边坐了很久，冥想着，不知这一切将如何收场；待到终于想得倦了，我便回到我自己的床上；这一半是怀着一种希望：明早醒来时，我不仅醒在自己的房间里，而且醒在自己的城堡里；我将走出去，踏上我自己的故土，发现仙境终究不过是一场夜的幻象。喷泉落水的声息把我漂送进了浑忘之境。

## XI / 第十一章

===Original===
> A wilderness of building, sinking far
> And self-withdrawn into a wondrous depth,
> Far sinking into splendor—without end!
> Fabric it seemed of diamond and of gold,
> With alabaster domes, and silver spires,
> And blazing terrace upon terrace, high
> Uplifted.

===Chinese===
> 一片营造的莽原，沉落向遥深的远处，
> 自行隐退，沉入一重奇异的深度，
> 愈沉愈深，沉入辉煌——无有穷尽！
> 它仿佛是金刚石与黄金筑成的营造，
> 有雪花石膏的穹顶、白银的塔尖，
> 炽燃的台地一层又一层，高高
> 耸起。

===Original===
But when, after a sleep, which, although dreamless, yet left behind it a sense of past blessedness, I awoke in the full morning, I found, indeed, that the room was still my own; but that it looked abroad upon an unknown landscape of forest and hill and dale on the one side—and on the other, upon the marble court, with the great fountain, the crest of which now flashed glorious in the sun, and cast on the pavement beneath a shower of faint shadows from the waters that fell from it into the marble basin below.

Agreeably to all authentic accounts of the treatment of travellers in Fairy Land, I found by my bedside a complete suit of fresh clothing, just such as I was in the habit of wearing; for, though varied sufficiently from the one removed, it was yet in complete accordance with my tastes. I dressed myself in this, and went out. The whole palace shone like silver in the sun. The marble was partly dull and partly polished; and every pinnacle, dome, and turret ended in a ball, or cone, or cusp of silver. It was like frost-work, and too dazzling, in the sun, for earthly eyes like mine. I will not attempt to describe the environs, save by saying, that all the pleasures to be found in the most varied and artistic arrangement of wood and river, lawn and wild forest, garden and shrubbery, rocky hill and luxurious vale; in living creatures wild and tame, in gorgeous birds, scattered fountains, little streams, and reedy lakes—all were here. Some parts of the palace itself I shall have occasion to describe more minutely.

===Chinese===
然而，一觉醒来——那一觉虽无梦境，身后却留下一缕过往福祉的余味——天已大亮，我发现房间确实仍是我自己的房间；只是凭窗望去，一侧是一片未知的森林、山丘与谷地的风景；另一侧，则是那大理石庭院，和庭中的大喷泉，泉顶此刻正在阳光里辉煌闪耀，那跌落注入下方大理石水池的水，在底下石板上洒下一阵依稀浮动的疏影。

与一切可信的记载里仙境款待旅人的规矩相合，我在床边找到一整套干净衣裳，恰是我平日惯穿的样式；因为它虽与脱下的那套大不相同，却又全然合乎我的喜好。我穿上这身衣服，走了出去。整座仙宫在阳光下银光灿然。大理石有的晦暗，有的磨得光亮；每一座尖顶、穹顶和角楼，都终结在一颗银球、银锥或银尖上。它像霜花凝成的宫殿，在太阳底下太过耀眼，不是我这双尘世之眼所能逼视。四围景致，我不打算描写了，只消说一句：在林木与河流、草坪与野林、园囿与灌木、石山与丰饶溪谷的最富变化、最具匠心的布置中能找到的一切乐趣；在野生与驯养的活物中，在华丽的禽鸟、四散的喷泉、小小的溪流与芦苇湖上——全都在这里。仙宫本身的某些部分，我将有机会细细描写。

===Original===
For this whole morning, I never thought of my demon shadow; and not till the weariness which supervened on delight brought it again to my memory, did I look round to see if it was behind me: it was scarcely discernible. But its presence, however faintly revealed, sent a pang to my heart, for the pain of which, not all the beauties around me could compensate. It was followed, however, by the comforting reflection that, peradventure, I might here find the magic word of power to banish the demon and set me free, so that I should no longer be a man beside myself. The Queen of Fairy Land, thought I, must dwell here: surely she will put forth her power to deliver me, and send me singing through the further gates of her country back to my own land. "Shadow of me!" I said; "which art not me, but which representest thyself to me as me; here I may find a shadow of light which will devour thee, the shadow of darkness! Here I may find a blessing which will fall on thee as a curse, and damn thee to the blackness whence thou hast emerged unbidden." I said this, stretched at length on the slope of the lawn above the river; and as the hope arose within me, the sun came forth from a light fleecy cloud that swept across his face; and hill and dale, and the great river winding on through the still mysterious forest, flashed back his rays as with a silent shout of joy; all nature lived and glowed; the very earth grew warm beneath me; a magnificent dragonfly went past me like an arrow from a bow, and a whole concert of birds burst into choral song.

===Chinese===
这一整个上午，我都没有想起我的魔影；直到欢愉之后袭来的疲乏把它重新带回记忆，我才回身看它是否还跟在背后：它几乎辨认不出。然而它的存在，无论显现得多么淡薄，都使我心中一阵刺痛，四围的美景再盛，也补偿不了这疼痛。不过紧接着来的是一个宽慰的念头：说不定，我能在这里找到那句有威力的魔语，逐走这魔魅，还我自由，使我从此不再是身外另拖着一个自己的人。我心想，仙国女王必定住在这里：她定会施展法力解救我，让我一路歌唱着穿过她国度的更远处的城门，回到我自己的国土。「我这属身的魔影啊！」我说；「你不是我，却把自己扮作我，呈现在我眼前；在这里，我或许能找到一道光的影子，把你这黑暗的影子吞吃掉！在这里，我或许能找到一个祝福，它落到你身上便成了咒诅，把你定罪扔回那团黑暗——你正是未经召唤、从那里私自冒出来的。」我说这话时，正伸直了四肢躺在河岸上方草坪的斜坡上；希望在我心中升起，太阳便从一缕掠过他面庞的轻软薄云中走了出来；山丘与谷地，还有那条蜿蜒流过静谧神秘森林的大河，一齐把他的光辉反射回来，仿佛一场无声的欢呼；整个自然都活了、亮了；连我身下的大地也暖了起来；一只华美的蜻蜓像离弦之箭般从我身旁掠过，满林的鸟雀齐声爆发出合唱。

===Original===
The heat of the sun soon became too intense even for passive support. I therefore rose, and sought the shelter of one of the arcades. Wandering along from one to another of these, wherever my heedless steps led me, and wondering everywhere at the simple magnificence of the building, I arrived at another hall, the roof of which was of a pale blue, spangled with constellations of silver stars, and supported by porphyry pillars of a paler red than ordinary.—In this house (I may remark in passing), silver seemed everywhere preferred to gold; and such was the purity of the air, that it showed nowhere signs of tarnishing.—The whole of the floor of this hall, except a narrow path behind the pillars, paved with black, was hollowed into a huge basin, many feet deep, and filled with the purest, most liquid and radiant water. The sides of the basin were white marble, and the bottom was paved with all kinds of refulgent stones, of every shape and hue. In their arrangement, you would have supposed, at first sight, that there was no design, for they seemed to lie as if cast there from careless and playful hands; but it was a most harmonious confusion; and as I looked at the play of their colors, especially when the waters were in motion, I came at last to feel as if not one little pebble could be displaced, without injuring the effect of the whole. Beneath this floor of the water, lay the reflection of the blue inverted roof, fretted with its silver stars, like a second deeper sea, clasping and upholding the first. This fairy bath was probably fed from the fountain in the court. Led by an irresistible desire, I undressed, and plunged into the water. It clothed me as with a new sense and its object both in one. The waters lay so close to me, they seemed to enter and revive my heart. I rose to the surface, shook the water from my hair, and swam as in a rainbow, amid the coruscations of the gems below seen through the agitation caused by my motion. Then, with open eyes, I dived, and swam beneath the surface. And here was a new wonder. For the basin, thus beheld, appeared to extend on all sides like a sea, with here and there groups as of ocean rocks, hollowed by ceaseless billows into wondrous caves and grotesque pinnacles. Around the caves grew seaweeds of all hues, and the corals glowed between; while far off, I saw the glimmer of what seemed to be creatures of human form at home in the waters. I thought I had been enchanted; and that when I rose to the surface, I should find myself miles from land, swimming alone upon a heaving sea; but when my eyes emerged from the waters, I saw above me the blue spangled vault, and the red pillars around. I dived again, and found myself once more in the heart of a great sea. I then arose, and swam to the edge, where I got out easily, for the water reached the very brim, and, as I drew near, washed in tiny waves over the black marble border. I dressed, and went out, deeply refreshed.

===Chinese===
日头的热力不久便太炽烈了，连静静承受也难。于是我起身，去寻一处拱廊遮蔽。我沿着一座座拱廊信步游荡，任随意的脚步把我引到哪里，一路上处处惊叹这建筑质朴的壮丽，末了来到另一座大厅：厅顶呈淡蓝色，缀着银星组成的星座，由斑岩的柱子支撑着，那种红比寻常的更浅。——在这座宫殿里（容我顺带一说），银似乎处处都压过了金；而且此地空气如此纯净，银上竟不见丝毫失去光泽的痕迹。——大厅的整个地面，除去柱子背后一条铺着黑石的小径，都被凿成一个巨大的凹盆，深达数尺，注满了最纯净、最流动、最明亮的水。盆壁是白大理石，盆底铺着各种灿灿生辉的石头，形状色泽无一不备。看它们的布置，乍一看你会以为毫无设计，因为它们仿佛是被一双漫不经心、嬉戏的手随手抛掷在那里的；然而那是一种最和谐的纷乱；我望着它们色彩的流转，尤其在水面动起来的时候，我终于觉得，哪怕挪动一颗小小的卵石，也会损害整体的效果。在这层水的地板之下，躺着那蓝色倒悬的穹顶的倒影，穹顶上的银星装点着它，像第二片更深的海，环抱着、托举着第一片。这仙家的浴池，想必是由庭院里那座大喷泉供水的。一种不可抗拒的欲望引着我，我脱了衣服，纵身入水。水披在我身上，仿佛一种崭新的感官连同它的对象合而为一。水如此贴切地偎着我，仿佛要沁进我的心，使它复苏。我浮出水面，抖去发上的水珠，在虹光里游着——透过我游动搅起的涟漪，看得见底下宝石的熠熠闪光。随后，我睁着眼睛潜下去，在水底游。这里又有一重新奇。从这样望出去，那水盆仿佛向四面八方伸展开去，像一片大海，各处有一群群仿佛海中礁石的东西，被无休止的浪涛凿成奇异的岩窟和怪状的尖峰。窟穴四周长着各种颜色的海草，珊瑚在其间发出光焰；远处，我望见一些隐约的微光，似乎是人形的生灵，在水里安居自得。我以为自己中了魔法；以为等我浮出水面，会发现自己已在离陆地许多英里之外，独自游在一片起伏的大海上；可当我的眼睛一出水，头顶上仍是那缀满银星的蓝色穹顶，四周仍是那些红柱。我再潜下去，发觉自己又一次置身于一片大海的中央。这回我浮上来，游到池边，很容易便上了岸，因为水一直齐着池沿，我一走近，细小的波浪便漫过黑大理石的边沿。我穿好衣服，走了出去，只觉身心焕然一新。

===Original===
And now I began to discern faint, gracious forms, here and there throughout the building. Some walked together in earnest conversation. Others strayed alone. Some stood in groups, as if looking at and talking about a picture or a statue. None of them heeded me. Nor were they plainly visible to my eyes. Sometimes a group, or single individual, would fade entirely out of the realm of my vision as I gazed. When evening came, and the moon arose, clear as a round of a horizon-sea when the sun hangs over it in the west, I began to see them all more plainly; especially when they came between me and the moon; and yet more especially, when I myself was in the shade. But, even then, I sometimes saw only the passing wave of a white robe; or a lovely arm or neck gleamed by in the moonshine; or white feet went walking alone over the moony sward. Nor, I grieve to say, did I ever come much nearer to these glorious beings, or ever look upon the Queen of the Fairies herself. My destiny ordered otherwise.

In this palace of marble and silver, and fountains and moonshine, I spent many days; waited upon constantly in my room with everything desirable, and bathing daily in the fairy bath. All this time I was little troubled with my demon shadow. I had a vague feeling that he was somewhere about the palace; but it seemed as if the hope that I should in this place be finally freed from his hated presence, had sufficed to banish him for a time. How and where I found him, I shall soon have to relate.

The third day after my arrival, I found the library of the palace; and here, all the time I remained, I spent most of the middle of the day. For it was, not to mention far greater attractions, a luxurious retreat from the noontide sun. During the mornings and afternoons, I wandered about the lovely neighborhood, or lay, lost in delicious daydreams, beneath some mighty tree on the open lawn. My evenings were by and by spent in a part of the palace, the account of which, and of my adventures in connection with it, I must yet postpone for a little.

===Chinese===
如今我开始在这座建筑的各处，依稀辨认出一些优雅的身形。有的结伴同行，倾心交谈。有的独自漫步。有的三五成群站着，仿佛在看一幅画或一座雕像，并谈论着。他们谁也不理会我。我的眼睛也看他们不真切。有时，正当我凝望，一群或一个身影会从我的视野里整个淡出。黄昏来临，月亮升起，清亮得如同西天太阳低垂时分海平线上那一轮浑圆的光晕，我便开始把他们看得更清楚了；尤其当他们走到我与月亮之间；而当我自己立在暗处时，就更加清楚。但即便那时，我有时也只看见一袭白衣如波掠过；或是一只可爱的手臂、一段颈项在月光里一闪；或是白色的脚掌独自走在月色浸染的草地上。可惜我不得不说，我始终没能与这些荣光中的存在更接近些，也始终未曾瞻望到仙国女王本人。然而我的命运另有旨意。

在这座大理石与白银、喷泉与月光的宫殿里，我度过了许多时日；房间里无时不有称心如意的东西伺候着，每天还在那仙家浴池里沐浴。这一切时候，我的魔影很少来搅扰我。我隐约觉得他就在宫中某处；但似乎是这样：只要我心里怀着在这个地方终于能摆脱他那可憎行迹的希望，就足以把他暂时逐开了。至于我怎样、在哪里又找到了他，我很快就要讲到。

到达后的第三天，我发现了宫中的藏书室；此后我逗留的全部时间里，每天的正午大多在这里度过。因为且不说它有远为动人的种种胜处，单是躲避正午的烈日，它就是一处豪华的退居。上午和下午，我在可爱的四近漫游，或者躺在开阔草坪上某株大树底下，沉入甜美的白日梦中。至于傍晚，我渐渐是在宫殿的另一处度过的；关于那处地方，以及我在那里经历的事情，还须暂缓再叙。

===Original===
The library was a mighty hall, lighted from the roof, which was formed of something like glass, vaulted over in a single piece, and stained throughout with a great mysterious picture in gorgeous coloring. The walls were lined from floor to roof with books and books: most of them in ancient bindings, but some in strange new fashions which I had never seen, and which, were I to make the attempt, I could ill describe. All around the walls, in front of the books, ran galleries in rows, communicating by stairs. These galleries were built of all kinds of colored stones; all sorts of marble and granite, with porphyry, jasper, lapis lazuli, agate, and various others, were ranged in wonderful melody of successive colors. Although the material, then, of which these galleries and stairs were built, rendered necessary a certain degree of massiveness in the construction, yet such was the size of the place, that they seemed to run along the walls like cords. Over some parts of the library, descended curtains of silk of various dyes, none of which I ever saw lifted while I was there; and I felt somehow that it would be presumptuous in me to venture to look within them. But the use of the other books seemed free; and day after day I came to the library, threw myself on one of the many sumptuous eastern carpets, which lay here and there on the floor, and read, and read, until weary; if that can be designated as weariness, which was rather the faintness of rapturous delight; or until, sometimes, the failing of the light invited me to go abroad, in the hope that a cool gentle breeze might have arisen to bathe, with an airy invigorating bath, the limbs which the glow of the burning spirit within had withered no less than the glow of the blazing sun without.

===Chinese===
藏书室是一座宏大的厅堂，光线从屋顶进来；屋顶像是玻璃的东西筑成，浑然一体地拱覆着，通体绘染着一大幅色彩绚丽的神秘图画。四壁从地到顶排满了书，又是书，还是书：大多装帧古旧，也有一些是我从未见过的奇异新式样，若让我试着描述，我也形容不好。沿墙在书的前面，一层层环廊依次排开，有楼梯相通。这些环廊用各种彩色的石头筑成；各样的大理石与花岗岩，连同斑岩、碧玉、青金石、玛瑙以及其他种种，按前后相续的色彩排成奇妙的旋律。固然，筑廊与梯的材料要求构造上具有一定的厚重，但地方如此宏大，那些环廊贴墙延伸，竟像几根细绳。藏书室有些部分的上方，垂着各色染成的丝绸帷幔，我在那里的时候从未见它们掀起过；而我不知怎地总觉得，擅去帷幔之内窥看，于我是僭越的。其余的书倒似乎听凭取用；我便一天又一天来到藏书室，把自己抛在地板上随处铺陈的华贵东方地毯中的一张上，读啊，读啊，读到倦时才罢——倘若那也配称作倦怠，那倒不如说是狂喜的沉醉带来的酥软；有时，也读到光色渐暗，邀我出门走走，盼着此时或已起了一阵清凉柔和的微风，好给四肢一个轻爽提神的空气之浴——那四肢，被内里燃烧的心灵的热焰所烤萎，也不下于外头烈日的灼光。

===Original===
One peculiarity of these books, or at least most of those I looked into, I must make a somewhat vain attempt to describe.

If, for instance, it was a book of metaphysics I opened, I had scarcely read two pages before I seemed to myself to be pondering over discovered truth, and constructing the intellectual machine whereby to communicate the discovery to my fellow men. With some books, however, of this nature, it seemed rather as if the process was removed yet a great way further back; and I was trying to find the root of a manifestation, the spiritual truth whence a material vision sprang; or to combine two propositions, both apparently true, either at once or in different remembered moods, and to find the point in which their invisibly converging lines would unite in one, revealing a truth higher than either and differing from both; though so far from being opposed to either, that it was that whence each derived its life and power. Or if the book was one of travels, I found myself the traveller. New lands, fresh experiences, novel customs, rose around me. I walked, I discovered, I fought, I suffered, I rejoiced in my success. Was it a history? I was the chief actor therein. I suffered my own blame; I was glad in my own praise. With a fiction it was the same. Mine was the whole story. For I took the place of the character who was most like myself, and his story was mine; until, grown weary with the life of years condensed in an hour, or arrived at my deathbed, or the end of the volume, I would awake, with a sudden bewilderment, to the consciousness of my present life, recognizing the walls and roof around me, and finding I joyed or sorrowed only in a book. If the book was a poem, the words disappeared, or took the subordinate position of an accompaniment to the succession of forms and images that rose and vanished with a soundless rhythm, and a hidden rhyme.

===Chinese===
这些书有一种特质——至少我看过的多数如此——我必须来试着描写一番，虽然多半是徒劳。

比如，我打开的是一部形而上学的书，我还没读完两页，便觉得自己正在反复推究一条已被发现的真理，并在建造一种智性的机器，好把这发现传达给我的同类。不过，这类书里也有几种，那过程仿佛被推到了远得多的源头；我是在探寻一种显现的根子，探寻一个物质幻象所从出的精神真理；或者把两个命题——两者显然都真，或同时为真，或在我记忆中不同的心境里各自为真——合在一起，找出它们那两条看不见地互相趋近的线将在哪一点上合而为一，从而显出一个比两者都高、又不同于两者的真理；这真理非但与两者毫不对立，倒是两者各自从它取得生命与力量。又比如那是一部游记，我便发现自己就是那旅人。新的国土、新鲜的经历、陌生的风俗，在我四围升起。我行走，我发现，我战斗，我受苦，我为自己的成功而欢欣。是史书么？我就是其中的主角。我承受落在自己身上的责难；我在落在自己身上的赞美里欢欣。读小说也是如此。整个故事都是我的。因为我替代了那个最像我的人物，他的故事便是我的故事；直到——被浓缩在一小时里的经年生活弄得倦了，或已走到自己的病榻前，或已到了卷末——我才猛然一怔，醒来，意识到自己目前的生活，认出四围的墙壁与屋顶，发觉自己不过是在一本书里悲喜过。倘若那是一部诗，字句便消失了，或者退居为一种伴奏，陪衬着那一系列形象与意象的连续显现——它们随一种无声的节奏升起又消隐，押着一种隐秘的韵。

===Original===
In one, with a mystical title, which I cannot recall, I read of a world that is not like ours. The wondrous account, in such a feeble, fragmentary way as is possible to me, I would willingly impart. Whether or not it was all a poem, I cannot tell; but, from the impulse I felt, when I first contemplated writing it, to break into rhyme, to which impulse I shall give way if it comes upon me again, I think it must have been, partly at least, in verse.

===Chinese===
其中有一部，书名神秘，我已想不起来，我在里面读到一个世界，不像我们的世界。那段奇异的记述，我愿尽我所能，以我这微弱而零碎的方式转述出来。它究竟是不是通体一首诗，我说不上；但当初打算把它写下来时，我曾感到一种要破格押韵的冲动——倘若这冲动再来，我也会依从它——由这一点想来，我想它至少有几分是用韵文写成的。

## XII / 第十二章

===Original===
> Chained is the Spring. The night-wind bold
> Blows over the hard earth;
> Time is not more confused and cold,
> Nor keeps more wintry mirth.
>
> Yet blow, and roll the world about;
> Blow, Time—blow, winter's Wind!
> Through chinks of Time, heaven peepeth out,
> And Spring the frost behind.

===Chinese===
> 春天被锁住了。夜风放肆，
> 吹过硬冷的大地；
> 时光不比这更迷乱、更寒凉，
> 也不再保有冬日的欢意。
>
> 然而吹吧，把世界吹得团团转；
> 吹吧，时光——吹吧，冬日的风！
> 从时光的罅隙里，天穹探头窥看，
> 春天正跟在霜后启程。

===Original===
They who believe in the influences of the stars over the fates of men, are, in feeling at least, nearer the truth than they who regard the heavenly bodies as related to them merely by a common obedience to an external law. All that man sees has to do with man. Worlds cannot be without an intermundane relationship. The community of the center of all creation suggests an interradiating connection and dependence of the parts. Else a grander idea is conceivable than that which is already imbodied. The blank, which is only a forgotten life, lying behind the consciousness, and the misty splendor, which is an undeveloped life, lying before it, may be full of mysterious revelations of other connections with the worlds around us, than those of science and poetry. No shining belt or gleaming moon, no red and green glory in a self-encircling twin-star, but has a relation with the hidden things of a man's soul, and, it may be, with the secret history of his body as well. They are portions of the living house wherein he abides.

===Chinese===
凡相信星辰能影响人的命运的人，至少在情感上，比那些认为天体与人仅因共同服从一条外在法则而相关联的人，更接近真理。凡人所见，皆与人有关。诸世界之间，断不能没有一种跨越世界的关系。受造万有之中心的那一共通性，暗示着各部分之间有一种相互辉映、彼此依存的联系。否则，人还可以设想出一种比这已经赋形实现者更宏大的观念。那一片空白——那不过是意识身后一段被遗忘的生命——和那一片迷蒙的辉煌——那不过是意识前方一段尚未展开的生命——其中也许满含着神秘的启示，显明我们与四围诸世界之间，除科学与诗所见的那些关联之外，另有种种关联。凡闪亮的星带、皎洁的月亮，凡自绕成环的双星之间红与绿交辉的光华，无一不同人的灵魂里隐藏的事物有某种关系；而且，说不定同他的肉体的秘密历史也有关系。它们都是他栖居其中的这座活的宅邸的各个部分。

===Original===
> Through the realms of the monarch Sun
> Creeps a world, whose course had begun,
> On a weary path with a weary pace,
> Before the Earth sprang forth on her race:
> But many a time the Earth had sped
> Around the path she still must tread,
> Ere the elder planet, on leaden wing,
> Once circled the court of the planet's king.

===Chinese===
> 在君王太阳的疆域之内，
> 匍匐着一个世界；它的行程早已开端，
> 踏着倦怠的步子，走一条倦怠的路，
> 远在地球跃起、奔上她的赛程之前：
> 而地球已经绕着那条
> 她至今仍须踏走的轨道，奔驰了许多遍，
> 那颗更年长的行星，才拖着铅重的翅膀，
> 绕行星之王的宫廷，走完一圈。

===Original===
> There, in that lonely and distant star,
> The seasons are not as our seasons are;
> But many a year hath Autumn to dress
> The trees in their matron loveliness;
> As long hath old Winter in triumph to go
> O'er beauties dead in his vaults below;

===Chinese===
> 在那颗孤远寥落的星辰上，
> 四季不似我们的四季这样奔忙；
> 秋天要用整整许多年，
> 才把树木装扮出主妇般娴雅的容光；
> 老冬天凯旋巡游，也须同样长久，
> 踏过他地下墓窖中死去的芬芳；

===Original===
> And many a year the Spring doth wear
> Combing the icicles from her hair;
> And Summer, dear Summer, hath years of June,
> With large white clouds, and cool showers at noon;
> And a beauty that grows to a weight like grief,
> Till a burst of tears is the heart's relief.

===Chinese===
> 春天也有许多年只顾这样妆扮：
> 一路梳理她发间的冰凌；
> 夏天，亲爱的夏天，有经年不歇的六月，
> 有大朵的白云，正午清凉的阵雨；
> 又有一种美，渐渐沉重得如同悲哀，
> 直到迸涌一场泪水，心头才得释怀。

===Original===
> Children, born when Winter is king,
> May never rejoice in the hoping Spring;
> Though their own heart-buds are bursting with joy,
> And the child hath grown to the girl or boy;
> But may die with cold and icy hours
> Watching them ever in place of flowers.

===Chinese===
> 生在冬天为王的时分的孩子们，
> 也许永不能在盼望的春光里欢欣；
> 尽管他们心头的蓓蕾涨满喜悦，
> 尽管孩子已长成女孩或男孩；
> 他们却可能死于寒凉冰封的时辰，
> 那时辰永远看守着他们，代替了花朵。

===Original===
> And some who awake from their primal sleep,
> When the sighs of Summer through forests creep,
> Live, and love, and are loved again;
> Seek for pleasure, and find its pain;
> Sink to their last, their forsaken sleeping,
> With the same sweet odors around them creeping.

===Chinese===
> 也有一些人，从他们原初的睡眠中醒来，
> 当夏天的叹息正爬过森林；
> 活着，爱着，又重新得到爱；
> 寻觅欢愉，觅得的却是它的疼痛；
> 终于沉入他们最后的、被弃置的睡眠，
> 四周爬绕的，仍是同样甜美的芬芳。

===Original===
Now the children, there, are not born as the children are born in worlds nearer to the sun. For they arrive no one knows how. A maiden, walking alone, hears a cry: for even there a cry is the first utterance; and searching about, she findeth, under an overhanging rock, or within a clump of bushes, or, it may be, betwixt gray stones on the side of a hill, or in any other sheltered and unexpected spot, a little child. This she taketh tenderly, and beareth home with joy, calling out, "Mother, mother"—if so be that her mother lives—"I have got a baby—I have found a child!" All the household gathers round to see;—"Where is it? What is it like? Where did you find it?" and suchlike questions, abounding. And thereupon she relates the whole story of the discovery; for by the circumstances, such as season of the year, time of the day, condition of the air, and suchlike, and, especially, the peculiar and never-repeated aspect of the heavens and earth at the time, and the nature of the place of shelter wherein it is found, is determined, or at least indicated, the nature of the child thus discovered. Therefore, at certain seasons, and in certain states of the weather, according, in part, to their own fancy, the young women go out to look for children. They generally avoid seeking them, though they cannot help sometimes finding them, in places and with circumstances uncongenial to their peculiar likings. But no sooner is a child found, than its claim for protection and nurture obliterates all feeling of choice in the matter. Chiefly, however, in the season of summer, which lasts so long, coming as it does after such long intervals; and mostly in the warm evenings, about the middle of twilight; and principally in the woods and along the river banks, do the maidens go looking for children just as children look for flowers. And ever as the child grows, yea, more and more as he advances in years, will his face indicate to those who understand the spirit of nature, and her utterances in the face of the world, the nature of the place of his birth, and the other circumstances thereof; whether a clear morning sun guided his mother to the nook whence issued the boy's low cry; or at eve the lonely maiden (for the same woman never finds a second, at least while the first lives) discovers the girl by the glimmer of her white skin, lying in a nest like that of the lark, amid long encircling grasses, and the upward-gazing eyes of the lowly daisies; whether the storm bowed the forest trees around, or the still frost fixed in silence the else flowing and babbling stream.

===Chinese===
在那里，孩子的诞生，不像在离太阳更近的诸世界里那样。因为他们来到世上，谁也不知其所以然。一个少女独自走着，听见一声啼哭——因为即便在那里，啼哭也是最初的言语——她四下寻找，便在一方悬岩底下，或一丛灌木里面，或者，说不定在山坡的灰石之间，或在任何别的避风而出人意外的所在，找到了一个小小的婴孩。她温柔地抱起他，欢欢喜喜地抱回家去，一路喊着：「妈妈，妈妈」——倘若她母亲还活着——「我得了一个娃娃——我拾到了一个孩子！」全家都围拢来看；——「在哪里？什么样儿？你在哪儿找着的？」诸如此类的问题，问个不停。于是她便把发现的始末原原本本讲出来；因为凭着当时的种种情形——比如一年的季节、一天的时辰、空气的状况等等，尤其是当时天与地那副独特的、绝不再现的面貌，以及婴孩被找到的那处庇身之所的性质——这孩子的品性便由此确定，至少由此显露。因此，每逢一定的季节、一定的天气，少女们便多少凭着自己的心意，出门去寻找孩子。凡是地方与情形同她们各人特有的喜好不合的，她们一般便避而不寻——虽然有时免不了仍会在那里拾到。但孩子一经拾到，他那要求保护与养育的权利，便把此事上一切拣选的念头都抹除了。不过，她们大抵是在夏天出门去寻——那夏天隔了这么久的空档才来，来了又这样长久；多半是在温暖的黄昏，薄暮中段的前后；而且主要是在树林里、河岸边，少女们去寻找孩子，就像孩子们寻找花朵一样。而这孩子越长越大，是的，年岁愈进愈是如此，他的面容便愈会向那些懂得自然之精灵、懂得她借世界面目所发的言语的人，显示他出生之地的性质，以及与此相关的种种情形：是晴朗的朝阳把他的母亲引到了那个角落——男孩的低啼正从那里发出；还是黄昏时分，那孤单的少女（因为同一个女人绝不会拾到第二个，至少在第一个活着的时候不会）凭着白皙肌肤的一点微光发现了女孩——她躺在云雀窠一样的巢里，四周是环拥的长草，和低贱的雏菊仰向上天的眼睛；当时是风暴压弯了四围的林木，还是凝定的霜默默封住了那条本来流泻潺潺的溪水。

===Original===
After they grow up, the men and women are but little together. There is this peculiar difference between them, which likewise distinguishes the women from those of the earth. The men alone have arms; the women have only wings. Resplendent wings are they, wherein they can shroud themselves from head to foot in a panoply of glistering glory. By these wings alone, it may frequently be judged in what seasons and under what aspects they were born. From those that came in winter, go great white wings, white as snow; the edge of every feather shining like the sheen of silver, so that they flash and glitter like frost in the sun. But underneath, they are tinged with a faint pink or rose-color. Those born in spring have wings of a brilliant green, green as grass; and towards the edges the feathers are enamelled like the surface of the grass-blades. These again are white within. Those that are born in summer have wings of a deep rose-color, lined with pale gold. And those born in autumn have purple wings, with a rich brown on the inside. But these colors are modified and altered in all varieties, corresponding to the mood of the day and hour, as well as the season of the year; and sometimes I found the various colors so intermingled, that I could not determine even the season, though doubtless the hieroglyphic could be deciphered by more experienced eyes. One splendor, in particular, I remember—wings of deep carmine, with an inner down of warm gray, around a form of brilliant whiteness. She had been found as the sun went down through a low sea-fog, casting crimson along a broad sea-path into a little cave on the shore, where a bathing maiden saw her lying.

===Chinese===
他们长大之后，男人与女人也极少在一起。他们之间有这样一种奇特的差别，这差别也同样把这里的女人同地球上的女人区分开来。只有男人才有手臂；女人只有翅膀。那是辉煌的翅膀，她们能把自己从头到脚裹在里面，像披上一副晶莹生辉的铠甲。单凭这双翅膀，往往就能断定她们生于哪一季节、哪一种天象之下。冬天来的那一些，长着雪白的大翅膀，白得像雪；每一根羽毛的边缘都闪着白银的光泽，以致它们在太阳里闪闪烁烁，像霜一般。但翅膀底下，却透着淡淡的粉红或玫瑰色。春天生的，翅膀是鲜亮的绿色，绿得像草；靠近边缘，羽毛像草叶的表面那样搪着釉彩。这些翅膀里面又是白的。夏天生的，翅膀是深玫瑰色，衬着淡金的里子。秋天生的，翅膀发紫，里面是浓郁的褐色。但这些颜色又随着那一天、那一个时辰的天色，也随着一年的季节，变出千般不同的样子；有时我发现各种颜色掺杂错综，连季节我也判断不出，虽然那象形文字，无疑要有经验些的眼睛才解读得开。有一幅光华我记得格外分明——深绯红的翅膀，内衬一层暖灰的绒羽，环拥着一个灿烂雪白的身形。她被拾到的时候，太阳正沉落下去，穿过一层低低的海雾，沿着宽阔的海上光径，把绯红投进岸边一个小小的岩窟；一个正在洗浴的少女看见她躺在那里。

===Original===
But though I speak of sun and fog, and sea and shore, the world there is in some respects very different from the earth whereon men live. For instance, the waters reflect no forms. To the unaccustomed eye they appear, if undisturbed, like the surface of a dark metal, only that the latter would reflect indistinctly, whereas they reflect not at all, except light which falls immediately upon them. This has a great effect in causing the landscapes to differ from those on the earth. On the stillest evening, no tall ship on the sea sends a long wavering reflection almost to the feet of him on shore; the face of no maiden brightens at its own beauty in a still forest-well. The sun and moon alone make a glitter on the surface. The sea is like a sea of death, ready to engulf and never to reveal: a visible shadow of oblivion. Yet the women sport in its waters like gorgeous seabirds. The men more rarely enter them. But, on the contrary, the sky reflects everything beneath it, as if it were built of water like ours. Of course, from its concavity there is some distortion of the reflected objects; yet wondrous combinations of form are often to be seen in the overhanging depth. And then it is not shaped so much like a round dome as the sky of the earth, but, more of an egg-shape, rises to a great towering height in the middle, appearing far more lofty than the other. When the stars come out at night, it shows a mighty cupola, "fretted with golden fires," wherein there is room for all tempests to rush and rave.

===Chinese===
不过，我虽说到太阳与海雾，说到海与岸，那里的世界在某些方面，却同人们生息其上的地球大不一样。比如，那里的水不映出任何形影。在不习惯的眼睛看来，只要水面无波，它们就像一面暗色金属的表面——只是后者还会模糊地照出些影子，而它们却全然不映，除非光直直落在它们身上。这使得那里的风景同地球上的大异其趣。在最静谧的黄昏，海上没有一艘高桅船把一道长长的、摇曳的倒影几乎送到岸上人的脚边；没有哪个少女的脸，在一泓幽静的林间井水里，为自己的美丽而愈见明媚。只有太阳和月亮在水面上洒下光斑。那海像一片死之海，只管吞没，从不显露：一片看得见的遗忘之影。然而女人们却在水里嬉游，像华美的海鸟。男人们下水的时候少些。与此相反，天空却把它底下的一切尽行映照，仿佛它也是水筑成的，同我们的天一样。当然，由于它是凹弧面，映出的物体有些变形；但在那高悬的深处，常常可以见到种种奇异的形体组合。而且那穹天也不像地球的天那样近似一个圆顶，却更近乎一只蛋形，中部高高耸起，显得远比我们的天高。夜里星辰出来时，它便显出一座巨大的穹庐，「缀满金色的火花」，其中自有足够的余地，容一切风暴奔突咆哮。

===Original===
One evening in early summer, I stood with a group of men and women on a steep rock that overhung the sea. They were all questioning me about my world and the ways thereof. In making reply to one of their questions, I was compelled to say that children are not born in the Earth as with them. Upon this I was assailed with a whole battery of inquiries, which at first I tried to avoid; but, at last, I was compelled, in the vaguest manner I could invent, to make some approach to the subject in question. Immediately a dim notion of what I meant, seemed to dawn in the minds of most of the women. Some of them folded their great wings all around them, as they generally do when in the least offended, and stood erect and motionless. One spread out her rosy pinions, and flashed from the promontory into the gulf at its foot. A great light shone in the eyes of one maiden, who turned and walked slowly away, with her purple and white wings half dispread behind her. She was found, the next morning, dead beneath a withered tree on a bare hillside, some miles inland. They buried her where she lay, as is their custom; for, before they die, they instinctively search for a spot like the place of their birth, and having found one that satisfies them, they lie down, fold their wings around them, if they be women, or cross their arms over their breasts, if they are men, just as if they were going to sleep; and so sleep indeed. The sign or cause of coming death is an indescribable longing for something, they know not what, which seizes them, and drives them into solitude, consuming them within, till the body fails. When a youth and a maiden look too deep into each other's eyes, this longing seizes and possesses them; but instead of drawing nearer to each other, they wander away, each alone, into solitary places, and die of their desire. But it seems to me, that thereafter they are born babes upon our earth: where, if, when grown, they find each other, it goes well with them; if not, it will seem to go ill. But of this I know nothing. When I told them that the women on the Earth had not wings like them, but arms, they stared, and said how bold and masculine they must look; not knowing that their wings, glorious as they are, are but undeveloped arms.

===Chinese===
初夏的一个傍晚，我同一群男人和女人站在一方俯临大海的峭岩上。他们都向我问起我的世界，和那里的种种情形。在回答其中一个问题时，我不得不说到，在地球上，孩子不像他们那里那样诞生。此言一出，我便遭到连珠炮似的盘问；起初我还想回避，但末了，我不得不尽我所能虚构出最含糊的说法，向那个题目稍稍凑近一步。立时，我的话里的那点朦胧意思，似乎在大多数女人心里透出了微光。她们中间有几个把自己那对大翅膀周身紧紧拢起——她们稍感被冒犯时多半如此——直挺挺地站着，一动不动。有一个展开她玫瑰色的翅膀，从海角一闪而下，投入崖脚的深渊。还有一个少女，眼中燃起一道强光，转过身慢慢走开，紫白相间的翅膀在背后半张半敛。第二天早晨，人们在离海数英里的内陆一面光秃的山坡上，一株枯树底下，发现她死了。他们就地把她葬了，这是他们的风俗；因为在死之前，他们会本能地寻一处同自己出生之地相像的地方；一旦找到一处合意的，便躺下去——若是女人，就把翅膀合拢在四周；若是男人，就把双臂交叉在胸前——仿佛就要入睡一般；于是果真睡去了。死之将临的征兆或缘由，是一种不可名状的渴念——渴念着什么，他们自己也不知道——它攫住他们，把他们赶进孤独，从内里把他们耗尽，直到身体衰竭。当一个青年和一个少女把彼此的眼睛望得太深，这渴念便攫住他们、占有他们；但他们并不彼此挨近，反倒各自走开，孤身流落到荒僻的地方，死于自己的渴望。可是在我看来，此后他们便作为婴孩降生在我们的地球上：在那里，长大之后若能彼此寻见，他们便过得好；若寻不见，日子看来就过得不好。但这事我一无可知。当我告诉他们，地球上的女人没有他们那样的翅膀，只有手臂，他们直瞪着眼，说那些女人看上去必定何等莽撞、何等像男人；却不知道她们那翅膀，纵然荣耀，不过是未得展开的手臂罢了。

===Original===
But see the power of this book, that, while recounting what I can recall of its contents, I write as if myself had visited the far-off planet, learned its ways and appearances, and conversed with its men and women. And so, while writing, it seemed to me that I had.

The book goes on with the story of a maiden, who, born at the close of autumn, and living in a long, to her endless winter, set out at last to find the regions of spring; for, as in our earth, the seasons are divided over the globe. It begins something like this:

===Chinese===
且看这本书的力量：我一面追述书中我尚能记起的内容，一面写着写着，竟仿佛自己亲往那颗遥远的行星游历过，熟识那里的风俗与面貌，同那里的男男女女交谈过。而写着写着，我也真觉得自己确曾去过。

书接着讲一个少女的故事：她生在秋末，活在一场漫长的、于她漫无尽头的冬天里，末了终于动身去寻找春天的疆土；因为同我们的地球一样，四季在那里也是划分在全球各处的。这一段开头大致如下：

===Original===
> She watched them dying for many a day,
> Dropping from off the old trees away,
> One by one; or else in a shower
> Crowding over the withered flower.

===Chinese===
> 许多天里，她看着它们死去，
> 一片片从老树上飘落，
> 一片，又一片；要不然便像一阵急雨，
> 纷纷覆在枯萎的花上。

===Original===
> For as if they had done some grievous wrong,
> The sun, that had nursed them and loved them so long,
> Grew weary of loving, and, turning back,
> Hastened away on his southern track;
> And helplessly hung each shriveled leaf,
> Faded away with an idle grief.

===Chinese===
> 仿佛是它们犯了什么深重的罪愆，
> 那养育它们、爱了它们这么久的太阳，
> 爱得厌倦了，转过身去，
> 匆匆踏上他南行的方向；
> 每一片枯皱的叶子无助地悬着，
> 带着一种徒然的悲哀憔悴而亡。

===Original===
> And the gusts of wind, sad Autumn's sighs,
> Mournfully swept through their families;
> Casting away with a helpless moan
> All that he yet might call his own,
> As the child, when his bird is gone forever,
> Flingeth the cage on the wandering river.

===Chinese===
> 一阵阵风，悲哀秋天的叹息，
> 哀哀地扫过它们的家族；
> 带着无助的呻吟，抛撇净尽
> 一切他还唤作自己的东西；
> 就像那孩子，当他的鸟儿永远去了，
> 把鸟笼抛进流浪的河流。

===Original===
> And the giant trees, as bare as Death,
> Slowly bowed to the great Wind's breath;
> And groaned with trying to keep from groaning
> Amidst the young trees bending and moaning.

===Chinese===
> 巨大的树木，赤裸得像死神，
> 向大风的气息缓缓俯身；
> 它们强忍着不呻吟，却因此呻吟出声，
> 夹在弯腰悲鸣的幼树中间。

===Original===
> And the ancient planet's mighty sea
> Was heaving and falling most restlessly,
> And the tops of the waves were broken and white,
> Tossing about to ease their might;
> And the river was striving to reach the main,
> And the ripple was hurrying back again.

===Chinese===
> 而那古老行星的浩瀚大海，
> 起伏汹涌，极不安宁，
> 浪峰碎裂，翻出白沫，
> 上下腾掷，想舒散它们的蛮劲；
> 河水竭力要奔抵大海，
> 波纹却又匆匆荡回原径。

===Original===
> Nature lived in sadness now;
> Sadness lived on the maiden's brow,
> As she watched, with a fixed, half-conscious eye,
> One lonely leaf that trembled on high,
> Till it dropped at last from the desolate bough—
> Sorrow, oh, sorrow! 'tis winter now.

===Chinese===
> 此刻，自然活在悲哀里；
> 悲哀活在少女的眉宇，
> 她用一种凝定的、半醒半梦的眼睛，
> 望着高处一片颤动的孤叶，
> 直到它终于从荒凉的枝头坠落——
> 悲哀，哦，悲哀！如今已是冬天。

===Original===
> And her tears gushed forth, though it was but a leaf,
> For little will loose the swollen fountain of grief:
> When up to the lip the water goes,
> It needs but a drop, and it overflows.

===Chinese===
> 她的泪水涌了出来，虽然那不过是一片叶；
> 因为涨满的悲泉，轻轻一碰便会倾泻：
> 当水已经升到唇边，
> 只须再一滴，它便漫溢而出。

===Original===
> Oh! many and many a dreary year
> Must pass away ere the buds appear;
> Many a night of darksome sorrow
> Yield to the light of a joyless morrow,
> Ere birds again, on the clothed trees,
> Shall fill the branches with melodies.

===Chinese===
> 哦！要熬过一年又一年凄凉的岁月，
> 蓓蕾才得显现；
> 一夜又一夜幽暗的悲哀，
> 才让位给无欢的明天；
> 然后，鸟儿才重新在披上衣裳的树上，
> 把满枝满桠填满流转的歌。

===Original===
> She will dream of meadows with wakeful streams;
> Of wavy grass in the sunny beams;
> Of hidden wells that soundless spring,
> Hoarding their joy as a holy thing;
> Of founts that tell it all day long
> To the listening woods, with exultant song;

===Chinese===
> 她会梦见草地，和醒着的溪流；
> 梦见阳光里起伏的草浪；
> 梦见无声涌出的隐秘井泉，
> 把欢欣当圣物珍藏；
> 梦见喷泉整日整日地
> 用欢跃的歌，讲给谛听的树林听；

===Original===
> She will dream of evenings that die into nights,
> Where each sense is filled with its own delights,
> And the soul is still as the vaulted sky,
> Lulled with an inner harmony;

===Chinese===
> 她会梦见黄昏，渐渐消融入夜，
> 每一种感官都满盛着自己的喜悦，
> 灵魂安静得像穹隆的天空，
> 被一种内在的和谐催眠；

===Original===
> And the flowers give out to the dewy night,
> Changed into perfume, the gathered light;
> And the darkness sinks upon all their host,
> Till the sun sail up on the eastern coast—
> She will wake and see the branches bare,
> Weaving a net in the frozen air.

===Chinese===
> 而花朵们把凝聚的光，化作芬芳，
> 吐给露水浸染的夜；
> 黑暗沉落在它们全体的身上，
> 直到太阳扬帆驶上东海岸——
> 她会醒来，看见光秃的枝条，
> 在冻结的空气里编织着一张网。

===Original===
The story goes on to tell how, at last, weary with wintriness, she traveled towards the southern regions of her globe, to meet the spring on its slow way northwards; and how, after many sad adventures, many disappointed hopes, and many tears, bitter and fruitless, she found at last, one stormy afternoon, in a leafless forest, a single snowdrop growing betwixt the borders of the winter and spring. She lay down beside it and died. I almost believe that a child, pale and peaceful as a snowdrop, was born in the Earth within a fixed season from that stormy afternoon.

===Chinese===
故事接着讲：她终于厌倦了冬寒，动身往她那个星球的南方去，迎着缓缓北上的春天而行；怎样历经许多悲伤的遭遇、许多落空的希望、许多苦涩而无益的眼泪之后，终于在一个风雨交加的午后，在一片叶子脱尽的森林里，在冬天与春天交界的地带，找到一朵独自开放的雪花莲。她在花旁躺下，死了。我几乎相信，就在那个风雨的午后之后的一段固定时节之内，地球上降生了一个婴孩，苍白而安宁，像一朵雪花莲。

## XIII / 第十三章

===Original===
> I saw a ship sailing upon the sea
> Deeply laden as ship could be;
> But not so deep as in love I am,
> For I care not whether I sink or swim.

> But Love is such a Mystery
> I cannot find it out:
> For when I think I'm best resolv'd,
> I then am in most doubt.

===Chinese===
> 我望见一艘船航行在海上，
> 载得满满的，如一船之所能承；
> 可我陷在情爱里，比它更深，
> 沉也罢，浮也罢，我全不在心。

> 可爱情是这样一桩奥秘，
> 我怎么参也参它不透：
> 每当自以为豁然分明，
> 却正是疑虑最深的时候。

===Original===
One story I will try to reproduce. But, alas! it is like trying to reconstruct a forest out of broken branches and withered leaves. In the fairy book, everything was just as it should be, though whether in words or something else, I cannot tell. It glowed and flashed the thoughts upon the soul, with such a power that the medium disappeared from the consciousness, and it was occupied only with the things themselves. My representation of it must resemble a translation from a rich and powerful language, capable of embodying the thoughts of a splendidly developed people, into the meager and half-articulate speech of a savage tribe. Of course, while I read it, I was Cosmo, and his history was mine. Yet, all the time, I seemed to have a kind of double consciousness, and the story a double meaning. Sometimes it seemed only to represent a simple story of ordinary life, perhaps almost of universal life; wherein two souls, loving each other and longing to come nearer, do, after all, but behold each other as in a glass darkly.

===Chinese===
有一则故事，我要试着把它重现出来。可是，唉！这就像想用断枝枯叶重新拼凑出一座森林。在那本仙家的书里，一切都恰如其所当是，虽然究竟是以文字承载，还是以别的什么，我说不上来。它把种种思想灼灼然辉耀、闪耀到灵魂上，力量如此之大，以致媒介本身从意识中消失了，意识里只余下事物本身。我对它的转述，必定像是这样一种翻译：从一种丰饶有力的语言——它足以承载一个高度昌盛的民族的思想——译成某个蒙昧部族贫乏而半不能达意的言语。当然，我读它的时候，我就是科斯莫，他的历史就是我的历史。然而自始至终，我似乎怀着一种双重的意识，而这故事也有双重的意义。有时，它似乎只是一个平常人生——或许几乎是人人共有的人生——的单纯故事：其中两个灵魂彼此相爱、渴望互相走近，到头来却只能如对着镜子观看，模糊不清。

===Original===
As through the hard rock go the branching silver veins; as into the solid land run the creeks and gulfs from the unresting sea; as the lights and influences of the upper worlds sink silently through the earth's atmosphere; so doth Faerie invade the world of men, and sometimes startle the common eye with an association as of cause and effect, when between the two no connecting links can be traced.

===Chinese===
正如分岔的银脉贯穿坚硬的岩石；正如永不止息的大海把溪湾与深湾切入坚固的陆地；正如诸高上世界的光辉与感召悄然沉降，透过尘世的大气；仙境也正是这样侵入人世，有时以仿佛因果相承的牵连令凡俗的眼睛吃惊，虽然在两者之间寻不出任何可供追索的关联环节。

===Original===
Cosmo von Wehrstahl was a student at the University of Prague. Though of a noble family, he was poor, and prided himself upon the independence that poverty gives; for what will not a man pride himself upon, when he cannot get rid of it? A favorite with his fellow students, he yet had no companions; and none of them had ever crossed the threshold of his lodging in the top of one of the highest houses in the old town. Indeed, the secret of much of that complaisance which recommended him to his fellows, was the thought of his unknown retreat, whither in the evening he could betake himself, and indulge undisturbed in his own studies and reveries. These studies, besides those subjects necessary to his course at the University, embraced some less commonly known and approved; for in a secret drawer lay the works of Albertus Magnus and Cornelius Agrippa, along with others less read and more abstruse. As yet, however, he had followed these researches only from curiosity, and had turned them to no practical purpose.

===Chinese===
科斯莫·冯·韦尔施塔尔（Cosmo von Wehrstahl）是布拉格大学（University of Prague）的一名学生。他虽出身贵族之家，却很贫穷，并且以贫穷所给予人的那份独立自傲；因为一个人既然摆脱不掉它，又有什么不能拿来自豪呢？他在同学中间颇得人心，却没有一个同伴；他们谁也不曾跨进过他寓所的门槛——那寓所设在本城旧区一座最高房屋的顶层。的确，他之所以深得同窗好感，其秘诀大半就在他心里想着那个无人知晓的退居之所：傍晚时分，他可以躲到那里去，不受搅扰地沉浸在自己的研究与遐想里。这些研究，除去他在大学课程所必需的那些科目之外，还包括一些不甚为人所知、也不甚为人称许的学问；因为在一个暗屉里，放着大阿尔伯特（Albertus Magnus）和科内利斯·阿格里帕（Cornelius Agrippa）的著作，还有一些更少人读、也更深奥的书。不过到那时为止，他探究这些只是出于好奇，还不曾把它们用于任何实际的用途。

===Original===
His lodging consisted of one large low-ceiled room, singularly bare of furniture; for besides a couple of wooden chairs, a couch which served for dreaming on both by day and night, and a great press of black oak, there was very little in the room that could be called furniture. But curious instruments were heaped in the corners; and in one stood a skeleton, half-leaning against the wall, half-supported by a string about its neck. One of its hands, all of fingers, rested on the heavy pommel of a great sword that stood beside it. Various weapons were scattered about over the floor. The walls were utterly bare of adornment; for the few strange things, such as a large dried bat with wings dispread, the skin of a porcupine, and a stuffed sea-mouse, could hardly be reckoned as such. But although his fancy delighted in vagaries like these, he indulged his imagination with far different fare. His mind had never yet been filled with an absorbing passion; but it lay like a still twilight open to any wind, whether the low breath that wafts but odors, or the storm that bows the great trees till they strain and creak. He saw everything as through a rose-colored glass. When he looked from his window on the street below, not a maiden passed, but she moved as in a story, and drew his thoughts after her till she disappeared in the vista. When he walked in the streets, he always felt as if reading a tale, into which he sought to weave every face of interest that went by; and every sweet voice swept his soul as with the wing of a passing angel. He was in fact a poet without words; the more absorbed and endangered, that the springing waters were dammed back into his soul, where, finding no utterance, they grew, and swelled, and undermined. He used to lie on his hard couch, and read a tale or a poem, till the book dropped from his hand; but he dreamed on, he knew not whether awake or asleep, until the opposite roof grew upon his sense, and turned golden in the sunrise. Then he arose too; and the impulses of vigorous youth kept him ever active, either in study or in sport, until again the close of the day left him free; and the world of night, which had lain drowned in the cataract of the day, rose up in his soul, with all its stars, and dim-seen phantom shapes. But this could hardly last long. Some one form must sooner or later step within the charmed circle, enter the house of life, and compel the bewildered magician to kneel and worship.

===Chinese===
他的寓所只有一间宽大而顶棚低矮的房间，家具出奇地少；除了两把木椅、一张日里夜里都供他遐想的躺榻，和一个黑橡木的大橱柜之外，屋里几乎没有什么可以称作家具的东西。但各个屋角堆积着奇异的器具；有一个屋角立着一具骷髅，半倚着墙，半靠系在颈骨上的一根绳子撑持着。它的一只手——满手都是指节骨——搭在身旁竖着的一柄巨剑沉重的剑柄圆头上。地板上四散着各式兵器。四壁全无装饰；因为那几件希罕东西，诸如一只双翼张开的大蝙蝠干制标本、一张豪猪皮、一只海鼠标本，实在算不得装饰。虽然他的癖好以这类怪诞之物为乐，他却拿全然不同的食粮喂养自己的想象。他的心此前从未被一种令人神魂俱夺的激情占满；它躺卧着，像一片静谧的暮色微明，向任何来风敞开——无论是只传送芬芳的低回气息，还是把大树压得弯下腰、压得枝干紧绷吱嘎作响的暴风。他看一切都像隔着一层玫瑰色的玻璃。当他从窗口俯望下面的街道，没有一个少女走过，不是仿佛行在一篇故事里，把他的思绪牵引进她的后尘，直到她在街景深处消失。当他走在街上，他总觉得自己是在读一篇传奇，要把迎面而来的每一张动人的面孔都编织进去；每一声甜润的语声掠过，都像一位过往天使的翅膀，从他灵魂上拂过。他其实是一位没有词语的诗人；正因为泉水被拦坝蓄回他的灵魂，找不到出口，只能涨起、涌起、暗中侵蚀根基，他也就愈深沉、愈危险。他常常躺在硬邦邦的躺榻上，读一篇故事或一首诗，直到书从手里滑落；梦却接着做下去，他自己也不知是醒是睡，直到对面的屋顶渐渐浮上他的知觉，在初升的日光里泛出金色。这时他也起身；蓬勃青春的种种冲动使他始终活跃，不是用功，就是嬉游，直到一天的尽头又还他自由；于是那在白昼奔流的瀑布之下沉没了的夜的世界，便带着它满天的星斗和隐约莫辨的幻影群形，在他灵魂里升起。可这样的光景难以长久。迟早总会有某一个身影踏进那施了法的圆环，走进生命之宅，迫使那惶惑无措的术士屈膝下拜。

===Original===
One afternoon, towards dusk, he was wandering dreamily in one of the principal streets, when a fellow-student roused him by a slap on the shoulder, and asked him to accompany him into a little back alley to look at some old armor which he had taken a fancy to possess. Cosmo was considered an authority in every matter pertaining to arms, ancient or modern. In the use of weapons, none of the students could come near him; and his practical acquaintance with some had principally contributed to establish his authority in reference to all. He accompanied him willingly. They entered a narrow alley, and thence a dirty little court, where a low arched door admitted them into a heterogeneous assemblage of everything musty, and dusty, and old, that could well be imagined. His verdict on the armor was satisfactory, and his companion at once concluded the purchase. As they were leaving the place, Cosmo's eye was attracted by an old mirror of an elliptical shape, which leaned against the wall, covered with dust. Around it was some curious carving, which he could see but very indistinctly by the glimmering light which the owner of the shop carried in his hand. It was this carving that attracted his attention; at least so it appeared to him. He left the place, however, with his friend, taking no further notice of it. They walked together to the main street, where they parted and took opposite directions.

===Chinese===
一天下午，将近黄昏，他正恍恍惚惚地在一条大街上漫步，一位同学在他肩上拍了一掌，把他惊醒，请他陪自己到一条小巷里去看看几件旧盔甲——他起了想置办的心思。凡与甲胄兵器有关的事，无论古近，科斯莫都被视为权威。说到兵器的使用，没有哪个学生能望其项背；而他对其中若干种的实际通晓，正是他在这一门类全体之上树立威信的主要根基。他欣然同往。两人走进一条狭窄的小巷，又从那里进了一个肮脏的小天井；一扇低矮的拱门把他们引入一堆纷然杂陈的东西中间——凡是所能想象的霉腐的、积尘的、古老的东西，应有尽有。他对那副盔甲的鉴定令人满意，同伴当即买定了。他们正要离开，科斯莫的眼睛却被一面古旧的椭圆镜子吸引住了：它靠墙立着，蒙满灰尘。镜子四周有一些奇特的雕饰，借着店主手中提着的那一点闪烁微光，他看不真切。吸引他注意的正是这雕饰；至少在他自己看来是如此。然而他还是随朋友离开了那里，不再多加理会。两人一同走到大街，在那里分了手，各奔东西。

===Original===
No sooner was Cosmo left alone, than the thought of the curious old mirror returned to him. A strong desire to see it more plainly arose within him, and he directed his steps once more towards the shop. The owner opened the door when he knocked, as if he had expected him. He was a little, old, withered man, with a hooked nose, and burning eyes constantly in a slow restless motion, and looking here and there as if after something that eluded them. Pretending to examine several other articles, Cosmo at last approached the mirror, and requested to have it taken down.

"Take it down yourself, master; I cannot reach it," said the old man.

Cosmo took it down carefully, when he saw that the carving was indeed delicate and costly, being both of admirable design and execution; containing withal many devices which seemed to embody some meaning to which he had no clue. This, naturally, in one of his tastes and temperament, increased the interest he felt in the old mirror; so much, indeed, that he now longed to possess it, in order to study its frame at his leisure. He pretended, however, to want it only for use; and saying he feared the plate could be of little service, as it was rather old, he brushed away a little of the dust from its face, expecting to see a dull reflection within. His surprise was great when he found the reflection brilliant, revealing a glass not only uninjured by age, but wondrously clear and perfect (should the whole correspond to this part) even for one newly from the hands of the maker. He asked carelessly what the owner wanted for the thing. The old man replied by mentioning a sum of money far beyond the reach of poor Cosmo, who proceeded to replace the mirror where it had stood before.

===Chinese===
科斯莫刚一落单，那面古怪旧镜的念头便回到他心里。一种想把它看得更分明的强烈愿望在胸中升起，他重新举步，朝那家铺子走去。他一敲门，店主便开了门，仿佛早就候着他似的。那是个干瘪的小老头，鹰钩鼻子，一双眼睛燃着火，不停地缓慢游移，东张西望，像在追逐什么总躲着它们的目光的东西。科斯莫装作察看别的几件货色，末了终于踱到镜子跟前，请主人把它取下来。

「请您自己动手取吧，先生；我够不着，」老人说。

科斯莫小心翼翼地把镜子取了下来，这才发现那雕饰果然精致华贵，构思与刀工都令人叹赏；其中还包含许多纹章图记，似乎寄寓着某种含义，他却无从索解。这对于一个有他那样的趣味与气质的人来说，自然更加深了他对这面旧镜的兴趣；以至于他此刻渴望把它据为己有，好从容研究它的镜框。然而他只佯称买它不过为了实用；又说镜面年深日久，只怕已不大中用了，一面伸手拂去镜面上的些许浮尘，只指望看见里面一片昏沉的反光。及至发现那反光竟灿然明亮，显出一面非但未为岁月所损、而且清澈完美得出奇的镜子——倘若整面都与这一处相符，那真是新出炉的匠人手制之物也未必能及——他的惊讶真是大极了。他漫不经意地问店主讨个什么价。老人报出一个数目，远非可怜的科斯莫所能企及；科斯莫便动手把镜子放回原先立着的地方。

===Original===
"You think the price too high?" said the old man.

"I do not know that it is too much for you to ask," replied Cosmo; "but it is far too much for me to give."

The old man held up his light towards Cosmo's face. "I like your look," said he.

Cosmo could not return the compliment. In fact, now he looked closely at him for the first time, he felt a kind of repugnance to him, mingled with a strange feeling of doubt whether a man or a woman stood before him.

"What is your name?" he continued.

"Cosmo von Wehrstahl."

"Ah, ah! I thought as much. I see your father in you. I knew your father very well, young sir. I dare say in some odd corners of my house, you might find some old things with his crest and cipher upon them still. Well, I like you: you shall have the mirror at the fourth part of what I asked for it; but upon one condition."

===Chinese===
「你嫌这价钱太高了？」老人说。

「我不敢说你要得太多，」科斯莫答道；「只是要我出，实在出不起。」

老人把灯举起来，照着科斯莫的脸。「我喜欢你的长相，」他说。

科斯莫却无法回敬这句恭维。其实，此刻他头一回凑近细看，便对这老头生出一种厌恶，其中还夹杂着一种古怪的疑心，说不清站在自己面前的究竟是男是女。

「你叫什么名字？」老头接着问。

「科斯莫·冯·韦尔施塔尔。」

「啊，啊！我猜也是。我在你身上看见了你父亲。我跟你父亲很熟，年轻的先生。我敢说，在我家某些偏僻的角落里，你还能找到些刻着他家纹章和花押的旧物件。好吧，我喜欢你：这面镜子就按我要价的四分之一卖给你；但有一个条件。」

===Original===
"What is that?" said Cosmo; for, although the price was still a great deal for him to give, he could just manage it; and the desire to possess the mirror had increased to an altogether unaccountable degree, since it had seemed beyond his reach.

"That if you should ever want to get rid of it again, you will let me have the first offer."

"Certainly," replied Cosmo, with a smile; adding, "a moderate condition indeed."

"On your honor?" insisted the seller.

"On my honor," said the buyer; and the bargain was concluded.

"I will carry it home for you," said the old man, as Cosmo took it in his hands.

"No, no; I will carry it myself," said he; for he had a peculiar dislike to revealing his residence to anyone, and more especially to this person, to whom he felt every moment a greater antipathy.

"Just as you please," said the old creature, and muttered to himself as he held his light at the door to show him out of the court: "Sold for the sixth time! I wonder what will be the upshot of it this time. I should think my lady had enough of it by now!"

===Chinese===
「什么条件？」科斯莫说；因为虽然这价钱对他仍是一大笔，他还勉强凑得出；而自从那镜子显得高不可攀之后，占有它的欲望反倒增长到全然无从解释的地步。

「倘若你日后想把它脱手，必须先来问我，让我有优先出价之权。」

「当然可以，」科斯莫微笑着答道，又补上一句：「这条件真是再宽厚不过了。」

「以你的荣誉担保？」卖主追问道。

「以我的荣誉担保，」买主说；交易就这样谈定了。

「我替你送回家去吧，」老人说，这时科斯莫已把镜子捧在手里。

「不用，不用；我自己拿，」他说；因为他素来极不愿向任何人泄露自己的住处，对这个人尤其是如此——他对此人的反感每过一刻便加深一分。

「随你的便，」老东西说，一面举着灯站在门口，照他走出天井，一面自言自语地咕哝：「已是第六回卖出了！不知这一回结局会怎样。我想我家夫人这会儿总该受够了吧！」

===Original===
Cosmo carried his prize carefully home. But all the way he had an uncomfortable feeling that he was watched and dogged. Repeatedly he looked about, but saw nothing to justify his suspicions.
