A cold gust of wind met me from behind the stone; and when I looked, I saw nothing but a great hole in the earth, into which I could find no way of entering. Had she fallen in? I could not tell. I must wait for the daylight. I sat down and wept, for there was no help.

===Chinese===
我唱着，那帷幕便一路升起；我唱着，生命的征兆便一路增长；直到那双眼睛在我面前睁开时，宛如破晓，带着那样一轮壮丽辉煌的日出——我那支微弱的歌所力图重新赋形的，正是这日出。奇怪的是，我竟没有被全然压倒，还能在那看不见的帷幕继续升起之时，把我的歌唱完。这能力唯独来自我发现自己置身其中的那种精神高扬的状态。只因为我已在歌中高升，才受得住那黎明的烈焰。但我说不清她看上去更像石像，还是更像女人；她仿佛已被移入幻想的那个境地，在那里一切都极度鲜活，却没有一样轮廓分明。末了，当我唱到她垂落的头发时，那灵魂的光辉渐渐黯淡下去，如一场消逝的日落。内在的一盏灯熄灭了，生命的宅子在冬日的清晨里泛出一片空白。她又成了一尊石像——但看得见了，这已是极大的收获。然而，从希望与圆满遽然跌回的这一阵震荡如此剧烈，以致我再也无法克制自己，一跃扑到她跟前，不顾那地方的法则，张开双臂抱住她，仿佛要把她从一个看得见的死神手中夺回来，把她从基座上抱下来，贴到我的心口。可她的双脚刚一离开那黑色的基座，她便浑身战栗起来；接着，不等我收紧双臂的环抱，她便从我的怀抱里挣脱出去，一声嗔责的叫喊——「你不该碰我！」——跃入长廊，闪身躲到那圆环外侧的一根柱子后面，不见了。我几乎同样迅疾地追去；但我还未赶到那柱子跟前，一声关门的声音——那有时是一切声音中最悲哀的声音——落进我的耳朵；待我赶到她消失的地方，我看见一扇沉重而粗粝的门，被悬在它上方的一盏淡黄色的灯照亮着，与我在仙宫里见过的所有门全然不同；因为那些门不是乌木的，便是象牙的，或者包着银片，或者用某种芬芳的木料制成，装饰得十分华美；而这扇门却似乎是老橡木的，钉着粗大的钉子和铁泡钉。尽管我追赶得那样急迫，我还是不由自主地读到了灯下那几个银色的字：「非女王允准，任何人不得入内。」可我追随的是我的白衣女郎，女王于我何干？我把那门猛力甩向墙壁，一跃而过。看哪！我站在一座荒凉多风的山丘上。四周耸立着一块块宛如墓碑的巨石。门没有了，仙宫也不见了。一个白色的身影从我身旁闪过，绞着双手，哭喊道：「啊！你本该对我唱歌的；你本该对我唱歌的！」随即消失在一块石头后面。我追了过去。一阵冷风从石头后面迎面扑来；待我张望，只见地上有一个大洞，此外什么也没有，而我找不到任何能进到那洞里去的路径。她是掉进去了吗？我说不上来。我必须等天亮。我坐下来哭泣，因为无计可施。

## XVII / 第十七章

===Original===
> Anfangs wollt’ ich fast verzagen,
 

 Und ich glaubt’ ich trüg’ es nie,
 

 Und ich hab’ es doch getragen—
 
 Aber fragt mich nur nicht: wie?

> First, I thought, almost despairing,
 

 This must crush my spirit now;
 

 Yet I bore it, and am bearing—
 
 Only do not ask me how.

===Chinese===
> Anfangs wollt’ ich fast verzagen,
 

 Und ich glaubt’ ich trüg’ es nie,
 

 Und ich hab’ es doch getragen—
 
 Aber fragt mich nur nicht: wie?

> 起初，我几乎绝望地想，
 

 这必会将我的精神压碎；
 

 然而我承受了，如今仍在承受——
 
 只是千万别问我怎样承受的。

===Original===
When the daylight came, it brought the possibility of action, but with it little of consolation. With the first visible increase of light, I gazed into the chasm, but could not, for more than an hour, see sufficiently well to discover its nature. At last I saw it was almost a perpendicular opening, like a roughly excavated well, only very large. I could perceive no bottom; and it was not till the sun actually rose, that I discovered a sort of natural staircase, in many parts little more than suggested, which led round and round the gulf, descending spirally into its abyss. I saw at once that this was my path; and without a moment’s hesitation, glad to quit the sunlight, which stared at me most heartlessly, I commenced my tortuous descent. It was very difficult. In some parts I had to cling to the rocks like a bat. In one place, I dropped from the track down upon the next returning spire of the stair; which being broad in this particular portion, and standing out from the wall at right angles, received me upon my feet safe, though somewhat stupefied by the shock. After descending a great way, I found the stair ended at a narrow opening which entered the rock horizontally. Into this I crept, and, having entered, had just room to turn round. I put my head out into the shaft by which I had come down, and surveyed the course of my descent. Looking up, I saw the stars; although the sun must by this time have been high in the heavens. Looking below, I saw that the sides of the shaft went sheer down, smooth as glass; and far beneath me, I saw the reflection of the same stars I had seen in the heavens when I looked up. I turned again, and crept inwards some distance, when the passage widened, and I was at length able to stand and walk upright. Wider and loftier grew the way; new paths branched off on every side; great open halls appeared; till at last I found myself wandering on through an underground country, in which the sky was of rock, and instead of trees and flowers, there were only fantastic rocks and stones. And ever as I went, darker grew my thoughts, till at last I had no hope whatever of finding the white lady: I no longer called her to myself *my* white lady. Wherever a choice was necessary, I always chose the path which seemed to lead downwards.

===Chinese===
天光来临时，它带来了行动的可能，却几乎没带来什么慰藉。亮光刚一显出增长，我便朝那裂壑深处凝望，可是在一个多钟头里，我都没能看得足够清楚，辨不明它的形貌。后来我终于看出，那几乎是一道垂直的开口，像一口粗加开凿的井，只是极其巨大。我望不见底；直到太阳真正升起，我才发现有一道天然石阶——许多处所不过依稀可辨——绕着深壑一圈又一圈，螺旋下行，通入它的深渊。我立刻明白，这就是我的路；我一刻也没有迟疑，甘愿撇下那无情瞪视着我的日光，开始了迂曲的下行。这条路极难走。有几处，我不得不像蝙蝠一样攀附在岩石上。有一处，我从径道上跌落下去，跌在下一圈折返的石阶尖脊上；那一段石阶恰好宽阔，又与岩壁成直角向外突出，便把我双脚安然接住——只是那一震令我有些发懵。下行了极深之后，我发现石阶尽头是一道狭窄的开口，水平没入岩体。我爬了进去；进去之后，刚有转身的余地。我把头探回我方才降下来的竖井里，察看我一路下行的来路。抬头仰望，我看见了星辰——虽然此刻太阳想必已高悬天际。俯身下望，只见竖井四壁笔直而下，光滑如镜；而在我脚下极深之处，我看见了同一些星辰的倒影——正是我抬头时在天上看见的那些。我重新转回身，向内又爬了一段，甬道渐渐开阔，我终于能够站立起来，直身而行。路越走越宽、越走越高；四面八方岔出新径；一座座敞开的大殿次第显现；到最后，我发觉自己已在一个地底国度里漫游，那里的天空是岩石做的，没有树木花草，只有奇形怪状的岩与石。而且我一路走着，思虑便一路转暗，到最后，我对于寻见白衣女郎已全然不抱希望：我不再在心里称她为*我的*白衣女郎。凡在必须抉择之处，我总是拣那条似乎通向更深处的路。

===Original===
At length I began to find that these regions were inhabited. From behind a rock a peal of harsh grating laughter, full of evil humor, rang through my ears, and, looking round, I saw a queer, goblin creature, with a great head and ridiculous features, just such as those described, in German histories and travels, as Kobolds. “What do you want with me?” I said. He pointed at me with a long forefinger, very thick at the root, and sharpened to a point, and answered, “*He! he! he!* what do *you* want here?” Then, changing his tone, he continued, with mock humility—“Honored sir, vouchsafe to withdraw from thy slaves the luster of thy august presence, for thy slaves cannot support its brightness.” A second appeared, and struck in: “You are so big, you keep the sun from us. We can’t see for you, and we’re so cold.” Thereupon arose, on all sides, the most terrific uproar of laughter, from voices like those of children in volume, but scrannel and harsh as those of decrepit age, though, unfortunately, without its weakness. The whole pandemonium of fairy devils, of all varieties of fantastic ugliness, both in form and feature, and of all sizes from one to four feet, seemed to have suddenly assembled about me. At length, after a great babble of talk among themselves, in a language unknown to me, and after seemingly endless gesticulation, consultation, elbow-nudging, and unmitigated peals of laughter, they formed into a circle about one of their number, who scrambled upon a stone, and, much to my surprise, and somewhat to my dismay, began to sing, in a voice corresponding in its nature to his talking one, from beginning to end, the song with which I had brought the light into the eyes of the white lady. He sang the same air too; and, all the time, maintained a face of mock entreaty and worship; accompanying the song with the travestied gestures of one playing on the lute. The whole assembly kept silence, except at the close of every verse, when they roared, and danced, and shouted with laughter, and flung themselves on the ground, in real or pretended convulsions of delight. When he had finished, the singer threw himself from the top of the stone, turning heels over head several times in his descent; and when he did alight, it was on the top of his head, on which he hopped about, making the most grotesque gesticulations with his legs in the air. Inexpressible laughter followed, which broke up in a shower of tiny stones from innumerable hands. They could not materially injure me, although they cut me on the head and face. I attempted to run away, but they all rushed upon me, and, laying hold of every part that afforded a grasp, held me tight. Crowding about me like bees, they shouted an insect-swarm of exasperating speeches up into my face, among which the most frequently recurring were—“You shan’t have her; you shan’t have her; he! he! he! She’s for a better man; how he’ll kiss her! how he’ll kiss her!”

===Chinese===
我终于开始发觉，这些地带是有居民栖息的。一块岩石背后，爆出一阵刺耳磨人的大笑，满含恶毒的兴味，响彻我的两耳；我回头一看，只见一个古怪的妖怪生物，大脑袋，五官滑稽，正像德国史地著作与游记里描述的那种科博尔德（Kobolds）。「你们要我怎样？」我说。他伸出一根长长的食指指着我——指根极粗，尖端削得锐细——答道：「*嘻！嘻！嘻！*倒是*你*要在这里怎样？」接着，他换了一副腔调，装出谦卑的样子继续道：「尊贵的老爷，求您容您的奴仆们避开您威仪赫赫的临在的光辉吧，因为您的奴仆们消受不起它的光亮。」第二个现出身来，插嘴道：「您太大了，把太阳都给我们挡住了。您在，我们就什么也看不见，我们还这么冷。」话音未落，四面八方腾起了最骇人的哄笑，论音量像孩童的嗓音，却又干瘪沙哑，赛过老朽衰年——可惜并没有老年的虚弱。仿佛仙境群魔的整个巢窟——形与貌无奇不有、其丑百态，身量从一英尺到四英尺不等——忽然一齐聚拢到我周围。他们先用一种我听不懂的语言彼此喧聒了好一大通，又做了仿佛无尽无休的手势、商议、以肘相碰、放肆地哄笑，末了围成一个圆圈，圈住他们中间的一个；那家伙爬上一块石头，令我大吃一惊、也颇为惶惑的是，他竟从头到尾唱起那支歌来——正是我用来把光明送进白衣女郎眼里的那一支；连曲调也唱得一样；而且自始至终摆着一副假意恳求与崇拜的面孔，一边唱，一边配着弹琉特琴者的歪拟手势。全体鸦雀无声，只是每唱完一节，他们便咆哮起来，又跳又叫，狂笑高呼，扑倒在地，作真心或假意的狂喜痉挛。他唱完了，歌者便从石头顶上翻跌下来，坠落途中连打了好几个跟斗；而他落地时，偏偏用头顶着地，就这样单凭脑袋蹦跳不休，两腿在空中做着最古怪滑稽的姿态。随之而来的是难以言表的哄笑，笑声里，无数只手抛来一阵小石子的急雨。这些石子伤不了我的筋骨，却把我头脸划破了几处。我试图逃走，他们便一齐扑上来，凡抓得住的地方一概抓住，把我紧紧拽住。他们像蜂群一样簇拥着我，把一窝昆虫般恼人的话朝我脸上一阵一阵嚷来，其中出现得最频繁的是——「你休想得到她；你休想得到她；嘻！嘻！嘻！她是留给一个更高贵的男人的；看他要怎样吻她！看他要怎样吻她！」

===Original===
The galvanic torrent of this battery of malevolence stung to life within me a spark of nobleness, and I said aloud, “Well, if he is a better man, let him have her.”

They instantly let go their hold of me, and fell back a step or two, with a whole broadside of grunts and humphs, as of unexpected and disappointed approbation. I made a step or two forward, and a lane was instantly opened for me through the midst of the grinning little antics, who bowed most politely to me on every side as I passed. After I had gone a few yards, I looked back, and saw them all standing quite still, looking after me, like a great school of boys; till suddenly one turned round, and with a loud whoop, rushed into the midst of the others. In an instant, the whole was one writhing and tumbling heap of contortion, reminding me of the live pyramids of intertwined snakes of which travellers make report. As soon as one was worked out of the mass, he bounded off a few paces, and then, with a somerset and a run, threw himself gyrating into the air, and descended with all his weight on the summit of the heaving and struggling chaos of fantastic figures. I left them still busy at this fierce and apparently aimless amusement. And as I went, I sang—

===Chinese===
这恶意的炮阵电流般的一阵轰击，把我胸中一点高贵的火花击得活了过来，我高声说道：「好吧，既然他更高贵，就让他得到她。」

他们立刻松开了我，向后退开一两步，发出一阵舷炮齐射般的咕哝与哼啧，像是意外而又怅然的赞许。我向前走了一两步，一条通路立时在这些龇牙咧嘴的小丑物中间让了出来；我经过时，他们还向两边极其有礼地朝我鞠躬。走出几码之后，我回头一望，只见他们全都一动不动地站着，目送着我，像一大群学童；忽然，其中一个转过身，大吼一声，扑进其余那些中间。霎时间，全场化作一堆扭结翻滚、蜷曲蠕动的乱团，让我想起旅人们记载过的那种活蛇盘绕的蛇塔。一个刚从堆里挣脱出来，便向前蹿出几步，接着一个跟斗加一段疾跑，把自己旋转着抛上空中，再整个身子砸落在那堆起伏挣扎、奇形怪状的乱象顶上。我离开时，他们还忙着这种凶猛而显然毫无目的的嬉戏。我一路走着，唱道——

===Original===
> If a nobler waits for thee,
 

 I will weep aside;
 

 It is well that thou should’st be,
 
 Of the nobler, bride.

===Chinese===
> 若有更高贵者等着你，
 

 我便退向一旁哭泣；
 
 你理当归去，这原是好事——
 
 做那更高贵者的新娘。

===Original===
> For if love builds up the home,
 

 Where the heart is free,
 

 Homeless yet the heart must roam,
 
 That has not found thee.

===Chinese===
> 因为唯有爱筑起的家，
 

 心在其中方得自由；
 

 那不曾寻见你的心，
 
 依旧无家，漂泊不休。

===Original===
> One must suffer: I, for her,
 

 Yield in her my part.
 

 Take her, thou art worthier—
 
 Still! be still, my heart!

===Chinese===
> 总得有人受苦：为了她，
 

 我把自己的一份让出。
 

 娶她去吧，你比我配得上——
 
 静！静下来，我的心！

===Original===
> Gift ungotten! largess high
 

 Of a frustrate will!
 

 But to yield it lovingly
 
 Is a something still.

===Chinese===
> 未曾得到的赠礼！这是落空的意志
 
 所颁的隆恩厚赏！
 
 然而怀着爱把它让渡，
 
 便仍然算得一点什么。

===Original===
Then a little song arose of itself in my soul; and I felt for the moment, while it sank sadly within me, as if I was once more walking up and down the white hall of Phantasy in the Fairy Palace. But this lasted no longer than the song; as will be seen.

===Chinese===
接着，一支小小的歌自行在我灵魂里升起；当它在我体内哀哀沉落的时候，我一时觉得，自己仿佛重新在仙宫里那幻想的白殿中走来走去。但这感觉仅与那支歌同其长短；后文自见分晓。

===Original===
> Do not vex thy violet
 

 Perfume to afford;
 

 Else no odor thou wilt get
 
 From its little hoard.

===Chinese===
> 切莫搅扰你的紫罗兰，
 

 逼它拿出芬芳；
 

 否则你将从它小小的窖藏里
 
 得不到一缕香气。

===Original===
> In thy lady’s gracious eyes
 

 Look not thou too long;
 

 Else from them the glory flies,
 
 And thou dost her wrong.

===Chinese===
> 在你淑女温雅的眼中，
 

 切莫凝望太久；
 

 否则荣耀将从那里飞逝，
 
 你也亏待了她。

===Original===
> Come not thou too near the maid,
 

 Clasp her not too wild;
 

 Else the splendor is allayed,
 
 And thy heart beguiled.

===Chinese===
> 切莫挨近那少女太近，
 

 切莫狂野地把她紧拥；
 

 否则光辉要黯淡下去，
 
 你的心也将受了欺哄。

===Original===
A crash of laughter, more discordant and deriding than any I had yet heard, invaded my ears. Looking on in the direction of the sound, I saw a little elderly woman, much taller, however, than the goblins I had just left, seated upon a stone by the side of the path. She rose, as I drew near, and came forward to meet me. She was very plain and commonplace in appearance, without being hideously ugly. Looking up in my face with a stupid sneer, she said: “Isn’t it a pity you haven’t a pretty girl to walk all alone with you through this sweet country? How different everything would look! wouldn’t it? Strange that one can never have what one would like best! How the roses would bloom and all that, even in this infernal hole! wouldn’t they, Anodos? Her eyes would light up the old cave, wouldn’t they?”

===Chinese===
一阵笑声轰然袭来，比我此前听过的任何笑声都更不谐和、更含讥诮，侵入了我的两耳。循声望去，只见一位矮小的老妇人——不过，比我方才离开的那些妖怪要高得多——坐在路旁一块石头上。我走近时，她站起身，迎上前来。她相貌十分平常而平庸，却还没到丑得骇人的地步。她仰起脸望着我，带着一种愚钝的讥诮说道：「你没有一位漂亮姑娘，陪你独个儿走过这甜美的乡野，岂不可惜？那样一来，一切看上去都会两样！对不对？奇怪，人最想要什么，偏就永远得不到什么！哪怕在这座地狱似的洞窟里，玫瑰也准会盛开，诸如此类——是不是，阿诺多斯（Anodos）？她的眼睛会把这老洞窟照亮，会吗？」

===Original===
“That depends on who the pretty girl should be,” replied I.

“Not so very much matter that,” she answered; “look here.”

I had turned to go away as I gave my reply, but now I stopped and looked at her. As a rough unsightly bud might suddenly blossom into the most lovely flower; or rather, as a sunbeam bursts through a shapeless cloud, and transfigures the earth; so burst a face of resplendent beauty, as it were *through* the unsightly visage of the woman, destroying it with light as it dawned through it. A summer sky rose above me, gray with heat; across a shining slumberous landscape, looked from afar the peaks of snow-capped mountains; and down from a great rock beside me fell a sheet of water mad with its own delight.

===Chinese===
「那得看这漂亮姑娘是谁了。」我答道。

「那倒不十分要紧，」她答道；「瞧这儿。」

我给出回答时本已转身要走，此刻却停住脚，望着她。仿佛一枚粗粝丑陋的蓓蕾，忽然绽放成最可爱的花朵；毋宁说，仿佛一束阳光穿破一团不成形的云，使大地霎时改容——一张光华照人的美丽面容，就这样仿佛*透过*那妇人其貌不扬的面孔迸现出来，光当它从其中透出之时，便把那面孔毁去了。一轮夏日的天空在我头顶升起，因炎热而灰白；一片闪耀的、酣睡般的风景之上，远处雪峰的尖顶遥遥在望；而从我身旁一块巨岩上，倾泻下一片为自己欢喜而迷狂的水。

===Original===
“Stay with me,” she said, lifting up her exquisite face, and looking full in mine.

I drew back. Again the infernal laugh grated upon my ears; again the rocks closed in around me, and the ugly woman looked at me with wicked, mocking hazel eyes.

“You shall have your reward,” said she. “You shall see your white lady again.”

===Chinese===
「与我同住吧，」她说，仰起她那绝美的脸，直直望着我的脸。

我退了回来。那地狱般的笑声又锉磨着我的两耳；岩石又在我四周合拢，丑妇人用一双邪恶而嘲弄的淡褐色眼睛望着我。

「你会得到你的报偿的，」她说。「你会再见到你的白衣女郎。」

===Original===
“That lies not with you,” I replied, and turned and left her.

She followed me with shriek upon shriek of laughter, as I went on my way.

I may mention here, that although there was always light enough to see my path and a few yards on every side of me, I never could find out the source of this sad sepulchral illumination.

===Chinese===
「那不由你做主，」我回答，转身离开了她。

我继续赶路，她跟在后面，一迭连声地尖笑不止。

我可以在此说明一句：虽然光始终足够亮，照得见我的路和我身旁几码之内，我却始终查不出这阴惨惨的、坟墓似的光照，究竟来自何处。

## XVIII / 第十八章

===Original===
> Im Sausen des Windes, im Brausen des Meers
 

 Und im Seufzen der eigenen Brust.

> In the wind’s uproar, the sea’s raging grim,
 

 And the sighs that are born in him.

> Ja, est wird zwar ein anderes Zeitalter kommen, wo es Licht wird, und wo der Mensch aus erhabnen Traümen erwacht, und die Traüme﻿—wider findet, weil er nichts verlor als den Schlaf.

> From dreams of bliss shall men awake
 

 One day, but not to weep:
 

 The dreams remain; they only break
 

 The mirror of the sleep.

===Chinese===
> 在风的呼啸里，在海的澎湃里，
 

 在自己胸中的叹息里。

> 风在咆哮，海在阴沉地翻涌，
 

 还有从他胸中降生的叹息。

> 是的，另一个时代终将到来，那时将有光明，人将从崇高的梦中醒来，并重又寻回他的梦——因为他所失去的，不过是睡眠而已。

> 人们终将从至福的梦中醒来，
 

 有一天，却不是为了哭泣：
 

 梦依然留存；破碎的只是
 

 睡眠那面镜子。

===Original===
How I got through this dreary part of my travels, I do not know. I do not think I was upheld by the hope that any moment the light might break in upon me; for I scarcely thought about that. I went on with a dull endurance, varied by moments of uncontrollable sadness; for more and more the conviction grew upon me that I should never see the white lady again. It may seem strange that one with whom I had held so little communion, should have so engrossed my thoughts; but benefits conferred awaken love in some minds, as surely as benefits received in others. Besides being delighted and proud that *my* songs had called the beautiful creature to life, the same fact caused me to feel a tenderness unspeakable for her, accompanied with a kind of feeling of property in her; for so the goblin Selfishness would reward the angel Love. When to all this is added, an overpowering sense of her beauty, and an unquestioning conviction that this was a true index to inward loveliness, it may be understood how it came to pass that my imagination filled my whole soul with the play of its own multitudinous colors and harmonies around the form which yet stood, a gracious marble radiance, in the midst of *its* white hall of phantasy. The time passed by unheeded; for my thoughts were busy. Perhaps this was also in part the cause of my needing no food, and never thinking how I should find any, during this subterraneous part of my travels. How long they endured I could not tell, for I had no means of measuring time; and when I looked back, there was such a discrepancy between the decisions of my imagination and my judgment, as to the length of time that had passed, that I was bewildered, and gave up all attempts to arrive at any conclusion on the point.

===Chinese===
我是怎样走过旅途中这段凄楚的路的，我自己也不知道。我想，支撑我的并不是那种随时会有光照到我身上的盼望；因为我几乎不曾想到这一层。我只是怀着一种麻木的忍耐往前走，中间时时被一阵阵无法自制的悲伤打断；因为我心里愈来愈确信：我再也见不到那位白衣女郎了。一个同我交往如此之少的人，竟这样占据了我的全部心思，说来也许奇怪；但施予的恩惠在某些人心中唤起爱，正如领受的恩惠在另一些人心中唤起爱一样确实。何况，*我*的歌竟唤起了这美丽的生命，这事实在令我欢喜骄傲之余，又使我对她生出一种难以言说的柔情，还伴着一种把她视作己有的感觉；因为「自私」这妖怪，正是这样来酬报「爱」那天使的。在这之上，再添上她的美所给我的那种压倒一切的感觉，再添上我那份毫不迟疑的确信——确信这美正是她内在可爱的真实标记——那么便可以明白，事情怎么会是这样：我的想象力绕着那形体——那形体依然立在那里，一片温雅的大理石光华，处在*它*那白色幻象殿堂的中央——以它自身纷繁多姿的色彩与和声的流转，填满了我的整个灵魂。时光在不知不觉中过去；因为我的心思一直忙碌。这或许也是部分的原因，使我在地底旅行的这一段里，不觉得需要食物，也从不想去哪里寻找食物。这段旅行延续了多久，我说不上来，因为我没有任何计量时间的办法；而当我回望时，对于已经流逝的时间的长短，我的想象力同我的判断力所得出的结论竟如此相悖，弄得我晕头转向，索性放弃了对这一点寻求任何结论的尝试。

===Original===
A gray mist continually gathered behind me. When I looked back towards the past, this mist was the medium through which my eyes had to strain for a vision of what had gone by; and the form of the white lady had receded into an unknown region. At length the country of rock began to close again around me, gradually and slowly narrowing, till I found myself walking in a gallery of rock once more, both sides of which I could touch with my outstretched hands. It narrowed yet, until I was forced to move carefully, in order to avoid striking against the projecting pieces of rock. The roof sank lower and lower, until I was compelled, first to stoop, and then to creep on my hands and knees. It recalled terrible dreams of childhood; but I was not much afraid, because I felt sure that this was my path, and my only hope of leaving Fairy Land, of which I was now almost weary.

===Chinese===
一片灰色的迷雾不断地在我身后聚拢。当我回望往昔，我的眼睛必须透过这雾费力张望，才能看见已经过去的事物；而白衣女郎的形体，已经退入一片未知的境地。终于，岩石之国又在我四围合拢起来，渐渐地、缓缓地窄下去，直到我发觉自己又一次走在一条岩石的廊道里，伸直两臂，两壁都摸得到。廊道继续收窄，窄得我不得不小心移步，以免撞上凸出的岩石。顶棚越沉越低，逼得我先弯下腰，末了只得手脚并用地爬行。这情景唤起童年可怕的梦魇；我却并不怎么害怕，因为我确信这就是我的路，是我离开仙境的唯一指望——仙境么，我如今对它已几乎厌倦了。

===Original===
At length, on getting past an abrupt turn in the passage, through which I had to force myself, I saw, a few yards ahead of me, the long-forgotten daylight shining through a small opening, to which the path, if path it could now be called, led me. With great difficulty I accomplished these last few yards, and came forth to the day. I stood on the shore of a wintry sea, with a wintry sun just a few feet above its horizon-edge. It was bare, and waste, and gray. Hundreds of hopeless waves rushed constantly shorewards, falling exhausted upon a beach of great loose stones, that seemed to stretch miles and miles in both directions. There was nothing for the eye but mingling shades of gray; nothing for the ear but the rush of the coming, the roar of the breaking, and the moan of the retreating wave. No rock lifted up a sheltering severity above the dreariness around; even that from which I had myself emerged rose scarcely a foot above the opening by which I had reached the dismal day, more dismal even than the tomb I had left. A cold, deathlike wind swept across the shore, seeming to issue from a pale mouth of cloud upon the horizon. Sign of life was nowhere visible. I wandered over the stones, up and down the beach, a human imbodiment of the nature around me. The wind increased; its keen waves flowed through my soul; the foam rushed higher up the stones; a few dead stars began to gleam in the east; the sound of the waves grew louder and yet more despairing. A dark curtain of cloud was lifted up, and a pale blue rent shone between its foot and the edge of the sea, out from which rushed an icy storm of frozen wind, that tore the waters into spray as it passed, and flung the billows in raving heaps upon the desolate shore. I could bear it no longer.

===Chinese===
终于，在挤过甬道里一处陡急的转弯——那里我非得奋力强行不可——之后，我看见前方几码外，日光透过一个小孔照进来，那是久已遗忘的日光；这条路——如果如今还配称作路——正通向那里。我费尽气力才走完这最后几码，来到天日之下。我站在一片冬日的海岸上，一轮冬日的太阳悬在海平线上不过几英尺。那里光秃、荒芜而灰暗。数百道绝望的浪涛不停地向岸上扑来，力竭而堕，跌碎在满滩松动的巨石上；那石滩朝两面伸展开去，仿佛绵延无数英里。眼睛所能见的，唯有交混的灰色浓淡；耳朵所能听的，唯有扑来的浪的奔涌、碎裂的浪的轰响、退却的浪的呻吟。没有一块岩石在四围的凄凉之上，高高竖起严峻而庇护的身姿；连我自己从中钻出来的那一块，也比那个把我引到这凄惨天日底下的孔口高不出一英尺——而这天日，竟比我方才离开的那座坟墓还要凄惨。一阵冰冷死寂的风扫过海岸，仿佛发端于地平线上一张苍白的云口。生命的征象，四望皆无。我在石上漫游，沿着滩头来回走动，成了四围那片自然的一个具人形的化身。风紧起来了；它那锋利的浪涛流过我的灵魂；泡沫冲上石滩更高处；几点死星开始在东方闪烁；波涛的声音愈来愈响，也愈来愈绝望。一幅黑云的帷幕被掀了起来，在它脚下与海的边缘之间，露出一线淡蓝色的裂罅；一股冻结的狂风凝成的酷寒风暴从裂罅里冲出，一路把海水撕成飞沫，把巨浪疯癫地成堆掷上荒凉的海岸。我再也不能忍受了。

===Original===
“I will not be tortured to death,” I cried; “I will meet it halfway. The life within me is yet enough to bear me up to the face of Death, and then I die unconquered.”

===Chinese===
「我不要被折磨至死，」我喊道；「我要迎上前去，在半路上与它相会。我里面的生命，还足以支撑我走到死神的面前；到那时我死去，也是不曾被征服的死。」

===Original===
Before it had grown so dark, I had observed, though without any particular interest, that on one part of the shore a low platform of rock seemed to run out far into the midst of the breaking waters. Towards this I now went, scrambling over smooth stones, to which scarce even a particle of seaweed clung; and having found it, I got on it, and followed its direction, as near as I could guess, out into the tumbling chaos. I could hardly keep my feet against the wind and sea. The waves repeatedly all but swept me off my path; but I kept on my way, till I reached the end of the low promontory, which, in the fall of the waves, rose a good many feet above the surface, and, in their rise, was covered with their waters. I stood one moment, and gazed into the heaving abyss beneath me; then plunged headlong into the mounting wave below. A blessing, like the kiss of a mother, seemed to alight on my soul; a calm, deeper than that which accompanies a hope deferred, bathed my spirit. I sank far into the waters, and sought not to return. I felt as if once more the great arms of the beech-tree were around me, soothing me after the miseries I had passed through, and telling me, like a little sick child, that I should be better tomorrow. The waters of themselves lifted me, as with loving arms, to the surface. I breathed again, but did not unclose my eyes. I would not look on the wintry sea, and the pitiless gray sky. Thus I floated, till something gently touched me. It was a little boat floating beside me. How it came there I could not tell; but it rose and sank on the waters, and kept touching me in its fall, as if with a human will to let me know that help was by me. It was a little gay-colored boat, seemingly covered with glistering scales like those of a fish, all of brilliant rainbow hues. I scrambled into it, and lay down in the bottom, with a sense of exquisite repose. Then I drew over me a rich, heavy, purple cloth that was beside me; and, lying still, knew, by the sound of the waters, that my little bark was fleeting rapidly onwards. Finding, however, none of that stormy motion which the sea had manifested when I beheld it from the shore, I opened my eyes; and, looking first up, saw above me the deep violet sky of a warm southern night; and then, lifting my head, saw that I was sailing fast upon a summer sea, in the last border of a southern twilight. The aureole of the sun yet shot the extreme faint tips of its longest rays above the horizon-waves, and withdrew them not. It was a perpetual twilight. The stars, great and earnest, like children’s eyes, bent down lovingly towards the waters; and the reflected stars within seemed to float up, as if longing to meet their embraces. But when I looked down, a new wonder met my view. For, vaguely revealed beneath the wave, I floated above my whole past. The fields of my childhood flitted by; the halls of my youthful labors; the streets of great cities where I had dwelt; and the assemblies of men and women wherein I had wearied myself seeking for rest. But so indistinct were the visions, that sometimes I thought I was sailing on a shallow sea, and that strange rocks and forests of sea-plants beguiled my eye, sufficiently to be transformed, by the magic of the phantasy, into well-known objects and regions. Yet, at times, a beloved form seemed to lie close beneath me in sleep; and the eyelids would tremble as if about to forsake the conscious eye; and the arms would heave upwards, as if in dreams they sought for a satisfying presence. But these motions might come only from the heaving of the waters between those forms and me. Soon I fell asleep, overcome with fatigue and delight. In dreams of unspeakable joy﻿—of restored friendships; of revived embraces; of love which said it had never died; of faces that had vanished long ago, yet said with smiling lips that they knew nothing of the grave; of pardons implored, and granted with such bursting floods of love, that I was almost glad I had sinned﻿—thus I passed through this wondrous twilight. I awoke with the feeling that I had been kissed and loved to my heart’s content; and found that my boat was floating motionless by the grassy shore of a little island.

===Chinese===
早在天色还没有黑到这般地步之前，我已经留意到——虽然并未特别在意——海岸有一处地方，一道低矮的石台仿佛远远伸入碎浪的中央。这时我便朝它走去，攀过一块块光滑的石头——石上连一星海草也不沾着；找到了石台，我便爬上去，循着它所指的方向——尽我所能猜测——走进那翻腾的混沌里去。顶着风和海，我几乎立不住脚。浪头一次次几乎把我从石台上扫下去；我却照旧走我的路，直到走到那道低矮的石岬的尽头：浪头跌落时，它高出水面许多英尺；浪头涌起时，它又被海水没顶。我站了片刻，俯望脚下起伏的深渊；随即头下脚上，纵身扎进下面涌起的巨浪里。一种祝福，像母亲的吻，仿佛落在我的灵魂上；一种平静——比盼望迟延之人所怀的更深的平静——浸浴了我的心灵。我朝水里深深沉去，并不想再浮上来。我觉得，仿佛山毛榉那巨大的手臂又一次环抱住我，在我历经的种种苦难之后抚慰我，又像对一个害病的小孩似的告诉我：明天就会好些了。海水自己把我托了起来，像用慈爱的手臂，托回水面。我又呼吸了，却不睁开眼睛。我不愿看那冬日的海，和那无情灰色的天空。我就这样漂浮着，直到有什么东西轻轻碰了我一下。那是一只小船，正漂在我身旁。它怎么会来到这里，我说不上来；但它在水面上一起一伏，每次下沉时总碰到我，仿佛带着一个人的意志，要让我知道救援就在身边。那是一只色彩欢快的小船，周身仿佛覆着鱼鳞一般的鳞片，闪闪发光，片片都是虹彩的鲜亮色泽。我爬进船去，躺在船底，感到一种极致精纯的安息。随后我把身旁一幅华美厚重的紫色布巾拉过来盖在身上；静静躺着，凭水声知道我的小舟正飞快前行。可是，我全然觉不出先前在岸上望见的那种风暴的颠簸，便睁开眼睛；先向上望，看见头顶上是温暖南国夜晚的深紫色天空；再抬起头，看见我正航行在一片夏日的海上，正走在南方暮色的最后一道边缘。太阳的光轮还把它最长光线那极淡的梢尖，射在海平线的浪头之上，并不收回。那是一种永恒的薄暮。星辰又大又诚挚，像孩子的眼睛，满怀爱意地向水面俯垂；水中倒映的星星仿佛向上浮起，渴望迎向它们的拥抱。可是当我低头下望，一幅新的奇景撞入眼帘。因为，波浪底下隐约显现出来的，是我的整个过去——我正漂浮在它上面。我童年的田野一闪而过；我青年劳作的厅堂；我居住过的大城的街道；还有男男女女的种种集会——我在其间寻求安息，直寻到疲惫不堪。但那些幻象如此隐约不明，有时我竟以为自己航行在一片浅海之上，以为奇异的岩石和海草的森林迷惑了我的眼睛，足以借幻想的魔力，化成熟识的器物与地域。然而，时不时地，一个可爱的形体仿佛紧贴在我身下的水中酣睡；眼睑会颤动，仿佛就要离开那有知觉的眼睛；手臂会向上抬起，仿佛在梦里寻求一个令人满足的临在。但这些动作，也可能只是那些形体与我之间的海水在起伏涌动。不久我便睡着了，被疲倦和欢喜一同压倒。在难以言说的欢乐的梦里——重续的友谊；复活的拥抱；那说自己从未死过的爱；久已消逝的面容，却用含笑的嘴唇说他们全然不知坟墓为何物；恳求的宽恕，又以那样进涌的爱之洪流赐下，叫我几乎庆幸自己曾犯过罪——就这样，我穿过了这奇妙的薄暮。我醒来时，满心感觉自己曾被亲吻、被爱到心满意足；发觉我的小舟正一动不动地漂在一座小岛青草萋萋的岸边。

## XIX / 第十九章

===Original===
> In stiller Ruhe, in wechselloser Einfalt führ ich ununterbrochen das Bewusstseyn der ganzen Menschheit in mir.

> In still rest, in changeless simplicity, I bear, uninterrupted, the consciousness of the whole of Humanity within me.

===Chinese===
> In stiller Ruhe, in wechselloser Einfalt führ ich ununterbrochen das Bewusstseyn der ganzen Menschheit in mir.

> 在恬静的安息中，在不移的单纯里，我从不间断地把整个人类的意识担在我的心怀之内。

===Original===
> … such a sweetness, such a grace,
> In all thy speech appear,
> That what to th' eye a beauteous face,
> That thy tongue is to the ear.

===Chinese===
> ……这般甘美，这般优雅，
> 在你的谈吐中处处显现，
> 你的言谈之于耳朵，
> 恰如姣好的容颜之于眼目。

===Original===
The water was deep to the very edge; and I sprang from the little boat upon a soft grassy turf. The island seemed rich with a profusion of all grasses and low flowers. All delicate lowly things were most plentiful; but no trees rose skywards, not even a bush overtopped the tall grasses, except in one place near the cottage I am about to describe, where a few plants of the gum-cistus, which drops every night all the blossoms that the day brings forth, formed a kind of natural arbor. The whole island lay open to the sky and sea. It rose nowhere more than a few feet above the level of the waters, which flowed deep all around its border. Here there seemed to be neither tide nor storm. A sense of persistent calm and fullness arose in the mind at the sight of the slow, pulse-like rise and fall of the deep, clear, unrippled waters against the bank of the island, for shore it could hardly be called, being so much more like the edge of a full, solemn river. As I walked over the grass towards the cottage, which stood at a little distance from the bank, all the flowers of childhood looked at me with perfect child-eyes out of the grass. My heart, softened by the dreams through which it had passed, overflowed in a sad, tender love towards them. They looked to me like children impregnably fortified in a helpless confidence. The sun stood halfway down the western sky, shining very soft and golden; and there grew a second world of shadows amidst the world of grasses and wild flowers.

===Chinese===
水一直深到岸边；我从小船上一跃，跳上一片柔软的草地。这座岛似乎遍地丰饶，长满各种各样的草和低处的花。一切纤弱卑微的植物都最为繁盛；却没有一株树向天空升起，连一丛灌木也不曾高过长草，只有一处例外——在我即将描述的那座小屋近旁，几株岩蔷薇（gum-cistus，它每夜都要抖落白日所绽放的全部花朵）搭成了一座天然的凉棚。整座岛向天空与大海敞开。岛上任何地方高出水面的部分都不超过几英尺，而水在它四周边界深处环流。这里似乎既无潮汐，也无风暴。望着那深沉、清澈、不起波纹的水沿岛岸缓缓起落、如同脉搏一般，心中便生出一种持久的安宁与圆满之感；那岸边其实很难称作岸，它更像一条丰盈、庄严的大河的水缘。当我踏着草地走向那座离岸不远处的小屋时，童年的一切花朵都从草丛里，用十足的孩童般的眼睛望着我。我的心已被一路经历的梦境变得柔软，朝它们漫溢出一种悲哀而温柔的爱。它们在我看来，像一群孩子以毫无防备的信赖，筑起了坚不可摧的堡垒。太阳悬在西天半途，照得十分柔和而金黄；于是在草与野花的世界中间，又生长出一个影子的世界。

===Original===
The cottage was square, with low walls, and a high pyramidal roof thatched with long reeds, of which the withered blossoms hung over all the eaves. It is noticeable that most of the buildings I saw in Fairy Land were cottages. There was no path to a door, nor, indeed, was there any track worn by footsteps in the island. The cottage rose right out of the smooth turf. It had no windows that I could see; but there was a door in the center of the side facing me, up to which I went. I knocked, and the sweetest voice I had ever heard said, "Come in." I entered. A bright fire was burning on a hearth in the center of the earthern floor, and the smoke found its way out at an opening in the center of the pyramidal roof. Over the fire hung a little pot, and over the pot bent a woman-face, the most wonderful, I thought, that I had ever beheld. For it was older than any countenance I had ever looked upon. There was not a spot in which a wrinkle could lie, where a wrinkle lay not. And the skin was ancient and brown, like old parchment. The woman's form was tall and spare: and when she stood up to welcome me, I saw that she was straight as an arrow. Could that voice of sweetness have issued from those lips of age? Mild as they were, could they be the portals whence flowed such melody? But the moment I saw her eyes, I no longer wondered at her voice: they were absolutely young—those of a woman of five-and-twenty, large, and of a clear gray. Wrinkles had beset them all about; the eyelids themselves were old, and heavy, and worn; but the eyes were very incarnations of soft light. She held out her hand to me, and the voice of sweetness again greeted me, with the single word, "Welcome." She set an old wooden chair for me, near the fire, and went on with her cooking. A wondrous sense of refuge and repose came upon me. I felt like a boy who has got home from school, miles across the hills, through a heavy storm of wind and snow. Almost, as I gazed on her, I sprang from my seat to kiss those old lips. And when, having finished her cooking, she brought some of the dish she had prepared, and set it on a little table by me, covered with a snow-white cloth, I could not help laying my head on her bosom, and bursting into happy tears. She put her arms round me, saying, "Poor child; poor child!"

===Chinese===
小屋是四方形的，墙很矮，屋顶是高高的金字塔形，覆着长长的芦苇，苇穗干枯的花絮垂挂在所有屋檐上。值得留意的是，我在仙境中所见的建筑，多半是这类小屋。没有一条通向门的路；其实，整座岛上也不曾有任何被脚步磨出的径迹。小屋就直直地从平整的草皮上耸立出来。在我能看见的范围内它没有窗；但朝着我这一面的正中有一扇门，我便朝那门走去。我敲了敲门，我平生听过的最甜美的声音说：「请进。」我走了进去。泥地正中的一方炉膛上燃着明亮的火，烟从金字塔形屋顶正中的开口寻路而出。火上悬着一只小锅，锅上俯着一张女人的脸——我想，那是我平生所见最奇特的一张脸。因为它比我见过的任何面容都要古老。凡是一道皱纹可以栖身的地方，无不栖着一道皱纹。皮肤古旧而呈棕色，像陈年的羊皮纸。那女人的身形高而清瘦；她起身迎我时，我看见她挺直如一支箭。那样甜美的声音，竟会出自那双苍老的唇吗？它们虽然温柔，难道真是流出这样乐音的门户吗？可我一看到她的眼睛，就不再为她的声音诧异了：那双眼睛是十足年轻的——像一个二十五岁女人的眼睛，又大，又是明澈的灰色。皱纹布满了它们的四周；眼皮本身苍老、沉重、疲惫；但那双眼瞳，简直是柔和的光的化身。她向我伸出手来，那甜美的声音又一次迎候我，只说了一个词：「欢迎。」她为我在炉火旁摆了一把旧木椅，便继续做饭。一种奇异的庇护与安歇之感临到我身上。我觉得自己像一个放学回家的男孩，翻过数英里的山岭，穿过一场大风大雪，终于到了家。我凝视着她，几乎要从座位上跳起来，去吻那双苍老的嘴唇。而当她做完饭，把她做好的菜端来一些，放在我身旁一张铺着雪白桌布的小桌上时，我忍不住把头靠在她的怀里，涌出幸福的泪水。她张开双臂搂住我，说：「可怜的孩子；可怜的孩子！」

===Original===
As I continued to weep, she gently disengaged herself, and, taking a spoon, put some of the food (I did not know what it was) to my lips, entreating me most endearingly to swallow it. To please her, I made an effort, and succeeded. She went on feeding me like a baby, with one arm round me, till I looked up in her face and smiled: then she gave me the spoon and told me to eat, for it would do me good. I obeyed her, and found myself wonderfully refreshed. Then she drew near the fire an old-fashioned couch that was in the cottage, and making me lie down upon it, sat at my feet, and began to sing. Amazing store of old ballads rippled from her lips, over the pebbles of ancient tunes; and the voice that sang was sweet as the voice of a tuneful maiden that singeth ever from very fullness of song. The songs were almost all sad, but with a sound of comfort. One I can faintly recall. It was something like this:

===Chinese===
我哭个不停，她便轻轻把自己脱开，拿起一把勺子，把一些食物（我不知道是什么）送到我唇边，无比亲爱地劝我咽下去。为了让她高兴，我努力了一下，咽了下去。她像喂婴儿一样继续喂我，一只手臂搂着我，直到我抬起头望着她的脸笑了：她这才把勺子递给我，叫我吃，说这对我有好处。我依了她，发觉自己精神为之一爽。然后她把屋里一张老式卧榻拉到火边，让我躺下，自己坐在我的脚边，开始唱歌。数不清的古老歌谣从她唇间潺潺流出，流过古调的卵石；那歌唱的声音，甜美如一位善歌少女的嗓音——她因满心怀歌而永远歌唱不止。这些歌几乎全是悲哀的，却带着安慰的声息。其中一首我还依稀记得。大致是这样的：

===Original===
> Sir Aglovaile through the churchyard rode;
> *Sing, All alone I lie:*
> Little recked he where'er he yode
> *All alone, up in the sky.*
> Swerved his courser, and plunged with fear;
> *All alone I lie:*

===Chinese===
> 阿格洛维尔爵士（Sir Aglovaile）骑行过教堂墓地；
> *唱吧，我孤零零地躺着：*
> 他一路纵辔前行，全不在意
> *孤零零地，在高天之上。*
> 他的骏马惊侧，惶恐腾跃；
> *我孤零零地躺着：*

===Original===
> His cry might have wakened the dead men near,
> *All alone, up in the sky.*
> The very dead that lay at his feet,
> Lapt in the mouldy winding-sheet.
> But he curbed him and spurred him, until he stood
> Still in his place, like a horse of wood,

===Chinese===
> 它的嘶鸣简直能惊醒近旁的死人，
> *孤零零地，在高天之上。*
> 正是那些躺在他脚边的死人，
> 一个个裹着霉烂的裹尸布；
> 但他勒住马、踢上马刺，直到那马
> 站定在原地，像一匹木头马，

===Original===
> With nostrils uplift, and eyes wide and wan;
> But the sweat in streams from his fetlocks ran.
> A ghost grew out of the shadowy air,
> And sat in the midst of her moony hair.
> In her gleamy hair she sat and wept;
> In the dreamful moon they lay and slept;

===Chinese===
> 鼻孔高高掀张，两眼睁得又大又白；
> 汗水成股地从腿踝的毛际流下。
> 一个鬼魂从阴翳的空气中显出，
> 端坐在她月光似的鬈发中央。
> 在她微亮的发间她坐着哭泣；
> 在如梦的月里他们躺着睡去；

===Original===
> The shadows above, and the bodies below,
> Lay and slept in the moonbeams slow.
> And she sang, like the moan of an autumn wind
> Over the stubble left behind:
> *Alas, how easily things go wrong!*
> *A sigh too much, or a kiss too long,*

===Chinese===
> 上面是影子，下面是尸身，
> 都在迟缓的月光里躺着睡沉。
> 于是她唱起来，像一阵秋风的呜咽
> 掠过收割后剩下的麦茬：
> *唉，事情多么容易走错！*
> *一声叹息多了，或一个吻长了，*

===Original===
> *And there follows a mist and a weeping rain,*
> *And life is never the same again.*
> *Alas, how hardly things go right!*
> *'Tis hard to watch on a summer night,*
> *For the sigh will come, and the kiss will stay,*
> *And the summer night is a winter day.*

===Chinese===
> *便有一片迷雾、一场哭泣的雨跟随而来，*
> *而人生再不复从前。*
> *唉，事情要走得对又是多么艰难！*
> *在夏夜里守望是难的，*
> *因为叹息总要来，吻总要停留，*
> *而夏夜原是一个冬日。*

===Original===
> "Oh, lovely ghosts my heart is woe,
> "To see thee weeping and wailing so.
> "Oh, lovely ghost," said the fearless knight,
> "Can the sword of a warrior set it right?
> "Or prayer of bedesman, praying mild,
> "As a cup of water a feverish child,

===Chinese===
> 「哦，可爱的亡魂，我心里悲伤，
> 「见你这样哭泣、这样哀号。
> 「哦，可爱的亡魂，」无畏的骑士说道，
> 「战士的剑可否把这事纠正？
> 「或是诵经人温和的祈祷，
> 「如同一杯凉水递给发烧的孩子，

===Original===
> "Soothe thee at last, in dreamless mood,
> "To sleep the sleep a dead lady should?
> "Thine eyes they fill me with longing sore,
> "As if I had known thee forevermore.
> "Oh, lovely ghost, I could leave the day,
> "To sit with thee in the moon away,

===Chinese===
> 「终于抚慰你，让你无梦无扰，
> 「睡去一个亡妇该睡的觉？
> 「你的眼睛使我满心灼痛地渴慕，
> 「仿佛我认识你已有万古。
> 「哦，可爱的亡魂，我可以舍弃白昼，
> 「到月亮里去陪你幽坐，

===Original===
> "If thou wouldst trust me, and lay thy head
> "To rest on a bosom that is not dead."
> The lady sprang up with a strange ghost cry,
> And she flung her white ghost-arms on high;
> And she laughed a laugh that was not gay,
> And it lengthened out till it died away;

===Chinese===
> 「只要你肯信我，把你的头
> 「枕上一副尚未死去的胸膛。」
> 那女子一跃而起，发出一声奇异的鬼哭，
> 把她雪白的鬼臂高高扬起；
> 她笑了一声，那笑并不欢快，
> 笑声拉长，久久不散，直到消逝；

===Original===
> And the dead beneath turned and moaned,
> And the yew-trees above they shuddered and groaned.
> "Will he love me twice with a love that is vain?
> "Will he kill the poor ghost yet again?
> "I thought thou wert good; but I said, and wept:
> " 'Can I have dreamed who have not slept?'

===Chinese===
> 下面的死人翻身呻吟，
> 上面的紫杉也战栗哀鸣。
> 「他会再一次用徒劳的爱来爱我吗？
> 「他会再一次杀死这可怜的鬼魂吗？
> 「我原以为你是好人；可我一边说，一边流泪：
> 「『不曾安睡的人，怎会有梦呢？』

===Original===
> "And I knew, alas! or ever I would,
> "Whether I dreamed, or thou wert good.
> "When my baby died, my brain grew wild.
> "I awoke, and found I was with my child."
> "If thou art the ghost of my Adelaide,
> "How is it? Thou wert but a village maid,

===Chinese===
> 「而唉！不等我愿意，我便知道
> 「我究竟是做梦，还是你是好人。
> 「我的婴孩死去时，我的神志昏乱了。
> 「我醒来，发觉我已和我的孩子同在。」
> 「若你是我阿德莱德（Adelaide）的亡魂，
> 「这是怎么回事？你本是乡间的姑娘，

===Original===
> "And thou seemest an angel lady white,
> "Though thin, and wan, and past delight."
> The lady smiled a flickering smile,
> And she pressed her temples hard the while:
> "Thou seest that Death for a woman can
> "Do more than knighthood for a man."

===Chinese===
> 「你看上去却是一位天使般的白衣贵妇，
> 「虽然消瘦、苍白，再无欢愉。」
> 那女子微微一笑，笑意明灭，
> 同时用力按着自己的太阳穴：
> 「你可见，死亡能为一个女人
> 「做成骑士风度做不成的事。」

===Original===
> "But show me the child thou callest mine.
> "Is she out tonight in the ghost's sunshine?"
> "In St. Peter's Church she is playing on,
> "At hide-and-seek, with Apostle John.
> "When the moonbeams right through the window go,
> "Where the twelve are standing in glorious show,

===Chinese===
> 「可让我看看你称为我的那个孩子。
> 「她今夜可在外面鬼魂的阳光里？」
> 「她在圣彼得教堂里玩耍个不停，
> 「和使徒约翰玩捉迷藏。
> 「当月光正好穿过窗户照进去，
> 「照着那十二位煌煌而立的使徒，

===Original===
> "She says the rest of them do not stir,
> "But one comes down to play with her.
> "Then I can go where I list, and weep,
> "For good St. John my child will keep."
> "Thy beauty filleth the very air.
> "Never saw I a woman so fair."

===Chinese===
> 「她说其余的人一动不动，
> 「只有一位下来陪她玩耍。
> 「那时我便能随意去往何处哭泣，
> 「因为好圣约翰会替我看守我的孩子。」
> 「你的美充满了这方空气。
> 「我从未见过如此美丽的女人。」

===Original===
> "Come, if thou darest, and sit by my side;
> "But do not touch me, or woe will betide.
> "Alas, I am weak: I might well know
> "This gladness betokens some further woe.
> "Yet come. It will come. I will bear it. I can.
> "For thou lovest me yet—though but as a man."

===Chinese===
> 「来吧，若你敢，坐到我身旁；
> 「但不要碰我，否则灾祸将至。
> 「唉，我虚弱：我分明知道
> 「这欢喜预示着更深的悲愁。
> 「但来吧。它终要来。我承受得住。我能。
> 「因为你毕竟还爱我——虽然不过是像爱一个人那样爱我。」

===Original===
> The knight dismounted in earnest speed;
> Away through the tombstones thundered the steed,
> And fell by the outer wall, and died.
> But the knight he kneeled by the lady's side;
> Kneeled beside her in wondrous bliss,
> Rapt in an everlasting kiss:

===Chinese===
> 骑士急切地翻身下马；
> 那骏马轰然穿过墓石驰去，
> 倒在外墙边，死去了。
> 骑士便跪在那女子身旁；
> 在奇异的至福里跪在她身边，
> 沉入一个永恒的吻：

===Original===
> Though never his lips come the lady nigh,
> And his eyes alone on her beauty lie.
> All the night long, till the cock crew loud,
> He kneeled by the lady, lapt in her shroud.
> And what they said, I may not say:
> Dead night was sweeter than living day.

===Chinese===
> 他的唇却从不曾挨近那女子，
> 他的眼睛只是凝在她的美上。
> 整整一夜，直到雄鸡高啼，
> 他跪在女子身旁，裹在她的裹尸布里。
> 他们说了什么，我不能说：
> 死沉沉的夜比活生生的白昼更甜。

===Original===
> How she made him so blissful glad
> Who made her and found her so ghostly sad,
> I may not tell; but it needs no touch
> To make them blessed who love so much.
> "Come every night, my ghost, to me;
> "And one night I will come to thee.

===Chinese===
> 她如何把他变得那样幸福欢喜——
> 他曾把她变得那样鬼魂般悲戚——
> 我不能讲；只是对于爱得这样深的人，
> 无须再添什么便可成为有福的。
> 「每夜都来吧，我的鬼魂，到我这里；
> 「将来有一夜，我会到你那里去。

===Original===
> " 'Tis good to have a ghostly wife:
> "She will not tremble at clang of strife;
> "She will only hearken, amid the din,
> "Behind the door, if he cometh in."
> And this is how Sir Aglovaile
> Often walked in the moonlight pale.

===Chinese===
> 「有一个鬼魂作妻子是好事：
> 「她不会在兵刃铿锵里发抖；
> 「她只是在那喧嚣之中留神静听，
> 「在门后，看他是否进得门来。」
> 阿格洛维尔爵士便常常这样
> 在苍白的月光里踱步。

===Original===
> And oft when the crescent but thinned the gloom,
> Full orbed moonlight filled his room;
> And through beneath his chamber door,
> Fell a ghostly gleam on the outer floor;
> And they that passed, in fear averred
> That murmured words they often heard.

===Chinese===
> 而当新月才把幽暗削薄之时，
> 满轮的月光便充满他的居室；
> 一道幽魂似的微光穿过他的房门底下，
> 落在外面的地板上；
> 路过的人都心有余悸地断言，
> 他们屡屡听见里面有人低语。

===Original===
> 'Twas then that the eastern crescent shone
> Through the chancel window, and good St. John
> Played with the ghost-child all the night,
> And the mother was free till the morning light,
> And sped through the dawning night, to stay
> With Aglovaile till the break of day.

===Chinese===
> 便在那时，东方的月牙照进
> 圣坛的窗，好圣约翰
> 整夜陪那鬼孩玩耍，
> 母亲便自由自在，直到天明，
> 她穿过拂晓前的夜色疾行，去陪伴
> 阿格洛维尔，直到破晓。

===Original===
> And their love was a rapture, lone and high,
> And dumb as the moon in the topmost sky.
> One night Sir Aglovaile, weary, slept,
> And dreamed a dream wherein he wept.
> A warrior he was, not often wept he,
> But this night he wept full bitterly.

===Chinese===
> 他们的爱是一阵狂喜，孤高而邈远，
> 缄默如最高天上的月亮。
> 一夜，阿格洛维尔爵士倦极睡去，
> 梦见一个梦，梦中的他哭泣。
> 他是个战士，不常流泪，
> 但这一夜他哭得万分悲切。

===Original===
> He woke—beside him the ghost-girl shone
> Out of the dark: 'twas the eve of St. John.
> He had dreamed a dream of a still, dark wood,
> Where the maiden of old beside him stood;
> But a mist came down, and caught her away,
> And he sought her in vain through the pathless day,

===Chinese===
> 他醒了——黑暗中，鬼女在他身旁
> 熠熠发光：那是圣约翰节前夜。
> 他梦见一个梦，梦见一片幽暗静谧的树林，
> 从前的少女就站在他身旁；
> 却有一团雾降下，把她攫走，
> 他在无路的白昼里寻她，枉然，

===Original===
> Till he wept with the grief that can do no more,
> And thought he had dreamt the dream before.
> From bursting heart the weeping flowed on;
> And lo! beside him the ghost-girl shone;
> Shone like the light on a harbor's breast,
> Over the sea of his dream's unrest;

===Chinese===
> 直到他泪尽于无可再痛的悲哀，
> 心想这梦从前也做过一回。
> 哭泣从他欲裂的心中涌流不断；
> 看哪！鬼女在他身旁发光；
> 像港湾胸膛上的一盏灯火，
> 照着他梦境不宁的海面；

===Original===
> Shone like the wondrous, nameless boon,
> That the heart seeks ever, night or noon;
> Warnings forgotten, when needed most,
> He clasped to his bosom the radiant ghost.
> She wailed aloud, and faded, and sank.
> With upturn'd white face, cold and blank,

===Chinese===
> 像那神奇而无名的恩物在发光——
> 心无论黑夜白昼都永在寻求它；
> 警戒在最需要的时刻被忘却，
> 他把那光华的鬼魂紧紧搂在怀中。
> 她放声哀哭，随即消隐、沉没。
> 仰起的白脸，又冷又空漠，

===Original===
> In his arms lay the corpse of the maiden pale,
> And she came no more to Sir Aglovaile.
> Only a voice, when winds were wild,
> Sobbed and wailed like a chidden child.

===Chinese===
> 他的怀中躺着那苍白少女的尸身，
> 她再没有到阿格洛维尔爵士这里来过。
> 只有一缕声音，在风声狂暴之时，
> 像受了呵责的孩子，呜咽哀啼。

===Original===
> *Alas, how easily things go wrong!*
> *A sigh too much, or a kiss too long,*
> *And there follows a mist and a weeping rain,*
> *And life is never the same again.*

===Chinese===
> *唉，事情多么容易走错！*
> *一声叹息多了，或一个吻长了，*
> *便有一片迷雾、一场哭泣的雨跟随而来，*
> *而人生再不复从前。*

===Original===
This was one of the simplest of her songs, which, perhaps, is the cause of my being able to remember it better than most of the others.
While she sung, I was in Elysium, with the sense of a rich soul upholding, embracing, and overhanging mine, full of all plenty and bounty. I felt as if she could give me everything I wanted; as if I should never wish to leave her, but would be content to be sung to and fed by her, day after day, as years rolled by. At last I fell asleep while she sang.

===Chinese===
这是她最简单的一首歌，或许正因如此，我对它的记忆才胜过其余的大多数。
她歌唱时，我置身极乐之境，感到一个丰盈的灵魂在我的灵魂下面撑持着、环抱着、覆罩着，满是丰盛与慷慨。我觉得她仿佛能给我想要的一切；仿佛我永远不会想离开她，而甘愿日复一日、年复一年，被她歌唱着、喂养着，便已满足。末了，我在她的歌声中睡着了。

===Original===
When I awoke, I knew not whether it was night or day. The fire had sunk to a few red embers, which just gave light enough to show me the woman standing a few feet from me, with her back towards me, facing the door by which I had entered. She was weeping, but very gently and plentifully. The tears seemed to come freely from her heart. Thus she stood for a few minutes; then, slowly turning at right angles to her former position, she faced another of the four sides of the cottage. I now observed, for the first time, that here was a door likewise; and that, indeed, there was one in the center of every side of the cottage. When she looked towards the second door, her tears ceased to flow, but sighs took their place. She often closed her eyes as she stood; and every time she closed her eyes, a gentle sigh seemed to be born in her heart, and to escape at her lips. But when her eyes were open, her sighs were deep and very sad, and shook her whole frame. Then she turned towards the third door, and a cry as of fear or suppressed pain broke from her; but she seemed to hearten herself against the dismay, and to front it steadily; for, although I often heard a slight cry, and sometimes a moan, yet she never moved or bent her head, and I felt sure that her eyes never closed. Then she turned to the fourth door, and I saw her shudder, and then stand still as a statue; till at last she turned towards me and approached the fire. I saw that her face was white as death. But she gave one look upwards, and smiled the sweetest, most child-innocent smile; then heaped fresh wood on the fire, and, sitting down by the blaze, drew her wheel near her, and began to spin. While she spun, she murmured a low strange song, to which the hum of the wheel made a kind of infinite symphony. At length she paused in her spinning and singing, and glanced towards me, like a mother who looks whether or not her child gives signs of waking. She smiled when she saw that my eyes were open. I asked her whether it was day yet. She answered, "It is always day here, so long as I keep my fire burning."

===Chinese===
我醒来时，不知是黑夜还是白昼。炉火已沉为几点红烬，仅够照亮那女人正站在离我几步远的地方，背对着我，面朝我进来的那扇门。她在哭，却哭得极轻、极多。泪水似乎从她心里畅快地涌出。她这样站了几分钟；然后，缓缓地转过一个直角，面对小屋四面的另一面。我这才头一次注意到这里也有一扇门；而且小屋每一面的正中，原来都各有一扇。她望向第二扇门时，泪水止住了，叹息接续了它们的位置。她站着时常常闭上眼睛；每次闭眼，一声轻轻的叹息便仿佛在她心里诞生，又从唇间逸出。可当她睁着眼睛时，她的叹息深沉而极悲哀，震得她整个身躯摇动。随后她转向第三扇门，一声仿佛出于恐惧或强抑之痛的叫喊从她身上迸出；但她似乎鼓起勇气直面那惊骇，坚定地迎着它；因为尽管我不时听见一声轻轻的叫喊，有时是一声呻吟，她却从不移动、从不低头，而且我确信她的眼睛从未合上。然后她转向第四扇门，我看见她打了个寒噤，随后便像一尊石像般站定；直到末了她转向我，走近炉火。我看见她的脸白得像死。可她朝天望了一眼，露出最甜美、最孩子般天真的微笑；接着往火上添了新柴，在火光旁坐下，把纺车拉近身边，开始纺纱。她纺着，低声哼一支奇异的歌，纺轮的嗡鸣为它作着一种无穷无尽的和声。终于她停下纺车和歌声，朝我瞥了一眼，像一位母亲看看孩子有没有醒来的动静。她见我睁着眼睛，便笑了。我问她天是否已经亮了。她答道：「只要我的火还燃着，这里永远是白天。」

===Original===
I felt wonderfully refreshed; and a great desire to see more of the island awoke within me. I rose, and saying that I wished to look about me, went towards the door by which I had entered.
"Stay a moment," said my hostess, with some trepidation in her voice. "Listen to me. You will not see what you expect when you go out of that door. Only remember this: whenever you wish to come back to me, enter wherever you see this mark."
She held up her left hand between me and the fire. Upon the palm, which appeared almost transparent, I saw, in dark red, a mark like this , which I took care to fix in my mind.

===Chinese===
我感到说不出的清爽；一种想多看看这座岛的强烈愿望在我心中醒来。我站起身，说想四处走走，便朝我进来的那扇门走去。
「且慢片刻，」我的女主人说，声音里带着几分惶急。「听我说。你走出那扇门时，所见绝不会是你预期的东西。只要记住这一点：无论何时你想回到我这里，凡见到这个印记的地方，就走进去。」
她把左手举在我与炉火之间。在那只仿佛近乎透明的掌心上，我看见一个深红色的印记，形如图样，我留心把它铭记在心里。

===Original===
She then kissed me, and bade me goodbye with a solemnity that awed me; and bewildered me too, seeing I was only going out for a little ramble in an island, which I did not believe larger than could easily be compassed in a few hours' walk at most. As I went she resumed her spinning.
I opened the door, and stepped out. The moment my foot touched the smooth sward, I seemed to issue from the door of an old barn on my father's estate, where, in the hot afternoons, I used to go and lie amongst the straw, and read. It seemed to me now that I had been asleep there. At a little distance in the field, I saw two of my brothers at play. The moment they caught sight of me, they called out to me to come and join them, which I did; and we played together as we had done years ago, till the red sun went down in the west, and the gray fog began to rise from the river. Then we went home together with a strange happiness. As we went, we heard the continually renewed larum of a landrail in the long grass. One of my brothers and I separated to a little distance, and each commenced running towards the part whence the sound appeared to come, in the hope of approaching the spot where the bird was, and so getting at least a sight of it, if we should not be able to capture the little creature. My father's voice recalled us from trampling down the rich long grass, soon to be cut down and laid aside for the winter. I had quite forgotten all about Fairy Land, and the wonderful old woman, and the curious red mark.

===Chinese===
然后她吻了我，以一种令我肃然生畏的郑重向我道别；这郑重也令我困惑，因为我不过是出去在一座小岛上随便走走——我并不相信这岛大得要用至多几小时的脚程才走得完。我出门时，她重新纺起纱来。
我推开门，走了出去。我的脚一触到平滑的草地，我就仿佛是从父亲庄园上一座旧谷仓的门里走出来的——炎热的午后，我常去那里的稻草堆里躺着读书。此刻我觉得自己方才就是在那里睡着了。田野里不远处，我看见两个兄弟在玩耍。他们一见我，就喊我过去同玩，我便去了；我们一起玩着，同多年前一样，直到红日在西边落下，灰色的雾开始从河上生起。于是我们怀着一种奇异的快乐一同回家。走着走着，我们听见长草丛中一只长脚秧鸡（landrail）一遍又一遍的鸣叫。我和一个兄弟分头稍稍离开，各自朝声音仿佛传来的方向奔跑，希望能靠近那鸟所在的地点，纵然捉不住这小生灵，至少也看它一眼。父亲的声音唤住了我们，不许我们踩倒那丰茂的长草——它们不久就要割下贮藏起来过冬。关于仙境，关于那奇妙的老妇人，关于那奇异的红色印记，我已忘得干干净净。

===Original===
My favorite brother and I shared the same bed. Some childish dispute arose between us; and our last words, ere we fell asleep, were not of kindness, notwithstanding the pleasures of the day. When I woke in the morning, I missed him. He had risen early, and had gone to bathe in the river. In another hour, he was brought home drowned. Alas! alas! if we had only gone to sleep as usual, the one with his arm about the other! Amidst the horror of the moment, a strange conviction flashed across my mind, that I had gone through the very same once before.
I rushed out of the house, I knew not why, sobbing and crying bitterly. I ran through the fields in aimless distress, till, passing the old barn, I caught sight of a red mark on the door. The merest trifles sometimes rivet the attention in the deepest misery; the intellect has so little to do with grief. I went up to look at this mark, which I did not remember ever to have seen before. As I looked at it, I thought I would go in and lie down amongst the straw, for I was very weary with running about and weeping. I opened the door; and there in the cottage sat the old woman as I had left her, at her spinning-wheel.

===Chinese===
我最喜欢的兄弟和我同睡一床。我们之间起了一场孩子气的小争执；尽管白天玩得那样快活，我们入睡前的最后几句话却不是好话。早晨醒来，我不见了他。他起得早，到河里洗澡去了。不出一个时辰，他被淹死着抬回家来。唉！唉！倘若我们照平日的样子入睡，一人搂着另一人，该多好！在那一刻的惊骇之中，一个奇异的念头闪过我的心：这一幕我从前已经历过一次。
我不知道为什么，冲出家门，抽噎痛哭。我在田野里漫无目的地痛苦奔跑，直到经过那座旧谷仓时，瞥见门上有一个红色印记。在最深的悲哀里，有时恰恰是最微不足道的小事攫住人的注意；心智与悲哀原是不相干的。我走上前去，端详这个印记——我不记得从前见过它。我望着它，心想不如进去躺在稻草堆里，我跑来跑去又哭了一场，实在疲乏极了。我推开门；小屋里那老妇人正像我才离开她时那样，坐在她的纺车边。

===Original===
"I did not expect you quite so soon," she said, as I shut the door behind me. I went up to the couch, and threw myself on it with that fatigue wherewith one awakes from a feverish dream of hopeless grief.
The old woman sang:

===Chinese===
「没想到你回来得这么快，」她说，我随手关上了门。我走向卧榻，一头扑在上面，带着人从一场绝望悲哀的热病之梦中醒来时的那种疲惫。
老妇人唱道：

===Original===
> The great sun, benighted,
> May faint from the sky;
> But love, once uplighted,
> Will never more die.

===Chinese===
> 伟大的太阳纵然入夜，
> 或会从天上昏沉坠落；
> 但爱一旦被点亮，
> 便永不再死灭。

===Original===
> Form, with its brightness,
> From eyes will depart:
> It walketh, in whiteness,
> The halls of the heart.

===Chinese===
> 形影纵然带着它的光华，
> 终要从眼前离去：
> 它却以一身皎白，
> 行在心殿的厅堂里。

===Original===
Ere she had ceased singing, my courage had returned. I started from the couch, and, without taking leave of the old woman, opened the door of Sighs, and sprang into what should appear.

===Chinese===
她还没唱完，我的勇气已经回来了。我从卧榻上一跃而起，没有向老妇人辞行，便打开那扇叹息之门（the door of Sighs），一跃而入那将要出现的一切。

===Original===
I stood in a lordly hall, where, by a blazing fire on the hearth, sat a lady, waiting, I knew, for someone long desired. A mirror was near me, but I saw that my form had no place within its depths, so I feared not that I should be seen. The lady wonderfully resembled my marble lady, but was altogether of the daughters of men, and I could not tell whether or not it was she. It was not for me she waited. The tramp of a great horse rang through the court without. It ceased, and the clang of armor told that his rider alighted, and the sound of his ringing heels approached the hall. The door opened; but the lady waited, for she would meet her lord alone. He strode in: she flew like a home-bound dove into his arms, and nestled on the hard steel. It was the knight of the soiled armor. But now the armor shone like polished glass; and, strange to tell, though the mirror reflected not my form, I saw a dim shadow of myself in the shining steel.

===Chinese===
我站在一座华贵的大厅里，炉膛中燃着熊熊的火，火旁坐着一位贵妇，我知道，她在等待一个盼望已久的人。我身旁有一面镜子，但我看见我的形体在它的深处没有位置，所以我不怕被人看见。那贵妇与我的大理石少女惊人地相像，却完全是人间女子的血统，我说不清她究竟是不是她。她等待的不是我。外面庭院里响起一匹高头大马的蹄声。蹄声停了，铠甲的铿锵宣告骑手下了马，他叮当作响的靴跟声逼近大厅。门开了；但贵妇不动，因为她要单独迎接她的夫君。他大步进来：她像一只归巢的鸽子般扑进他的怀里，偎在那坚硬的钢铁上。这正是那位铠甲蒙尘的骑士（the knight of the soiled armor）。可此刻那铠甲亮得像磨光的玻璃；说来奇怪，尽管镜子照不出我的形体，我却在闪亮的钢铁上看见了自己一个暗淡的影子。

===Original===
"O my beloved, thou art come, and I am blessed."
Her soft fingers speedily overcame the hard clasp of his helmet; one by one she undid the buckles of his armor; and she toiled under the weight of the mail, as she *would* carry it aside. Then she unclasped his greaves, and unbuckled his spurs; and once more she sprang into his arms, and laid her head where she could now feel the beating of his heart. Then she disengaged herself from his embrace, and, moving back a step or two, gazed at him. He stood there a mighty form, crowned with a noble head, where all sadness had disappeared, or had been absorbed in solemn purpose. Yet I suppose that he looked more thoughtful than the lady had expected to see him, for she did not renew her caresses, although his face glowed with love, and the few words he spoke were as mighty deeds for strength; but she led him towards the hearth, and seated him in an ancient chair, and set wine before him, and sat at his feet.

===Chinese===
「哦，我的爱人，你回来了，我真是有福。」
她柔软的手指很快就胜过了他头盔上坚硬的搭扣；她一件一件解开他铠甲的扣环；她在锁子甲的重压下吃力地撑着，仿佛她*定*要把它搬到一旁。然后她解开他的胫甲，褪下他的马刺；再一次扑进他的怀里，把头贴在她此刻能听见他心跳的地方。随后她从他怀抱里脱出身来，退后一两步，凝视着他。他站在那里，一个伟岸的身形，顶着高贵的头颅，一切悲哀都已消失，或者已被庄严的决心吸收。不过我想，他看上去比贵妇预想的更多了几分沉思，因为她没有重新爱抚他，虽然他脸上焕发着爱，而他说的寥寥数语，其力量堪比赫赫战功；她把他引到炉边，让他在一把古旧的椅子上坐下，把酒摆在他面前，自己坐在他的脚边。

===Original===
"I am sad," he said, "when I think of the youth whom I met twice in the forests of Fairy Land; and who, you say, twice, with his songs, roused you from the death-sleep of an evil enchantment. There was something noble in him, but it was a nobleness of thought, and not of deed. He may yet perish of vile fear."
"Ah!" returned the lady, "you saved him once; and for that I thank you; for may I not say that I somewhat loved him? But tell me how you fared, when you struck your battle-axe into the ash-tree, and he came and found you; for so much of the story you had told me, when the beggar-child came and took you away."

===Chinese===
「我心里难过，」他说，「每当想起我在仙境的森林里遇见过两次的那个青年；而你说，他曾两次用歌声把你从邪术的死亡沉睡中唤醒。他身上有某种高贵的东西，但那是思想的高贵，不是行动的高贵。他终有一天会死于卑劣的恐惧。」
「啊！」贵妇答道，「你救过他一次；为此我感谢你；因为难道我不可以说，我也有几分爱过他吗？可是告诉我，当你把战斧劈进那棵梣树、他前来找你的时候，你情形如何？这段故事你只讲到这里，乞儿就来把你带走了。」

===Original===
"As soon as I saw him," rejoined the knight, "I knew that earthly arms availed not against such as he; and that my soul must meet him in its naked strength. So I unclasped my helm, and flung it on the ground; and, holding my good axe yet in my hand, gazed at him with steady eyes. On he came, a horror indeed, but I did not flinch. Endurance must conquer, where force could not reach. He came nearer and nearer, till the ghastly face was close to mine. A shudder as of death ran through me; but I think I did not move, for he seemed to quail, and retreated. As soon as he gave back, I struck one more sturdy blow on the stem of his tree, that the forest rang; and then looked at him again. He writhed and grinned with rage and apparent pain, and again approached me, but retreated sooner than before. I heeded him no more, but hewed with a will at the tree, till the trunk creaked, and the head bowed, and with a crash it fell to the earth. Then I looked up from my labor, and lo! the specter had vanished, and I saw him no more; nor ever in my wanderings have I heard of him again."
"Well struck! well withstood! my hero," said the lady.

===Chinese===
「我一看见他，」骑士答道，「就知道人间的兵器奈何不了他这等东西；我的灵魂必须以赤裸的力量与他会面。于是我解开头盔，掷在地上；手里仍然握着我那柄好斧，用沉静的眼睛瞪着他。他逼上前来，当真是可怖之极，但我不曾退缩。武力达不到的地方，坚忍必能取胜。他越逼越近，直到那张可怕的脸贴近我的脸。一阵死亡般的战栗传遍我的全身；但我想我没有动，因为他似乎怯了，退了回去。他一退，我便朝他树上又是一斧，砍得整座森林震响；然后我又看他一眼。他扭动起来，咬牙切齿，又怒又痛，再次向我逼近，却退得比先前更快。我不再理他，只是一心一意地砍那棵树，直到树干嘎吱作响，树冠俯下头来，轰然一声倒在地上。我停手抬头一看，看哪！那幽灵已经不见了，我再没有看见他；此后在我四处漂泊的路上，也再没有听人说起过他。」
「砍得好！顶得好！我的英雄，」贵妇说。

===Original===
"But," said the knight, somewhat troubled, "dost thou love the youth still?"
"Ah!" she replied, "how can I help it? He woke me from worse than death; he loved me. I had never been for thee, if he had not sought me first. But I love him not as I love thee. He was but the moon of my night; thou art the sun of my day, O beloved."
"Thou art right," returned the noble man. "It were hard, indeed, not to have some love in return for such a gift as he hath given thee. I, too, owe him more than words can speak."

===Chinese===
「可是，」骑士说，有几分不安，「你还爱那个青年吗？」
「啊！」她答道，「我怎么能够不爱？他把我从比死亡更坏的境地里唤醒；他爱过我。若不是他先寻见我，我永远不会属于你。但我爱他，不像爱你。他不过是我黑夜里的月亮；你才是我白昼的太阳，哦，我的爱人。」
「你说得对，」那高贵的人答道。「受了他给你的这样一份厚礼，若连一点爱都不回报，也实在说不过去。我也欠他的，比言语所能表达的更多。」

===Original===
Humbled before them, with an aching and desolate heart, I yet could not restrain my words:
"Let me, then, be the moon of thy night still, O woman! And when thy day is beclouded, as the fairest days will be, let some song of mine comfort thee, as an old, withered, half-forgotten thing, that belongs to an ancient mournful hour of uncompleted birth, which yet was beautiful in its time."

===Chinese===
我谦卑地立在他们面前，怀着一颗疼痛而荒凉的心，却仍不能约束我的言语：
「那么，女人啊，就让我仍旧做你黑夜里的月亮吧！当你的白昼蒙上阴云——最美的日子也难免如此——愿我的某支歌安慰你，像一件苍老、枯萎、半被遗忘的东西，它属于一个古老而哀伤的、未曾诞生完成的时辰，然而它在其时也曾是美丽的。」

===Original===
They sat silent, and I almost thought they were listening. The color of the lady's eyes grew deeper and deeper; the slow tears grew, and filled them, and overflowed. They rose, and passed, hand in hand, close to where I stood; and each looked towards me in passing. Then they disappeared through a door which closed behind them; but, ere it closed, I saw that the room into which it opened was a rich chamber, hung with gorgeous arras. I stood with an ocean of sighs frozen in my bosom. I could remain no longer. She was near me, and I could not see her; near me in the arms of one loved better than I, and I would not see her, and I would not be by her. But how to escape from the nearness of the best beloved? I had not this time forgotten the mark; for the fact that I could not enter the sphere of these living beings kept me aware that, for me, I moved in a vision, while they moved in life. I looked all about for the mark, but could see it nowhere; for I avoided looking just where it was. There the dull red cipher glowed, on the very door of their secret chamber. Struck with agony, I dashed it open, and fell at the feet of the ancient woman, who still spun on, the whole dissolved ocean of my sighs bursting from me in a storm of tearless sobs. Whether I fainted or slept, I do not know; but, as I returned to consciousness, before I seemed to have power to move, I heard the woman singing, and could distinguish the words:

===Chinese===
他们静坐无言，我几乎以为他们在倾听。贵妇眼睛的颜色越来越深；缓缓的泪水积聚起来，盈满眼眶，漫溢而出。他们站起身，手牵着手，从我站立之处近旁走过；经过时，两人都朝我望了一眼。随后他们穿过一扇门不见了，门在他们身后合上；但在门合上之前，我看见门后那间屋子是一间华丽的寝室，挂着璀璨的壁毯。我站在那里，一片叹息的海洋冻结在我胸中。我不能再待下去了。她近在咫尺，我却看不见她；她近在咫尺，在一个被爱得胜过我的人的怀抱里，我不愿看见她，也不愿待在她身边。可怎样才能逃离这至爱之人的近旁呢？这一回我没有忘记那印记；因为我无法进入这些活人的天地，这一事实使我一直明白：我是行在幻象里，而他们行在生命里。我四下寻找那印记，却哪儿也找不到；因为我恰恰避开了它所在的地方，不肯去看。它就在那里，暗红的印记发着光，正在他们密室的门上。剧痛袭来，我猛地把门推开，扑倒在老妇人脚前——她仍旧纺着她的纱——我那融化的叹息的整个海洋，在一阵无泪的呜咽风暴中从我胸中迸发出来。我是昏过去了还是睡着了，我不知道；但当我回复知觉时，在我似乎还有力气动弹之前，我听见老妇人在唱歌，而且能分辨出那些歌词：

===Original===
Now I could weep. When she saw me weeping, she sang:
> O light of dead and of dying days!
> O Love! in thy glory go,
> In a rosy mist and a moony maze,
> O'er the pathless peaks of snow.

===Chinese===
现在我能够哭了。她见我哭泣，便唱道：
> 哦，死去的日子与垂死的日子之光！
> 哦，爱！披着你的荣光前行，
> 在玫瑰色的雾霭与月色的迷径里，
> 越过无路可循的雪峰。

===Original===
> But what is left for the cold gray soul,
> That moans like a wounded dove?
> One wine is left in the broken bowl—
> 'Tis—*To love, and love, and love.*

===Chinese===
> 但对那冰冷灰暗的灵魂，
> 对那像受伤鸽子哀鸣的灵魂，
> 还剩下什么？残破的碗里剩着一种酒——
> 那就是——*去爱，再去爱，永远爱。*

===Original===
> Better to sit at the waters' birth,
> Than a sea of waves to win;
> To live in the love that floweth forth,
> Than the love that cometh in.

===Chinese===
> 宁坐在泉水的源头，
> 胜过赢得一片波浪的海洋；
> 宁活在涌流而出的爱里，
> 胜过收取流入的爱。

===Original===
> Be thy heart a well of love, my child,
> Flowing, and free, and sure;
> For a cistern of love, though undefiled,
> Keeps not the spirit pure.

===Chinese===
> 愿你的心是一口爱的泉井，我的孩子，
> 流淌着，自由而恒定；
> 因为爱的蓄水池纵然洁净，
> 也不能叫心灵保持纯净。

===Original===
I rose from the earth, loving the white lady as I had never loved her before.
Then I walked up to the door of Dismay, and opened it, and went out. And lo! I came forth upon a crowded street, where men and women went to and fro in multitudes. I knew it well; and, turning to one hand, walked sadly along the pavement. Suddenly I saw approaching me, a little way off, a form well known to me (*well-known!*—alas, how weak the word!) in the years when I thought my boyhood was left behind, and shortly before I entered the realm of Fairy Land. Wrong and Sorrow had gone together, hand-in-hand, as it is well they do. Unchangeably dear was that face. It lay in my heart as a child lies in its own white bed; but I could not meet her.

===Chinese===
我从地上起来，爱着那位白衣女郎，比从前任何时候都爱她。
随后我走向那扇畏惧之门，打开它，走了出去。看哪！我出来站在一条拥挤的街上，男男女女成群结队地来来往往。那条街我十分熟悉；我转向一边，郁郁地沿着人行道走去。忽然，我看见不远处的路上，有一个身影朝我走来——那是我十分熟识的身影（*熟识！*——唉，这词多么无力！），那时我自以为少年时代已被抛在身后，不久之后我便踏入了仙境的国度。过错与哀伤曾一同前来，手牵着手——它们本该如此同行。那张脸亲爱如初，永不改变。它安卧在我心里，如一个孩子躺在自己洁白的小床上；可我不能与她相见。

===Original===
"Anything but that," I said, and, turning aside, sprang up the steps to a door, on which I fancied I saw the mystic sign. I entered—not the mysterious cottage, but her home. I rushed wildly on, and stood by the door of her room.
"She is out," I said, "I will see the old room once more."

===Chinese===
「除了这个都行，」我说着，转身闪开，几步跃上一道台阶，来到一扇门前——我恍惚看见门上有那神秘的记号。我走了进去——不是那间神秘的小屋，而是她的家。我发狂似的往前冲，站在她房间的门边。
「她不在家，」我说，「我要再看一眼那间旧屋子。」

===Original===
I opened the door gently, and stood in a great solemn church. A deep-toned bell, whose sounds throbbed and echoed and swam through the empty building, struck the hour of midnight. The moon shone through the windows of the clerestory, and enough of the ghostly radiance was diffused through the church to let me see, walking with a stately, yet somewhat trailing and stumbling step, down the opposite aisle, for I stood in one of the transepts, a figure dressed in a white robe, whether for the night, or for that longer night which lies too deep for the day, I could not tell. Was it she? and was this her chamber? I crossed the church, and followed. The figure stopped, seemed to ascend as it were a high bed, and lay down. I reached the place where it lay, glimmering white. The bed was a tomb. The light was too ghostly to see clearly, but I passed my hand over the face and the hands and the feet, which were all bare. They were cold—they were marble, but I knew them. It grew dark. I turned to retrace my steps, but found, ere long, that I had wandered into what seemed a little chapel. I groped about, seeking the door. Everything I touched belonged to the dead. My hands fell on the cold effigy of a knight who lay with his legs crossed and his sword broken beside him. He lay in his noble rest, and I lived on in ignoble strife. I felt for the left hand and a certain finger; I found there the ring I knew: he was one of my own ancestors. I was in the chapel over the burial-vault of my race. I called aloud: "If any of the dead are moving here, let them take pity upon me, for I, alas! am still alive; and let some dead woman comfort me, for I am a stranger in the land of the dead, and see no light." A warm kiss alighted on my lips through the dark. And I said, "The dead kiss well; I will not be afraid." And a great hand was reached out of the dark, and grasped mine for a moment, mightily and tenderly. I said to myself: "The veil between, though very dark, is very thin."

===Chinese===
我轻轻推开门，站在一座庄严的大教堂里。一口深沉的钟正敲响午夜——钟声悸动着、回荡着、漂游着，穿过空旷的殿堂。月光从高侧窗照进来，幽灵般的光辉弥漫在教堂里，恰好让我看见——因为我站在一侧的翼廊里——对面长廊上，一个身穿白袍的身影正款款走来，步态庄严，却有些拖沓蹒跚；那白袍是为今夜而穿，还是为那沉在白昼之下更深处的更长的夜而穿，我说不上来。是她吗？这里是她的寝房吗？我穿过教堂，跟了上去。那身影停下，仿佛登上了一张高床，躺了下来。我来到它躺着的地方，那里隐隐闪着白光。那床是一座坟墓。光太幽惨，看不清楚，但我用手抚过那张脸、那双手、那双脚——它们都裸露着。它们是冷的——它们是大理石，可我认得它们。四周黑了下来。我转身原路折返，但不久便发觉自己已走入一处仿佛是小礼拜堂的地方。我摸索着，寻找门。我摸到的一切都属于死者。我的手落在一位骑士冰冷的卧像上，他双腿交叠躺着，断剑横在身旁。他躺在高贵的安息里，我却在不名誉的纷争中活着。我去摸那只左手和某个指头；我摸到了我认得的那个戒指：他是我自己的先祖。我正站在我家宗族墓穴之上的礼拜堂里。我高声呼唤：「若有什么死人在这里走动，求他们可怜可怜我，因为唉！我还活着；求哪一位死去的女妇人安慰安慰我，因为我在死者的国度里是个异乡人，看不见光。」黑暗中，一个温暖的吻落在我的唇上。我说：「死人吻得多好；我不再害怕了。」一只大手从黑暗中伸出来，握住我的手，片刻，既有力又温柔。我对自己说：「那隔在中间的帷幕，虽然极暗，却也极薄。」

===Original===
Groping my way further, I stumbled over the heavy stone that covered the entrance of the vault: and, in stumbling, descried upon the stone the mark, glowing in red fire. I caught the great ring. All my effort could not have moved the huge slab; but it opened the door of the cottage, and I threw myself once more, pale and speechless, on the couch beside the ancient dame. She sang once more:—

===Chinese===
我再往深处摸索，绊在那块盖着墓穴入口的沉重石板上；就在绊倒之际，我瞥见石板上那个印记，燃着红色的火光。我抓住那只巨大的铁环。用尽全身力气我也不可能挪动那块巨石；可它打开的却是小屋的门，我又一次扑到卧榻上，面无人色，说不出话，倒在老妇人身旁。她再一次唱道：——

===Original===
> Thou dreamest: on a rock thou art,
> High o'er the broken wave;
> Thou fallest with a fearful star,
> But not into thy grave;
> For, waking in the morning's light,
> Thou smilest at the vanished night.

===Chinese===
> 你在做梦：你立在一块岩石上，
> 高高在碎裂的波浪之上；
> 你随一颗可怕的流星坠落，
> 却不是坠入你的坟墓；
> 因为你会在清晨的光里醒来，
> 对那消散的夜报以微笑。

===Original===
> So wilt thou sink, all pale and dumb,
> Into the fainting gloom;
> But ere the coming terrors come,
> Thou wak'st—where is the tomb?
> Thou wak'st—the dead ones smile above,
> With hovering arms of sleepless love.

===Chinese===
> 你也要这样沉落，满面的苍白与沉默，
> 沉入那令人昏厥的幽暗；
> 但在那临头的恐怖到来之前，
> 你醒了——坟墓在哪里？
> 你醒了——死者在上面微笑，
> 张开不眠之爱的臂膀，盘旋守护。

===Original===
She paused; then sang again:
> We weep for gladness, weep for grief;
> The tears they are the same;
> We sigh for longing, and relief;
> The sighs have but one name.
> And mingled in the dying strife,
> Are moans that are not sad;

===Chinese===
她停了停，又唱道：
> 我们为欢喜哭，也为悲哀哭；
> 泪水原是一样的泪；
> 我们为渴慕叹息，也为解脱叹息；
> 叹息只有一个名字。
> 而在那垂死的挣扎之中，
> 混着并不悲哀的呻吟；

===Original===
> The pangs of death are throbs of life,
> Its sighs are sometimes glad.
> The face is very strange and white:
> It is Earth's only spot
> That feebly flickers back the light
> The living seëth not.

===Chinese===
> 死的剧痛是生的悸动，
> 它的叹息有时是欢喜的。
> 那张脸无比陌生，无比苍白：
> 那是尘世唯一的一块地方，
> 微弱地闪回那光——
> 活着的人看不见的光。

===Original===
I fell asleep, and slept a dreamless sleep, for I know not how long. When I awoke, I found that my hostess had moved from where she had been sitting, and now sat between me and the fourth door. I guessed that her design was to prevent my entering there. I sprang from the couch, and darted past her to the door. I opened it at once and went out. All I remember is a cry of distress from the woman: "Don't go there, my child! Don't go there!" But I was gone.
I knew nothing more; or, if I did, I had forgot it all when I awoke to consciousness, lying on the floor of the cottage, with my head in the lap of the woman, who was weeping over me, and stroking my hair with both hands, talking to me as a mother might talk to a sick and sleeping, or a dead child. As soon as I looked up and saw her, she smiled through her tears; smiled with withered face and young eyes, till her countenance was irradiated with the light of the smile. Then she bathed my head and face and hands in an icy cold, colorless liquid, which smelt a little of damp earth. Immediately I was able to sit up. She rose and put some food before me. When I had eaten, she said:

===Chinese===
我睡着了，睡了一场没有梦的觉，不知睡了多久。醒来时，我发现女主人已从原来的座位挪开，此刻正坐在我和第四扇门之间。我猜她的用意是拦着我进那扇门。我从卧榻上跳起来，从她身边直冲到门前，立刻开了门走了出去。我只记得那女人一声痛苦的呼喊：「别去那儿，我的孩子！别去那儿！」可我已经出去了。
此后我便什么都不知道了；就算知道，待我恢复知觉时也已忘得精光——我躺在小屋的泥地上，头枕在老妇人的膝头，她在我上方哭着，双手抚着我的头发，对我说话，像一位母亲对一个病中的、沉睡的、或是死去的孩子说话那样。我一抬头望见她，她便含泪笑了；用那张枯萎的脸和年轻的眼睛笑，直到她整个面容都被那微笑的光照亮。随后她用一种冰凉而无色的液体洗涤我的头、脸和手，那液体微微带着潮土的气味。我立刻就能坐起来了。她起身端了些食物放在我面前。我吃过后，她说：

===Original===
"Listen to me, my child. You must leave me directly!"
"Leave you!" I said. "I am so happy with you. I never was so happy in my life."
"But you must go," she rejoined sadly. "Listen! What do you hear?"
"I hear the sound as of a great throbbing of water."

===Chinese===
「听我说，我的孩子。你必须立刻离开我！」
「离开你！」我说。「我和你在一起这么幸福。我一生从来没有这样幸福过。」
「可你必须走，」她悲哀地答道。「听！你听见什么？」
「我听见一种声音，像大水在搏动。」

===Original===
"Ah! you do hear it? Well, I had to go through that door—the door of the Timeless" (and she shuddered as she pointed to the fourth door)—"to find you; for if I had not gone, you would never have entered again; and because I went, the waters around my cottage will rise and rise, and flow and come, till they build a great firmament of waters over my dwelling. But as long as I keep my fire burning, they cannot enter. I have fuel enough for years; and after one year they will sink away again, and be just as they were before you came. I have not been buried for a hundred years now." And she smiled and wept.
"Alas! alas!" I cried. "I have brought this evil on the best and kindest of friends, who has filled my heart with great gifts."

===Chinese===
「啊！你果然听见了？唉，我不得不穿过那扇门——那扇无时之门（the door of the Timeless）」（她指着第四扇门，打了个寒噤）「——去找你；因为我若不去，你就再也不会进到这里来了；而因为我去了，我小屋四周的水将要涨起又涨起，流来又流来，直到它们在我的住所上空筑起一片水的苍穹。但只要我的火燃着，它们就进不来。我的柴火够烧好几年；一年之后，水又会退落下去，同你来之前一模一样。到如今，我已有一百年不曾被淹没了。」她微笑着，又流下泪来。
「唉！唉！」我叫道。「我把这祸事带给了一位至好至亲的朋友，她曾把重大的恩赐充满我的心。」

===Original===
"Do not think of that," she rejoined. "I can bear it very well. You will come back to me some day, I know. But I beg you, for my sake, my dear child, to do one thing. In whatever sorrow you may be, however inconsolable and irremediable it may appear, believe me that the old woman in the cottage, with the young eyes," (and she smiled), "knows something, though she must not always tell it, that would quite satisfy you about it, even in the worst moments of your distress. Now you must go."
"But how can I go, if the waters are all about, and if the doors all lead into other regions and other worlds?"
"This is not an island," she replied; "but is joined to the land by a narrow neck; and for the door, I will lead you myself through the right one."

===Chinese===
「不要想这个，」她答道。「我完全承受得起。我知道，总有一天你会回到我这里来。但我求你，为了我，我亲爱的孩子，做一件事。无论你陷入怎样的悲哀，那悲哀显得多么无法安慰、无可挽回，都要相信我：小屋里这位长着年轻眼睛的老妇人」（她微笑了）「知道一些事情，虽然她不总能说出来，却能让你对那悲哀全然安心，哪怕在你苦痛最坏的时刻。现在你必须走了。」
「可我怎么走呢，水已四面围拢，而这些门又都通向别的地带、别的世界？」
「这原不是一座岛，」她答道；「它由一道狭窄的颈地与陆地相连；至于门，我亲自领你走那扇对的。」

===Original===
She took my hand, and led me through the third door; whereupon I found myself standing in the deep grassy turf on which I had landed from the little boat, but upon the opposite side of the cottage. She pointed out the direction I must take, to find the isthmus and escape the rising waters.
Then putting her arms around me, she held me to her bosom; and as I kissed her, I felt as if I were leaving my mother for the first time, and could not help weeping bitterly. At length she gently pushed me away, and with the words, "Go, my son, and do something worth doing," turned back, and, entering the cottage, closed the door behind her.
I felt very desolate as I went.

===Chinese===
她牵起我的手，领我穿过第三扇门；我发觉自己正站在那片我从小船上登岸时踏过的深草地上，却是在小屋的另一侧。她给我指明了方向，教我找到那道地峡，逃开正在上涨的水。
随后她张开双臂搂住我，把我拥在她怀中；我吻她时，觉得自己仿佛是头一回离开母亲，忍不住痛哭起来。终于她轻轻把我推开，说了句「去吧，我的儿子，去做一件值得做的事」，便转身回去，走进小屋，在身后关上了门。
我离去时，觉得说不出的凄凉。

## XX / 第二十章

===Original===
> Thou hadst no fame; that which thou didst like good
> Was but thy appetite that swayed thy blood
> For that time to the best; for as a blast
> That through a house comes, usually doth cast
> Things out of order, yet by chance may come
> And blow some one thing to his proper room,
> So did thy appetite, and not thy zeal,
> Sway thee by chance to do some one thing well.
>
> The noble hart that harbors vertuous thought,
> And is with childe of glorious great intent,
> Can never rest, untill it forth have brought
> Th' eternall brood of glorie excellent.

===Chinese===
> 你并无令名；你偶所嘉许的善行，
> 不过是嗜欲驱使了你的血气，
> 使你那一刻趋于至善；正如一阵穿堂的风，
> 惯常把满室器物吹得颠倒散乱，
> 却也许偶或把某一件吹还原位；
> 你的嗜欲也是如此，并非出于热诚，
> 只是偶然驱使你把一件事做得好。
>
> 高贵的心怀藏有德性的思虑，
> 孕着光荣而弘大的志向，
> 便永不得安息，直至它诞育出
> 那卓越荣光的永恒的苗裔。

===Original===
I had not gone very far before I felt that the turf beneath my feet was soaked with the rising waters. But I reached the isthmus in safety. It was rocky, and so much higher than the level of the peninsula, that I had plenty of time to cross. I saw on each side of me the water rising rapidly, altogether without wind, or violent motion, or broken waves, but as if a slow strong fire were glowing beneath it. Ascending a steep acclivity, I found myself at last in an open, rocky country. After traveling for some hours, as nearly in a straight line as I could, I arrived at a lonely tower, built on the top of a little hill, which overlooked the whole neighboring country. As I approached, I heard the clang of an anvil; and so rapid were the blows, that I despaired of making myself heard till a pause in the work should ensue. It was some minutes before a cessation took place; but when it did, I knocked loudly, and had not long to wait; for, a moment after, the door was partly opened by a noble-looking youth, half-undressed, glowing with heat, and begrimed with the blackness of the forge. In one hand he held a sword, so lately from the furnace that it yet shone with a dull fire. As soon as he saw me, he threw the door wide open, and standing aside, invited me very cordially to enter. I did so; when he shut and bolted the door most carefully, and then led the way inwards. He brought me into a rude hall, which seemed to occupy almost the whole of the ground floor of the little tower, and which I saw was now being used as a workshop. A huge fire roared on the hearth, beside which was an anvil. By the anvil stood, in similar undress, and in a waiting attitude, hammer in hand, a second youth, tall as the former, but far more slightly built. Reversing the usual course of perception in such meetings, I thought them, at first sight, very unlike; and at the second glance, knew that they were brothers. The former, and apparently the elder, was muscular and dark, with curling hair, and large hazel eyes, which sometimes grew wondrously soft. The second was slender and fair, yet with a countenance like an eagle, and an eye which, though pale blue, shone with an almost fierce expression. He stood erect, as if looking from a lofty mountain crag, over a vast plain outstretched below. As soon as we entered the hall, the elder turned to me, and I saw that a glow of satisfaction shone on both their faces. To my surprise and great pleasure, he addressed me thus:

===Chinese===
我还没走出多远，就觉得脚下的草皮已被上涨的水浸透了。但我平安抵达了那道地峡。地峡多岩石，比半岛的水平面高出许多，我有充裕的时间走过它。我看见两侧的水涨得飞快，全然没有风，没有剧烈的涌动，也没有碎裂的浪，却仿佛水底燃着一场缓慢而强劲的火。登上一面陡坡之后，我发现自己终于置身于一片开阔多石的山野。我尽量笔直地走了几个钟头，来到一座孤零零的塔楼跟前；塔建在一座小山的顶上，俯瞰着四周的整片乡野。走近时，我听见铁砧的铿锵；锤声那样急促，我怕在活计停歇之前，任凭怎样呼喊也无人听见。过了几分钟，锤声方才止息；我随即大声敲门，没等多久；片刻之后，门半开着被拉开一道缝，开门的是一个相貌高贵的青年，衣衫半解，热气蒸腾，满脸蒙着锻炉的黑灰。他一手提着一柄剑，那剑才出炉不久，还泛着幽暗的火光。他一见我，便把门敞到全开，侧身让在一旁，十分恳切地请我进去。我进去了；他便极仔细地关好门、上了闩，然后领路向里。他带我进了一间粗陋的大厅，那厅似乎占去了小塔底层的几乎全部地面，我看得出来，如今正被用作工场。炉膛里一大炉火轰然烧着，旁边是一座铁砧。铁砧旁立着第二个青年，衣着同样随意，一手提锤，一副候命的姿态；他同前者一样高，身架却纤细得多。与这类相逢中知觉惯常的次序相反，我第一眼觉得他们全然不像，第二眼却知道他们是兄弟。前者显然是兄长，筋骨结实，肤色黝黑，一头鬈发，一双榛色的大眼睛，有时会变得出奇地温柔。第二个清瘦白皙，然而容貌如鹰，眼珠虽是淡蓝，却闪着近乎凌厉的光。他挺身直立，仿佛正从高峻的山岩上，俯瞰脚下铺展的茫茫平原。我们刚进大厅，做兄长的便转向我；我看见两人脸上都焕发着满意的光。令我吃惊而又大喜的是，他这样对我开口：

===Original===
"Brother, will you sit by the fire and rest, till we finish this part of our work?"

I signified my assent; and, resolved to await any disclosure they might be inclined to make, seated myself in silence near the hearth.

The elder brother then laid the sword in the fire, covered it well over, and when it had attained a sufficient degree of heat, drew it out and laid it on the anvil, moving it carefully about, while the younger, with a succession of quick smart blows, appeared either to be welding it, or hammering one part of it to a consenting shape with the rest. Having finished, they laid it carefully in the fire; and, when it was very hot indeed, plunged it into a vessel full of some liquid, whence a blue flame sprang upwards, as the glowing steel entered. There they left it; and, drawing two stools to the fire, sat down, one on each side of me.

===Chinese===
「兄弟，你可愿坐在火边歇一歇，等我们做完手头这一段活计？」

我表示同意；打定主意，无论他们乐于吐露什么，我都静候着，便在炉边默默坐下了。

做兄长的随即将剑送进火里，好好覆埋起来，待剑烧到足够的火候，便抽出来放上铁砧，一面小心地翻转挪动；弟弟则接连挥出一串利落的重锤，看样子不是在锻接，便是把剑的某一部分锤打得与其余各部浑然相合。活做完后，他们把剑小心地放回火中；待它烧得通红透热，便将它浸入一只盛满某种液体的容器——炽红的钢一入其中，便有一道蓝色的火焰腾起。他们把剑留在那里，拉了两只凳子到火边，在我两侧坐下。

===Original===
"We are very glad to see you, brother. We have been expecting you for some days," said the dark-haired youth.

"I am proud to be called your brother," I rejoined; "and you will not think I refuse the name, if I desire to know why you honor me with it?"

"Ah! then he does not know about it," said the younger. "We thought you had known of the bond betwixt us, and the work we have to do together. You must tell him, brother, from the first."

So the elder began:

===Chinese===
「见到你我们非常高兴，兄弟。我们等你已经等了好几天了，」黑发的青年说。

「蒙你们唤作兄弟，我深感荣幸，」我答道；「但倘若我想先知道，你们何以把这样的称呼加在我身上，你们总不会以为我是在推辞吧？」

「啊！这么说，他还不知道这件事，」弟弟说。「我们还以为你早已知道我们之间的盟约，知道我们须得同做的功课。你得从头讲给他听，哥哥。」

于是做兄长的开了口：

===Original===
"Our father is king of this country. Before we were born, three giant brothers had appeared in the land. No one knew exactly when, and no one had the least idea whence they came. They took possession of a ruined castle that had stood unchanged and unoccupied within the memory of any of the country people. The vaults of this castle had remained uninjured by time, and these, I presume, they made use of at first. They were rarely seen, and never offered the least injury to anyone; so that they were regarded in the neighborhood as at least perfectly harmless, if not rather benevolent beings. But it began to be observed, that the old castle had assumed somehow or other, no one knew when or how, a somewhat different look from what it used to have.
