Not only were several breaches in the lower part of the walls built up, but actually some of the battlements which yet stood, had been repaired, apparently to prevent them from falling into worse decay, while the more important parts were being restored. Of course, everyone supposed the giants must have a hand in the work, but no one ever saw them engaged in it. The peasants became yet more uneasy, after one, who had concealed himself, and watched all night, in the neighborhood of the castle, reported that he had seen, in full moonlight, the three huge giants working with might and main, all night long, restoring to their former position some massive stones, formerly steps of a grand turnpike stair, a great portion of which had long since fallen, along with part of the wall of the round tower in which it had been built. This wall they were completing, foot by foot, along with the stair. But the people said they had no just pretext for interfering: although the real reason for letting the giants alone was, that everybody was far too much afraid of them to interrupt them.

===Chinese===
「我们的父亲是这国土的王。我们出生之前，国中已经出现了三个巨人兄弟。谁也说不准他们出现于何时，更没有一个人猜得出他们从何而来。他们占据了一座荒废的城堡；在乡人们的记忆所及的岁月里，那城堡始终原样空置，无人居住。城堡的地窖不曾为岁月所损毁，我想，他们起初便是利用了那里。人们很少见到他们，他们也从未伤过任何人；因此乡邻们即便不把他们当作乐善好施之辈，至少也视为全然无害的存在。然而渐渐地有人注意到，那座古堡不知在何时、也不知怎地，模样已同往昔有些不同了。不只城墙下部的几处豁口被砌补起来，就连尚存的几段雉堞竟也修葺过了，显然是趁着较要紧的部分还在修复，先教它们免于更甚的颓败。众人自然猜到这是巨人们的手笔，却从没有人见过他们劳作。后来有一人藏身在城堡左近守了整夜，回报说他在满月的光辉里亲眼看见三个巨大的巨人彻夜奋力做工，把一些硕大的石块复位归原——那原是一座宏伟旋梯的踏步，梯身连同所属圆塔的部分塔墙，早已坍塌多时。他们正逐尺逐尺地，把那段墙连同旋梯补筑起来。但人们说，他们没有干预的正当借口：其实任凭巨人安然做事的真正缘故，是人人怕他们怕得要命，谁也不敢去搅扰。

===Original===
"At length, with the help of a neighboring quarry, the whole of the external wall of the castle was finished. And now the country folks were in greater fear than before. But for several years the giants remained very peaceful. The reason of this was afterwards supposed to be the fact, that they were distantly related to several good people in the country; for, as long as these lived, they remained quiet; but as soon as they were all dead the real nature of the giants broke out. Having completed the outside of their castle, they proceeded, by spoiling the country houses around them, to make a quiet luxurious provision for their comfort within. Affairs reached such a pass, that the news of their robberies came to my father's ears; but he, alas; was so crippled in his resources, by a war he was carrying on with a neighboring prince, that he could only spare a very few men, to attempt the capture of their stronghold. Upon these the giants issued in the night, and slew every man of them. And now, grown bolder by success and impunity, they no longer confined their depredations to property, but began to seize the persons of their distinguished neighbors, knights and ladies, and hold them in durance, the misery of which was heightened by all manner of indignity, until they were redeemed by their friends, at an exorbitant ransom. Many knights have adventured their overthrow, but to their own instead; for they have all been slain, or captured, or forced to make a hasty retreat. To crown their enormities, if any man now attempts their destruction, they, immediately upon his defeat, put one or more of their captives to a shameful death, on a turret in sight of all passersby; so that they have been much less molested of late; and we, although we have burned, for years, to attack these demons and destroy them, dared not, for the sake of their captives, risk the adventure, before we should have reached at least our earliest manhood. Now, however, we are preparing for the attempt; and the grounds of this preparation are these. Having only the resolution, and not the experience, necessary for the undertaking, we went and consulted a lonely woman of wisdom, who lives not very far from here, in the direction of the quarter from which you have come. She received us most kindly, and gave us what seems to us the best of advice. She first inquired what experience we had had in arms. We told her we had been well exercised from our boyhood, and for some years had kept ourselves in constant practice, with a view to this necessity.

===Chinese===
「后来，借了附近一处采石场之助，城堡的外墙终于全部完工。至此，乡民们比先前更加惶恐了。但此后数年，巨人们倒一直颇为安分。事后想来，缘故或许在于：他们同国中几家善良人家有疏远的亲缘；只要那些人还在世，他们便安静度日；待到那些人尽数亡故，巨人们的本性便显露出来了。城堡的外部一经完工，他们便着手糟蹋四围的乡间宅第，好在墙内为自己安排一份安静奢华的享用。事态糟到这等地步，他们劫掠的消息终于传进我父亲的耳朵；可叹啊，他正同一个邻邦的王公交战，财力因之战伤，只能拨出寥寥数人，去试取巨人的巢穴。夜里，巨人们倾巢而出，把这些人杀了个一个不剩。自此，他们得逞于前又逍遥于后，胆子愈发大了，掠夺不再限于财物，开始掳捉他们那些显贵的邻人——骑士和贵妇——囚禁起来，囚禁之苦又因百般凌辱而愈重，直到亲友们付出天价的赎金，才得赎回。许多骑士曾冒险以求颠覆他们，结果颠覆的却是自己；因为他们个个不是被杀，就是被擒，再不然便是被迫仓皇败退。更兼一件：如今若再有人尝试诛灭他们，那人一败，他们便立刻在过路人尽收眼底的一座角楼上，把一名或数名俘虏处死，死状极羞辱；因此近来敢去搅扰他们的人少得多了。而我们，虽然多年来心如火焚，渴望攻打这些恶魔、将他们剪灭，却为了那些俘虏的缘故，不敢轻举冒险，总得至少长成最早的成年，方才有望一试。不过如今，我们已在着手准备了；准备的缘由是这样的：我们空有担当此事的决心，却缺乏所需的历练，便去请教一位独居的智慧妇人；她就住在离此地不远处，正是你来的那个方向。她极其和蔼地接待了我们，给了我们一服在我们看来再好不过的方子。她先问我们在武艺上有什么阅历。我们告诉她，我们自幼便勤加操练，这几年来更一直不间断地练习，正是为了应付今日之需。

===Original===
" 'But you have not actually fought for life and death?' said she.

We were forced to confess we had not.

" 'So much the better in some respects,' she replied. 'Now listen to me. Go first and work with an armorer, for as long time as you find needful to obtain a knowledge of his craft; which will not be long, seeing your hearts will be all in the work. Then go to some lonely tower, you two alone. Receive no visits from man or woman. There forge for yourselves every piece of armor that you wish to wear, or to use, in your coming encounter. And keep up your exercises. As, however, two of you can be no match for the three giants, I will find you, if I can, a third brother, who will take on himself the third share of the fight, and the preparation. Indeed, I have already seen one who will, I think, be the very man for your fellowship; but it will be some time before he comes to me. He is wandering now without an aim. I will show him to you in a glass, and, when he comes, you will know him at once. If he will share your endeavors, you must teach him all you know, and he will repay you well, in present song, and in future deeds.'

===Chinese===
「『可你们并没有真正为生死搏斗过？』她说。

我们只得承认并没有。

「『在有些方面，这样反倒更好，』她答道。『如今听我说。你们先去盔甲匠那里做工，做工的时间，以你们觉得足以习得这门手艺为准；这不会太久，因为你们的心必全然扑在这活计上。然后你们两个单独去寻一座孤塔。无论男女，一概不得接待来访。在那里，你们为你们自己锻打你们想在临到的交锋中穿戴、使用的每一件甲胄兵器。操练也不要荒废。不过，你们两个决不是三个巨人的对手，我若能办到，便为你们寻来第三位兄弟，让他把这场搏斗和事先的准备都担下三分之一。说真的，我已经见过一个人，我想他正是与你们结伴的合意人选；只是他还要过些时日才会到我这里来。他此刻正漫无目的地游荡。我会在一面镜子里把他显给你们看；他来的时候，你们一眼就会认出他。他若肯与你们同担辛劳，你们须把所知的一切倾囊教他，他必会好好报偿你们：眼下以歌，日后以功业。』

===Original===
"She opened the door of a curious old cabinet that stood in the room. On the inside of this door was an oval convex mirror. Looking in it for some time, we at length saw reflected the place where we stood, and the old dame seated in her chair. Our forms were not reflected. But at the feet of the dame lay a young man, yourself, weeping.

" 'Surely this youth will not serve our ends,' said I, 'for he weeps.'

The old woman smiled. 'Past tears are present strength,' said she.

" 'Oh!' said my brother, 'I saw you weep once over an eagle you shot.'

" 'That was because it was so like you, brother,' I replied; 'but indeed, this youth may have better cause for tears than that—I was wrong.'

" 'Wait a while,' said the woman; 'if I mistake not, he will make you weep till your tears are dry forever. Tears are the only cure for weeping. And you may have need of the cure, before you go forth to fight the giants. You must wait for him, in your tower, till he comes.'

===Chinese===
「她打开了立在房中的一只奇特的旧橱柜的门。门的背面镶着一面椭圆的凸镜。我们对镜望了好一阵，终于看见映出我们所立的地方，和端坐椅上的老妇人。我们自己的身形却没有映现。但在老妇人的脚边，躺着一个哭泣的年轻人——正是你自己。

「『这青年想必成不了我们的助力，』我说，『因为他在哭。』

老妇人微微一笑。『已往的泪水，便是眼前的力量，』她说。

「『哦！』我弟弟说，『我曾见你为一只你亲手射杀的鹰哭过一回。』

「『那是因为它太像你了，弟弟，』我答道；『不过说真的，这青年流泪的缘由，或许比那更正当——是我说错了。』

「『等一等，』老妇人说；『我若没有看错，他必叫你们大哭一场，直哭到泪水永远干涸。泪水是哭泣唯一的解药。而在你们出发去战巨人之前，也许正用得着这解药。你们必须在塔中等他，直等到他来。』

===Original===
"Now, if you will join us, we will soon teach you to make your armor; and we will fight together, and work together, and love each other as never three loved before. And you will sing to us, will you not?"

"That I will, when I can," I answered; "but it is only at times that the power of song comes upon me. For that I must wait; but I have a feeling that if I work well, song will not be far off to enliven the labor."

This was all the compact made: the brothers required nothing more, and I did not think of giving anything more. I rose, and threw off my upper garments.

===Chinese===
「如今，你若肯加入我们，我们很快便会教你打制你自己的甲胄；我们将一同作战，一同做工，彼此相爱，从前从未有三个兄弟爱得像我们这样。你还要为我们唱歌，是不是？」

「只要我能，我一定唱，」我答道；「只是歌的力量只在某些时刻才降临到我身上。这须得等待；但我有一种预感：只要我做得好，歌便不会离得太远，自会来为劳作助兴。」

盟约便尽于此：兄弟俩别无所求，我也没想到再许诺什么。我站起身，脱去上衣。

===Original===
"I know the uses of the sword," I said. "I am ashamed of my white hands beside yours so nobly soiled and hard; but that shame will soon be wiped away."

"No, no; we will not work today. Rest is as needful as toil. Bring the wine, brother; it is your turn to serve today."

The younger brother soon covered a table with rough viands, but good wine; and we ate and drank heartily, beside our work. Before the meal was over, I had learned all their story. Each had something in his heart which made the conviction, that he would victoriously perish in the coming conflict, a real sorrow to him. Otherwise they thought they would have lived enough. The causes of their trouble were respectively these:—

===Chinese===
「剑的用场我是知道的，」我说。「站在你们那双高贵地染上尘黑、磨出硬茧的手旁边，我这双白手实在叫人羞愧；不过这份羞愧很快就会擦掉了。」

「不，不；今天我们不做了。歇息同劳作一样要紧。把酒拿来，弟弟；今天轮到你伺候。」

弟弟很快用粗粝的饭食摆满一桌，酒却是好酒；我们守着活计，痛快地又吃又喝。饭还没有吃完，他们的身世我已尽数听闻。两人心中各藏着一样东西，使那信念——他将在来临的搏斗中虽胜而亡——成了各自真实的悲哀。若非如此，他们便觉得自己这一生已经活够了。他们各自的隐痛是这样来的：——

===Original===
While they wrought with an armorer, in a city famed for workmanship in steel and silver, the elder had fallen in love with a lady as far beneath him in real rank, as she was above the station he had as apprentice to an armorer. Nor did he seek to further his suit by discovering himself; but there was simply so much manhood about him, that no one ever thought of rank when in his company. This is what his brother said about it. The lady could not help loving him in return. He told her when he left her, that he had a perilous adventure before him, and that when it was achieved, she would either see him return to claim her, or hear that he had died with honor. The younger brother's grief arose from the fact, that, if they were both slain, his old father, the king, would be childless. His love for his father was so exceeding, that to one unable to sympathise with it, it would have appeared extravagant. Both loved him equally at heart; but the love of the younger had been more developed, because his thoughts and anxieties had not been otherwise occupied. When at home, he had been his constant companion; and, of late, had ministered to the infirmities of his growing age. The youth was never weary of listening to the tales of his sire's youthful adventures; and had not yet in the smallest degree lost the conviction, that his father was the greatest man in the world. The grandest triumph possible to his conception was, to return to his father, laden with the spoils of one of the hated giants. But they both were in some dread, lest the thought of the loneliness of these two might occur to them, in the moment when decision was most necessary, and disturb, in some degree, the self-possession requisite for the success of their attempt. For, as I have said, they were yet untried in actual conflict. 'Now,' thought I, 'I see to what the powers of my gift must minister.' For my own part, I did not dread death, for I had nothing to care to live for; but I dreaded the encounter because of the responsibility connected with it. I resolved however to work hard, and thus grow cool, and quick, and forceful.

===Chinese===
他们在一个以钢铁与银器工艺闻名的城中同受雇于一位盔甲匠时，做兄长的爱上了一位贵妇：论真实的品级，她远在他之下；论他做盔甲匠学徒的身份，她又远在其上。他也不曾想靠表露真身来推进他的求婚；只是他周身那样充溢着丈夫的气概，以致人们与他相处时，从来想不到品级二字。关于这段情事，他的弟弟如是说。那位贵妇不由自主地以爱相报。别离时他告诉她：前头有一场凶险的冒险等着他；待它成就之日，她要么见他回来迎娶，要么听说他已光荣地死去。弟弟的隐痛则起于这样一桩事：倘若他们双双战死，他们年迈的父亲——那位国王——便要断了子嗣。他对父亲的爱如此过分的浓烈，在不能体谅这爱的人看来，简直近于无度。两人心里同样爱父亲；只是弟弟的爱长养得更足，因为他的心思与忧念不曾别有所系。在家的时候，他常伴父王左右；近来更一直照料着他日渐衰老的种种虚弱。这青年听父亲讲他年轻时代的冒险，从不厌倦；而且丝毫未曾失掉那个信念：他的父亲是世上最伟大的人。在他所能想见的至高凯旋里，便是满载所憎巨人之一的战利品，回到父亲跟前。但他们两人都有些忧惧：唯恐这老父孤身无依的念头，会在最需要决断的时刻浮上心头，多少搅扰了此行成功所必需的镇定。因为，如我所说，他们还不曾在真的搏杀中受过试炼。「如今我明白了，」我心想，「我这禀赋的力量，原该为怎样的目标效劳。」至于我自己，我并不怕死，因为我没有非活着不可的牵挂；我怕的是那场交锋本身，因为同它相连的干系太重。但我决心发奋做工，好让自己练得冷静、迅捷、刚猛。

===Original===
The time passed away in work and song, in talk and ramble, in friendly fight and brotherly aid. I would not forge for myself armor of heavy mail like theirs, for I was not so powerful as they, and depended more for any success I might secure, upon nimbleness of motion, certainty of eye, and ready response of hand. Therefore I began to make for myself a shirt of steel plates and rings; which work, while more troublesome, was better suited to me than the heavier labor. Much assistance did the brothers give me, even after, by their instructions, I was able to make some progress alone. Their work was in a moment abandoned, to render any required aid to mine. As the old woman had promised, I tried to repay them with song; and many were the tears they both shed, over my ballads and dirges. The songs they liked best to hear were two which I made for them. They were not half so good as many others I knew, especially some I had learned from the wise woman in the cottage; but what comes nearest to our needs we like the best.

===Chinese===
时光便在做工与歌唱中、在交谈与漫游中、在切磋的比试与兄弟的扶持中度过了。我不愿像他们那样为自己锻打沉重的铠甲，因为我没有他们那样的膂力；我若想有所成，靠的更是身法的轻捷、眼力的确切、和手的应对如响。于是我动手为自己造一件钢片与钢环缀成的锁子衫；这活计虽然繁难些，却比那重活更合于我。兄弟俩给了我许多帮助，甚至在我经他们指点、已能独自有些进境之后，依然如此。只要我的活计需要帮忙，他们的活计立刻就撂下。正如那老妇人所言，我试着以歌报答他们；听我的歌谣与挽歌，他们两个不知流了多少眼泪。他们最爱听的，是我为他们写的两首歌。比起我会唱的许多别的歌——尤其是我从小屋里那位智慧老妪处学来的几首——它们远不及一半的好；但最切近我们所需的东西，我们总是最爱。

===Original===
> The king sat on his throne,
> Glowing in gold and red;
> The crown in his right hand shone,
> And the gray hairs crowned his head.
>
> His only son walks in,
> And in walls of steel he stands:
> "Make me, O father, strong to win,
> With the blessing of holy hands."
>
> He knelt before his sire,
> Who blessed him with feeble smile;
> His eyes shone out with a kingly fire,
> But his old lips quivered the while.
>
> "Go to the fight, my son,
> Bring back the giant's head;
> And the crown with which my brows have done,
> Shall glitter on thine instead."
>
> "My father, I seek no crown,
> But unspoken praise from thee;
> For thy people's good, and thy renown,
> I will die to set them free."
>
> The king sat down and waited there,
> And rose not, night nor day;
> Till a sound of shouting filled the air,
> And cries of a sore dismay.
>
> Then like a king he sat once more,
> With the crown upon his head;
> And up to the throne the people bore
> A mighty giant dead.
>
> And up to the throne the people bore
> A pale and lifeless boy.
> The king rose up like a prophet of yore,
> In a lofty, deathlike joy.
>
> He put the crown on the chilly brow:
> "Thou should'st have reigned with me
> But Death is the king of both, and now
> I go to obey with thee.
>
> "Surely some good in me there lay,
> To beget the noble one."
> The old man smiled like a winter day,
> And fell beside his son.

===Chinese===
> 国王坐在王座上，
> 黄金与绯红交映生光；
> 王冠在他右手中闪亮，
> 灰白的头发覆着他的头。
>
> 他的独子走了进来，
> 立在钢铁的壁衣之间：
> 「父亲啊，愿圣洁之手为我祝福，
> 赐我强健，使我得胜。」
>
> 他跪在父王跟前，
> 父王含着虚弱的微笑为他祝祷；
> 他眼中燃着王者之火，
> 苍老的嘴唇却在颤摇。
>
> 「去吧，我的儿子，去战斗，
> 把巨人的头颅带回来；
> 我这双眉戴到头的王冠，
> 将改在你额上闪耀生辉。」
>
> 「父亲，我不求王冠，
> 只求你无言的称许；
> 为你子民的安乐，为你的威名，
> 我甘愿一死，使他们得自由。」
>
> 国王坐在那里等候，
> 不分昼夜，从不起身；
> 直到一片欢呼声充满了长空，
> 夹着凄惶的惊叫。
>
> 于是他又像王一般端坐，
> 头上戴着王冠；
> 人们向王座抬上来
> 一具巨人的庞然死躯。
>
> 人们向王座抬上来的，
> 还有一个苍白而失了生气的少年。
> 国王如古时的先知一般立起，
> 怀着高华的、死一般的欢喜。
>
> 他把王冠戴上那冰冷的额头：
> 「你本该同我一同为王；
> 但死亡是我们二人的王，此刻
> 我便随你一同去他座前听命。
>
> 「我身内必定原有一点善，
> 才生得出这高贵的一个。」
> 老人像一片冬日似的微笑着，
> 倒在他儿子的身边。

===Original===
> "O lady, thy lover is dead," they cried;
> "He is dead, but hath slain the foe;
> "He hath left his name to be magnified
> "In a song of wonder and woe."
>
> "Alas! I am well repaid," said she,
> "With a pain that stings like joy:
> "For I feared, from his tenderness to me,
> "That he was but a feeble boy.
>
> "Now I shall hold my head on high,
> "The queen among my kind;
> "If ye hear a sound, 'tis only a sigh
> "For a glory left behind."

===Chinese===
> 「夫人啊，你的爱人死了，」人们喊道；
> 「他死了，却已诛杀了仇敌；
> 他留下了他的名字，要在一支
> 惊异与悲哀之歌里被传扬尊大。」
>
> 「唉！我的报偿已经足够，」她说，
> 「是一种疼痛，螫刺起来却像欢喜：
> 因为我从前只怕，从他待我的温柔看，
> 他不过是个柔弱的孩子。
>
> 「如今我要昂起我的头，
> 做我族类中的女王；
> 你们若听得一点声响，那只是一声叹息，
> 为一顶遗落在身后的荣光。」

===Original===
The first three times I sang these songs they both wept passionately. But after the third time, they wept no more. Their eyes shone, and their faces grew pale, but they never wept at any of my songs again.

===Chinese===
头三回我唱这些歌，他们俩都热泪迸流。但第三回之后，他们便不再哭了。他们的眼睛熠熠发亮，脸色渐渐发白，却再也不曾为我的任何一支歌流泪。

## XXI / 第二十一章

===Original===
> I put my life in my hands.

===Chinese===
> 我把性命放在我手中。

===Original===
At length, with much toil and equal delight, our armor was finished. We armed each other, and tested the strength of the defence, with many blows of loving force. I was inferior in strength to both my brothers, but a little more agile than either; and upon this agility, joined to precision in hitting with the point of my weapon, I grounded my hopes of success in the ensuing combat. I likewise labored to develop yet more the keenness of sight with which I was naturally gifted; and, from the remarks of my companions, I soon learned that my endeavors were not in vain.

The morning arrived on which we had determined to make the attempt, and succeed or perish—perhaps both. We had resolved to fight on foot; knowing that the mishap of many of the knights who had made the attempt, had resulted from the fright of their horses at the appearance of the giants; and believing with Sir Gawain, that, though mare's sons might be false to us, the earth would never prove a traitor. But most of our preparations were, in their immediate aim at least, frustrated.

We rose, that fatal morning, by daybreak. We had rested from all labor the day before, and now were fresh as the lark. We bathed in cold spring water, and dressed ourselves in clean garments, with a sense of preparation, as for a solemn festivity. When we had broken our fast, I took an old lyre, which I had found in the tower and had myself repaired, and sung for the last time the two ballads of which I have said so much already. I followed them with this, for a closing song:—

===Chinese===
几经辛劳，也伴着同样多的欢欣，我们的铠甲终于造成。我们彼此替对方披挂，用许多含着爱意的重击，试炼甲胄的坚固。我的气力不及两位兄弟，却比他们各自都灵便些；我便把随后这场搏斗里得胜的希望，寄托在这份灵便上，再添上用武器尖端精准命中的一点功夫。我还努力磨炼我天生俱来的锐利目力；从两位同伴的言语之间，我很快得知，我的苦功并没有白费。

我们决意动手的那个早晨终于到了：此行要成，要死——或者两者兼有。我们早已决定步战；因为知道，从前许多骑士的失手，都由于坐骑一见巨人便惊溃；我们也怀着高文爵士（Sir Gawain）那样的信念：母马之子或许会负我们，大地却决不做叛徒。但我们多数的准备，至少在其眼前的目的上，都落了空。

那致命的早晨，我们在拂晓起身。前一天，我们歇了一切劳作，此刻清爽得像云雀。我们在冷冽的泉水中沐浴，穿上洁净的衣裳，怀着一种整装待发的心情，仿佛要赴一场庄严的庆典。用过早点，我取来那把旧琴——原是我在塔中寻见、亲手修好的——最后一次唱起那两首歌谣，就是我在前面说得那样多的两首；唱罢，又续上这一首，作为终曲：——

===Original===
> Oh, well for him who breaks his dream
> With the blow that ends the strife;
> And, waking, knows the peace that flows
> Around the pain of life!

===Chinese===
> 哦，那打碎自己梦的人，何其有福，
> 他用那终结争斗的一击；
> 醒来时，便知道有一种安宁流涌，
> 环绕着人生的疼痛！

===Original===
> We are dead, my brothers! Our bodies clasp,
> As an armor, our souls about;
> This hand is the battle-axe I grasp,
> And this my hammer stout.

===Chinese===
> 我们死了，我的兄弟们！我们的身体相拥，
> 像一副铠甲，把我们的灵魂环护；
> 这只手，便是我紧握的战斧，
> 而这，就是我结实的锤。

===Original===
> Fear not, my brothers, for we are dead;
> No noise can break our rest;
> The calm of the grave is about the head,
> And the heart heaves not the breast.

===Chinese===
> 不要怕，我的兄弟们，我们已经死了；
> 没有什么喧响能搅扰我们的安息；
> 坟墓的宁静四合在头顶，
> 心再不会把胸膛鼓起。

===Original===
> And our life we throw to our people back,
> To live with, a further store;
> We leave it them, that there be no lack
> In the land where we live no more.

===Chinese===
> 我们把生命掷还给我们的人民，
> 给他们留作日后过活的一份积储；
> 我们把它留给他们，好叫
> 我们不再存身的国土不致匮乏。

===Original===
> Oh, well for him who breaks his dream
> With the blow that ends the strife
> And, waking, knows the peace that flows
> Around the noise of life!

===Chinese===
> 哦，那打碎自己梦的人，何其有福，
> 他用那终结争斗的一击
> 醒来时，便知道有一种安宁流涌
> 环绕着人生的喧嚣！

===Original===
As the last few tones of the instrument were following, like a dirge, the death of the song, we all sprang to our feet. For, through one of the little windows of the tower, towards which I had looked as I sang, I saw, suddenly rising over the edge of the slope on which our tower stood, three enormous heads. The brothers knew at once, by my looks, what caused my sudden movement. We were utterly unarmed, and there was no time to arm. But we seemed to adopt the same resolution simultaneously; for each caught up his favorite weapon, and, leaving his defence behind, sprang to the door. I snatched up a long rapier, abruptly, but very finely pointed, in my sword-hand, and in the other a sabre; the elder brother seized his heavy battle-axe; and the younger, a great, two-handed sword, which he wielded in one hand like a feather. We had just time to get clear of the tower, embrace and say goodbye, and part to some little distance, that we might not encumber each other's motions, ere the triple giant-brotherhood drew near to attack us. They were about twice our height, and armed to the teeth. Through the visors of their helmets their monstrous eyes shone with a horrible ferocity. I was in the middle position, and the middle giant approached me. My eyes were busy with his armor, and I was not a moment in settling my mode of attack. I saw that his body-armor was somewhat clumsily made, and that the overlappings in the lower part had more play than necessary; and I hoped that, in a fortunate moment, some joint would open a little, in a visible and accessible part. I stood till he came near enough to aim a blow at me with the mace, which has been, in all ages, the favorite weapon of giants, when, of course, I leaped aside, and let the blow fall upon the spot where I had been standing. I expected this would strain the joints of his armor yet more. Full of fury, he made at me again; but I kept him busy, constantly eluding his blows, and hoping thus to fatigue him. He did not seem to fear any assault from me, and I attempted none as yet; but while I watched his motions in order to avoid his blows, I, at the same time, kept equal watch upon those joints of his armor, through some one of which I hoped to reach his life. At length, as if somewhat fatigued, he paused a moment, and drew himself slightly up; I bounded forward, foot and hand, ran my rapier right through to the armor of his back, let go the hilt, and passing under his right arm, turned as he fell, and flew at him with my sabre. At one happy blow, I divided the band of his helmet, which fell off, and allowed me, with a second cut across the eyes, to blind him quite; after which I clove his head, and turned, uninjured, to see how my brothers had fared. Both the giants were down, but so were my brothers. I flew first to the one and then to the other couple. Both pairs of combatants were dead, and yet locked together, as in the death struggle. The elder had buried his battle-axe in the body of his foe, and had fallen beneath him as he fell. The giant had strangled him in his own death-agonies. The younger had nearly hewn off the left leg of his enemy; and, grappled with in the act, had, while they rolled together on the earth, found for his dagger a passage betwixt the gorget and cuirass of the giant, and stabbed him mortally in the throat. The blood from the giant's throat was yet pouring over the hand of his foe, which still grasped the hilt of the dagger sheathed in the wound. They lay silent. I, the least worthy, remained the sole survivor in the lists.

===Chinese===
当乐器最后几点余音，像挽歌一般追随着那歌的死而去时，我们全都一跃而起。因为我唱歌时一直望着的那扇塔中小窗之外，三颗硕大无朋的头颅，突然从我们这座塔所立的山坡棱线上升了起来。两位兄弟凭我的神色，立刻明白了这骤然的举动。我们全然没有武装，也来不及武装。但我们似乎同时立下了同一个决心：各人抓起自己最心爱的兵器，撇下甲胄，冲向门口。我用持剑的手抓起一柄长细剑——剑尖陡峭，却磨得极锐——另一只手抓起一柄马刀；兄长抄起他那柄沉重的战斧；弟弟抄起一柄双手大剑，他单手挥动，轻如羽翎。我们刚好来得及离开塔身，相拥作别，再各自退开一小段距离，免得彼此妨碍腾挪；这时那三个一伙的巨人兄弟已经逼近来攻。他们约莫有我们两个高，武装到了牙齿。头盔的面甲后面，一双双凶怪的眼睛闪着可怖的凶光。我站在中间的位置，中间那一个巨人便朝我走来。我的眼睛忙于端详他的铠甲，顷刻之间便定下了进攻的路数。我看出他的身甲做得有些笨拙，下腹几处叠压的甲片，松动得过了分寸；我指望在某个幸运的时刻，某一处关节会微微绽开，露在看得见、够得着的地方。我立着不动，直到他逼近到能用钉头锤朝我劈来——自亘古以来，钉头锤一直是巨人们心爱的兵器——我自然向旁一跃，让那一击落在我方才立身的地方。我料想这一击会把他的甲缝绷得更松。他满怀暴怒，再度朝我扑来；我却缠住他不放，不断闪避他的打击，指望这样把他拖得力竭。他似乎全不惧怕我的进袭，我也暂且不作进袭之想；但我一面盯住他的动作，以避其锋芒，一面同样盯住他铠甲的那几处关节——我指望借其中某一道，够到他的性命。末了，他像是乏了，停得一停，身子微微挺起；我手脚并进，猛扑向前，一剑直贯到他的背甲，撒开剑柄，从他右臂之下钻过，趁他倾倒时转过身来，挥着马刀扑向他。一刀走运，我劈断了他头盔的束带，头盔脱落；再横过一剑，掠过他的双眼，把他劈得全瞎；随后我劈开他的头颅，自己毫发无损，转身去看兄弟们的战况如何。两个巨人都倒了，可我的兄弟们也倒了。我先飞奔到这一对跟前，又奔到那一对跟前。两对交战的人都已经死了，却仍死死锁在一处，像还缠在垂死的搏斗里。兄长把战斧埋进了仇敌的身体，仇敌倒下时，他也随之倒在那身躯底下。巨人在自己临死的抽搐里扼死了他。弟弟几乎劈断了仇敌的左腿；就在那一击之际被扭住，两人在地上滚作一团时，他把短剑从巨人的护喉与胸甲之间寻得一条通路，一剑刺进咽喉，给了他致命的一击。巨人咽喉里的血，还漫过仇敌的手——那只手仍旧紧攥着插在伤口里的短剑柄。他们静静地躺着。我，最不配的一个，倒成了这场武斗里唯一的生还者。

===Original===
As I stood exhausted amidst the dead, after the first worthy deed of my life, I suddenly looked behind me, and there lay the Shadow, black in the sunshine. I went into the lonely tower, and there lay the useless armor of the noble youths—supine as they. Ah, how sad it looked! It was a glorious death, but it was death. My songs could not comfort me now. I was almost ashamed that I was alive, when they, the truehearted, were no more. And yet I breathed freer to think that I had gone through the trial, and had not failed. And perhaps I may be forgiven, if some feelings of pride arose in my bosom, when I looked down on the mighty form, that lay dead by my hand.

"After all, however," I said to myself, and my heart sank, "it was only skill. Your giant was but a blunderer."

===Chinese===
我平生第一桩配得上称道的功业做完了，正力竭地站在死者的中间，忽然回头一望：那魔影就躺在那里，黑黑地卧在阳光里。我走进那座孤塔，高贵的青年们那副再无用处的铠甲就躺在那里——同他们一样仰面朝天。啊，那模样何等凄凉！那是荣耀的死，可它终归是死。此刻我的歌安慰不了我。他们，那些忠心的人，已经不在了，而我竟还活着，我几乎为此羞愧。然而一想到我经受了这场试炼而没有失败，呼吸倒也自由了些。当我俯视那具因我之手倒毙的巨大身形时，胸中涌起几分自豪——这一点，或许可以得到宽宥。

「可是，说到底，」我自语道，心头一沉，「那不过是指画的灵巧罢了。你的那个巨人，不过是个笨拙的莽汉。」

===Original===
I left the bodies of friends and foes, peaceful enough when the death-fight was over, and, hastening to the country below, roused the peasants. They came with shouting and gladness, bringing wagons to carry the bodies. I resolved to take the princes home to their father, each as he lay, in the arms of his country's foe. But first I searched the giants, and found the keys of their castle, to which I repaired, followed by a great company of the people. It was a place of wonderful strength. I released the prisoners, knights and ladies, all in a sad condition, from the cruelties and neglects of the giants. It humbled me to see them crowding round me with thanks, when in truth the glorious brothers, lying dead by their lonely tower, were those to whom the thanks belonged. I had but aided in carrying out the thought born in their brain, and uttered in visible form before ever I laid hold thereupon. Yet I did count myself happy to have been chosen for their brother in this great deed.

After a few hours spent in refreshing and clothing the prisoners, we all commenced our journey towards the capital. This was slow at first; but, as the strength and spirits of the prisoners returned, it became more rapid; and in three days we reached the palace of the king. As we entered the city gates, with the huge bulks lying each on a wagon drawn by horses, and two of them inextricably intertwined with the dead bodies of their princes, the people raised a shout and then a cry, and followed in multitudes the solemn procession.

===Chinese===
我撇下友与敌的尸身——死斗既毕，他们安静得很——匆匆赶往坡下的乡野，唤起了农人们。他们欢呼叫嚷着来了，赶着大车来运载尸首。我决意把两位王子送回家去见父亲，各人怎样躺着便怎样抬走，还拥在他们国家的仇敌怀抱之中。但我先搜检了巨人的遗骸，找出他们城堡的钥匙；我随即往那座城堡去，身后跟着一大群民众。那是一座坚固得出奇的堡垒。我把囚徒们——骑士与淑女，个个形容凄惨——从巨人的暴虐与苛待之中解救出来。看见他们纷纷围拢上来向我道谢，我心里只有惶愧：因为真正的谢忱，属于那两位荣耀的兄弟，他们正躺在孤塔旁，已然长逝。我不过是略尽绵薄，协助实现了一个诞生在他们头脑中的构想；那构想早已化为可见的形态，先于我把它握在手中。然而，能被拣选作他们的兄弟，同襄这桩大功业，我确实自认为幸福。

花了几小时让囚徒们饮食休憩、披上衣衫之后，我们全体启程，向都城进发。起初走得缓慢；但随着囚徒们气力与精神渐渐回复，行程也快了起来；第三日，我们抵达了王宫。进城门的时候，那庞大的尸躯各卧在一辆马车之上，其中两具还与两位王子的遗体死死缠结，解拆不开；民众先是欢呼，继而恸哭，成群结队地追随那庄严的行列。

===Original===
I will not attempt to describe the behavior of the grand old king. Joy and pride in his sons overcame his sorrow at their loss. On me he heaped every kindness that heart could devise or hand execute. He used to sit and question me, night after night, about everything that was in any way connected with them and their preparations. Our mode of life, and relation to each other, during the time we spent together, was a constant theme. He entered into the minutest details of the construction of the armor, even to a peculiar mode of riveting some of the plates, with unwearying interest. This armor I had intended to beg of the king, as my sole memorials of the contest; but, when I saw the delight he took in contemplating it, and the consolation it appeared to afford him in his sorrow, I could not ask for it; but, at his request, left my own, weapons and all, to be joined with theirs in a trophy, erected in the grand square of the palace. The king, with gorgeous ceremony, dubbed me knight with his own old hand, in which trembled the sword of his youth.

During the short time I remained, my company was, naturally, much courted by the young nobles. I was in a constant round of gaiety and diversion, notwithstanding that the court was in mourning. For the country was so rejoiced at the death of the giants, and so many of their lost friends had been restored to the nobility and men of wealth, that the gladness surpassed the grief. "Ye have indeed left your lives to your people, my great brothers!" I said.

===Chinese===
我不打算去描摹那位威严的老国王的举止。为儿子们所怀的欢欣与骄傲，压过了丧子的哀恸。他把心所能构想、手所能办到的每一种慈爱，尽数倾注在我身上。他惯于夜复一夜地坐着盘问我，凡与他们、与他们的筹划有丝毫关联的种种，无所不及。我们同居共处那段时间里的生活方式，和彼此之间的情谊，是他永不厌倦的话题。铠甲营造的种种细微之处，连某几块甲片一种特别的铆接之法，他也怀着不倦的兴味一一问遍。这副铠甲，我原想向国王讨来，作为这场战斗留给我的唯一纪念；但当我看见他抚视铠甲时的欢喜，看见它在他哀伤中给他的慰藉，我便开不得口了；倒是应他所请，把我自己的那副——连同兵器——留下，与他们的合竖为一座战功纪念，立在王宫的大广场上。国王行了盛大的典礼，用他自己苍老的手为我册封骑士；那只手里，还颤巍巍地握着他年轻时代的剑。

我短暂逗留期间，我的交游自然大受年轻贵族们的趋奉。宫廷虽在丧中，我却终日流转于欢宴与游艺之间。因为全国都在为巨人之死欢庆，又有这么多的亲朋故旧，从囚禁中归还给贵族与富室，欢欣便盖过了哀恸。「你们果真把生命留给你们的人民了，我伟大的兄弟们！」我说道。

===Original===
But I was ever and ever haunted by the old shadow, which I had not seen all the time that I was at work in the tower. Even in the society of the ladies of the court, who seemed to think it only their duty to make my stay there as pleasant to me as possible, I could not help being conscious of its presence, although it might not be annoying me at the time. At length, somewhat weary of uninterrupted pleasure, and nowise strengthened thereby, either in body or mind, I put on a splendid suit of armor of steel inlaid with silver, which the old King had given me, and, mounting the horse on which it had been brought to me, took my leave of the palace, to visit the distant city in which the lady dwelt, whom the elder prince had loved. I anticipated a sore task, in conveying to her the news of his glorious fate; but this trial was spared me, in a manner as strange as anything that had happened to me in Fairy Land.

===Chinese===
但那道旧魔影却时时萦绕着我——我在塔中劳作的整段日子里，始终不曾见过它。即使在宫廷淑女们的相伴之中——她们似乎只把一件事当作本分，就是让我在此地的逗留尽可能愉快——我也不由自主地觉出它在场，虽然它那时未必正在搅扰我。末了，我对这毫无间断的欢娱渐渐生倦，身心又未因它添得半分气力，便穿上老国王赐我的那副嵌银钢甲，跨上那匹曾把它驮来赠我的马，辞别王宫，往那座遥远的城去——年长的王子曾爱着的那位淑女，就住在那里。把他的荣耀结局报知于她，我料想必是一件苦差；可这场试炼竟得幸免，其经过之奇，不亚于我在仙境遭遇过的任何一桩事。

## XXII / 第二十二章

===Original===
> Niemand hat meine Gestalt als der *Ich*.

> No one has my form but the *I*.

> Joy's a subtle elf.

I think man's happiest when he forgets himself.

===Chinese===
> Niemand hat meine Gestalt als der *Ich*.

> 唯有「我」，才拥有我的形体。

> 欢乐是个难以捉摸的精灵。

我想，人在忘我之时，才最为快乐。

===Original===
On the third day of my journey, I was riding gently along a road, apparently little frequented, to judge from the grass that grew upon it. I was approaching a forest. Everywhere in Fairy Land forests are the places where one may most certainly expect adventures. As I drew near, a youth, unarmed, gentle, and beautiful, who had just cut a branch from a yew growing on the skirts of the wood, evidently to make himself a bow, met me, and thus accosted me:
"Sir knight, be careful as thou ridest through this forest; for it is said to be strangely enchanted, in a sort which even those who have been witnesses of its enchantment, can hardly describe."
I thanked him for his advice, which I promised to follow, and rode on. But the moment I entered the wood, it seemed to me that, if enchantment there was, it must be of a good kind; for the Shadow, which had been more than usually dark and distressing, since I had set out on this journey, suddenly disappeared. I felt a wonderful elevation of spirits, and began to reflect on my past life, and especially on my combat with the giants, with such satisfaction, that I had actually to remind myself, that I had only killed one of them; and that, but for the brothers, I should never have had the idea of attacking them, not to mention the smallest power of standing to it. Still I rejoiced, and counted myself amongst the glorious knights of old; having even the unspeakable presumption—my shame and self-condemnation at the memory of it are such, that I write it as the only and sorest penance I can perform—to think of myself (will the world believe it?) as side by side with Sir Galahad! Scarcely had the thought been born in my mind, when, approaching me from the left, through the trees, I espied a resplendent knight, of mighty size, whose armor seemed to shine of itself, without the sun. When he drew near, I was astonished to see that this armor was like my own; nay, I could trace, line for line, the correspondence of the inlaid silver to the device on my own. His horse, too, was like mine in color, form, and motion; save that, like his rider, he was greater and fiercer than his counterpart. The knight rode with beaver up. As he halted right opposite to me in the narrow path, barring my way, I saw the reflection of my countenance in the center plate of shining steel on his breastplate. Above it rose the same face—his face—only, as I have said, larger and fiercer. I was bewildered. I could not help feeling some admiration of him, but it was mingled with a dim conviction that he was evil, and that I ought to fight with him.

===Chinese===
启程后的第三天，我沿一条大路缓缓骑行；从路上生满的青草看，这条路显然少有人走。我正行近一座森林。在仙境的任何地方，森林都是最可指望遇上奇遇的所在。将近林边时，迎面来了一个青年：不带兵刃，温文秀美，刚从林缘一株紫杉上砍下一根枝条，显然是要给自己做一张弓；他这样招呼我：
「骑士先生，您骑马穿过这座森林时务须小心；因为据说它被施了奇异的魔法，那魔法是何等样，就连亲眼见过它施法的人，也难以描述。」
我谢过他的忠告，答应照办，继续前行。可一踏入林中，我就觉得，倘若真有魔法，那也必是善的；因为自从这趟旅程开始以来，一向格外晦暗、格外折磨我的那道魔影，忽然消失了。我精神大为振作，开始回想自己往昔的生涯，尤其是同巨人交战的那一场，心中满是快意，以致我真的不得不提醒自己：我只杀死了他们中间的一个；而且若不是有那几位弟兄相助，我连攻打他们的念头都不会有，更谈不上有一丝一毫抵挡他们的力气。然而我还是欢欣鼓舞，把自己列进古代那些荣耀骑士的行列；甚至怀着一种难以言说的狂妄——如今忆起，我的羞愧与自责如此之深，把它写下来，就算是我唯一能做的、也是最痛切的赎罪——竟自以为（世人肯信么？）与加拉哈德爵士（Sir Galahad）并肩同列！这念头在我心里才一诞生，我便瞥见左手边的树林里，一位辉煌的骑士正朝我而来：身躯魁伟，那身铠甲无需阳光照映，仿佛自己便会发光。待他行近，我惊异地看到，那铠甲与我的一模一样；不，我竟能逐线辨出，那嵌入的银丝与我甲上的纹章一一相应。他的马也一样，毛色、形体、步态都酷似我的马；只是像它的骑手一样，比我的那一匹更高大、更凶猛。那骑士掀着面甲骑行。当他在窄路上正对着我停住，拦住去路时，我看见他胸甲正中那块闪亮的钢板上，映着我自己的面容。钢板上方立着的，是同一张脸——他的脸——只是如我所说，更大、更凶。我心绪茫然。我不由得对他生出几分钦佩，但这钦佩又搀着一种隐隐的确信：他属邪恶，我应当同他一战。

===Original===
"Let me pass," I said.
"When I will," he replied.
Something within me said: "Spear in rest, and ride at him! else thou art forever a slave."
I tried, but my arm trembled so much, that I could not couch my lance. To tell the truth, I, who had overcome the giant, shook like a coward before this knight. He gave a scornful laugh, that echoed through the wood, turned his horse, and said, without looking round, "Follow me."
I obeyed, abashed and stupefied. How long he led, and how long I followed, I cannot tell. "I never knew misery before," I said to myself. "Would that I had at least struck him, and had had my deathblow in return! Why, then, do I not call to him to wheel and defend himself? Alas! I know not why, but I cannot. One look from him would cow me like a beaten hound." I followed, and was silent.

===Chinese===
「让我过去，」我说。
「待我愿意时，」他答道。
我里面有声音说：「放平长枪，策马向他冲去！不然你永远做奴隶。」
我试了试，可手臂抖得厉害，怎么也无法端平长枪。说实话，我这个战胜过巨人的我，在这位骑士面前，竟像懦夫一样发抖。他轻蔑地大笑一声，笑声在林间回荡，随即拨转马头，头也不回地说：「跟我来。」
我服从了，又羞又懵，六神无主。他领了我多久，我跟了他多久，我说不上来。「我从前从不知道何为苦难，」我对自己说。「但愿我至少劈中过他一击，纵然换回致命的一剑也好！那么，我为什么不唤他拨马回身、自卫应战？唉！不知为什么，我就是不能。他看我一眼，就能把我慑住，像慑住一条挨了打的猎犬。」我跟着，默不作声。

===Original===
At length we came to a dreary square tower, in the middle of a dense forest. It looked as if scarce a tree had been cut down to make room for it. Across the very door, diagonally, grew the stem of a tree, so large that there was just room to squeeze past it in order to enter. One miserable square hole in the roof was the only visible suggestion of a window. Turret or battlement, or projecting masonry of any kind, it had none. Clear and smooth and massy, it rose from its base, and ended with a line straight and unbroken. The roof, carried to a center from each of the four walls, rose slightly to the point where the rafters met. Round the base lay several little heaps of either bits of broken branches, withered and peeled, or half-whitened bones; I could not distinguish which. As I approached, the ground sounded hollow beneath my horse's hoofs. The knight took a great key from his pocket, and reaching past the stem of the tree, with some difficulty opened the door. "Dismount;" he commanded. I obeyed. He turned my horse's head away from the tower, gave him a terrible blow with the flat side of his sword, and sent him madly tearing through the forest.
"Now," said he, "enter, and take your companion with you."
I looked round: knight and horse had vanished, and behind me lay the horrible shadow. I entered, for I could not help myself; and the shadow followed me. I had a terrible conviction that the knight and he were one. The door closed behind me.

===Chinese===
终于，我们来到一座阴郁的方形塔楼跟前，它立在一座密林的正中。看样子，当初为给它腾地，几乎连一棵树也不曾砍倒。正对着门的，斜斜地长着一株树的树身，粗大得只留下刚够挤身而入的一点空隙。屋顶上一个寒碜的小方孔，是唯一见得出窗子模样的东西。塔台也罢，雉堞也罢，任何外凸的石筑，它一概没有。它自基座拔起，明晰、平滑、厚重，到顶收作一条笔直不断的线。屋顶从四面墙体向中央收拢，微微隆起，止于椽木相合的那一点。塔基四围散着几小堆东西，不是折断的枝条——枯槁而剥落——便是半已泛白的骨头；我分辨不出是哪一样。我走近时，马蹄之下的地面发出空洞的声响。骑士从袋中取出一把大钥匙，探身越过树身，颇费了些气力才把门打开。「下马；」他命令道。我依从了。他把我的马头拨向背离塔楼的方向，用剑背狠狠一击，那马便发疯似的纵身冲进密林里去了。
「现在，」他说，「进去吧，把你的同伴也带上。」
我回头一望：骑士与马都无影无踪，我身后躺着的，正是那道可怕的魔影。我走了进去，因为我已身不由己；魔影也跟着我进去。我怀着可怕的确信：那骑士与他原是一体。门在我身后关上了。

===Original===
Now I was indeed in pitiful plight. There was literally nothing in the tower but my shadow and me. The walls rose right up to the roof; in which, as I had seen from without, there was one little square opening. This I now knew to be the only window the tower possessed. I sat down on the floor, in listless wretchedness. I think I must have fallen asleep, and have slept for hours; for I suddenly became aware of existence, in observing that the moon was shining through the hole in the roof. As she rose higher and higher, her light crept down the wall over me, till at last it shone right upon my head. Instantaneously the walls of the tower seemed to vanish away like a mist. I sat beneath a beech, on the edge of a forest, and the open country lay, in the moonlight, for miles and miles around me, spotted with glimmering houses and spires and towers. I thought with myself, "Oh, joy! it was only a dream; the horrible narrow waste is gone, and I wake beneath a beech-tree, perhaps one that loves me, and I can go where I will." I rose, as I thought, and walked about, and did what I would, but ever kept near the tree; for always, and, of course, since my meeting with the woman of the beech-tree far more than ever, I loved that tree. So the night wore on. I waited for the sun to rise, before I could venture to renew my journey. But as soon as the first faint light of the dawn appeared, instead of shining upon me from the eye of the morning, it stole like a fainting ghost through the little square hole above my head; and the walls came out as the light grew, and the glorious night was swallowed up of the hateful day. The long dreary day passed. My shadow lay black on the floor. I felt no hunger, no need of food. The night came. The moon shone. I watched her light slowly descending the wall, as I might have watched, adown the sky, the long, swift approach of a helping angel. Her rays touched me, and I was free. Thus night after night passed away. I should have died but for this. Every night the conviction returned, that I was free. Every morning I sat wretchedly disconsolate. At length, when the course of the moon no longer permitted her beams to touch me, the night was dreary as the day. When I slept, I was somewhat consoled by my dreams; but all the time I dreamed, I knew that I was only dreaming. But one night, at length, the moon, a mere shred of pallor, scattered a few thin ghostly rays upon me; and I think I fell asleep and dreamed. I sat in an autumn night before the vintage, on a hill overlooking my own castle. My heart sprang with joy. Oh, to be a child again, innocent, fearless, without shame or desire! I walked down to the castle. All were in consternation at my absence. My sisters were weeping for my loss. They sprang up and clung to me, with incoherent cries, as I entered. My old friends came flocking round me. A gray light shone on the roof of the hall. It was the light of the dawn shining through the square window of my tower. More earnestly than ever, I longed for freedom after this dream; more drearily than ever, crept on the next wretched day. I measured by the sunbeams, caught through the little window in the trap of my tower, how it went by, waiting only for the dreams of the night.

===Chinese===
此刻我的处境真真是凄惨极了。塔楼里除了魔影与我，实在一无所有。四壁直上屋顶；屋顶上，正如我先前在外面所见，有一个小小的方孔。如今我知道，这便是塔楼仅有的一扇窗。我在地上颓然坐下，凄惶无聊。我想我必定是睡着了，而且睡了几个钟头；因为我忽然觉出自己还在活着——那是看见月亮正从屋顶的孔洞里照进来。她越升越高，月光便沿壁爬下，漫过我身，末了恰好照在我的头顶。刹那间，塔楼的四壁仿佛化雾而去。我坐在森林边缘的一株山毛榉底下，旷阔的乡野在月光里铺展，一连数英里又数英里，环拥着我，星星点点，闪烁着屋舍、塔尖与楼塔的微光。我心中暗想：「哦，欢喜！原来只是一场梦；那可怕逼仄的囚牢没有了，我在一株山毛榉树下醒来——说不定正是爱我的那一株——我爱去哪里，就可以去哪里了。」我站起身——我自己以为是站起身——四下走动，随心所欲，却始终不离那株树；因为我一向爱树，而自从遇见山毛榉女神，自然更胜往昔。夜就这样消磨过去。我等太阳升起，才敢重新上路。可清晨第一线熹微的光一露头，并不从清晨的眼瞳照到我身上，却像个奄奄一息的幽魂，从我头顶那小小的方孔里偷偷渗入；光愈明，墙愈显，光辉的黑夜就被可憎的白昼吞没了。漫长而沉闷的一日挨了过去。魔影黑黑地躺在地上。我不觉饥饿，不想进食。夜来了。月亮升起。我望着她的光沿墙缓缓下行，如同仰望天上一位救援的天使，长途疾驰、翩然而近。她的光触到我，我便自由了。夜复一夜，皆如是挨过。若非如此，我早已死了。每夜，那自由的信念总会归回。每晨，我又凄然枯坐，万念俱灰。后来，月亮的行程终于不再让她的光触到我了，黑夜便同白昼一样沉闷难熬。入睡时，梦境尚能给我几分安慰；但做梦的全程，我都知道自己不过在做梦。终于有一夜，月亮——只剩一弯淡淡的苍白——向我洒下几缕稀薄的、幽灵似的光；我想我睡着了，做起梦来。我坐在葡萄收获前的一个秋夜里，坐在一座小山上，俯望我自己的城堡。我的心欢喜得直跳。哦，重新做个孩子吧——无罪无惧，无羞无欲！我走下城堡。全家正为我失踪而惊惶。妹妹们正哭我的失去。我进门时，她们跳起身来扑在我身上，语不成声地喊叫。旧友们纷纷围拢过来。大厅屋顶上闪着一线灰白的光。那是黎明之光，正透过我塔楼的方窗照进来。这一梦之后，我比以往任何时候都更切切地渴慕自由；而随后那个悲惨的白昼，也爬得比以往任何时候都更沉闷。我凭着小窗漏进这座囚笼的日光，计量光阴如何挨过，一心只等夜里的梦。

===Original===
About noon, I started as if something foreign to all my senses and all my experience, had suddenly invaded me; yet it was only the voice of a woman singing. My whole frame quivered with joy, surprise, and the sensation of the unforeseen. Like a living soul, like an incarnation of Nature, the song entered my prison-house. Each tone folded its wings, and laid itself, like a caressing bird, upon my heart. It bathed me like a sea; inwrapt me like an odorous vapor; entered my soul like a long draught of clear spring-water; shone upon me like essential sunlight; soothed me like a mother's voice and hand. Yet, as the clearest forest-well tastes sometimes of the bitterness of decayed leaves, so to my weary, prisoned heart, its cheerfulness had a sting of cold, and its tenderness unmanned me with the faintness of long-departed joys. I wept half-bitterly, half-luxuriously; but not long. I dashed away the tears, ashamed of a weakness which I thought I had abandoned. Ere I knew, I had walked to the door, and seated myself with my ears against it, in order to catch every syllable of the revelation from the unseen outer world. And now I heard each word distinctly. The singer seemed to be standing or sitting near the tower, for the sounds indicated no change of place. The song was something like this:—

> The sun, like a golden knot on high,
> Gathers the glories of the sky,
> And binds them into a shining tent,
> Roofing the world with the firmament.
> And through the pavilion the rich winds blow,
> And through the pavilion the waters go.
> And the birds for joy, and the trees for prayer,
> Bowing their heads in the sunny air,
> And for thoughts, the gently talking springs,
> That come from the center with secret things—
> All make a music, gentle and strong,
> Bound by the heart into one sweet song.
> And amidst them all, the mother Earth
> Sits with the children of her birth;
> She tendeth them all, as a mother hen
> Her little ones round her, twelve or ten:
> Oft she sitteth, with hands on knee,
> Idle with love for her family.
> Go forth to her from the dark and the dust,
> And weep beside her, if weep thou must;
> If she may not hold thee to her breast,
> Like a weary infant, that cries for rest;
> At least she will press thee to her knee,
> And tell a low, sweet tale to thee,
> Till the hue to thy cheek, and the light to thine eye,
> Strength to thy limbs, and courage high
> To thy fainting heart, return amain,
> And away to work thou goest again.
> From the narrow desert, O man of pride,
> Come into the house, so high and wide.

===Chinese===
约在正午时分，我猛然一震，仿佛有什么全然陌生于我一切感官、一切经验的东西，猝然侵入了我的囚室；其实那不过是一个女子唱歌的声音。我全身因欢喜、惊愕与这不期之感而战栗。那歌像一个活的灵魂，像大自然的化身，走进了我这座监牢。每一个音符都收拢翅膀，像一只依人的小鸟，落在我心上。它像大海浴我全身；像芬芳的雾霭将我裹住；像一泓清冽的泉水，长长地流进我的灵魂；像本源之日光照耀着我；像母亲的声音与手掌抚慰着我。然而，正如最清澈的林间井水，有时也含一点腐叶的苦味，这歌声的欢畅，落在我这颗疲惫的、被囚的心上，也带着一丝冷的刺痛；它的温柔，拿那些早已逝去的欢乐的余晕，叫我软弱得站立不住。我哭了，半是苦涩，半是贪恋；但没有哭多久。我拭去泪水，羞于这种软弱——我以为自己早已弃绝了它。不知不觉间，我已走到门前，把耳朵贴着门坐下来，为要不漏掉那来自看不见的外间世界的启示的每一个音节。这时我听得字字分明。唱歌的人似乎就站在塔楼近旁，或是坐在那里，因为声音显示她的位置并没有移动。那支歌大致如下：—

> 太阳像高悬的一枚金色的结，
> 把满天光华收拢，
> 又系成一顶光耀的帐幕，
> 用穹苍覆盖大地。
> 华帐之中，长风习习吹过，
> 华帐之中，流水汤汤淌过。
> 鸟儿为欢喜，树木为祈祷，
> 在满是阳光的空气里俯首低垂，
> 还有那些细语呢喃的清泉，吐着沉思，
> 携着地心深处的秘密涌流而至——
> 这一切汇成一支乐音，轻柔而雄浑，
> 由心系成一支甜美的歌。
> 而在这万事中间，大地母亲
> 同她亲生的孩子们坐在一起；
> 她照看他们所有的人，像一只母鸡
> 护着环立身边的十来只雏儿：
> 她时常这样坐着，双手搭在膝上，
> 为爱她的一家，安然闲逸。
> 从黑暗和尘土里出来，到她那里去吧，
> 倘若非哭不可，就在她身旁哭；
> 倘若她不能把你抱在怀里，
> 像哭喊着要安歇的困倦婴孩；
> 她至少会把你搂在膝前，
> 给你讲一个低柔甜美的故事，
> 直到红晕回到你的脸颊，光回到你的眼，
> 力气回到你的四肢，高昂的勇气
> 回到你昏沉的心头——一切迅即归位，
> 你便再动身，做你的工去。
> 骄傲的人啊，从那狭窄的荒漠里
> 走进这又高又阔的房屋来吧。

===Original===
Hardly knowing what I did, I opened the door. Why had I not done so before? I do not know.
At first I could see no one; but when I had forced myself past the tree which grew across the entrance, I saw, seated on the ground, and leaning against the tree, with her back to my prison, a beautiful woman. Her countenance seemed known to me, and yet unknown. She looked at me and smiled, when I made my appearance.
"Ah! were you the prisoner there? I am very glad I have wiled you out."

===Chinese===
我几乎不知道自己在做什么，就把那扇门打开了。我先前为什么不这样做呢？我不知道。
起初我什么人也看不见；但当我硬着头皮，从那株横在门口的树旁挤过去之后，我看见一个美丽的女子坐在地上，背倚着那株树，背对我的囚室。她的面容我似曾相识，却又陌生。我一露面，她便望着我微笑。
「啊！你就是关在那里面的囚徒吗？我把你哄了出来，真是高兴极了。」

===Original===
"Do you know me then?"
"Do you not know me? But you hurt me, and that, I suppose, makes it easy for a man to forget. You broke my globe. Yet I thank you. Perhaps I owe you many thanks for breaking it. I took the pieces, all black, and wet with crying over them, to the Fairy Queen. There was no music and no light in them now. But she took them from me, and laid them aside; and made me go to sleep in a great hall of white, with black pillars, and many red curtains. When I woke in the morning, I went to her, hoping to have my globe again, whole and sound; but she sent me away without it, and I have not seen it since. Nor do I care for it now. I have something so much better. I do not need the globe to play to me; for I can sing. I could not sing at all before. Now I go about everywhere through Fairy Land, singing till my heart is like to break, just like my globe, for very joy at my own songs. And wherever I go, my songs do good, and deliver people. And now I have delivered you, and I am so happy."
She ceased, and the tears came into her eyes.

===Chinese===
「那么，你认得我吗？」
「你不认得我了吗？可你伤过我；我想，这总叫人容易忘事。你打碎了我的玻璃球（globe）。可我还是要谢谢你。也许为了你打碎它，我该大大谢你呢。我把那些碎片——全都黑了，又被我的眼泪哭湿——带去见仙国女王。里面已经没有音乐，也没有光了。她从我手里接过碎片，搁在一边；又让我在一座白色大厅里睡觉，厅中立着黑色的柱子，挂着许多红色的帷帘。早晨醒来，我去见她，指望把我的球完完整整、好好儿地要回来；她却不给，打发我走了，从那以后我再没见过它。不过如今我也不稀罕它了。我有了好得多的东西。我不需要球儿给我奏乐了，因为我会唱歌。从前我一点儿也不会唱。如今我走遍仙境各处，唱歌唱到心都要碎——正像我的球一样——只因听自己的歌，欢喜到了极处。凡我所到之处，我的歌都行善事，都救人脱离困境。如今我也把你救出来了，我真高兴。」
她住了口，泪水涌上了她的眼睛。

===Original===
All this time, I had been gazing at her; and now fully recognised the face of the child, glorified in the countenance of the woman. I was ashamed and humbled before her; but a great weight was lifted from my thoughts. I knelt before her, and thanked her, and begged her to forgive me.
"Rise, rise," she said; "I have nothing to forgive; I thank you. But now I must be gone, for I do not know how many may be waiting for me, here and there, through the dark forests; and they cannot come out till I come."
She rose, and with a smile and a farewell, turned and left me. I dared not ask her to stay; in fact, I could hardly speak to her. Between her and me, there was a great gulf. She was uplifted, by sorrow and well-doing, into a region I could hardly hope ever to enter. I watched her departure, as one watches a sunset. She went like a radiance through the dark wood, which was henceforth bright to me, from simply knowing that such a creature was in it. She was bearing the sun to the unsunned spots. The light and the music of her broken globe were now in her heart and her brain. As she went, she sang; and I caught these few words of her song; and the tones seemed to linger and wind about the trees after she had disappeared:

===Chinese===
这一切时候，我一直凝望着她；此刻我完全认出来了：那是那孩子的脸，在女子的面容中得了荣耀。我在她面前又羞愧又谦卑；但一块千斤重担从我心上卸下了。我跪在她面前，向她道谢，求她饶恕。
「起来，起来，」她说；「我没有什么可饶恕的；倒是我要谢你。只是我现在必须走了，因为不知道有多少人在等我，这里那里，散在黑暗的森林各处；我不去，他们出不来。」
她站起身，微微一笑，道了一声别，转身去了。我不敢求她留下；事实上，我几乎不能同她说话。在她与我之间，隔着一片深渊。忧伤与善行，已把她高举到一个我几乎不敢指望踏入的境地。我望着她远去，像人们凝望日落。她像一片光华，穿过幽暗的树林去了；而那片树林，只因为知道有这样一位生灵行于其中，从此在我便成了明亮之地。她正把太阳带往照不到太阳的地方。她那碎裂的球的光与乐音，如今都在她的心里、她的头脑里了。她一面走，一面唱；她的歌我只听清了这几句；待她身影消失之后，那歌声的音调似乎还在树间萦绕盘桓：

===Original===
> Thou goest thine, and I go mine—
> Many ways we wend;
> Many days, and many ways,
> Ending in one end.
>
> Many a wrong, and its curing song;
> Many a road, and many an inn;
> Room to roam, but only one home
> For all the world to win.

===Chinese===
> 你走你的，我走我的——
> 我们行过许多条路；
> 许多的日子，许多的途径，
> 归结却是同一个终点。
>
> 许多的冤屈，与医治它的歌；
> 许多的长路，许多的客栈；
> 遨游自有余地，家却只有一个，
> 留给全世界去赢得。

===Original===
And so she vanished. With a sad heart, soothed by humility, and the knowledge of her peace and gladness, I bethought me what now I should do. First, I must leave the tower far behind me, lest, in some evil moment, I might be once more caged within its horrible walls. But it was ill walking in my heavy armor; and besides I had now no right to the golden spurs and the resplendent mail, fitly dulled with long neglect. I might do for a squire; but I honored knighthood too highly, to call myself any longer one of the noble brotherhood. I stripped off all my armor, piled it under the tree, just where the lady had been seated, and took my unknown way, eastward through the woods. Of all my weapons, I carried only a short axe in my hand. Then first I knew the delight of being lowly; of saying to myself, "I am what I am, nothing more." "I have failed," I said, "I have lost myself—would it had been my shadow." I looked round: the shadow was nowhere to be seen. Ere long, I learned that it was not myself, but only my shadow, that I had lost. I learned that it is better, a thousandfold, for a proud man to fall and be humbled, than to hold up his head in his pride and fancied innocence. I learned that he that will be a hero, will barely be a man; that he that will be nothing but a doer of his work, is sure of his manhood. In nothing was my ideal lowered, or dimmed, or grown less precious; I only saw it too plainly, to set myself for a moment beside it. Indeed, my ideal soon became my life; whereas, formerly, my life had consisted in a vain attempt to behold, if not my ideal in myself, at least myself in my ideal. Now, however, I took, at first, what perhaps was a mistaken pleasure, in despising and degrading myself. Another self seemed to arise, like a white spirit from a dead man, from the dumb and trampled self of the past. Doubtless, this self must again die and be buried, and again, from its tomb, spring a winged child; but of this my history as yet bears not the record. Self will come to life even in the slaying of self; but there is ever something deeper and stronger than it, which will emerge at last from the unknown abysses of the soul: will it be as a solemn gloom, burning with eyes? or a clear morning after the rain? or a smiling child, that finds itself nowhere, and everywhere?

===Chinese===
她就这样消失了。我怀着悲哀的心——那悲哀既受谦卑的抚慰，又有她那平安与欢喜的景况为安慰——思量着此刻该做什么。第一件，我必须把那座塔楼远远抛在身后，免得在什么邪恶的时辰，再度被囚进它那可怖的四壁之中。可是穿着沉重的铠甲赶路终归不便；况且那金马刺与辉煌的甲胄，久经荒疏，黯淡得正合其分，如今我也无权再僭用了。我充其量做得一名侍从；而我太尊崇骑士的尊严，不肯再称自己为那高贵团体的一员。我褪下全身甲胄，堆在那株树下，恰放在那位女子方才坐过的地方，随后取一条未知之路，向东穿过树林而去。一切兵刃之中，我手里只带了一柄短斧。我这才头一回尝到卑微的甜美；尝到对自己说「我就是我，仅此而已」的甜美。「我失败了，」我说，「我把自己丢失了——但愿丢失的是我的魔影。」我回头四望：魔影已无影无踪。不久我便明白：我丢失的并非我自己，只是我的影子。我明白了：骄傲的人跌倒而自卑，强似他昂着头，仗着骄傲与自以为的清白站立，何止千倍。我明白了：定意要作英雄的，几乎就算不得一个人；定意只作自己本分之事的行事的，才确实保有他为人之道。我的理想并未因此贬低、黯淡、或减损其珍贵分毫；我只是把它看得太清楚，再不肯把自己放在它旁边片刻。的确，我的理想不久便成了我的生命；而在从前，我的生命只在于一种徒劳的企图——倘若不能在我自己身上看见我的理想，至少要在我的理想里看见我自己。然而起初，我在鄙弃自己、贬抑自己之中，取了一种兴许是错谬的乐趣。另一个自我仿佛站立起来，像一个白色的精灵从死人身上升起，从那往昔喑哑的、饱受践踏的自我里升起。无疑，这个自我还须再死、再被埋葬，再从它的坟墓里，迸出一个有翅膀的孩子；但这一节，我的历史至今尚未记载。自我甚至在杀死自我的行动里也会活转来；但总有一种比它更深、更强者在，终要从灵魂那未知的深渊里浮现出来：那将是什么呢——是一片燃着眼睛的庄严幽暗？是雨后清朗的早晨？还是一个含笑的孩子，发觉自己无处不在、又无一处可寻？

## XXIII / 第二十三章

===Original===
> High erected thought, seated in a heart of courtesy.

> A sweet attractive kinde of grace,

 A full assurance given by lookes,

 Continuall comfort in a face,

 The lineaments of Gospel bookes.

===Chinese===
> 高卓耸立的思想，端坐于彬彬有礼的心中。

> 一种甜美动人的优雅风致，

 目光里给出的十足信实，

 面庞上长驻不去的安慰，

 福音书册上的眉目形相。

===Original===
I had not gone far, for I had but just lost sight of the hated tower, when a voice of another sort, sounding near or far, as the trees permitted or intercepted its passage, reached me. It was a full, deep, manly voice, but withal clear and melodious. Now it burst on the ear with a sudden swell, and anon, dying away as suddenly, seemed to come to me across a great space. Nevertheless, it drew nearer; till, at last, I could distinguish the words of the song, and get transient glimpses of the singer, between the columns of the trees. He came nearer, dawning upon me like a growing thought. He was a knight, armed from head to heel, mounted upon a strange-looking beast, whose form I could not understand. The words which I heard him sing were like these:—

===Chinese===
我并没有走多远——才刚刚望不见那座可憎的高塔——便有一种另样的声音传了过来；随着树木的阻隔或放行，那声音忽远忽近。那是一个饱满、低沉、男子汉的声音，却又清亮悦耳。它时而骤然高涨，轰然灌进耳朵；时而又同样骤然消沉，仿佛隔着辽阔的空间飘到我这里。但它毕竟愈来愈近；终于，我能分辨出那歌中的词句，也能在一根根树干的立柱之间，瞥见歌者一闪而过的身影。他愈走愈近，像一个渐渐长大的念头，在我眼前如破晓般显露。那是一位骑士，从头到脚披挂着甲胄，骑在一头模样古怪的坐骑上，那牲口的形体我全然无法理解。我听见他唱的词句，大致如下：——

===Original===
> Heart be stout,

> And eye be true;

> Good blade out!

> And ill shall rue.

===Chinese===
> 心要坚定，

> 眼要真切；

> 好刀出鞘！

> 恶徒必有悔时。

===Original===
> Courage, horse!

> Thou lackst no skill;

> Well thy force

> Hath matched my will.

===Chinese===
> 鼓起劲来，马儿！

> 你不缺技艺；

> 你的气力

> 正与我心志相匹。

===Original===
> For the foe

> With fiery breath,

> At a blow,

> Is still in death.

===Chinese===
> 那仇敌

> 呼吐着火焰，

> 一击之下，

> 便僵卧死地。

===Original===
> Gently, horse!

> Tread fearlessly;

> ’Tis his corse

> That burdens thee.

===Chinese===
> 稳步，马儿！

> 放胆踏行；

> 那是他的尸身

> 压在你背上。

===Original===
> The sun’s eye

> Is fierce at noon;

> Thou and I

> Will rest full soon.

===Chinese===
> 太阳的眼睛

> 正午最烈；

> 你和我

> 很快就能歇息。

===Original===
> And new strength

> New work will meet;

> Till, at length,

> Long rest is sweet.

===Chinese===
> 新生的气力

> 自有新的事功相迎；

> 直到末了，

> 长眠方是甘美。
===Original===
And now horse and rider had arrived near enough for me to see, fastened by the long neck to the hinder part of the saddle, and trailing its hideous length on the ground behind, the body of a great dragon. It was no wonder that, with such a drag at his heels, the horse could make but slow progress, notwithstanding his evident dismay. The horrid, serpent-like head, with its black tongue, forked with red, hanging out of its jaws, dangled against the horse’s side. Its neck was covered with long blue hair, its sides with scales of green and gold. Its back was of corrugated skin, of a purple hue. Its belly was similar in nature, but its color was leaden, dashed with blotches of livid blue. Its skinny, bat-like wings and its tail were of a dull gray. It was strange to see how so many gorgeous colors, so many curving lines, and such beautiful things as wings and hair and scales, combined to form the horrible creature, intense in ugliness.

===Chinese===
这时人马已走近得让我看清了：一条巨龙的尸身，被长长的脖颈拴在马鞍后部，可怖的长躯拖曳在后面的地上。怪不得这马拖着这样一件重坠，尽管分明惊惶不安，也只能走得这样迟缓。那颗狰狞的蛇形头颅，黑舌头带着红色的分叉，耷拉在颌外，贴着马的肋侧晃来晃去。龙的脖颈覆着长长的蓝色毛发，体侧覆着绿金相间的鳞片。背部是皱缩的皮，呈紫色。腹部性质相仿，颜色却是铅灰，溅染着青黑的斑块。它那皮包骨的蝙蝠状双翼和尾巴是暗灰色。说来奇怪：那么多华美的颜色、那么多弯曲的线条、那么多美的事物——翅膀、毛发、鳞片——竟拼合成这样一个可怕的东西，丑得如此强烈刺目。

===Original===
The knight was passing me with a salutation; but, as I walked towards him, he reined up, and I stood by his stirrup. When I came near him, I saw, to my surprise and pleasure likewise, although a sudden pain, like a birth of fire, sprang up in my heart, that it was the knight of the soiled armor, whom I knew before, and whom I had seen in the vision, with the lady of the marble. But I could have thrown my arms around him, because she loved him. This discovery only strengthened the resolution I had formed, before I recognised him, of offering myself to the knight, to wait upon him as a squire, for he seemed to be unattended. I made my request in as few words as possible. He hesitated for a moment, and looked at me thoughtfully. I saw that he suspected who I was, but that he continued uncertain of his suspicion. No doubt he was soon convinced of its truth; but all the time I was with him, not a word crossed his lips with reference to what he evidently concluded I wished to leave unnoticed, if not to keep concealed.

===Chinese===
骑士向我行了个礼，径直走过；但我朝他走去，他便勒住了马，我便站在他的马镫旁。走近他时，我又惊又喜地看出——虽然一阵突如其来的疼痛，像一团火的诞生，从我心里窜起——这正是我从前认识的那个铠甲蒙尘的骑士（the knight of the soiled armor），我在异象中见过他，与大理石少女在一起。而正因为是她爱着他，我简直可以张开双臂去抱他。这一发现，只是让我在认出他之前便已定下的决心愈加坚定：我要投身到这位骑士门下，做他的侍从随侍左右，因为他似乎并无随员。我用尽可能简短的话说出了我的请求。他迟疑了片刻，若有所思地看着我。我看得出他疑心我是谁，却又拿不定这疑心。毫无疑问，他很快便确知了真相；但在我与他相处的全部时光里，对于那件他分明断定我但愿无人提起、甚至但愿深藏不露的事，他不曾吐露过一个字。

===Original===
“Squire and knight should be friends,” said he: “can you take me by the hand?” And he held out the great gauntleted right hand. I grasped it willingly and strongly. Not a word more was said. The knight gave the sign to his horse, which again began his slow march, and I walked beside and a little behind.

===Chinese===
「侍从与骑士应当为友，」他说，「你可愿意握住我的手？」说着伸出他那只戴着铁护手的大右手。我甘愿而用力地握住了它。再多的话，一句也没有。骑士向他的马做了个手势，马又开始了它缓慢的行进，我走在旁边，稍稍靠后一点。

===Original===
We had not gone very far before we arrived at a little cottage; from which, as we drew near, a woman rushed out with the cry:

“My child! my child! have you found my child?”

“I have found her,” replied the knight, “but she is sorely hurt. I was forced to leave her with the hermit, as I returned. You will find her there, and I think she will get better. You see I have brought you a present. This wretch will not hurt you again.” And he undid the creature’s neck, and flung the frightful burden down by the cottage door.

===Chinese===
我们没走多远，便来到一座小小的农舍；我们刚一走近，一个女人从里面冲了出来，喊道：

「我的孩子！我的孩子！你们找到我的孩子了吗？」

「我找到她了，」骑士答道，「可她伤得很重。我回来时，不得不把她留在隐士那里。你们到那儿便能找到她，我想她会好起来的。你们看，我给你们带回了一件礼物。这孽畜再也不能伤害你们了。」说着他解开拴着龙颈的绳结，把那骇人的重负抛在农舍门前。

===Original===
The woman was now almost out of sight in the wood; but the husband stood at the door, with speechless thanks in his face.

“You must bury the monster,” said the knight. “If I had arrived a moment later, I should have been too late. But now you need not fear, for such a creature as this very rarely appears, in the same part, twice during a lifetime.”

“Will you not dismount and rest you, Sir Knight?” said the peasant, who had, by this time, recovered himself a little.

“That I will, thankfully,” said he; and, dismounting, he gave the reins to me, and told me to unbridle the horse, and lead him into the shade. “You need not tie him up,” he added; “he will not run away.”

===Chinese===
那女人这时已在林中跑得几乎望不见了；她丈夫站在门口，满脸都是说不出的感激。

「你们得把这恶兽埋掉，」骑士说。「我若再晚到片刻，就赶不及了。不过如今你们不必害怕了，因为这样的孽物极少在同一带地方、一生之内出现两回。」

「骑士先生可愿下马来歇息歇息？」那农人说；这时他已稍稍定了神。

「愿意，多谢了，」他说着下得马来，把缰绳交给我，叫我卸下马的嚼子，牵它到荫凉处。「不必把它拴住，」他又说，「它不会跑掉的。」

===Original===
When I returned, after obeying his orders, and entered the cottage, I saw the knight seated, without his helmet, and talking most familiarly with the simple host. I stood at the open door for a moment, and, gazing at him, inwardly justified the white lady in preferring him to me. A nobler countenance I never saw. Loving-kindness beamed from every line of his face. It seemed as if he would repay himself for the late arduous combat, by indulging in all the gentleness of a womanly heart. But when the talk ceased for a moment, he seemed to fall into a reverie. Then the exquisite curves of the upper lip vanished. The lip was lengthened and compressed at the same moment. You could have told that, within the lips, the teeth were firmly closed. The whole face grew stern and determined, all but fierce; only the eyes burned on like a holy sacrifice, uplift on a granite rock.

===Chinese===
我办完他吩咐的事回来，走进农舍，只见骑士已经坐下，摘了头盔，正同那位质朴的主人熟不拘礼地交谈。我在敞开的门口站了片刻，凝望着他，心里暗自承认：白衣女郎舍我而择他，是择得对的。这样高贵的面容，我从未见过。仁爱与亲切从他脸的每一道纹路里放射出来。仿佛他要以一个女人的心的全部温柔来纵情一番，好补报方才那场艰苦的搏斗。但谈话一停，他仿佛便坠入了沉思。于是上唇那精美的曲线消失了。嘴唇在同一刻变得修长而抿紧。你可以看出，唇齿之间，牙关紧紧咬闭。整张脸变得严峻而果决，几乎近于凶猛；唯有那双眼睛仍在灼灼燃烧，像一场供举在花岗岩上的圣祭。

===Original===
The woman entered, with her mangled child in her arms. She was pale as her little burden. She gazed, with a wild love and despairing tenderness, on the still, all but dead face, white and clear from loss of blood and terror.

The knight rose. The light that had been confined to his eyes, now shone from his whole countenance. He took the little thing in his arms, and, with the mother’s help, undressed her, and looked to her wounds. The tears flowed down his face as he did so. With tender hands he bound them up, kissed the pale cheek, and gave her back to her mother. When he went home, all his tale would be of the grief and joy of the parents; while to me, who had looked on, the gracious countenance of the armed man, beaming from the panoply of steel, over the seemingly dead child, while the powerful hands turned it and shifted it, and bound it, if possible even more gently than the mother’s, formed the center of the story.

===Chinese===
那女人抱着血肉模糊的孩子走了进来。她脸色同她怀中的小负担一样苍白。她怀着一种近乎疯狂的爱与绝望的温柔，凝望着那张静静的、几乎已死去的小脸——失血与惊恐使它白得透明。

骑士站起身来。原本只聚在他眼中那道光，此刻从他整张脸上照耀出来。他把小家伙抱在怀里，在母亲的帮助下解开她的衣裳，查看她的伤口。他这样做时，泪水顺着脸颊流下。他用温柔的手替她包扎，吻了吻那苍白的小脸，把她交还给母亲。他回家之后，讲起来必定全是那对父母的悲与喜；而在一旁目睹的我，这故事的核心却是那位披甲之人仁慈的面容——它从钢铁的全副铠甲中焕发光辉，俯照着那仿佛已死去的孩子；那双有力的手翻动她、挪移她、包扎她，若说有什么不同，竟比母亲的手还要温柔。

===Original===
After we had partaken of the best they could give us, the knight took his leave, with a few parting instructions to the mother as to how she should treat the child.

I brought the knight his steed, held the stirrup while he mounted, and then followed him through the wood. The horse, delighted to be free of his hideous load, bounded beneath the weight of man and armor, and could hardly be restrained from galloping on. But the knight made him time his powers to mine, and so we went on for an hour or two. Then the knight dismounted, and compelled me to get into the saddle, saying: “Knight and squire must share the labor.”

===Chinese===
我们吃过他们倾其所有拿出来的食物之后，骑士便起身告辞，临行又嘱咐了那母亲几句，该如何照料孩子。

我把骑士的坐骑牵来，扶镫待他上马，随后跟着他穿过树林。那马摆脱了那副可憎的重载，欢喜雀跃，驮着人与甲胄的重量在下面腾跃，几乎勒不住它奔驰的性子。但骑士叫它把气力合着我的脚步来使，于是我们这样又走了一两个钟头。其后骑士下马来，定要我跨上马鞍，说道：「骑士与侍从须得同担劳苦。」

===Original===
Holding by the stirrup, he walked along by my side, heavily clad as he was, with apparent ease. As we went, he led a conversation, in which I took what humble part my sense of my condition would permit me.

“Somehow or other,” said he, “notwithstanding the beauty of this country of Faerie, in which we are, there is much that is wrong in it. If there are great splendors, there are corresponding horrors; heights and depths; beautiful women and awful fiends; noble men and weaklings. All a man has to do, is to better what he can. And if he will settle it with himself, that even renown and success are in themselves of no great value, and be content to be defeated, if so be that the fault is not his; and so go to his work with a cool brain and a strong will, he will get it done; and fare none the worse in the end, that he was not burdened with provision and precaution.”

===Chinese===
他扶着马镫，在我身旁步行；虽然甲胄沉重在身，却显得毫不费力。一路上，是他引着谈话；我则怀着对自身处境的自知，尽我所能谦卑地陪谈几句。

「不知怎的，」他说，「尽管我们身处的这个仙国何等美丽，其中却有许多不对的东西。有极大的壮丽，便有与之相应的可怖；有高峰，便有深渊；有美丽的女人，便有骇人的妖魔；有高贵的人，便有懦弱的庸才。一个人所要做的，只是尽其所能把事情改良。倘若他肯同自己讲定：连声名与成功本身也并无多大价值，并且甘心于失败——只要那过错不在自己——然后头脑冷静、意志坚强地去做他的工作，他便能做成；而且到头来，境况绝不至于更坏，正因为他不曾背着盘算与顾虑的重负。」

===Original===
“But he will not always come off well,” I ventured to say.

“Perhaps not,” rejoined the knight, “in the individual act; but the result of his lifetime will content him.”

“So it will fare with you, doubtless,” thought I; “but for me—”

Venturing to resume the conversation after a pause, I said, hesitatingly:

===Chinese===
「可他未必回回都能全身而退，」我壮着胆子说。

「或许不能，」骑士答道，「就单独一事而言；但他一生的结果，会使他自己满足。」

「你无疑会有这样的结果，」我心里想，「可对我——」

歇了片刻，我大着胆子重新拾起话头，迟疑地说：
===Original===
“May I ask for what the little beggar-girl wanted your aid, when she came to your castle to find you?”

He looked at me for a moment in silence, and then said:—

===Chinese===
「我可否问一问，那小小的乞儿到您的城堡来找您时，求您帮的是什么忙？」

他默默看了我片刻，然后说道：——

===Original===
“I cannot help wondering how you know of that; but there is something about you quite strange enough to entitle you to the privilege of the country; namely, to go unquestioned. I, however, being only a man, such as you see me, am ready to tell you anything you like to ask me, as far as I can. The little beggar-girl came into the hall where I was sitting, and told me a very curious story, which I can only recollect very vaguely, it was so peculiar. What I can recall is, that she was sent to gather wings. As soon as she had gathered a pair of wings for herself, she was to fly away, she said, to the country she came from; but where that was, she could give no information. She said she had to beg her wings from the butterflies and moths; and whenever she begged, no one refused her. But she needed a great many of the wings of butterflies and moths to make a pair for her; and so she had to wander about day after day, looking for butterflies, and night after night, looking for moths; and then she begged for their wings. But the day before, she had come into a part of the forest, she said, where there were multitudes of splendid butterflies flitting about, with wings which were just fit to make the eyes in the shoulders of hers; and she knew she could have as many of them as she liked for the asking; but as soon as she began to beg, there came a great creature right up to her, and threw her down, and walked over her. When she got up, she saw the wood was full of these beings stalking about, and seeming to have nothing to do with each other. As soon as ever she began to beg, one of them walked over her; till at last, in dismay, and in growing horror of the senseless creatures, she had run away to look for somebody to help her. I asked her what they were like. She said, like great men, made of wood, without knee or elbow-joints, and without any noses or mouths or eyes in their faces. I laughed at the little maiden, thinking she was making child’s game of me; but, although she burst out laughing too, she persisted in asserting the truth of her story.”

===Chinese===
「我正纳闷你怎么会知道这件事；不过你身上有一种十足的古怪，足够让你享有这个国度的特权——即通行无阻，不受盘问。然而我不过是一个人，如你所见，凡你爱问什么，我必尽我所能告诉你。那小小的乞儿走进我坐着的大厅，给我讲了一个非常古怪的故事，讲得太奇特了，我只能极其模糊地记起。我还记得的是：她是被打发来收集翅膀的。她说，一旦给自己凑成一对翅膀，她就要飞回她来的那个国度去；但那国度在哪里，她却说不上来。她说她得向蝴蝶和飞蛾乞讨翅膀；而她每逢开口，从没有谁拒绝她。可是要给她拼成一对翅膀，需要许许多多蝴蝶和飞蛾的翅膀；于是她只好日复一日地游荡，白天找蝴蝶，夜复一夜地找飞蛾；找到了，就乞求它们的翅膀。可就在前一天，她说她走进了森林中的一处，那里有无数绚烂的蝴蝶翩跹飞舞，它们的翅膀恰好可以配作她那对翅膀肩上的眼斑；她知道只要开口，要多少就有多少；但她刚一开始乞求，一个巨大的家伙径直朝她走来，把她撞翻在地，从她身上踏了过去。她爬起来，看见树林里到处都是这样的家伙，大摇大摆地走动着，彼此似乎互不相干。她刚一开始乞求，它们中间就有一个从她身上踏过去；到末了，她惊惶失措，对这群无知无觉的东西愈来愈恐惧，便逃出来寻找什么人搭救她。我问她那些东西是什么样子。她说，像木头做的大个子，没有膝关节也没有肘关节，脸上没有鼻子，没有嘴，也没有眼睛。我笑那小姑娘，以为她在拿小孩子的把戏哄我；可是虽然她也放声大笑，却始终坚持她的故事千真万确。」

===Original===
“ ‘Only come, knight, come and see; I will lead you.’

“So I armed myself, to be ready for anything that might happen, and followed the child; for, though I could make nothing of her story, I could see she was a little human being in need of some help or other. As she walked before me, I looked attentively at her. Whether or not it was from being so often knocked down and walked over, I could not tell, but her clothes were very much torn, and in several places her white skin was peeping through. I thought she was humpbacked; but on looking more closely, I saw, through the tatters of her frock—do not laugh at me—a bunch on each shoulder, of the most gorgeous colors. Looking yet more closely, I saw that they were of the shape of folded wings, and were made of all kinds of butterfly-wings and moth-wings, crowded together like the feathers on the individual butterfly pinion; but, like them, most beautifully arranged, and producing a perfect harmony of color and shade. I could now more easily believe the rest of her story; especially as I saw, every now and then, a certain heaving motion in the wings, as if they longed to be uplifted and outspread. But beneath her scanty garments complete wings could not be concealed, and indeed, from her own story, they were yet unfinished.

===Chinese===
「『骑士，只管来吧，来看看；我给你带路。』

「于是我披挂整齐，以防任何不测，跟着那孩子去了；因为她的故事我虽然全然听不明白，却看得出她是一个需要人搭救的小小的人。她走在我前面，我留神端详她。不知是不是因为被撞翻、被践踏得太多，她的衣裳破得厉害，好几处都露出了雪白的皮肤。我当她是个驼背；可再凑近一看，我看见——你且别笑我——透过她连衣裙的破片，两边肩头各鼓着一团色彩最为绚烂的东西。再细看下去，我看出了那是一对收拢的翅膀的形状，是用种种蝴蝶翅膀和飞蛾翅膀拼成的，密密地攒聚在一起，就像一只蝴蝶翅膀上那些细羽拥挤排列的样子；而且同它们一样，安排得极美，色彩与浓淡相配得十分和谐。这下我便更容易相信她故事的其余部分了；尤其是我不时看见那对翅膀有一种起伏的涌动，仿佛渴望着振举开来。可她那点单薄的衣裳底下，遮不住完整的翅膀；的确，照她自己讲的，翅膀也还没有长成。

===Original===
“After walking for two or three hours (how the little girl found her way, I could not imagine), we came to a part of the forest, the very air of which was quivering with the motions of multitudes of resplendent butterflies; as gorgeous in color, as if the eyes of peacocks’ feathers had taken to flight, but of infinite variety of hue and form, only that the appearance of some kind of eye on each wing predominated. ‘There they are, there they are!’ cried the child, in a tone of victory mingled with terror. Except for this tone, I should have thought she referred to the butterflies, for I could see nothing else. But at that moment an enormous butterfly, whose wings had great eyes of blue surrounded by confused cloudy heaps of more dingy coloring, just like a break in the clouds on a stormy day towards evening, settled near us. The child instantly began murmuring: ‘Butterfly, butterfly, give me your wings;’ when, the moment after, she fell to the ground, and began crying as if hurt. I drew my sword and heaved a great blow in the direction in which the child had fallen. It struck something, and instantly the most grotesque imitation of a man became visible. You see this Fairy Land is full of oddities and all sorts of incredibly ridiculous things, which a man is compelled to meet and treat as real existences, although all the time he feels foolish for doing so. This being, if being it could be called, was like a block of wood roughly hewn into the mere outlines of a man; and hardly so, for it had but head, body, legs, and arms—the head without a face, and the limbs utterly formless. I had hewn off one of its legs, but the two portions moved on as best they could, quite independent of each other; so that I had done no good. I ran after it, and clove it in twain from the head downwards; but it could not be convinced that its vocation was not to walk over people; for, as soon as the little girl began her begging again, all three parts came bustling up; and if I had not interposed my weight between her and them, she would have been trampled again under them. I saw that something else must be done. If the wood was full of the creatures, it would be an endless work to chop them so small that they could do no injury; and then, besides, the parts would be so numerous, that the butterflies would be in danger from the drift of flying chips. I served this one so, however; and then told the girl to beg again, and point out the direction in which one was coming. I was glad to find, however, that I could now see him myself, and wondered how they could have been invisible before. I would not allow him to walk over the child; but while I kept him off, and she began begging again, another appeared; and it was all I could do, from the weight of my armor, to protect her from the stupid, persevering efforts of the two. But suddenly the right plan occurred to me. I tripped one of them up, and, taking him by the legs, set him up on his head, with his heels against a tree. I was delighted to find he could not move. Meantime the poor child was walked over by the other, but it was for the last time. Whenever one appeared, I followed the same plan—tripped him up and set him on his head; and so the little beggar was able to gather her wings without any trouble, which occupation she continued for several hours in my company.”

===Chinese===
「我们走了两三个钟头（那小女孩是怎么认路的，我想象不出），来到森林中的一处，那里的空气都因无数光辉灿烂的蝴蝶的翻飞而颤动；它们色彩之绚烂，就像孔雀翎上的眼斑都腾空飞了起来，只是色彩与形状千变万化，唯一占上风的，是每片翅膀上都有一种眼斑模样的东西。『就在那儿，就在那儿！』那孩子喊道，声音里胜利与恐惧混在一起。若不是这声调，我准以为她指的是那些蝴蝶，因为我别的一概看不见。可就在这时，一只巨大的蝴蝶在我们近旁落了下来，它的翅膀上生着蓝色的大眼斑，四围绕着一团团昏混沌沌的暗色，活像雷雨黄昏时天上云层裂开的一道豁口。那孩子立刻喃喃起来：『蝴蝶，蝴蝶，把你的翅膀给我吧；』话音刚落，她就倒在了地上，哭喊起来，像是受了伤。我拔出剑，朝孩子倒下的方向狠狠劈了一记。剑砍中了什么东西，霎时间，一个对人的最怪诞的模拟物显出了形。你要知道，这仙境满是稀奇古怪、令人难以置信的荒唐东西，人不得不与它们照面，把它们当作真实的存在来对待，虽然一边这样做，一边总觉得自己愚蠢。这东西——倘若还称得上东西的话——像一截木头，粗粗砍出一个人的轮廓；连轮廓也谈不上，因为它只有头、身、腿、臂——头上没有脸，四肢全然不成形状。我劈掉了它一条腿，可那两截身子各自竭力往前挪动，全然互不相干；我这一下等于白费。我追上去，把它从头顶往下劈成两半；可它怎么也不肯相信它的天职并不是从人身上踏过去；因为那小姑娘刚一重新开口乞求，那三截东西就都忙忙乱乱地凑上前来；我若不是以我全身的重量挡在她和它们中间，她又要被它们踩在底下了。我看清了必须另想办法。倘若林子里满是这种东西，要把它们砍剁到再不能害人的地步，那是没完没了的活计；况且剁下来的碎块那么多，飞溅的木片会使蝴蝶陷于危险。不过这一只，我到底还是照这样处置了它；随后我叫姑娘重新乞求，并指出下一个是从哪个方向来的。不过叫我高兴的是，这回我自己也看得见它了，我心里奇怪先前它们怎么会看不见。我不许它从孩子身上踏过去；可我挡着它、她重新乞求的时候，另一个又出现了；凭我甲胄的重量，我才勉强护住她，不让那两个蠢物那股死心塌地的劲头得逞。可忽然间，正当的法子在我心里一亮。我把其中一个绊倒，揪住它的腿，让它头朝下倒竖起来，两脚跟抵着一棵树。我发现它动弹不得，不由大喜。这当儿，那可怜的孩子被另一个从身上踏了过去，但那是最后一次了。此后每出现一个，我就照这法子办——绊倒它，把它头朝下竖起来；于是那小小的乞儿便毫无阻碍地收集起她的翅膀来，这营生她在我陪伴之下，又做了好几个钟头。」

===Original===
“What became of her?” I asked.

“I took her home with me to my castle, and she told me all her story; but it seemed to me, all the time, as if I were hearing a child talk in its sleep. I could not arrange her story in my mind at all, although it seemed to leave hers in some certain order of its own. My wife—”

Here the knight checked himself, and said no more. Neither did I urge the conversation farther.

Thus we journeyed for several days, resting at night in such shelter as we could get; and when no better was to be had, lying in the forest under some tree, on a couch of old leaves.

===Chinese===
「『她后来怎么样了？』我问。」

「『我把她带回家，带回我的城堡，她把她的故事原原本本讲给我听；可我一直觉得，仿佛在听一个孩子说梦话。她的故事在我心里怎么也理不出头绪，虽然它自己倒像有一定的次序。我的妻子——』」

说到这里，骑士忽然收住，不再往下说了。我也没有把话头再往前引。

我们就这样赶了几天的路，夜里有什么遮蔽便在什么里面歇宿；寻不着更好的去处时，便躺在林中哪棵树底下，以陈年的落叶为床。

===Original===
I loved the knight more and more. I believe never squire served his master with more care and joyfulness than I. I tended his horse; I cleaned his armor; my skill in the craft enabled me to repair it when necessary; I watched his needs; and was well repaid for all by the love itself which I bore him.

“This,” I said to myself, “is a true man. I will serve him, and give him all worship, seeing in him the embodiment of what I would fain become. If I cannot be noble myself, I will yet be servant to his nobleness.” He, in return, soon showed me such signs of friendship and respect, as made my heart glad; and I felt that, after all, mine would be no lost life, if I might wait on him to the world’s end, although no smile but his should greet me, and no one but him should say, “Well done! he was a good servant!” at last. But I burned to do something more for him than the ordinary routine of a squire’s duty permitted.

===Chinese===
我愈来愈爱这位骑士。我相信从来没有一个侍从，比我更尽心、更欢喜地侍奉过他的主上。我照料他的马；我擦拭他的甲胄；我凭着一手匠人的技艺，必要时还能把它修补；我留意他的一切所需；而这一切所得的报酬，便是我对他怀有的那份爱本身，这已经足够丰厚了。

「这，」我对自己说，「才是一个真正的人。我要侍奉他，向他献上全部的敬爱，在他身上看见我巴不得自己能成为的那种人的化身。我若不能自己成为高贵的人，我也要做他的高贵之气的仆人。」而他那边，也很快向我显出友情与敬重的种种表示，使我心头欢喜；我便觉得，归根到底，我这一生并不算虚度，倘若我能伺候他直到世界的尽头，哪怕迎接我的只有他的微笑，哪怕到头来只有他一个人说一句：「做得好！他是个忠仆！」但我不满足于侍从职责的日常本分，我渴望为他做更多的事。

===Original===
One afternoon, we began to observe an appearance of roads in the wood. Branches had been cut down, and openings made, where footsteps had worn no path below. These indications increased as we passed on, till, at length, we came into a long, narrow avenue, formed by felling the trees in its line, as the remaining roots evidenced. At some little distance, on both hands, we observed signs of similar avenues, which appeared to converge with ours, towards one spot. Along these we indistinctly saw several forms moving, which seemed, with ourselves, to approach the common center. Our path brought us, at last, up to a wall of yew-trees, growing close together, and intertwining their branches so, that nothing could be seen beyond it. An opening was cut in it like a door, and all the wall was trimmed smooth and perpendicular. The knight dismounted, and waited till I had provided for his horse’s comfort; upon which we entered the place together.

===Chinese===
一天下午，我们开始察觉林中显出了道路的迹象。一些树枝被砍倒了，在脚下本没有行人踏出路径的地方辟出了开口。我们越往前走，这类迹象越多，末了终于走进一条狭长的甬道，是沿着一条直线砍倒树木辟成的——留在地上的树根可以为证。稍远处，左右两边都有类似甬道的痕迹，似乎与我们所走的这一条会合，通向同一个地点。沿着那些甬道，我们影影绰绰看见几个身影在移动，仿佛同我们一样，正向那个共同的中心靠近。我们的路终于把我们引到一堵紫杉树的墙前；那些树长得紧紧相接，枝桠交错，墙外什么也望不见。墙上开着一个切口，像一扇门；整面墙都修剪得平整而垂直。骑士下了马，等我安顿好他的马，我们便一同走了进去。
===Original===
It was a great space, bare of trees, and enclosed by four walls of yew, similar to that through which we had entered. These trees grew to a very great height, and did not divide from each other till close to the top, where their summits formed a row of conical battlements all around the walls. The space contained was a parallelogram of great length. Along each of the two longer sides of the interior, were ranged three ranks of men, in white robes, standing silent and solemn, each with a sword by his side, although the rest of his costume and bearing was more priestly than soldierly. For some distance inwards, the space between these opposite rows was filled with a company of men and women and children, in holiday attire. The looks of all were directed inwards, towards the further end. Far beyond the crowd, in a long avenue, seeming to narrow in the distance, went the long rows of the white-robed men. On what the attention of the multitude was fixed, we could not tell, for the sun had set before we arrived, and it was growing dark within. It grew darker and darker. The multitude waited in silence. The stars began to shine down into the enclosure, and they grew brighter and larger every moment. A wind arose, and swayed the pinnacles of the treetops; and made a strange sound, half like music, half like moaning, through the close branches and leaves of the tree-walls. A young girl who stood beside me, clothed in the same dress as the priests, bowed her head, and grew pale with awe.

===Chinese===
那是一大片空场，不长树木，四面围着紫杉的墙，与我们进来的那一面相同。这些树长得极高，直到近顶处才彼此分开，树梢在墙头四周形成一排圆锥形的雉堞。墙内的地方是一片狭长的长方形。沿着内部两条长边，各列着三排身穿白袍的人，肃立无声而庄严，个个腰间佩着剑，虽然装束与仪态的其余部分，更像祭司，不像军人。往里去，隔着一段距离，这两列相对的队伍之间，挤满了盛装的人群——有男人，有女人，也有孩子。所有人的目光都朝向里面，望向远处那一端。人群再往远处，一条长长的夹道望去似乎渐渐收窄，两旁是白袍人的长列。众人的注意力究竟凝注在什么上面，我们无从知道，因为太阳在我们到达之前就落了，里面愈来愈暗。愈来愈暗了。人群默默地等待着。星星开始照进这片围场，一刻比一刻更亮、更大。起风了，摇撼着树梢的峰尖；穿过密合的枝叶组成的树墙，发出一种奇怪的声音，一半像音乐，一半像呻吟。站在我身旁的一个年轻姑娘，穿着同祭司一样的衣服，低下头去，因敬畏而变得苍白。

===Original===
The knight whispered to me, “How solemn it is! Surely they wait to hear the voice of a prophet. There is something good near!”

But I, though somewhat shaken by the feeling expressed by my master, yet had an unaccountable conviction that here was something bad. So I resolved to be keenly on the watch for what should follow.

===Chinese===
骑士对我耳语道：「何等庄严啊！他们一定是在等候聆听一位先知的声音。附近有什么美好的事！」

但我虽然被我主上所表露的情绪动摇了几分，心里却有一种无从解释的确信：这里面的东西是邪恶的。于是我决意打起全副精神，紧盯接下来的一切。

===Original===
