Suddenly a great star, like a sun, appeared high in the air over the temple, illuminating it throughout; and a great song arose from the men in white, which went rolling round and round the building, now receding to the end, and now approaching, down the other side, the place where we stood. For some of the singers were regularly ceasing, and the next to them as regularly taking up the song, so that it crept onwards with gradations produced by changes which could not themselves be detected, for only a few of those who were singing ceased at the same moment. The song paused; and I saw a company of six of the white-robed men walk up the center of the human avenue, surrounding a youth gorgeously attired beneath his robe of white, and wearing a chaplet of flowers on his head. I followed them closely, with my keenest observation; and, by accompanying their slow progress with my eyes, I was able to perceive more clearly what took place when they arrived at the other end. I knew that my sight was so much more keen than that of most people, that I had good reason to suppose I should see more than the rest could, at such a distance. At the farther end a throne stood upon a platform, high above the heads of the surrounding priests. To this platform I saw the company begin to ascend, apparently by an inclined plane or gentle slope. The throne itself was elevated again, on a kind of square pedestal, to the top of which led a flight of steps. On the throne sat a majestic-looking figure, whose posture seemed to indicate a mixture of pride and benignity, as he looked down on the multitude below. The company ascended to the foot of the throne, where they all kneeled for some minutes; then they rose and passed round to the side of the pedestal upon which the throne stood. Here they crowded close behind the youth, putting him in the foremost place; and one of them opened a door in the pedestal, for the youth to enter. I was sure I saw him shrink back, and those crowding behind push him in. Then, again, arose a burst of song from the multitude in white, which lasted some time. When it ceased, a new company of seven commenced its march up the center. As they advanced, I looked up at my master: his noble countenance was full of reverence and awe. Incapable of evil himself, he could scarcely suspect it in another, much less in a multitude such as this, and surrounded with such appearances of solemnity. I was certain it was the really grand accompaniments that overcame him; that the stars overhead, the dark towering tops of the yew-trees, and the wind that, like an unseen spirit, sighed through their branches, bowed his spirit to the belief, that in all these ceremonies lay some great mystical meaning, which, his humility told him, his ignorance prevented him from understanding.

===Chinese===
忽然间，一颗大星，像一轮太阳，出现在圣殿上空的高处，把它整个照彻；白袍人中升起一支宏大的歌，绕着这座建筑滚滚回旋，一时退向远端，一时又沿另一侧回到我们站立的地方。因为歌者中总有一些人按着次序停下，紧挨着他们的人便按着次序接续唱起，于是那歌便一级一级向前爬行，造成这种渐变的那些交替本身却察觉不出，因为同一次停下的只是歌者中的少数几个。歌声停顿；我看见六个白袍人组成的一队，沿着人夹道的中央走来，围拥着一个青年——白袍底下盛装华彩，头上戴着一只花冠。我目不转睛地紧盯着他们，用我最锐利的观察；眼睛追着他们缓缓的行进，他们走到另一端时发生的事，我便能看得更清楚些。我知道我的视力比大多数人敏锐得多，我有充分的理由认为，隔着这样远的距离，我看到的会比旁人多。在远端，一座王座立在高台之上，高出四围祭司们的头顶。我看见那一队人开始登上这座高台，显然是循着一道斜面或平缓的坡道。王座本身又高起在一座方形的座基上，有一级台阶通向座基顶上。王座上坐着一个仪表威严的形象，他俯视底下的众人时，那姿态仿佛透出骄傲与慈和的混合。那一队人登上王座脚下，在那里全体跪了几分钟；然后起身，绕到王座所立的座基侧面。在这里，他们紧紧挤在那青年背后，把他推在最前；其中一个打开座基上的一扇门，让青年进去。我确信我看见他往后退缩，而挤在后面的人把他推了进去。接着，白袍的人群中再度爆发出一阵歌声，持续了好一阵。歌声止息后，新的一队七个人开始沿着中央行进。他们向前走时，我抬头看我主上：他高贵的面容满是崇敬与畏慑。他自己不可能作恶，便几乎不能疑心别人作恶，更不会疑心这样一大群人、又被这样庄严的表象围拱着的一群人。我确信，正是那真正宏大的排场征服了他；头顶的星辰、紫杉黑黢黢高耸的树梢、还有那像一位无形精灵般在枝叶间叹息的风，把他的心灵压服得相信：这一切仪式之中必藏着某种伟大的神秘意义，而他的谦卑告诉他，他的无知使他无法领会。

===Original===
More convinced than before, that there was evil here, I could not endure that my master should be deceived; that one like him, so pure and noble, should respect what, if my suspicions were true, was worse than the ordinary deceptions of priestcraft. I could not tell how far he might be led to countenance, and otherwise support their doings, before he should find cause to repent bitterly of his error. I watched the new procession yet more keenly, if possible, than the former. This time, the central figure was a girl; and, at the close, I observed, yet more indubitably, the shrinking back, and the crowding push. What happened to the victims, I never learned; but I had learned enough, and I could bear it no longer. I stooped, and whispered to the young girl who stood by me, to lend me her white garment. I wanted it, that I might not be entirely out of keeping with the solemnity, but might have at least this help to passing unquestioned. She looked up, half-amused and half-bewildered, as if doubting whether I was in earnest or not. But in her perplexity, she permitted me to unfasten it, and slip it down from her shoulders. I easily got possession of it; and, sinking down on my knees in the crowd, I rose apparently in the habit of one of the worshippers.

===Chinese===
我比先前更确信这里面有邪恶；我不能忍受我主上受欺骗，不能忍受像他这样纯洁高贵的人，去敬重那种东西——倘若我的疑心是真的，它比祭司们的寻常骗术还要恶劣。我说不准，在找到痛悔自己错误的因由之前，他会被引到多远，去纵容、去以别的方式赞助他们的勾当。我注视新的那一列队伍，比先前那一列盯得更紧——如果可能的话。这一次，中央的人物是一个女孩；到末了，我看得分明得无可再分明：那退缩，那拥挤着的一推。那些牺牲者后来遭遇了什么，我始终无从得知；但我已经知道得够多了，再也不能忍受。我弯下腰，悄声向站在我身旁的那年轻姑娘借她的白袍。我要它，是为了不至于同这场庄严全然格格不入，至少借它之助，可以不受盘问地走过去。她抬起头，半觉有趣，半觉茫然，疑心我是不是当真。但在她的惶惑中，她容许我解开袍子，从她肩上褪了下来。我不难把它拿到手；在人群中屈膝蹲下身去，再站起来时，我身上显然已是一个礼拜者的装束。

===Original===
Giving my battle-axe to the girl, to hold in pledge for the return of her stole, for I wished to test the matter unarmed, and, if it was a man that sat upon the throne, to attack him with hands bare, as I supposed his must be, I made my way through the crowd to the front, while the singing yet continued, desirous of reaching the platform while it was unoccupied by any of the priests. I was permitted to walk up the long avenue of white robes unmolested, though I saw questioning looks in many of the faces as I passed. I presume my coolness aided my passage; for I felt quite indifferent as to my own fate; not feeling, after the late events of my history, that I was at all worth taking care of; and enjoying, perhaps, something of an evil satisfaction, in the revenge I was thus taking upon the self which had fooled me so long. When I arrived on the platform, the song had just ceased, and I felt as if all were looking towards me. But instead of kneeling at its foot, I walked right up the stairs to the throne, laid hold of a great wooden image that seemed to sit upon it, and tried to hurl it from its seat. In this I failed at first, for I found it firmly fixed. But in dread lest, the first shock of amazement passing away, the guards would rush upon me before I had effected my purpose, I strained with all my might; and, with a noise as of the cracking, and breaking, and tearing of rotten wood, something gave way, and I hurled the image down the steps. Its displacement revealed a great hole in the throne, like the hollow of a decayed tree, going down apparently a great way. But I had no time to examine it, for, as I looked into it, up out of it rushed a great brute, like a wolf, but twice the size, and tumbled me headlong with itself, down the steps of the throne. As we fell, however, I caught it by the throat, and the moment we reached the platform, a struggle commenced, in which I soon got uppermost, with my hand upon its throat, and knee upon its heart. But now arose a wild cry of wrath and revenge and rescue. A universal hiss of steel, as every sword was swept from its scabbard, seemed to tear the very air in shreds. I heard the rush of hundreds towards the platform on which I knelt. I only tightened my grasp of the brute’s throat. His eyes were already starting from his head, and his tongue was hanging out. My anxious hope was, that, even after they had killed me, they would be unable to undo my grip of his throat, before the monster was past breathing. I therefore threw all my will, and force, and purpose, into the grasping hand. I remember no blow. A faintness came over me, and my consciousness departed.

===Chinese===
我把战斧交给那姑娘，作为归还她白袍的信物押在她手里，因为我想不拿兵器来试一试这件事；倘若坐在王座上的是个人，我就赤手空拳向他攻击——料想他也必然赤手。歌唱还未停止，我便从人群中挤向前去，想趁高台上还没有祭司的时候赶到那里。人们容许我沿着白袍的长夹道一直走上去，无人阻拦，虽然我走过时，看见许多脸上带着探究的神色。想必是我的镇静成全了我的通行；因为我对自己命运的如何，全然无所谓了——经过我经历中近来的种种变故，我已不觉得自己值得谁去照管；也许还怀着几分邪恶的快意——我这是在向那个愚弄了我这么久的自我复仇。我登上高台时，歌声刚刚止息，我觉得仿佛所有人都朝我望过来。但我没有在台下屈膝，径直踏上台阶，走到王座前，一把抓住那个仿佛端坐其上的巨大木像，想把它从座位上掀下去。起初我没有成功，因为它固定得很牢。但我满心恐惧，怕头一阵惊愕过去之后，卫队会在我得手之前一拥而上；我便用尽全力一挣，随着一阵腐木开裂、断裂、撕碎般的声响，有什么东西松脱了，我把木像摔下台阶去。它一挪开，王座上便露出一个大洞，像朽树的中空，看来深得很。但我已没有时间查看，因为我正朝里面张望，一头巨兽从中直窜出来，像狼，却有狼的两倍大，把我也带得一头栽倒，一同滚下王座的台阶。然而就在跌落时，我扼住了它的咽喉；我们一落到台上，搏斗便开始了，不久我便占了上风，一手掐着它的喉咙，一膝抵住它的心口。可这时四面响起了狂野的呼喊——愤怒、报复、营救。钢铁齐鸣的嘶响扫过，每柄剑都出了鞘，仿佛把空气也要撕成碎片。我听见数百人向我跪着的高台涌来。我只是把兽喉掐得更紧。它的眼珠已经从眼眶里凸出来，舌头也耷拉在外面。我焦灼的指望是：纵然他们杀了我，在那怪物断气之前，他们也解不开我掐住它喉咙的手。于是我把全部的意志、气力与决心，都灌注进那只扼握着的手里。挨没挨打，我不记得了。一阵昏晕漫上来，我的知觉便离我而去了。

## XXIV / 第二十四章

===Original===
> We are ne’er like angels till our passions die.

> This wretched *Inn*, where we scarce stay to bait,

 We call our *Dwelling-Place*:

 We call one *Step* a *Race*:

 But angels in their full enlightened state,

 Angels, who *Live*, and know what ’tis to *Be*,

 Who all the nonsense of our language see,

 Who speak *things*, and our *words*, their ill-drawn *pictures*, scorn,

 When we, by a foolish figure, say,

 *Behold an old man dead!* then they

 Speak properly, and cry, *Behold a man-child born!*

===Chinese===
> 激情不灭，我们终不似天使。

> 这凄凉的*客栈*，我们连歇脚打尖也难得久留，

 我们却称之为我们的*安居之所*：

 我们把*一步*唤作一场*赛程*：

 而天使们在他们全然启明的境地，

 天使，他们*活着*，知道*存在*究竟是什么，

 他们看尽我们语言里的全部无稽，

 他们言说*事物*，而我们的*言词*——那些画坏的*图影*——只受他们鄙夷，

 当我们用一句愚妄的修辞说道，

 *看哪，一个老人死了！*他们便

 说得切当，呼喊道：*看哪，一个男婴降生了！*

===Original===
I was dead, and right content. I lay in my coffin, with my hands folded in peace. The knight, and the lady I loved, wept over me. Her tears fell on my face.
“Ah!” said the knight, “I rushed amongst them like a madman. I hewed them down like brushwood. Their swords battered on me like hail, but hurt me not. I cut a lane through to my friend. He was dead. But he had throttled the monster, and I had to cut the handful out of its throat, before I could disengage and carry off his body. They dared not molest me as I brought him back.”
“He has died well,” said the lady.

===Chinese===
我死了，而且心满意足。我躺在我的棺中，双手安详地交叠着。骑士和我所爱的女郎在我身上哭泣。她的眼泪落在我的脸上。
「啊！」骑士说道，「我像疯子一般冲进他们中间，把他们像柴草一样砍倒。他们的剑像冰雹般砸在我身上，却伤不着我。我杀开一条路，直抵我的朋友跟前。他已经死了。但他扼死了那怪物；那只攥在它喉咙里的手，我得先割取出来，才解得开他的身体，把他带走。我带着他回来时，他们不敢阻拦我。」
「他死得其所，」女郎说道。

===Original===
My spirit rejoiced. They left me to my repose. I felt as if a cool hand had been laid upon my heart, and had stilled it. My soul was like a summer evening, after a heavy fall of rain, when the drops are yet glistening on the trees in the last rays of the down-going sun, and the wind of the twilight has begun to blow. The hot fever of life had gone by, and I breathed the clear mountain-air of the land of Death. I had never dreamed of such blessedness. It was not that I had in any way ceased to be what I had been. The very fact that anything can die, implies the existence of something that cannot die; which must either take to itself another form, as when the seed that is sown dies, and arises again; or, in conscious existence, may, perhaps, continue to lead a purely spiritual life. If my passions were dead, the souls of the passions, those essential mysteries of the spirit which had imbodied themselves in the passions, and had given to them all their glory and wonderment, yet lived, yet glowed, with a pure, undying fire. They rose above their vanishing earthly garments, and disclosed themselves angels of light. But oh, how beautiful beyond the old form! I lay thus for a time, and lived as it were an unradiating existence; my soul a motionless lake, that received all things and gave nothing back; satisfied in still contemplation, and spiritual consciousness.

===Chinese===
我的精神欢欣鼓舞。他们离去了，留我独自安息。我觉得仿佛一只清凉的手按在我的心上，把它抚得止息了。我的灵魂像一场大雨过后的夏日黄昏——落日最后的光线里，水珠还在树上闪烁，薄暮的风已开始吹起。生命那灼热的热病已经过去，我呼吸着死亡之国清冽的山间空气。这样的福乐，我从未梦见过。这并不是说，我在任何方面不再是原来的我。凡物能够死——单是这个事实，就意味着必有某种不能死之物的存在；它要么另取一个形体，如同所播的种子死了，又复生起；要么，在有意识的存在之中，或许继续过着一种纯然属灵的生活。如果说我的诸般激情死了，那么诸激情的灵魂——那些寓形于激情之中、赋激情以全部光荣与惊奇的心灵本质奥秘——却依然活着，依然以纯洁不灭的火燃亮着。它们从那渐渐消逝的尘世衣裳上面升起，显明自己乃是光的天使。可是哦，比旧日的形体美了多少！我这样躺了一些时候，过着一种仿佛无所外射的存在；我的灵魂像一泓静定的湖，接纳万物，一无所还；在静观与属灵的意识里自得满足。

===Original===
Ere long, they bore me to my grave. Never tired child lay down in his white bed, and heard the sound of his playthings being laid aside for the night, with a more luxurious satisfaction of repose than I knew, when I felt the coffin settle on the firm earth, and heard the sound of the falling mold upon its lid. It has not the same hollow rattle within the coffin, that it sends up to the edge of the grave. They buried me in no graveyard. They loved me too much for that, I thank them; but they laid me in the grounds of their own castle, amid many trees; where, as it was springtime, were growing primroses, and bluebells, and all the families of the woods.

===Chinese===
不久，他们把我抬往我的坟墓。从没有一个倦极的孩子，躺进他的白床、听着玩耍的物件正为过夜而收起的声音时，怀着比我那时所知道的更丰盈甘美的安息之满足——那时我感到棺木安稳地落在坚实的泥土上，听见落土敲在棺盖上的声响。落在棺盖上的泥土，响声并不像它一路传到墓穴边缘时那样空洞。他们没有把我葬在任何墓园里。他们太爱我了，不肯那样做，我感谢他们；他们把我安放在他们自己城堡的园地里，置身许多树木中间；那里正当春天，报春花、蓝铃花，和树林的一切家族，都在生长。

===Original===
Now that I lay in her bosom, the whole earth, and each of her many births, was as a body to me, at my will. I seemed to feel the great heart of the mother beating into mine, and feeding me with her own life, her own essential being and nature. I heard the footsteps of my friends above, and they sent a thrill through my heart. I knew that the helpers had gone, and that the knight and the lady remained, and spoke low, gentle, tearful words of him who lay beneath the yet wounded sod. I rose into a single large primrose that grew by the edge of the grave, and from the window of its humble, trusting face, looked full in the countenance of the lady. I felt that I could manifest myself in the primrose; that it said a part of what I wanted to say; just as in the old time, I had used to betake myself to a song for the same end. The flower caught her eye. She stooped and plucked it, saying, “Oh, you beautiful creature!” and, lightly kissing it, put it in her bosom. It was the first kiss she had ever given me. But the flower soon began to wither, and I forsook it.

===Chinese===
如今我躺在她的怀抱里，整个大地，以及她众多所生的每一个，都能随我的心意，作我的身体。我仿佛感到那位母亲的伟大心脏搏动到我的心里，用她自己的生命、她自己的本质存在与天性喂养着我。我听见朋友们在上面的脚步声，脚步声使我的心掠过一阵震颤。我知道帮助者们已经离去，只剩骑士和女郎留在那里，低声说着温柔而含泪的话语，说着那躺在犹带新伤的草皮之下的人。我升进一朵独自生在墓边的大报春花里，从它那谦卑而信赖的脸庞这扇窗口，端端正正望进女郎的面容。我觉得我能够借这报春花显出我自己；它说出了我想说的话的一部分；正如在从前的时日，我常为同一目的而求助于一支歌。那朵花引住了她的目光。她俯下身把它摘下，说道：「哦，你这美丽的生灵！」又轻轻吻了它一下，放进她的怀里。这是她有生以来给我的第一个吻。但那朵花不久便开始枯萎，我便离弃了它。

===Original===
It was evening. The sun was below the horizon; but his rosy beams yet illuminated a feathery cloud, that floated high above the world. I arose, I reached the cloud; and, throwing myself upon it, floated with it in sight of the sinking sun. He sank, and the cloud grew gray; but the grayness touched not my heart. It carried its rose-hue within; for now I could love without needing to be loved again. The moon came gliding up with all the past in her wan face. She changed my couch into a ghostly pallor, and threw all the earth below as to the bottom of a pale sea of dreams. But she could not make me sad. I knew now, that it is by loving, and not by being loved, that one can come nearest the soul of another; yea, that, where two love, it is the loving of each other, and not the being beloved by each other, that originates and perfects and assures their blessedness. I knew that love gives to him that loveth, power over any soul beloved, even if that soul know him not, bringing him inwardly close to that spirit; a power that cannot be but for good; for in proportion as selfishness intrudes, the love ceases, and the power which springs therefrom dies. Yet all love will, one day, meet with its return. All true love will, one day, behold its own image in the eyes of the beloved, and be humbly glad. This is possible in the realms of lofty Death. “Ah! my friends,” thought I, “how I will tend you, and wait upon you, and haunt you with my love.”

===Chinese===
那是傍晚。太阳已落到地平线下，但他玫瑰色的光辉还照着一缕羽毛似的云，那云高高地飘在世界的上空。我升起来，够到了那朵云；我把自身投在上面，随着它漂浮，望着那西沉的太阳。他沉落下去了，云变成灰色；但这灰色触不到我的心。我把玫瑰色的光彩携在身内；因为如今我能够爱，而不需要再被爱了。月亮悄悄升上来，苍白的脸上带着全部的往昔。她把我的卧榻染成幽灵般的苍白，又把底下整个大地照得仿佛一片浅色的梦之海的海底。但她不能使我悲伤。我如今明白，人唯有借着爱，而不是借着被爱，才能最切近另一个人的灵魂；是的，两个人相爱之时，使他们的福乐得以发生、得以成全、得以确保的，是彼此的爱，而不是彼此被爱。我明白，爱把一种权能赐给那爱着的人，可以及于任何被爱的灵魂，纵然那灵魂并不认识他，也使他在内里贴近那个心灵；这权能不可能不为善；因为私己一侵入，爱便止息，那从爱而出的权能也就死去。然而一切的爱，终有一天会遇见它的回报。一切真实的爱，终有一天会在所爱者的眼中看见它自己的形象，而谦卑地欢喜。在崇高的死亡之疆域里，这是可能的。「啊，我的朋友们，」我想，「我要怎样看顾你们，伺候你们，带着我的爱萦绕你们。」

===Original===
My floating chariot bore me over a great city. Its faint dull sound steamed up into the air—a sound—how composed? “How many hopeless cries,” thought I, “and how many mad shouts go to make up the tumult, here so faint where I float in eternal peace, knowing that they will one day be stilled in the surrounding calm, and that despair dies into infinite hope, and the seeming impossible there, is the law here! But, O pale-faced women, and gloomy-browed men, and forgotten children, how I will wait on you, and minister to you, and, putting my arms about you in the dark, think hope into your hearts, when you fancy no one is near! Soon as my senses have all come back, and have grown accustomed to this new blessed life, I will be among you with the love that healeth.”

===Chinese===
我漂浮的战车载着我经过一座大城市。城中微弱而沉闷的声响，像蒸汽一般升上空中——那声响——是由什么汇成的？「多少绝望的呼喊，」我想，「和多少疯狂的叫嚣，混成了这片喧嚣；在我这漂浮于永恒安宁之中的地方，它显得那样微弱；而我知道，它们终有一天会在四围的宁静里止息，绝望会死入无穷的希望，在那边看似不可能的事，在这里乃是法则！可是，哦，苍白的妇女们，愁眉的男人们，被遗忘的孩子们，我要怎样伺候你们，照料你们，在黑暗里用双臂环抱你们，把希望想进你们心里，在你们以为身边无人的时候！一旦我的感官全都回来，习惯了这新的福乐生活，我就要带着那能医治人的爱，到你们中间去。」

===Original===
With this, a pang and a terrible shudder went through me; a writhing as of death convulsed me; and I became once again conscious of a more limited, even a bodily and earthly life.

===Chinese===
正当此时，一阵剧痛与可怕的战栗贯穿了我；一阵如死般的扭动使我痉挛；我再一次意识到一种更受局限的生活——甚至是肉身的、尘世的生活。

## XXV / 第二十五章

===Original===
> Unser Leben ist kein Traum, aber es soll und wird vielleicht einer werden.

> Our life is no dream; but it ought to become one, and perhaps will.

===Chinese===
> Unser Leben ist kein Traum, aber es soll und wird vielleicht einer werden.

> 我们的生活并不是梦；但它应当成为一场梦，而且也许终将成为一场梦。

===Original===
> And on the ground, which is my modres gate,
 
 I knocke with my staf, erlich and late,
 
 And say to hire, Leve mother, let me in.

===Chinese===
> 而这大地，是我母亲的家门，

 我以行杖叩之，朝朝暮暮，

 对她说：慈母，让我进去吧。

===Original===
Sinking from such a state of ideal bliss, into the world of shadows which again closed around and infolded me, my first dread was, not unnaturally, that my own shadow had found me again, and that my torture had commenced anew. It was a sad revulsion of feeling. This, indeed, seemed to correspond to what we think death is, before we die. Yet I felt within me a power of calm endurance to which I had hitherto been a stranger. For, in truth, that I should be able if only to think such things as I had been thinking, was an unspeakable delight. An hour of such peace made the turmoil of a lifetime worth striving through.

I found myself lying in the open air, in the early morning, before sunrise. Over me rose the summer heaven, expectant of the sun. The clouds already saw him, coming from afar; and soon every dewdrop would rejoice in his individual presence within it. I lay motionless for a few minutes; and then slowly rose and looked about me. I was on the summit of a little hill; a valley lay beneath, and a range of mountains closed up the view upon that side. But, to my horror, across the valley, and up the height of the opposing mountains, stretched, from my very feet, a hugely expanding shade. There it lay, long and large, dark and mighty. I turned away with a sick despair; when lo! I beheld the sun just lifting his head above the eastern hill, and the shadow that fell from me, lay only where his beams fell not. I danced for joy. It was only the natural shadow, that goes with every man who walks in the sun. As he arose, higher and higher, the shadow-head sank down the side of the opposite hill, and crept in across the valley towards my feet.

Now that I was so joyously delivered from this fear, I saw and recognised the country around me. In the valley below, lay my own castle, and the haunts of my childhood were all about me hastened home. My sisters received me with unspeakable joy; but I suppose they observed some change in me, for a kind of respect, with a slight touch of awe in it, mingled with their joy, and made me ashamed. They had been in great distress about me. On the morning of my disappearance, they had found the floor of my room flooded; and, all that day, a wondrous and nearly impervious mist had hung about the castle and grounds. I had been gone, they told me, twenty-one days. To me it seemed twenty-one years. Nor could I yet feel quite secure in my new experiences. When, at night, I lay down once more in my own bed, I did not feel at all sure that when I awoke, I should not find myself in some mysterious region of Fairy Land. My dreams were incessant and perturbed; but when I did awake, I saw clearly that I was in my own home.

===Chinese===
从那样一种理想至福的境地沉落下来，回到那个重新合拢、将我包裹在内的影子世界，我最初的恐惧——这也原是怪不得我的——是我的魔影又找到了我，我的折磨又要重新开始了。那是一种令人悲哀的心绪激变。而这情形，说真的，倒正合于我们未死之先对死所想象的样子。然而我感到自己心里有一种平静的坚忍之力，这是我此前一直陌生的。因为，说实话，我居然还能够思想——哪怕只是想我方才一直想着的那些事——这本身就是说不出的欢愉。有这样平安的一个钟头，便叫一生的纷扰都值得奋力熬过。

我发觉自己躺在露天的地上，时值清晓，太阳还未升起。夏日的天空在我上头高高张起，满怀着对太阳的期待。云朵已经望见他从远方而来；不多时，每一颗露珠都将欢欣喜乐，因为太阳各自显现在它里面。我一动不动地躺了几分钟，然后慢慢起身，环顾四周。我站在一座小山的山顶；山下卧着一条谷地，一侧有一列山脉截断了望去的视野。可是，叫我惊骇的是，一道不断扩展开来的巨大暗影，正从我脚下起，横过谷地，一直爬上对面山峦的高处。它就横躺在那里，又长又大，又黑又有威势。我满怀病态的绝望转过身去；忽然，看哪！我望见太阳正从东边的山头探出头来，而从我身上落下的那道影子，只投在他的光芒照不到的地方。我欢喜得手舞足蹈。那不过是自然的影子，凡在日光下行走的人，无不带着这样一道影子。随着他越升越高，那影子的头便沿着对面山坡渐渐沉落下去，跨过谷地，悄悄朝我的脚边爬近。

如今我已从这恐惧中欢喜地脱身，便看清并认出了周围的地方。谷地里卧着我的城堡；我童年流连的旧地都在我四周。我急忙赶回家去。妹妹们以难以言喻的欢乐迎接我；但我想她们是察觉到我身上有了某种变化，因为一种敬意——其中带着一丝敬畏——掺进了她们的欢乐，使我心生羞惭。她们曾为我陷入极大的忧苦。在我失踪的那个早晨，她们发现我房间的地板漫了水；而那一整天，一片奇异而几乎无法穿透的浓雾，一直笼罩着城堡和庭园。她们告诉我，我离家去了二十一天。在我看来，却仿佛二十一年。而我对自己这一段新的经历，还不能感到全然安稳。夜里，我重新躺回自己的床上，心里全无把握：不知道一觉醒来，会不会发觉自己已在仙境中某个神秘的地域。我的梦接连不断，纷扰不宁；但当我真的醒来，我便清楚地看见，我是在自己家里。

===Original===
My mind soon grew calm; and I began the duties of my new position, somewhat instructed, I hoped, by the adventures that had befallen me in Fairy Land. Could I translate the experience of my travels there, into common life? This was the question. Or must I live it all over again, and learn it all over again, in the other forms that belong to the world of men, whose experience yet runs parallel to that of Fairy Land? These questions I cannot answer yet. But I fear.

Even yet, I find myself looking round sometimes with anxiety, to see whether my shadow falls right away from the sun or no. I have never yet discovered any inclination to either side. And if I am not unfrequently sad, I yet cast no more of a shade on the earth, than most men who have lived in it as long as I. I have a strange feeling sometimes, that I am a ghost, sent into the world to minister to my fellow-men, or, rather, to repair the wrongs I have already done. May the world be brighter for me, at least in those portions of it, where my darkness falls not.

Thus I, who set out to find my Ideal, came back rejoicing that I had lost my Shadow.

===Chinese===
我的心境不久便平静下来；我便开始履行我这新的身份所负的职分，心里指望着，我在仙境中的种种遭遇，多少已给了我一些教导。我能否把我在彼处游历的经验，译入寻常的生活？问题正在于此。还是说，我必须在属人的世界所具有的另一种形式里，把这一切重新活过一遍、重新学过一遍——虽然人的世界的经验，与仙境的经验原是彼此并行的？这些问题，我如今还回答不了。但我心里惧怕。

直到如今，我有时还会不安地环顾四周，看我的影子是否端正地背日而落。我从未发现它向任何一侧偏斜。我虽然常常陷于悲伤，但我投在这世上的阴翳，并不比大多数在这世上活得同我一般久的人更多。有时我有一种奇怪的感觉，觉得自己是一个鬼魂，被打发到世上来服事我的同胞，或者毋宁说，来修补我已经做下的过错。愿这世界因我而更明亮——至少在世界的那些角落里，在我的黑暗落不到的地方。

就这样，我这当初出门去寻找我的『理想』的人，归来时却满心欢喜，庆幸自己已经失掉了我的魔影。

===Original===
When the thought of the blessedness I experienced, after my death in Fairy Land, is too high for me to lay hold upon it and hope in it, I often think of the wise woman in the cottage, and of her solemn assurance that she knew something too good to be told. When I am oppressed by any sorrow or real perplexity, I often feel as if I had only left her cottage for a time, and would soon return out of the vision, into it again. Sometimes, on such occasions, I find myself, unconsciously almost, looking about for the mystic mark of red, with the vague hope of entering her door, and being comforted by her wise tenderness. I then console myself by saying: “I have come through the door of Dismay; and the way back from the world into which that has led me, is through my tomb. Upon that the red sign lies, and I shall find it one day, and be glad.”

I will end my story with the relation of an incident which befell me a few days ago. I had been with my reapers, and, when they ceased their work at noon, I had lain down under the shadow of a great, ancient beech-tree, that stood on the edge of the field. As I lay, with my eyes closed, I began to listen to the sound of the leaves overhead. At first, they made sweet inarticulate music alone; but, by and by, the sound seemed to begin to take shape, and to be gradually molding itself into words; till, at last, I seemed able to distinguish these, half-dissolved in a little ocean of circumfluent tones: “A great good is coming—is coming—is coming to thee, Anodos;” and so over and over again. I fancied that the sound reminded me of the voice of the ancient woman, in the cottage that was four-square. I opened my eyes, and, for a moment, almost believed that I saw her face, with its many wrinkles and its young eyes, looking at me from between two hoary branches of the beech overhead. But when I looked more keenly, I saw only twigs and leaves, and the infinite sky, in tiny spots, gazing through between. Yet I know that good is coming to me—that good is always coming; though few have at all times the simplicity and the courage to believe it. What we call evil, is the only and best shape, which, for the person and his condition at the time, could be assumed by the best good. And so, *Farewell*.

===Chinese===
当我想起自己在仙境中死过之后所经历的那种至福，觉得它高得使我无从把握、无从在其中寄托盼望时，我便常常想起方屋里那位智慧老妪，想起她那句庄严的确言：她知道一件事，好得不能述说。每当我为某种悲哀或真正的困惑所压，我常常觉得，自己仿佛只是暂时离开了她的方屋，很快就要从这幻象中出来，再回到里面去。有时，在这样的时刻，我发现自己几乎是不知不觉地四下张望，寻找那神秘的红色印记，怀着一种模糊的指望，想走进她的门，从她那智慧的温柔里得安慰。于是我便这样安慰自己：『我已经穿过了畏惧之门；从那门引我进入的世界回转来的路，必得经过我的坟墓。红色印记就躺在那坟上；有一天我必寻见它，并且欢喜。』

我要以叙述几天前发生在我身上的一件事，来结束我的故事。我同我的收割人在一起；正午他们歇了工，我便躺到一株巨大而古老的山毛榉树的影子底下——那树立在田边。我躺着，闭着眼睛，开始谛听头顶树叶的声响。起初，它们只奏出一种甜美的、不成字句的音乐；但不多时，那声响似乎开始有了形状，渐渐把自己塑造成言语；直到末了，我仿佛能够分辨出这些话来——它们半消融在一片环转流动的声调汇成的小小海洋里：『一件大善正在来临——正在来临——正朝你而来，阿诺多斯；』如此一遍又一遍。我觉得这声音使我想起方屋中那位智慧老妪的声音。我睁开眼睛，有一瞬间，几乎相信自己看见了她的脸——满脸皱纹，却有一双年轻的眼睛——正从头顶山毛榉的两条灰白枝桠之间，向我望来。可等我更锐利地看去，就只见细枝与叶子，还有那无限的天空，从中间的点点罅隙里凝望进来。然而我知道，善正朝我而来——善永远正在到来；虽然很少有人在一切时刻都保有相信它的单纯与勇气。我们所谓恶的，乃是那至善者为着这个人、并他当时的境况，所能采取的唯一形状，而且是最好的形状。就这样，*别了*。
